Civ3 rules compendium

How Civilization III plays, written to guide the engine's rules and AI. It covers the base game (1.29f), Play the World (1.27f) and Conquests (1.22), with Conquests as the target. Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes (Regicide, Mass Regicide, Elimination, Capture the Flag/Princess, victory-point games) are left out.

Every rule cites its source: a CivFanatics or Apolyton thread, a Firaxis statement, a patch readme, or the Civ3 Complete install (Civilopedia.txt, the editor help, labels.txt, and conquests.biq read with QueryCiv3). Each section ends with what is Disputed and what is UNVERIFIED. Don't treat those as rules: an engine choice made for one of them says so in a code comment, and the project owner's own rulings take precedence over forum sources.

Quotes taken through a web summarizer may differ slightly from the page. Check the page before relying on exact wording.

  • 1. Combat and units
  • 2. City economy
  • 3. Happiness, culture and borders
  • 4. Science, techs and wonders
  • 5. Governments
  • 6. Diplomacy, espionage and AI attitude
  • 7. Map, terrain, resources, workers, barbarians and huts
  • 8. AI behaviour, difficulty levels, civ traits, scoring and victory
  • Appendix: where the game still differs

1. Combat and units

Scope: base game, Play the World (PTW) and Conquests (C3C), patched (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes (Regicide, Elimination, Capture the Princess, victory-point games) are out of scope. C3C is the target ruleset. Where vanilla or PTW differ, the difference is called out.

Sources used

Local files are from a Steam Civilization III Complete install (.../Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete/). Quotes from local files are exact. Quotes from web pages were pulled out with a fetch tool that summarizes pages, so short web quotes should be spot-checked against the page before anything leans on their exact wording.

Key Source

APO-1GL https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/50260-1.htm ("One leader per elite unit!", May 2002; alexman relays a rule "Mike B. (Firaxis) confirmed")

Used only for its descriptions of stock 1.22 behaviour ("Under the standard game rules", "In the base game"), which come from reading the executable. The mod's own options are out of scope.
C3X-THREAD https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (the C3X mod's forum thread, 192 pages, 2021-2026). Cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (the post's URL is the thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable; his descriptions of what "the base game" or "the original game" does are code readings. Other posters' claims are play reports and may describe modded games. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".
CFC-FAQ https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ and its forum copy https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/frequently-asked-questions-civ3-play-the-world-conquests.170282/ (Ginger_Ale, 2006, covers vanilla, PTW and C3C)

Note: the repo file C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt is OpenCiv3's own text ("It is not Civ3's Civilopedia"), so it is not used as a source. The repo's ruleset also carries a promotionChance per experience level; that field is not in the BIQ (the BIQ EXPR record holds only a name, base hit points and a retreat bonus, see QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Expr.cs), so it is not a source either.

1.1 Combat resolution

  • Four kinds of combat. Ground/naval combat, bombardment, missile attacks and air combat. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "There are four types of combat: ground/naval combat, bombardment, missile attacks, and air combat."
  • A battle is fought in rounds until one side has no hit points left; the loser dies, the winner may be damaged. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "The two units trade blows until one of them loses its health bar and is destroyed. The winner may be damaged (its health bar is reduced)."
  • Each round costs the loser exactly 1 hit point. Civ3 has no firepower. Every round removes one HP.
    • CFC-CSE (Valant2): "During battle each time the unit loses on a battle roll they loose 1 hp." and "If the unit wins the turn, it causes 1hp of damage to the opponent."
    • https://forum.freeciv.org/f/viewtopic.php?p=97232 (Akechi, from memory): "There is no Firepower status in Civ 3. all combat round deals 1 damage to each round loser."
    • Pre-release Firaxis statements on firepower are not available; the Apolyton thread https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/45717-probability-and-combat (Grunthex) says: "Uh, units have between 2 and 5 hp, not 10. Depending on their veterancy."
  • Per-round odds are A / (A + D), with A the attacker's modified attack and D the defender's modified defense. Community-derived, never confirmed by Firaxis in the sources found.
    • CFC-CSE (Valant2): "Crude Turn Win % = (attackers offensive rating)/ ((attackers offensive rating)+(defenders defensive rating))"
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/made-attacker-win-probability-page.7886/page-2 (Grendel): "the probability of the attacker winning one round (causing a HP loss in the defender) is a/(a+x)."
    • The same thread gives the whole-battle odds as a negative binomial: "(a / (a+x))^y * (x / (a+x))^z * nCr(y+z-1, y-1)", summed over z = 0 .. b-1 (b = attacker HP, y = defender HP, z = rounds the attacker loses).
    • alexman (APO-WALLS #25, Jun 2003, PTW), describing bombardment in terms of normal combat: "Bombardment works like normal combat, except that the odds are determined using the bombard strength" / "of the attacking unit, instead of its attack strength." / "The defender's strength and modifiers are the same in both cases."
    • Xerxes314's vanilla combat calculator (https://civfanatics.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/01/civ3combatcalc.html, readable JavaScript) uses the same model: no firepower, per-round odds from the two strengths, HP 2/3/4/5 and 3x that for armies.
  • The game stores round odds as an integer out of 1024 (C3C 1.22 code, via C3X). C3X wraps the stock Fighter_get_combat_odds (address listed in https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/civ_prog_objects.csv) and converts its return value for its odds display as 1.0 - ((double)odds / 1024.0), the attacker's per-round win chance (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c, attacker_round_win_chance_from_fighter_odds). So the stock function returns the defender's share of a round on a 0-1024 scale, and the mod forces a certain loss by returning 1025. The mod's display then sums the same negative binomial as above (calc_attacker_combat_win_chance_from_round_chance), one HP per round from the two units' current HP. For tile improvements under bombardment, the mod's re-implementation says it keeps "the game's integer rounding and 1..1023 threshold": defense = base_defense * (100 + terrain_bonus + building_bonus) / 100 (integer division), then threshold = clamp(1, 1023, 1024 * defense / (defense + Bombard_Strength)), one rand_int(1024) roll per point of rate of fire, any roll at or above the threshold succeeding (get_tile_bombard_defense_threshold, patch_Fighter_roll_for_bombard). Read together: strengths are integer-truncated after percentage bonuses that are added together, and no single roll is ever certain (1/1024 at the extremes). Only the tile-bombard path is shown in full; that unit-vs-unit odds use the same truncation and clamp is an inference.
  • C3C beta "less streaky" combat change: announced, but not in the shipped patch notes. Tavis's patch notice (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ-iii-conquests-patch-notice.71474/, #1, 9 Dec 2003, beta 1.10): "Modified combat calculations to make combat appear less "streaky."" and (#13) "The Change was having the random number generator (rng) calculate the Results 4 times instead of 1." Tall Stranger (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/107356-1.htm, #29, Jan 2004, second-hand): "this 4 roll combat change was never added to the C3C patch." README-C3C (1.20 to 1.22) lists no combat-calculation change. Treat 1.22 combat as the single-roll model above.
  • Bombard odds use the same ratio, with bombard strength in place of attack. EDHELP's own example shows a 50% chance at equal values. EDHELP Building (Defensive Bonuses): "a unit with a bombard strength of 4 would have a 50% chance of destroying a building with a defense rating of 4." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/bombard-question.19648 (dunk): a bombard-8 cannon against a defense-3 cavalry has "an 8 out of 11 chance of hurting the cavalry."
  • Random seed is saved with the game (reloading replays the same results) unless the rule is turned off. README-V 1.21f: "Added Preserve Random Seed rule. Turning this off will allow random seeds to be changed when a saved game is reloaded (which makes combat different...)." Dan Magaha (Firaxis), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/as-promised-a-civ-iii-combat-test-map.15208/: "the game was designed so that save + reload wouldn't be a very effective or efficient way to cheat"
  • Combat arithmetic is integer, and there is one simple random generator (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16902126, page-166 #3309, C3C 1.22): corruption and combat "are all integers under the hood", and "the only places I recall seeing floating point ops in game logic are for worker strength and the science age bonus". C3X-THREAD page-165 #3294 (Flintlock): "Its main, and probably only, random number generator is a basic linear congruential generator". This fits the 1024-scale integer odds above.
  • Defender choice: the unit with the highest defense value defends. EDHELP Stealth Attack Barrier (Improvement Flag) describes a normal attack as hitting "the defending unit currently with the highest defense value". Players found that current HP also counts, so a healthy weaker unit can defend before a badly damaged stronger one:
    • https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/83449-why-do-offensive-units-defend-before-defensive-ones (Dec 2002): Dominae, "The game will always select the unit in a stack that has the best chances at winning a combat on defense." / "This is a combination of Defense value and HPs."; IthacaMike, "I've always figured that it was HPs times Defense."; theNiceOne, "But notice that factors like fortifying counts."
    • DeepO (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/59461-1.htm, Aug 2002): "defense goes to that unit who has the highest combination of HPs and Defense stat".
    • Ties (community, untested): IthacaMike, "In case of ties the defender seems to be the unit with the lowest Attack."; bongo, "In ties the weakest attacker will defend."; Dominae and theNiceOne guess the cheapest unit.
    • Ships (Soren Johnson, Firaxis, second-hand via Jaybe in thread 83449): "if there are two ships of same defensive strength (e.g., two transports), the one withOUT cargo will defend."
    • The exact weighting of defense against HP is UNVERIFIED (no test or code reading found).
    • C3X-INI (code reading) names two keys before the tie-breaks, "the same effective defense strength and the same king priority", and says shield cost is not one of the stock tie-breaks (its option to "prefer the unit with the lower shield cost before applying the base game's remaining tie-breakers" is a mod change). King units "always [are] the last to defend in a stack".
  • Units without attack can't attack; units without defense are captured, not fought (see 1.13).
  • Stealth attack lets the attacker pick its target in a stack (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Stealth_Attack: "Stealth attack allows a unit to specify which enemy unit -- in a stack -- it will attack." Submarines have it (PEDIA PRTO_Submarine: "Submarines have $LINK<Stealth Attack=GCON_Stealth_Attack>").
    • Stock 1.22 offers the choice only when the stack holds at least two eligible targets (code reading). With one eligible target the attack goes against the normal defender. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16043842, page-3 #41) describes his fix of the stock check: "So instead I implemented a more basic fix for this issue, just patching a check for "> 1" into ">= 1"." (the choice now appears with a single eligible target). Civinator (post-16853888, page-148 #2955): "In normal C3C stealth attack can only be performed if there are at least two valid stealth attack targets in a stack." Flintlock (post-16378096, page-60 #1195): "In the base game, a stealth attack won't trigger when Snake attacks Sam Hammer alone on a tile". The players treat it as a flaw; whether Firaxis meant it is not stated. The AI never uses the ability: Flintlock (post-16853988, page-148 #2959): "I don't see anywhere that the unit AI considers stealth attack at all."
  • Collateral damage (C3C ability). EDHELP Create Craters and Collateral Damage: if the defender "takes damage, then: If the defending unit is not in a city, the artillery/bombard value of the attacking unit is used to conduct a bombard attack on that space to determine if any terrain improvements are also destroyed. If the defending unit is in a city, the the artillery/bombard value of the attacking unit is used to conduct a bombard attack on that city".
  • Craters (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Craters: "When a bombardment attack which eliminates the last unit or terrain improvement in a square, a crater may be formed. Like pollution, the presence of craters reduces a square's production, commerce, and food output by one."
  • "Starts Golden Age" units. EDHELP Unit Abilities: "If victorious in combat, this unit triggers a golden age".

Healing

  • 1 HP per turn, only in friendly or neutral territory, only if the unit does not move. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "adding one $LINK<block=GCON_Experience> per turn back to its health bar. To do so, it ^* must be in friendly or neutral territory. ^* must not move for a complete turn."
  • Full heal in one turn: ground units in a city with Barracks, ships with a Harbor, air units with an Airport. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "A city with a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks> heals ground units completely in one turn." (same wording for Harbor and Airport).
  • Battlefield Medicine (small wonder, needs 5 Hospitals) allows healing in enemy territory. PEDIA BLDG_Battlefield_Medicine: "Allows military units to heal in enemy territory. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have at least five hospitals".
  • A city without a Barracks heals 2 HP per turn. BIQ heal-rate table: friendly_field 1, neutral_field 1, hostile_field 0, city 2. CFC-FAQ (Ginger_Ale): "If the unit is in a city, but it has no barracks, it will heal 2 Hit Points per turn;". Theoden (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-concerning-healing-units.113864/, #10, 2005): "I can confirm. Unts will always heal 2pts in cities." Quintillus (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/fortification.266551/, #7, 2008): "Military units inside cities without barracks/harbor/airport heal at 2 damage per turn." Whether fortifying matters is disputed in thread 113864 (shadow2k: 1 unfortified, 2 fortified; k-a-bob: "Fortification has no effect."); the BIQ has no such field. Arexander (C3X-THREAD post-17000888, page-192 #3826) restates it: "By default units in cities heal two HP per turn". Flintlock (post-16714924, page-122 #2426) adds that one stock function sets every unit's healing rate, and that the game processes every unit at the start of each turn, healing them and sinking galleys at sea (post-16644827, page-110 #2200, paraphrase).
  • Upgrading a unit heals it fully (community). Pyrrhos (thread 266551, #9): "Now there remains only one, rarely used, way to heal even redlined troops in one turn to mention - to upgrade them." massa (thread 113864, #3), on the no-move rule for healing: "Exceptions are upgrades and promotions."
  • Ships heal only in port; in C3C their passengers heal at sea (community). The Professor (thread 266551, #2): "Ships can only heal in port". PaperBeetle (same thread, #11, C3C): passengers heal aboard; "To get this healing, you need to be in friendly or neutral waters." UNVERIFIED by a test.

1.2 Experience and hit points

  • Hit points by experience level. BIQ EXPR and PEDIA agree.

    Level HP (blocks) Retreat bonus (BIQ)
    Conscript 2 34
    Regular 3 50
    Veteran 4 58
    Elite 5 66

    PEDIA GCON_Experience: "Conscripts 2 ^Regulars 3 ^Veterans 4 ^Elite units 5".

  • Unit HP bonus (editor, since 1.21f): added to the level's base HP, from -20 to +20, never below 1 HP. All stock units have 0 (UNVERIFIED for every unit; the BIQ hpBonus field is 0 for the units checked). EDHELP HP Bonus: "This bonus is added to the base hitpoint value (determined by the unit's experience level) ... the unit's total number of HP will never drop below 1."

  • What produces each level. Cities build regulars; a Barracks (Harbor for ships, Airport for planes) builds veterans; draft and barbarian-hut units are conscripts; elite only comes from combat. PEDIA GCON_Experience: "Normally, cities produce regulars. But if a city has a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks>, it produces [veterans]. ... [Elite units] cannot be built: they gain this status only through actual combat experience." PEDIA BLDG_Harbor / BLDG_Airport: "produce $LINK<veteran=GCON_Experience> naval units" / "air units instead of regulars." Sun Tzu's Art of War gives a Barracks in every city on its continent (PEDIA BLDG_Art_of_War: "Puts a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks> in every city on the same continent.").

  • Battlefield promotion after a win. PEDIA GCON_Experience: "When one of your units is victorious in battle, there is a chance it will gain combat experience and be promoted to the next level." The chances are not in the Civilopedia or BIQ. Community figures:

    Promotion Non-militaristic Militaristic
    Conscript to Regular 1/2 doubled
    Regular to Veteran 1/4 1/2
    Veteran to Elite 1/8 1/4
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/barbarians-gaining-experience.115547 (Theoden, #18): odds roughly 1 in 2, 1 in 4 and 1 in 8, "Double the chances for militaristic civs and halve them when fighting barbarians."
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/heroic-epic-militaristic-civs.26455 (Catt, #2): "A veteran militaristic unit has a 1 in 4 chance of being promoted to elite after a victorious battle against an AI civ." and "A veteran non-militaristic unit has a 1 in 8 chance." Catt (#6) credits this to a Soren Johnson post that is not reproduced.
    • PEDIA GCON_Strengths (Militaristic): "combat $LINK<experience=GCON_Experience> is gained more quickly."
    • CFC-FAQ (Ginger_Ale) gives the full table, saying the chance "depends on 3 things" (current level, Militaristic, barbarian opponent): non-militaristic 1 in 2 / 1 in 4 / 1 in 8, halved against barbarians (1 in 4 / 1 in 8 / 1 in 16); militaristic 1 in 1 / 1 in 2 / 1 in 4, and 1 in 2 / 1 in 4 / 1 in 8 against barbarians. So a militaristic conscript that wins against a civ always becomes a regular. No source for the table is given.
    • The C3C 1.22 code confirms the table (CFC-LCODE, Antal1987, #8, class_Unit::Process_Combat_Victory, re-read in the second pass). The ratio is set by the winner's level: "_Comb_Exp_Promote_Ratio = 2;" for experience 0, 4 for 1 and 8 for 2. Against barbarians (the loser's civ ID is 0): "if ( !Target_Unit->Body.CivID ) _Comb_Exp_Promote_Ratio *= 2;". Then a race check, "if ( BIC_Data.Races[Civilizations[_this->Body.CivID].RaceID] .vtable->CheckBonus(" ... "0) )", halves it: "_Comb_Exp_Promote_Ratio /= 2;". The roll is "if ( _this->Body.Status & 2 || !class_Random::Get_Random(&Random_1, _Comb_Exp_Promote_Ratio) )", so the chance is 1/ratio. Antal1987 concluded "Promotion of unit's combat experience doesn't depends on Civ having Militaristic flag", but he did not identify the bonus being checked. In the C3X mod's header for the same executable (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/Civ3Conquests.h) the race method is byte (__fastcall * CheckBonus) (Race *, __, enum RaceBonuses); and RB_Militaristic = 0x0. So CheckBonus(race, 0) is the Militaristic test, and the code gives exactly the CFC-FAQ table: conscript 1/2, regular 1/4, veteran 1/8; doubled for Militaristic (1/1, 1/2, 1/4); halved against barbarians. What the Status & 2 bit is (a forced promotion) is not explained; it might be what lies behind watorrey's claim below, but that is a guess.
    • watorrey (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/57625-1.htm, 2002, opinion): "any unit winning 2 battles in one turn is automatically promoted". No test; UNVERIFIED.

  • Experience is only the named level (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD page-170 #3399): "the game doesn't track experience outside of conscript, regular, etc." and "some unit stats like fire rate are properties of unit types not the units themselves".

  • Free defenders given with a captured or flipped city keep the default level (stock bug). Flintlock (post-16624151, page-107 #2125): "the game simply never sets the experience level on the free defenders". ynot56 (post-16681754, page-116 #2317) saw a city taken by culture or a spy give a regular unit although it had a Barracks; Flintlock calls that a bug. The intended rule is presumably the city's normal build level (veteran with a Barracks); UNVERIFIED.

  • Barbarians do not promote (C3C 1.22 test). Same thread 115547 (Theoden, #14, C3C 1.22): "all dying without any of the mech infs gaining any experience." This is about barbarian units gaining levels, not about your units promoting while fighting barbarians (see Disputed).

  • A damaged unit keeps its level. Experience is the level, not the current HP. A veteran at 2/4 HP is still a veteran for retreat and promotion. Apolyton (Ethelred) https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/75784-fast-units-no-longer-retreating-at-low-health: "The editor's retreat setting is for the experience level not the hit points."

  • A unit makes at most one leader, until it is upgraded. Units that made a leader are marked. README-V 1.29f: "Added an '*' next to experience level name for units that have produced a leader." The rule, relayed by alexman as something "Mike B. (Firaxis) confirmed" (APO-1GL #1, May 2002), quoted by nato (#11): "Each unit can produce at most one great leader." / "Each time a unit is upgraded, however, it can once again produce a great leader." The C3C code skips the leader roll for a unit that is "already extra elite" (CFC-LCODE #1). An upgraded elite drops to veteran (see 1.14), so it must be promoted again before it can make a second leader (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/90368-1.htm, CerberusIV: "if you later upgrade the unit it goes back to being veteran and can again be promoted to Elite").

1.3 Great Leaders (military)

  • A leader can appear when an elite unit wins a battle. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A {Military leader} is born in combat. This [can] occur when an $LINK<elite unit=GCON_Experience> wins a battle."
  • Chance per elite win: 1/16; 1/12 with the Heroic Epic; half that when the win is on defense. Not in the BIQ or Civilopedia. The C3C 1.22 executable confirms it (CFC-LCODE, Antal1987, #1): "Military leader appears if random(0, Ratio) == 0. That means the odds = 1 / Ratio." / "Ratio = 4 * (Small_Wonder_Count <= 0) + 12, where Small_Wonder_Count is count of Small Wonders" / "with flag "Increases_Chance_Of_Leader_Appearance"" / "If the combat is defence, Ratio doubles". That gives 1/16 attacking, 1/12 attacking with the Heroic Epic, 1/32 and 1/24 on defense (#7: "Minimum is 1 / 32, when you don't have required small wonder and a unit is in defense."). The 4 and 12 are hard-coded (#8). Community figures agree:
    • CFC-LEAD: "Each time one of your elite units wins a fight against a rival, there is a 1 in 16 chance that a leader will appear." / "your chance of getting a leader improves to 1 in 12 when you win a fight with an elite." / "The chances of getting a leader from a successful defense are 1/2 the chances while attacking."
    • https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-play-the-world/80836-how-a-leader-is-born (Catt): "The chance of a leader generation is 1/12, 1/16, 1/24, or 1/32, depending on circumstances".
    • PEDIA BLDG_Epic: "Increases the chances of the appearance of $LINK<leaders=GCON_Leaders> from victorious combat."
  • When no leader can appear (C3C 1.22 code). CFC-LCODE (Antal1987, #1) lists the cases that skip the roll: "if the unit is not elite" / "if it is naval or air" / "if it kills barbarian unit" / "if it is already extra elite" / "if it is inside an army" / "if current battle created unit count > 0; Default battle created unit type is "Leader"". So in C3C elite ships and planes never make leaders. Moosezilla (#14) recalls a leader from a naval battle in older PTW games; Puppeteer (#15) answers that PTW and C3C differ. Whether naval leaders were possible before C3C is UNVERIFIED.
  • No leaders from fighting barbarians. CFC-LEAD: "You can not get a leader by fighting a barbarian." Confirmed by the code list above ("if it kills barbarian unit").
  • The militaristic trait does not change leader odds (it only raises promotion odds). Thread 26455 (Catt): "The odds of leader generation for ALL civs, militaristic or not, is 1/16 without the Heroic Epic". CFC-LCODE (#8): "It seems, that there is no checking for militaristic flags of civilizations."
  • Armies do not generate leaders. Thread 80836 (Catt): "Armies cannot generate leaders." Killing an army can (Theseus, same thread: "You can, however, generate a GL by an elite killing an Army."). The C3C code skips the roll for a unit "inside an army" (CFC-LCODE).
  • Only one military leader alive at a time. CFC-LEAD: "You can only have one leader at a time." The rule relayed from Firaxis in APO-1GL (#11): "You can have more than one great leader per game but only one at a time." In the C3C code the roll is skipped while the civ owns any unit of the Battle-Created Unit type (CFC-LCODE: "if current battle created unit count > 0"). CFC-LEAD's "You can however produce more than one leader in one game turn" fits this only if the first leader was used (made an army or hurried) before the next elite win; that reading is an inference. C3X-THREAD agrees: Erlend Philip Wood (post-16934723, page-175 #3488, testing): "It seems there is zero chance to get another battle-created unit if you already have one!"; Flintlock (post-16935268, #3491) names "the blocker that's stopping battle created units from being spawned for players that already have one".
  • Only a win over a unit with a defence value can make a leader (community, consistent with the code's "elite unit wins a battle"). Spoonwood (C3X-THREAD post-16307796, page-43 #854): "One can only get a military great leader in the classic game by an elite defeating a unit with defense value." and "Leader fishing on AI artillery is impossible." (Capturing a defenceless unit is not a battle.)
  • Leader unit. BIQ: Leader costs 0, A/D 0/0, movement 3. The unit the editor spawns is the "Battle Created Unit" (CFC-RULES: "Determines which unit is spawned when an elite unit wins a battle."; BIQ battleCreatedUnit: Leader). A leader cannot fight and is captured or destroyed if caught alone (see 1.13).
  • Uses (C3C): make an army, or finish an improvement or small wonder in the city it is in. It cannot hurry a Great Wonder. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A Military Leader can be used to either create an $LINK<army=PRTO_Army> or to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> production of an Improvement or Small Wonder in a city. When a military leader does either of these actions he disappears."
  • Vanilla and PTW: leaders hurry anything, including Great Wonders. PEDIA-V / PEDIA-PTW GCON_Leaders: "Leaders can be used to either create an $LINK<army=GCON_Armies> or to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> production in a city." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-is-a-leader-and-how-do-i-get-one.21132 (Fez_Monk): "Using the leader is the only way of hurrying a wonder."
  • C3C scientific leaders (from being first to a tech) can hurry Great Wonders. They belong with science, not combat. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A Scientific Leader can also be used to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> the construction of any [improvement] (including a Great Wonder)".
  • A unit that makes a leader can be renamed. README-V: "Added the ability to rename units if they produce a great leader during combat."

1.4 Armies

  • Capacity 3 units, 4 with the Pentagon. The Pentagon needs 3 armies in the field. PEDIA BLDG_Pentagon: "[The Pentagon] increases the unit capacity of all the civilization's armies from three to four. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have at least three $LINK<armies=GCON_Armies> in the field." (BIQ Army capacity 3.)
  • Built by a leader in any city, or built (400 shields in every version) in the city with the Military Academy. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "Armies can be built in cities that have the $LINK<Military Academy=BLDG_Military_Academy> or a $LINK<Leader=GCON_Leaders> in them." BIQ: Army shieldCost 400. The CivFanatics FAQ v3.0 (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/misc/civilization-iii-faqstrategy-guide-v3-0/) says "you can use 200 shields to construct an army". The vanilla and PTW rule files don't support that: a raw read of the decompressed civ3mod.bic (1.29f) and civ3PTW/civ3X.bix (PTW 1.27f), at the C3C PRTO field offsets, gives Army cost 40 in both, and those files store unit costs in tens (Settler 3, Warrior 1, Spearman 2, Tank 10, against C3C's 30, 10, 20, 100). So the Army is 400 shields in all three versions (see Disputed 4).
  • At most one army per 4 cities. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "you must have at least four cities for every army you create." EDHELP Cities Needed to Support an Army: "Default is 4 cities." CFC-ARMY: dropping below the ratio keeps existing armies but blocks new ones.
  • Military Academy and Heroic Epic both need a victorious army first. PEDIA BLDG_Military_Academy: "The city with the [Military Academy] can build $LINK<armies=GCON_Armies> without the benefit of a $LINK<leader=GCON_Leaders>. Armies from this civilization are more effective in combat. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have had a victorious $LINK<army=GCON_Armies>." PEDIA BLDG_Epic has the same requirement.
  • Units in an army pool their hit points; the whole army must be killed to kill any unit in it. README-V ("Changes" from the manual): "Armies now literally share the hit points of the units within it. For example, an army of 3 regular spearman would have hit points equal to 9. To kill any unit in the army, the entire army must be eliminated."
  • Loading. Units can be loaded or unloaded only while the army is still in the city that made it; after it leaves, the contents are fixed. README-V: "Once that army leaves the city of it's construction, you cannot add or remove units from the army." BIQ allowUnloadFromArmy: false. Units in an army cannot be upgraded (CFC-ARMY: "Units in armies cannot be upgraded."). Only units with both attack and defense can be loaded (CFC-ARMY: "Only combat units (those with an attack and defense) can be loaded into an army.").
  • Abilities. Armies have zone of control and blitz (vanilla 1.17f on), movement = slowest unit + 1 (C3C), pillage (PTW 1.04f on), and see 2 tiles.
    • README-V 1.17f: "Armies now have the zone of control and blitz ability."
    • README-C3C: "an Army unit also has the "Blitz" capability (it can attack twice in the same turn), its movement rate has been increased by one, and units in an Army heal faster than they would if they were not within the Army unit."
    • README-V: "The only unit abilities that an army can inherit is mobility, and only when all units in that army have that ability."
    • README-PTW 1.04f: "Added the Pillage ability to Armies."
    • PEDIA GCON_Armies: "An army can "see" up to two squares away, due to its intrinsic scouts."
    • CFC-ARMY: "When no units have yet loaded into the army, the army will only have a movement of one." C3X-INI calls this a stock bug: "Empty armies have one less than a full move's worth of movement points regardless of how many moves their unit type has set in the editor. Under the standard game rules, this means they can only move two tiles along roads." (Two road tiles cost 2/3 of a move, so "one less than a full move" leaves under one MP.)
    • AI-only army bugs (C3X-INI): "the AI's ability to form armies with leader units is not gated by those units having the "build army" special ability", and a "major bug that prevented the AI from filling its armies".
    • Pillage (C3C): CFC-FAQ: "Armies in Conquests can also pillage without using up any movement points." TimBentley (thread 113864, #13): "Pillaging counts as a move for units other than armies."
  • Army combat strength (C3C). The fighting unit gets a share of the others' strength: Attack = A + (TA / N), N = 4 with the Military Academy, else 6, rounded down. Defense is the same with defense values. CFC-ARMY: "A is the attack of the unit currently doing the fighting." / "TA is the attacks of all the units added together." / "N is 4 if you own the military academy, else just 6." / "This formula is rounding fractions down." TA includes the fighting unit: Oystein's worked example in the article's forum thread (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-basics-of-armies.99688/, #15) sums the whole army, and he adds that terrain bonuses apply to the army's full improved defense. Arathorn (quoted by Puppeteer in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/weak-armies-in-vanilla-and-ptw.489054/, #2): "The bonus to A is equal to the sum of the A values of all units in the army divided by 6 and rounded down". The Firaxis editor help describes the Military Academy's flag differently (see Disputed: EDHELP says +25%).
  • Vanilla and PTW armies get no strength bonus. Puppeteer (thread 489054, #2): "Before Conquests an army was just a group of units that fought with collective hit points." / "A longbow army would attack with strength 4."
  • Armies get normal defensive bonuses. alexman (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/58980-1.htm, #11, 2002): "Yes, the defender bonus applies to whomever is not the attacker."
  • Which unit fights. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "An army uses one, some, or all its internal units to fight a battle, depending upon the course of the fight." CFC-CSE (Valant2, vanilla): "When an army attacks or defends the first unit continues to fight/defend until the unit is down to 1hp." CFC-ARMY (Theoden): "the strongest unit in the army will begin then descending down and ending with the weakest unit." A tested account (Theseus, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/59461-1.htm, Aug 2002): the remaining HP "are divided between all units at the start of each battle event", and the strongest unit for the job fights with its share before handing off. Code (third round): the stock routine is Unit_select_army_member_for_combat (army, hp_remaining, attacking), and it takes the army's remaining HP as input. C3X's comments on it (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c): "the base function preserves the army's HP-band rotation" and "Keep the base selector's HP allocation and tie-breaking". That is Theseus's model: the fighter is chosen by bands of the pooled HP, not by one unit fighting to 1 HP. When an army dies, its contents always die too ("If the unit has the army ability, the game will always despawn the passengers", patch_Unit_has_army_ability_for_passenger_despawn).
  • Units in an army can be promoted, but only the one doing the fighting (community). Tomoyo (thread 99688, #6); Bagatur (#5): "units in armies can get promotion with blitz."
  • Army healing. CFC-ARMY: "Armies heal at the rate of one HP per unit in the army." and "armies are also able to heal in one turn in a city containing barracks." CFC-ARMY also says armies heal in enemy territory without Battlefield Medicine ("This can also be done in enemy territory even before battlefield medicine."). Arathorn (via thread 489054, C3C): "Armies also heal in enemy territory (one hp/unit pre-Battlefield Medicine and two hp/unit after)". Vanilla was slower: Alan (thread 99688, #14, vanilla): "only one unit per turn heals, even in a barracks".
  • Army retreat. Whether armies can withdraw is disputed (see Disputed).
  • Code readings on armies (C3X-THREAD, Flintlock, C3C 1.22).
    • Loading needs only matching unit classes (post-16512698, page-79 #1575): "the condition for armies is only that the two units must have matching classes." (CFC-ARMY's "attack and defense" rule above is the stock Army unit's own limit; how the two combine is not stated.)
    • The game keeps a passenger count only for armies (post-16893099, page-161 #3205): the game keeps no general passenger count, and "For whatever reason, they added a counter only for armies." Loaded army members are handled like units in a transport (post-16607583, page-103 #2053).
    • Armies can board ships, and take more room than a single unit (post-16838218, page-142 #2832: (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "armies take up more space in transports than single units"). tjs282 (post-16826417, page-137 #2737): "Armies can be loaded onto ships because they aren't transport-units as such".
    • Pillaging costs an army no movement (post-16401798, page-64 #1270): the code "checks whether the unit has the army ability" and then skips the movement-point deduction. This confirms CFC-FAQ's "without using up any movement points".
    • The player record holds a single army count (post-16587093, page-95 #1897): "the game only stores a single army count per player".

1.5 Retreat (withdrawal)

  • Fast units withdraw when losing, unless the opponent is also fast. PEDIA PRTO_Horseman: "move fast and will [withdraw] from combat if losing (unless fighting another fast unit)." (PRTO_Cavalry has the same wording.)
  • Since 1.17f, withdrawal is a die roll that depends on experience. README-V 1.17f: "Mobile units now have to make a die roll to determine if they withdraw. Success is also modified by their experience level." Before that, fast units always withdrew (thread 100878, The Last Conformist: "In Original Release Vanilla, units always retreated if able.").
  • Retreat bonus by level (BIQ, editable since 1.21f): conscript 34, regular 50, veteran 58, elite 66. README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Added Retreat Bonus controls to Combat Experience page." CFC-RULES: "Determines the ratio of retreating when a fast unit is fighting a slow unit." EDHELP Retreat Bonus: "The higher the number listed for the retreat bonus, the more likely it is that the unit will successfully retreat when losing a fight."
  • "Fast" means more movement points than the opponent. CFC-CSE (Valant2): retreat requires higher movement and unused movement points. Jaybe (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/47749-1.htm, #2, Apr 2002, before 1.21f): "If both units have more than 1MP, there is NO retreat." He adds "The movement point and hit point rules are official (either documentation or Firaxian)" but cites neither. On that reading "fast" is base movement above 1, and two units with 2+ MP never retreat from each other. Steph (C3C modder, from memory, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/useful-patches-for-civ-3-conquests-v1-22.526285/page-3 #57) says the same: "If both units have more than 1 MP, then retreat never happens, even if one unit is faster", and (#60) "A unit with 10 MP has the same chance to withdraw than a unit with 2 MP, if they have the same experience." Whether a 3-MP unit can retreat from a 2-MP unit is still untested (two independent "no" recollections). C3X-INI (code reading) points the same way. Among its alternatives to "standard: No change from base game rules" it lists "all-units: All non-immobile units may retreat, even if they have only one move or are up against a faster opponent" and "if-faster: Units may retreat when they are faster than their opponent, for example a cavalry may retreat from a knight". Offering cavalry-from-knight as a change implies the stock rule does not allow it. It does not spell out the stock rule. C3X-THREAD posters state it outright, as a description of the stock rule the mod's options change: WildWeazel (post-16306973, page-43 #845): "Default, 2+ can retreat from 1"; Shmelkin (post-16207917, page-28 #543): "In original game fast unit can retreat only against slow unit which is hardcoded to have 1 movement". So a 3-MP unit does not retreat from a 2-MP unit (community, consistent with Jaybe, Steph and C3X-INI). Flintlock adds an oddity of the stock check (post-16747059, page-129 #2564): "Oddly, immobile units can retreat in the base game as long as they have more than one move."
  • Cases with no retreat at all (CFC-RSTUDY, BomberEscort, #19, PTW): "Retreat is not possible (no matter the experience of the unit); these instances are:" / "(1) When the fast-mover has only 1 HP left (at the beginning of the attack)" / "(2) When both units are fast-movers" / "(3) When there is no available tile for retreat" / "(4) When the fast-mover is fortified in a city (or colony?)" / "(5) When the Defender has 1hp remaining." Item 4 matches an older vanilla report (Kriton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/35589-1.htm: "if your cavalry/fast unit is fortified, it will not disengage."). C3X-INI (C3C 1.22 code reading) confirms items 1 and 4 in a stricter form and adds water: "units cannot retreat when they start combat with 1 HP or are defending a city", and "Under the base game rules, no unit may retreat defensively if it's located on a water tile." So any defender in a city is excluded, fortified or not, and ships never retreat when attacked.
  • Bombardment never triggers retreat. BomberEscort (CFC-RSTUDY, #33): "Retreating does not apply to bombardment, since bombardment is a free attack for the bombarding unit..." (A unit damaged by defensive bombard can still retreat later in the fight, #35.)
  • When the roll happens (community-tested): when the losing fast unit is about to drop to its last HP, the opponent has 2 or more HP, and the fast unit has movement left.
    • CFC-RET 104978 (Offa, #9): "Retreat possible if attacking unit drops from 2 HP to 1 HP, and is implemented before the attacker attacks with 1HP left." / "Zero chance if defender has 1 HP, or fast defender." / "Only one chance per turn to retreat, barring odd circumstances."
    • CFC-RET 100878 (boogaboo): the roll applies only if the opponent has 2HP or more, the retreating unit has 1HP, and it would have at least 1/3 movement point left after the attack.
    • A retreating unit survives with 1 HP and does not move (IbnSina, 100878: "Retreat does not require any special ground - no movement actually takes place.").
  • Retreat formula (confirmed from the C3C 1.22 code): P(retreat) = attacker retreat bonus / (defender retreat bonus + 50). Antal1987 read the check in the 1.22 executable (class_Combat_Controller::process_Combat, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/useful-patches-for-civ-3-conquests-v1-22.526285/page-4, #62): "Cavalry retreats only if random(Hoplite.Retreat_Bonus + 50) < Cavalry.Retreat_Bonus." His code line compares Get_Random(BIC_Data.CombatExperience[...Defence_Unit...Combat_Experience].Retreat_Bonus + 50) with the attack unit's Retreat_Bonus, so both units' experience levels enter (CyberChrist, #67: "the code already makes use of both defender and attacker Exp. Lvls"). If Get_Random(n) returns 0..n-1 (assumed, not stated), regular vs regular is 50/100 and an attacker bonus of (defender bonus + 50) or more always retreats. The thread does not show what checks run before this roll (movement, HP), so the measured cap near 66 below is not explained by it. He also found the RNG state is stored in the save, so reloading repeats the result (#62: "the game stores in save-file current value of a randomizer"), and that the check "toggles only after 1-st round complete". The community form came first: CFC-RET 104978 (Offa): "prob of retreat = (attacker retreat bonus) / (defender retreat bonus + 50)", described as "thought to be". BomberEscort (quoted in 100878): "(100*Attacker Retreat Bonus)/(50+Defense Retreat Bonus)" and "When the Attackers retreat value is 50 percentage points greater than the Defender, the Attacker always retreats." Flintlock confirms the roll (C3X-THREAD post-16308752, page-43 #857, code reading): in "the original game" the unit trying to retreat "rolls a number between 0 and its opponent's retreat bonus + 50"; it retreats if the roll is "less than its own retreat bonus". His test (post-16308991, page-44 #863), "attacking 40 infantry with 40 1-HP horsemen": (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "None of them retreated, 39 died, 1 won." That confirms the 1-HP exclusion below. Measured success ranges for fast attackers in valid cases (same post): "elite fast-attacker is 55-75% retreat"; veteran 50-66%; regular 43-56%; conscript 29-38%. The original study is CFC-RSTUDY (11,274 trials; the formula is Oystein's, #44: "Maybe the formula is 100a/(d+50)"). It fits most results but not all (the 80-vs-80 case measured 56.75% against 61.54% predicted). Further findings there:
    • The slow unit's own retreat bonus matters. BomberEscort (#51): "The slow-Defenders retreat bonus does factor into the ability of the fast-Attacker to retreat." / "There is no way to create a slow-unit that will always block a fast-units retreat." Oystein (#23, vanilla 1.29f): "Seems like defenders retreat bouns decreases the chance for the attacker if above 50."
    • Edited bonuses above 66 seem capped. BomberEscort (#26): "Retreat bonuses 50 or under seem to work. Anything higher that 66% seems to get capped at 66%." Krayzeenbk (#22): "the 100% retreat horsemen continued to fight in 29.69% of cases". This conflicts with the "always retreats" line above, which came from edited values; UNVERIFIED.
    • The overall chance that a fast attacker retreats in a battle is the chance it reaches 1 HP first (with the opponent on 2+ HP) times the retreat roll (Krayzeenbk, #22: "multiply that by his actual chance to retreat").
  • Defending fast units can also withdraw when attacked by a slower unit, using the same formula. CFC-RET 100878 (boogaboo): the retreat bonus applies "both to retreating when failing to attack and to retreaing when failing to defend." BomberEscort (CFC-RSTUDY, #51): "They seem to use the same formula for slow-attacker v. fast-defender...". A defender needs a free tile to retreat into (case 3 above; Krayzeenbk, #22: "this is really only a problem when the fast unit is being attacked"). Offa's "Zero chance if defender has 1 HP, or fast defender" is best read as "against a fast opponent".

1.6 Defense modifiers

  • Terrain bonuses (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat and BIQ TERR agree:

    Terrain Defense Move cost (BIQ) Wheeled can enter (BIQ)
    Grassland, Plains, Desert, Floodplain, Tundra +10% 1 yes
    Coast, Sea, Ocean (and Fresh Water Lake) +10% 1 n/a
    Marsh (C3C) +20% 2 no
    Forest +25% 2 yes
    Jungle +25% 3 no
    Hills +50% 2 yes
    Volcano (C3C) +80% 3 no
    Mountains +100% 3 no

    PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "^Marsh 20% ^River 25%* ^Forest 25% ^Jungles 25% ^Hills 50% ^Volcano 80% ^Mountains 100%". Vanilla and PTW have no Marsh or Volcano rows (PEDIA-V and PEDIA-PTW tables stop at the 14 terrain rows plus River).

  • River: +25% for a defender attacked across a river. PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "* This bonus applies to the defender if attacked from the other side of a river." BIQ/EDHELP River: "Default is 25." Code (third round, C3X-SRC counter_attack_crosses_river): "Match the base game: only the defender tile's river edge toward the attacker counts for the river defense bonus". A river elsewhere on the defender's tile gives nothing.

  • Structural bonuses (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat:

    Source Bonus
    Fortified +25%
    Fortress +50%
    Barricade (C3C) +100%
    Town Walls (town only) +50%
    Civil Defense +50%
    City (size 7-12) +50%
    Metropolis (size 13+) +100%

    "^Fortified Units +25% ^Fortress +50% ^Barricade +100% ^Town Walls +50% ^Civil Defense +50% ^City +50% ^Metro +100%". EDHELP gives the defaults for the General-page fields: Town: "Default is 0 (no bonus)." City: "Default is 50." Metropolis: "Default is 100." Fortifications: "Default is 25." Fortress: "Default is 50." City size levels (BIQ): town up to 6, city up to 12 (maximumLevel1CitySize 6, maximumLevel2CitySize 12). Vanilla's table has no Civil Defense row (PEDIA-V); PTW adds it (PEDIA-PTW).

  • Bonuses add up (they are not multiplied). EDHELP Fortifications: "Note that this bonus is cumulative with other terrain and fortification-related defensive bonuses." EDHELP Defense Bonus (Improvement Properties): "This bonus is cumulative with other improvement and general defense bonuses". CFC-CSE (Valant2): "Bonus percentages add, so 2 50% bonuses result in a 100% total bonus." Xerxes314's calculator code adds terrain, fortress or city size, river and fortify and then multiplies once: defbase*(100.0+defbonus)/100. WarpStorm (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/71511-1.htm, Dec 2002, PTW): "You add the bonuses first". See Disputed for the multiplicative view.

  • Walls: +50% in towns only, and land bombard defense 8. PEDIA BLDG_Walls: "[Walls] can be built only in town-size settlements and provide a land bombardment defense of eight, and a 50% defensive bonus to units defending in the town. When a town becomes a city, the walls no longer provide a defensive bonus." BIQ: Walls combatDefenseBonus 0.5, bombardDefense 8, flag canOnlyBeBuiltInTowns. Walls do not add to the size 7+ bonus (Mike B., Firaxis, quoted by Catt in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/70027-question-about-great-wall: "It has no effect on Cities or Metropolises (since walls are ineffective for these pop. levels)."). README-PTW: "Fixed bug which caused walls not to be obsoleted by growing from a town to a city."

  • "Walls" means any improvement with a bombard defense. EDHELP Notes & Tips: "Any Improvements or Wonders that have a defensive value against bombardment (> 0) is considered walled." alexman (APO-WALLS #10, PTW) explains the PTW bug fixed above: "In vanilla civ3, walls were defined as any improvement with either a non-zero defensive bonus," "or a non-zero bombard defense." / "Firaxis decided to change the definition of walls to be any improvement with a non-zero bombard defense." / "Unfortunately, they forgot to make all other properties besides the bombard defense" "(such as the defensive bonus) of such improvements become obsolete after size 6." / "Note that the bombard defense still becomes negated after size 6, even in PTW."

  • The wall bonus also applies against air attack; the wall's bombard defense does not. alexman (APO-WALLS #1): "When you attack a city with a ground unit (or a marine), the defender gets the defensive bonus of the wall." / "When you attack a city with an air unit, the bombard defense of walls do not apply, but the defensive bonus does."

  • Civil Defense: +50% at any size. PEDIA BLDG_Civil_Defense: "[Civil Defense] can be built in any size settlement and provides a 50% defensive bonus to units defending in the town. Civil Defense can only be built in a city that has a barracks." README-C3C: "Civil Defense requires Electronics".

  • Building defense bonuses don't add up: only the best one counts (code re-implementation). C3X's optimize_improvement_loops option, described as rewriting stock loops "to improve efficiency", replaces the stock City_get_building_defense_bonus with a loop that keeps the largest multiplier * improv->Combat_Defence over the city's active defensive buildings, if (building_defense > tr) tr = building_defense; (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c, patch_City_get_building_defense_bonus). Buildings with a bombard defense ("walls") count only while the city is at town size (Population.Size <= MaximumSize_Town), and their bonus is doubled (multiplier = 2) when the owner has a wonder with the "Doubles City Defenses" flag. So in C3C a town with both Walls and Civil Defense gets +50%, not +100%, and at size 7+ only Civil Defense counts. A raw read of the rule files shows which wonder has that flag: the vanilla and PTW Great Wall has both "Doubles City Defenses" and "Double Combat vs Barbarians"; the C3C Great Wall has only the barbarian flag (and gives free walls instead). That matches the two Civilopedias below. Flintlock describes the same flag from the code (C3X-THREAD post-16676988, page-115 #2296): "That flag doubles the defense strength of any improvement with a bombard combat value > 0" and "Level 2 & 3 cities can't benefit from that effect since they don't get any defense bonus from walls." It is empire-wide (post-16677107, #2299: "it's global as in it applies to all cities in your empire") and touches only the defence bonus, not the bombard defence (post-16677715, page-116 #2303: "It only doubles the regular defense stat."). Predator145 (post-16677223, #2301) agrees that in C3C the flag is unused by the Great Wall.

  • Great Wall. C3C: a free Wall in every city on its continent, and double combat values against barbarians. PEDIA BLDG_Great_Wall: "Doubles unit combat values versus $LINK<Barbarians=GCON_Barbarians>. ^Places a $LINK<Wall=BLDG_Walls> in each city of the continent on which it is built." Vanilla/PTW: doubles walls instead. PEDIA-V BLDG_Great_Wall: "The [Great Wall] doubles the effects of $LINK<Walls=BLDG_Walls> in cities that have them." Mike B. (Firaxis, via thread 70027): "The Great Wall doubles the defensive value of Walls in all Towns that have them."

  • Cities in resistance give no defense bonus. PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "Cities with resisters do not give defensive bonuses."

  • Terrain under a city still counts. CFC-CSE (Xerxes314): "Terrain under a city does affect its defense value." The printed manual (p. 110, quoted by royfurr in thread 7886): "Terrain bonuses are figured in BEFORE THE CITY SIZE AND WALLS TAKE EFFECT."

  • Fortify. +25%; fortify, fortress and walls stack per EDHELP's "cumulative" wording. Some players believed the wall or fortress replaces fortify (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/defense-bonuses-question.21715). See Disputed. Which units cannot fortify is UNVERIFIED. Sentry gives the same bonus, per two C3X-THREAD posters (community): Arexander (post-16670295, page-114 #2278): sentried units "get the same defensive boost as with fortifying"; Erlend Philip Wood (post-16670390, #2280): "if both options are checked (like all vanilla units), the bonus is applied." Fortifying a unit that has no moves left is a stock bug: Flintlock (post-16645129, page-111 #2204): "The code for fortify all doesn't ask for state 34 directly, that's just what it gets by trying to fortify exhausted units." / "It simply resets the unit to idle." He is not sure what state 34 is. (Intended: the unit fortifies.)

  • Fortress. +50%, gives zone of control to units inside. PEDIA GCON_Fortresses: "They get a 50% defensive bonus if attacked. ^* They have a $LINK<zone of control=GCON_ZOC> and fire at passing enemy units without receiving return fire." Built after Construction (same entry).

  • Barricade (C3C). Built on a fortress, +100%, stops enemy ground movement into the tile. PEDIA GCON_Barricades: "barricades automatically stops movement of a ground unit entering its square (unless that unit belongs to the same civilization currently controlling the barricade's square, or if that unit has a $LINK<Right of Passage=GCON_Agreements> agreement through that land." / "Barricades become a fortress if their square is pillaged." CFC-FAQ: a barricade "provides 100% bonus to defense as well as uses up all of a unit's movement points."

  • Radar Tower (C3C): +25% attack and defense to friendly units within 2 tiles, once. PEDIA GCON_Radar_Towers: "The range of the effected area is 2 tiles. Friendly units within the Radar Towers range get a +25% attack and defense bonus. This bonus can only be applied once, even if multiple towers overlap."

  • Coastal Fortress: naval bombard defense 8, and it bombards passing enemy ships. It gives no defense bonus to land units. PEDIA BLDG_Coastal_Fortress: "A [Coastal Fortress] gives the city a naval bombardment defense of eight. ^The fortress automatically bombards passing enemy ships." README-PTW: "Fixed Civilopedia entry for Coastal Fortress by removing reference to, "50% defense bonus from naval attacks". There are no direct naval attacks on land units -- only bombardment." (The BIQ still holds a naval defense bonus of 50 for it, which per this note has no target.)

  • Attacker bonuses. Terrain gives no attack bonus (CFC-CSE: "terrain does not give any offensive bonuses what so ever"). The only attack modifiers found are the Radar Tower and the bonus against barbarians.

  • Rounding of modified strengths. Unknown. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-combat-calculator.454569/ (vmxa): "I think it is rounded down, after all modifiers are applied." Xerxes314 (CFC-CSE): "I don't know why anybody would think that there's rounding involved at any stage of the calculation." UNVERIFIED.

Versus barbarians

  • Attack bonus against barbarians, by difficulty (editor DIFF field, added in 1.16f). README-V 1.16f: "Editor: Added Barbarian Combat Bonus to difficulty level tab." Values from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-difficulty-level-bonuses.37490/ (Pyrkaige, read from the PTW/1.29f editor): "Attack bonus against barbarians: 800 400 200 100 50 0" for Chieftain, Warlord, Regent, Monarch, Emperor, Deity. Arathorn's later post in the same thread gives Demigod 25 and Sid 0 (C3C levels).

    Chieftain Warlord Regent Monarch Emperor Deity Demigod Sid
    +800% +400% +200% +100% +50% 0 +25%? 0

    EDHELP Attack Bonus Against Barbarians: "Determines the attack bonus enjoyed by any player (human or AI) against barbarians." Whether it is attack only or also defense is UNVERIFIED (the field name says attack). Xerxes314's vanilla calculator applies it both when attacking and when defending against barbarians, with smaller values ("Chieftan" 400, Warlord 200, Regent 100, Monarch 50, Emperor 25, Deity 0; its changelog says "Version 1.1 - added correct bonuses for Barbarians at multiple difficulty levels"). That is probably the pre-1.16f vanilla default, before the field became editable; UNVERIFIED.

  • Great Wall doubles combat values against barbarians (all versions; quoted above). The editor help for the underlying wonder flag is narrower. EDHELP Miscellaneous Effect Flags (Wonder Properties): "Double Combat Strength vs Barbarians: Doubles defense value vs Barbarians of city that the wonder resides in". See Disputed.

1.7 Bombardment

  • Bombard stats: strength, range, rate of fire. EDHELP Rate of Fire: "Rate of fire defines how many times a bombarding unit will attack per turn." CFC-RULES: "Determines the number of times the unit initiates a bombard check when bombarding. (0-10)". EDHELP Bombard Range: "how many squares away a unit may bombard cities or units."

  • Non-lethal bombard can't kill: it stops at 1 HP. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "Bombardment inflicts losses to the defender's health bar but never completely destroys them." CFC-RULES: lethal bombard lets a unit "kill units when bombarding, and to bombard units with 1 HP."

  • A failed bombard roll hurts nobody; the bombarding unit never takes damage. freeciv thread (Akechi): "If attacker lose round during this combat, attacker bombard unit does not receive damage." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/artillery-civ3-vs-civ4.145967 (search excerpt): a bombard "does not expose the artillery to harm."

  • Each hit removes 1 HP; a bombard is RoF separate rolls against the target's modified defense. CFC-CSE (Valant2): "I assume all successful bombarbments on a military units only results in 1hp lost." Thread 19648 (warpstorm): "You fire twice. Each shot is tallied up individually." alexman (APO-WALLS #25, PTW): "If the attacker wins, the defender gets one HP of damage. If the attacker loses, nothing happens." / "Bombardment consists of as many rounds of combat as the ROF value of the attacking unit." / "So each bomardment can result in between zero and ROF hit points of damage." Against a unit the defense used is "the modified defensive strength of the defending unit (which always includes any wall and terrain bonus)" (#1). BomberEscort's calculator thread (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ-iii-combat-calculator-exe.75765/, #19) gives the chance of no damage as "(BombardAttack / (BombardAttack + ModifiedDefense))^RateOfFire" (he means the defender's share; his example is (6/10)^3 = 0.216).

  • Bombarding a city can damage a unit, kill 1 citizen, or destroy a building. Thread 19648 (Park Ranger) lists "destroy an improvement," "kill 1 population point," and "damage a defending unit"; and "bombarding will not kill citizens if the city is only size 1."

  • Which target a city bombard hits depends on the version.

    • Vanilla (early 2002): one third each. Whoha (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/44680-1.htm, citing korn469's tests): "it does a random check for what to target", a 1/3 chance each for population, building or unit, then "another random chance with the defense of the pop point/building/unit to determine if it actually hits that."
    • PTW (alexman, APO-WALLS, 2003): #25 "When you target a city, you have a 50% chance of targetting the top unit," / "a 25% chance of targetting population, and a 25% chance of targetting improvements." / "If during an attack an improvement is targetted but the city has no improvements, it's an automatic miss." / "Similarly, if population is tagretted but the city is of size 1, it's a also a sure miss."
    • C3C 1.22 (CFC-BOMB122, justanick, #1): a fixed priority order by attacker type. Land artillery: "City walls are hit first." / "If there is are no city walls or the settlement has already reached city level, then land units are hit." / "If neither city walls nor land units are left to be hit, then citizens or buildings are hit," / "apparently with same probability." / "Ships and air units cannot be hit with land based artillery." Ships: "A coastal fortress is hit first." then ships, then air units, then land units, then citizens or buildings. Air units: "If air units are left in the settlement, then air units or citizens are hit." / "If no air units are left in the settlement, then ships, citizens or buildings are hit." then land units, citizens or buildings. Cruise missile: "Ships are hits first." then air units, land units, citizens or buildings. "Left to be hit" means units that can still lose HP: a unit at 1 HP no longer counts against non-lethal bombard (PaperBeetle, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-makes-bombardment-fail.307328/, #5, C3C: "units are always targetted while they still have hp left to lose."). Lanzelot (page 2) suggests the order may be a strong bias rather than a fixed rule ("city walls are hit with 95% probability"). C3X-INI describes the stock C3C rule as fixed: "PTW-like means there is a 1-in-3 chance they will target each population, buildings, and units. This is unlike Conquests artillery targeting, which always goes after units first." (It agrees with justanick that units come before citizens and buildings; it says nothing about walls.)
    • C3X-THREAD on the same point. Flintlock, C3C code (post-16393188, page-63 #1247): "the normal bombard method prioritizes damaging units." and (post-16311320, page-44 #878): "The bombard code is inflexible in that it rolls first to determine a target then rolls for damage." Predator145 (post-16262382, page-32 #637, community): "In C3C artillery is "smart" and only targets units until all of them have been redlined", air bombard "can lethally affect air units and will smartly target them first", and cruise missiles "will always target sea units first". These agree with justanick's order.
    • PTW: three accounts disagree. alexman's test (above) gives 50% the top unit, 25% population, 25% buildings. Predator145 (same post): "In vanilla and PTW, artillery units target cities the same way air bombard does" with "a 1/3 chance to either hit the units, the improvements or the population"; BlueSox14 (post-16523719, page-81 #1620), summarising Sullla: "In PTW, bombardment would have a random 1/3 chance of striking either units, population or buildings."; Flintlock (post-16369990, page-56 #1116): PTW artillery "had a 2/3 chance of attacking buildings or pop in a city instead of the garrisoning units" (a recollection; he reads the C3C executable, not PTW's). alexman's figures come from a test, so they are the stronger source for PTW. The vanilla 1/3 split (korn469's tests, above) is not in doubt.
    • Aircraft in an airfield can't be damaged by bombardment, and they shield the tile. C3X-INI: "The game rules do not permit aircraft in an airfield to be damaged by bombardment", and "if you bombard a tile with a road, airfield, and fighter on it, you will do no damage because the attack will target the fighter which is invulnerable".
    • Tile improvements have a fixed bombard defense. C3X-INI: "In the base game, tile defense is hardcoded at 16." The Citizen and Building values in the editor are "base strength values", not bonuses. Flintlock gives the tile roll in words (C3X-THREAD post-16584231, page-95 #1889): "the chance to do damage on each roll equals 1 - X/(S + X)", with S the bombard strength and X = "floor((100 + D) * 16 / 100)", D the tile's total defence in %; (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "The attacker gets to roll multiple times up to its rate of fire and if it succeeds on any roll then the tile is damaged." The defence (post-16583488, #1884): "The chance to do damage depends only on the tile's terrain, whether it has a fortress or barricade" / (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "I don't see any references to the building or citizen defense bonuses." This matches the integer form in 1.1.
    • Land artillery can't damage aircraft; ships can kill them on land (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16021251, page-1 #10): "land artillery can't damage aircraft" and "naval artillery can not only damage but kill aircraft on land". This agrees with justanick's order (land artillery never hits ships or air units; ships hit air units). Flintlock also mentions a stock "weird little bombard rule that limits what land artillery can damage" (post-16507213, page-78 #1558), probably the same rule.

  • Civil Defense absorbs building hits (C3C). CFC-BOMB122 (#1): "If a regular building(meaning neither a coastal fortress nor city walls) is hit and a civil defence is in the settlement," / "then it is the civil defence that is hit."

  • Building and citizen defense against bombard: 16 (since 1.17f; BIQ 16). README-V 1.17f: "Building and citizen defense against bombardment increased to 16." BIQ buildingDefensiveBonus 16, citizenDefensiveBonus 16. alexman (APO-WALLS #25, PTW): "Population and improvements are treated like a unit with a defensive strength of 16." Steph (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/defensive-artillery-in-city.329600/, #17): "if it's 16 and the bombardment strength is 8, then the bombardment has 33% to destroy the building." EDHELP still says "Default is 4" (text left over from before 1.17f).

  • Walls and Coastal Fortresses are a shield that must be broken first; their 8 is not added to the 16. alexman (APO-WALLS #25): "When you use a ground unit to bombard a city with walls, you have to first destroy the walls" "(by damaging them as if they were a unit with a defensive strength of 8) before you can harm anything else." / "After the walls are destroyed, each subsequent bombard unit can destroy a unit, improvement, or population in the city." (#1): "If the walls win, then all improvements, citizens, and units in the city are safe." / "This happens only if the city is size 6 or smaller." Naval bombardment works the same way with a Coastal Fortress instead of walls (#1); against ships and planes, walls are an ordinary improvement (#7: "walls are just as likely to be destroyed as any other improvement in the city").

  • Great Wall walls make a town immune to land bombard (C3C bug). Walls from the Great Wall are hit first and are rebuilt at once. Lanzelot (CFC-BOMB122, #13): "In a town with "Great Wall - induced" walls, all buildings and units" / "are immune to bombardment." / "(Because the wall is always hit first, and if it gets destroyed, the Great Wall immediately "resurrects" it...)". He adds that the Great Wall's own city does not get the free wall: "The town that builds the Great Wall does not get a free wall from that!" (tmn, #14, disputes this.) jarred! (page 2): "It seems the walls are immediately restored." C3X-THREAD posters describe the bug differently: the hit goes to another building instead. sirati97 (post-16319627, page-46 #916): "bombarding a city with a wall given by a wonder will instead destroy random other buildings"; Pifou42 (post-16891617, page-160 #3187) calls it a known vanilla bug where "bombarding a city with virtual walls given by Great Wall" can destroy the next building. Both are play reports, and both call it a bug. The intended handling of a hit on a free wall is not documented; the engine should not reproduce either form.

  • Great Wonders can be destroyed by bombardment until the owner reaches the Industrial Age (C3C 1.22, community). CFC-BOMB122 (#1): "If the bombardement occurs against a settlement of a civilization that has reached the industrial age," / "then any world wonder in this settlement is immune against any bombardment." / "Before the target civilization has reached the industrial age this is not the case". zerksees (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/using-bombardment-to-wreck-great-wonder.278312/, #1, C3C 1.22): "Last night I destroyed Sun Tzu Art of war with a trebuchet!" Dominus Romae (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/offensive-defense-the-tactical-and-strategic-values-of-the-great-wall.128868/, #9) says the opposite: "No, you can't destroy Great Wonders with bombardment." See Disputed. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16725029, page-124 #2465) gives a narrower window: "the game has a really weird little rule that you can destroy wonders by bombardment but only in the ancient era." Earlier, as hearsay (post-16320772, page-46 #918): "I'd heard it's possible to destroy wonders through bombardment under some circumstances". He does not say whose era counts (Disputed 15).

  • Air units in a city can be killed by any bombard; land and sea units only by a lethal one. CFC-BOMB122 (#1): "Land units can only be destroyed by bombardment, if the bombarding unit has the capability "lethal land bombardment"." / "Air units can be destroyed by bombardment." / "There is no such attribute as "lethal air bombardment"." justanick (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/air-battle-questions-only.565813/, #2): "your bombers and your battleships can destroy enemy aircrafts while they are at their base by bombing the base."

  • Bombard can hit tile improvements outside cities, and PTW+ airfields, outposts and radar towers. README-PTW 1.01f: "You can destroy through bombarding Airfields, Outposts and Radar Towers." README-PTW: "Fixed bug where auto-bombard would not auto-bomb multiple terrain improvements in neutral territory."

  • Ships in port are extra vulnerable (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Bombardment_in_Ports: "naval units "in port" (i.e., in a coastal city square being bombarded) are especially vulnerable to bombardment attacks." The size of the effect is UNVERIFIED.

  • Lethal bombard (editor ability since 1.21f; used by stock units in C3C only). README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Added new unit abilities to allow bombarding to be lethal against land and/or sea units." C3C stock units with it (PEDIA): Cruise Missile, Bomber, Stealth Bomber, Stealth Fighter, F-15, Hwach'a (land and sea); Fighter, Jet Fighter, Dromon (sea only). Examples: PRTO_Bomber: "The Bomber has both Lethal Land and Sea Bombardment." PRTO_Fighter: "The Fighter has Lethal Sea Bombardment." Vanilla/PTW have no lethal units in the stock rules (freeciv thread, Akechi: "If vanilla Civ 3, there is no Lethal Land/Sea Bombardment unit."). The Cruise Missile ability implies lethal bombard by itself (community): Predator145 (C3X-THREAD post-16401109, page-64 #1266): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "giving a unit the cruise missile ability will automatically give it lethal land and sea bombard"; Belofon (post-16305295, page-41 #809, "IIRC") says the same. Before C3C no bombard unit could kill: Sullla, quoted by Ozymandias (post-16319042, page-46 #912): pre-C3C bombard units "could NEVER kill other units", the cruise missile being the only exception.

  • Stock bombard units (BIQ, C3C): bombard / range / rate of fire.

    Unit B R RoF
    Catapult 4 1 1
    Trebuchet (C3C) 6 1 1
    Cannon / Hwach'a 8 1 1
    Artillery 12 2 2
    Radar Artillery 16 2 3
    Cruise Missile 16 4 3
    Archer / Bowman 1 0 1
    Longbowman / Musketeer 2 0 1
    Guerilla 3 0 1
    TOW Infantry 6 0 1
    Frigate 3 1 2
    Ironclad / Destroyer / AEGIS Cruiser 6 1 / 1 / 2 2
    Cruiser 7 1 2
    Battleship 8 2 2
    Bomber 12 0 3
    Stealth Bomber 18 0 3

    Range 0 units can only bombard defensively (see next item). Air units use operational range instead of bombard range.

  • Defensive bombard ("Artillery Free Shot"). When a land tile holding a bombard-capable unit is attacked, each such unit (other than the one defending) fires once at the attacker before the battle. README-V ("Changes"): "Artillery Free Shot: When a land tile with a bombard capable unit in it is attacked, that bombard unit will take one free shot at the attacking unit." APO-ZOC (Harovan): "The defense bombard is used once in a turn per unit with this feature and only, if the unit isn't the defender itself." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/zones-of-control.682034/ (Flintlock, #20): "Yes, defensive bombard only uses bombard strength." In C3C, Archers and Longbowmen got a range-0 bombard for this (APO-ZOC, Willem: "As of Conquests, Archer/Longbowmen units now get one as well"). tjs282 (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/coastal-forts-and-bombardment.690690/, #4, 2024, C3C): "DB happens when a stack containing a bombard-capable unit gets attacked, provided that the bombarding unit" / "(1) has not already fired a DB during that interturn, and (2) is not the unit being attacked." / "IIRC the DB-unit's B-value is compared with the attacking unit's A-value, using the usual combat-success RNG dice-roll" / "if the DB hits, the attacker loses 1 HP before it engages its actual target." So each bombard unit fires once per enemy turn, not once per attack; README-V's "one free shot" fits that. Which strength of the attacker is used (attack or defense) is UNVERIFIED (tjs282 says "IIRC"). The free shot never kills, and only land and sea bombard units fire it (code reading). C3X-INI lists, as mod options that change the stock rule: "lethal: Attackers may be destroyed by defensive bombard if the bombarding unit has the appropriate lethal land/sea bombard ability", "aerial: Air units may perform defensive bombard", "not-invisible: Attacking units are immune to defensive bombard if they are invisible and not detected", "blitz: Units with blitz may perform defensive bombard multiple times per turn" and "docked-vs-land: Docked ships (i.e. in a city) can perform defensive bombard against land units attacking their city". So in stock 1.22 the shot can't kill even with a lethal unit, aircraft don't fire it, undetected invisible attackers are still shot at, blitz gives no extra shots, and ships in a city don't fire at land attackers. One shot per attack, from the strongest eligible bombarder (code, third round). The stock routine is Fighter_find_defensive_bombarder (attacker, defender). It returns a single unit, and C3X wraps it (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c, patch_Fighter_find_defensive_bombarder). When any of its DB options are on, the mod re-creates the choice, and with them off it calls the stock function. The re-creation gives no shot when the attacker's defence strength is below 1 or the attacker has 1 HP left. Otherwise it takes the unit on the defending tile with the highest bombard strength, among units with bombard > 0 that are not cruise missiles, have not fired DB this turn (unit status bit USF_USED_DEFENSIVE_BOMBARD = 0x40, "the unit has performed def. bombard already this turn", Civ3Conquests.h), are not the defender or the defender's army, and are of the attacker's class (land against land, sea against sea). So each attack draws at most one free shot, and a stack with three catapults answers three separate attacks, not one attack three times. This refines README-V's "one free shot", and the opening line of this bullet should be read that way. Skipping attackers with no defence fits the shot rolling bombard against the attacker's defence, as a normal bombard does, but that is an inference: the damage roll itself (Fighter_damage_by_defensive_bombard) is not re-implemented. Flintlock states the stock rule directly (C3X-THREAD post-16536435, page-83 #1655): "In the base game, defensive bombard can do at most one damage and will never activate against an attacker with one HP, so it can never outright kill an attacker." / "They roll only once. Defensive bombard doesn't take rate of fire into account at all." So a DB shot is one roll, at most 1 HP, whatever the unit's rate of fire. Civinator (post-16536154, #1651) agrees: (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the programming always cut it down to a ROF of 1". Multiplayer bug (play report). Sutsuj (post-16194164, page-26 #510): "In multiplayer, defensive bombardment units get extreme buffs." / (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "The more players in the game, the stronger they are." A shipped bug if true; the engine follows the single-player rule.

  • Cruise missiles die after bombarding. CFC-RULES: "a unit which, when bombarding, will die after it is done bombarding." PEDIA GCON_Combat: missiles "never return to its base and the attacks can destroy enemy units."

  • Artillery have no attack or defense and must be escorted. PEDIA PRTO_Artillery: "Artillery has no attack or defense value, so they should be protected by an escort." If caught alone they are captured (see 1.13).

  • Bombard uses the attack for the turn. freeciv thread (Akechi): "if unit does not have Blitz ability, this unit can only one attack per turn". Whether bombarding costs all movement is UNVERIFIED.

1.8 Zone of control

  • ZOC does not block movement; it gives a free shot at an enemy moving past. PEDIA GCON_ZOC: "Such units attack enemies that enter the zone of control without initiating a full battle." EDHELP / CFC-RULES: "Determines if the unit will automatically bombard passing units." APO-ZOC (Harovan): "In Civ2 or CtP the movement would be blocked due to ZoC, in Civ3 it is still possible, but the other civ gets a free shot on the passing unit."
  • Units with ZOC (C3C): AEGIS Cruiser, Armies, Cavalry, German Panzer, Mech Infantry, Modern Armor, Mongol Keshik, Ottoman Sipahi, Modern Paratroops, Radar Artillery, Russian Cossack, Spanish Conquistador, Tank, and any unit in a fortress or barricade (PEDIA GCON_ZOC, GCON_Fortresses, GCON_Barricades). Vanilla also lists Marines and (plain) Paratroops and lacks the PTW/C3C unique units (PEDIA-V GCON_ZOC).
  • Trigger: an enemy moves from one tile next to the ZOC unit to another tile next to it.
