APO-ZOC (geniemalin): the shot triggers only when an enemy moves between two squares adjacent to the unit. https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/110683-how-do-the-zone-of-control-work (CerberusIV): "It only works when an enemy unit moves from a tile adjacent to the fortress to another tile also adjacent to the fortress".
  • Each shot takes 0 or 1 HP and never kills. Thread 682034 (Theov): "The outcome is binary and costs one HP." Ozymandias (same thread, quoting civ-wiki.de): "The control zone has no effect on units that have only one health point."
  • Hit chance (reverse-engineered, C3C): 1 - (D*16 / (D*16 + A)), D = the moving unit's defense with no terrain bonus, A = the higher of the ZOC unit's attack or bombard, using the strongest ZOC unit in the stack. Thread 682034 (Flintlock, #3): "1 - (D16 / (D16 + A))"; "The game will always select the strongest available unit to try to do damage."; Ozymandias: "The defense value of the moving units does not enjoy bonuses from the terrain." This implies low odds (for example A 16 vs D 2: 16/48 = 33%). Flintlock restates it in C3X-THREAD (post-16537815, page-84 #1671): "The formula for the chance to do damage by ZoC is: 1 - (D16 / (D16 + A))", with D the moving unit's defence and A the ZoC unit's attack or bombard strength; it "also applies to coastal fortresses". His examples: strength 160 against defence 8 gives 55.6%, strength 8 gives 5.9%. He adds: "I don't think the AI understands ZoC at all". A later post (post-16559552, page-90 #1785: "They use their bombard value", with attack usable only against the same class) answers a question about air units, which have ZOC only under C3X's rules, so it most likely describes the mod and is not used for stock. The shot comes only once the unit actually moves (post-16589155, page-96 #1907): "the unit doesn't experience ZoC until it moves and it doesn't move until it has dealt with any enemy units".
  • Coastal Fortress quirks (community). Erlend Philip Wood (C3X-THREAD post-16536518, page-83 #1659): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "If a ship already adjacent to the Fortress moves to another adjacent tile, then the CF will (attempt to) shoot at it." / "a CF inside a bay (where there is only 1 coastal tile adjacent) will never fire at enemy ships." Both follow from the adjacent-to-adjacent trigger above.
  • Walls in a town give its units ZOC; the effect ends at size 7 (community, C3C). Thread 682034 (Ozymandias, quoting civ-wiki.de): "All units in a settlement (size 1 to 6) with a city wall automatically have a control zone." justanick (thread 690690, #5, 2024), after tjs282 notes that fortresses give ZOC: "This is also true for towns with walls. Notably this effect is lost when the town becomes a city(size 7+)." Not confirmed by a Firaxis text.
  • Stock limits on who exerts ZOC (C3X-INI, mod options that change the stock rule): "amphibious: Land units may exert zone of control over sea units and vice-versa", "lethal: Units may be destroyed by zone of control", "aerial: Air units can exert zone of control", "not-from-inside: Land units may not exert zone of control while inside a transport". So in stock 1.22 land and sea ZOC don't cross, ZOC never kills, air units have none, and land units exert ZOC even from inside a transport.
  • One unit can take several ZOC shots per enemy turn (unlike defensive bombard). tjs282 (thread 690690, #4): "But unlike DB, the same unit can make multiple ZoC-shots per interturn."
  • Coastal Fortress ZOC uses the same trigger. tjs282 (thread 690690, #2): "The CF Zone of Control can/will only activate if a ship moves from one of the 8 (Coast) tiles" around it to another of them.

1.9 Movement

  • Terrain costs are in the table in 1.6 (BIQ): 1 for flat land and water, 2 for hills, forest and marsh, 3 for mountains, jungle and volcano. EDHELP Movement Cost: "Determines the base movement cost for a tile of the selected terrain type."
  • Roads: 1/3 movement point per tile regardless of terrain. PEDIA TFRM_Road: "units moving along roads expend one third of a movement point per square, regardless of terrain type." BIQ movementAlongRoads 3.
  • Railroads: zero movement. PEDIA TFRM_Railroad: "Units moving along a railroad expend zero movement points." Railroads need Steam Power plus Iron and Coal (same entry). README-C3C: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."
  • Rivers give no movement bonus in Civ3 (rivers run between tiles). Jon Shafer, in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/58801-rivers (April 2002, before he joined Firaxis): "Rivers are placed in between spaces in Civ III, unlike in Civ II".
  • Roads cross rivers only after Engineering. PEDIA TECH_Engineering (all three versions): "{New Ability} Roads now bridge rivers." Before Engineering, a road move across a river does not get the road rate; how much it costs instead is not in the Civilopedia. Community claims that crossing an unbridged river costs a full movement point: TheBB (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/rivers-and-roads.120751/, #2, 2005, C3C): "a whole movement point is lost when crossing a river"; mastertyguy (#3): "It is also in vanilla". UNVERIFIED by a test. Bridges go when the road is pillaged (Mark Anthony, #8: "to destroy the bridge (available only after engineering), you must also destroy the road leading up to it.").
  • Wheeled units can't enter mountains, volcano, jungle or marsh except along a road. PEDIA PRTO_Catapult: "catapults are wheeled so they may not enter $LINK<mountain=TERR_Mountains>, $LINK<volcano=TERR_Volcano>, $LINK<jungle=TERR_Jungle> or $LINK<marsh=TERR_Marsh> terrain unless along a road." BIQ impassableByWheeled is set on exactly those four terrains.
  • "All Terrain as Roads" (Explorer) and "Ignore Movement Cost" per terrain. PEDIA PRTO_Explorer: "unarmed, non-combat units that treat all terrain as if it were a road." EDHELP Ignore Movement Cost: "Units with a terrain movement bonus will ignore movement costs of the selected terrain types." README-V 1.21f: "Updated All Terrain As Roads unit ability to work with water units."
  • Air units ignore terrain costs; water units pay terrain cost. README-V 1.29f: "Changed air units to ignore movement costs of terrain types". README-V 1.21f: "Updated water unit movement to take into account the cost of the terrain type."
  • Naval movement bonuses: Great Lighthouse +1 (PEDIA BLDG_Lighthouse: "all naval unit movement rates are increased by one."), Magellan's +1 (PEDIA BLDG_Circumnavigation: "The movement rate of all naval units is increased by one."), Seafaring trait (PEDIA GCON_Strengths: "Ships move faster and are less likely to sink in the sea or ocean.").
  • Partial moves. A unit with 1/3 move left can still enter any tile. Community support only: general-jcl (C3X-THREAD post-16827533, page-138 #2751), on the stock rule a mod option would change, says a unit (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "can still climb a nearby mountain" with too few moves left. No test or code reading found.
  • Movement points (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD page-6 #110): "In the base game, assuming road movement rate is left at 3, one full move is 3 movement points (MP)". Railroad moves cost nothing: Tsubasanut (post-16044203, page-3 #44) found the stock rail cost zeroed by an XOR in the move-cost code. Flintlock (page-4 #70): "Movement in the base game doesn't scale with map size".
  • Movement quirks (code readings, C3X-THREAD, Flintlock).
    • A unit can't enter a tile holding another civ's units, Hidden Nationality or not (post-16569253, page-91 #1808): the code "prevents units from moving onto tiles that are occupied by other civs' units, ignoring HN status".
    • Skipping a turn empties the unit's moves (post-16441638, page-71 #1419): "Skipping a unit's turn removes all its remaining moves."
    • Goto paths are recomputed as the unit moves (page-148 #2945): "The game simply stores the destination tile and recomputes the path along the way." Pathfinder bugs: it stores remaining MP per step in one byte (#1419), and it (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "gives up" after adding 256 directions (page-148 #2943).
    • A stack moved together stops where its slowest member must (ynot56, post-16163674, page-22 #425): it "goes to the maximum distance of the unit with the lowest MP".
  • Foreign roads and railroads give no movement bonus inside their owner's territory without a Right of Passage. PEDIA GCON_Territory: "Rival civilizations cannot benefit from the roads or rails within your territory without a $LINK<right of passage=GCON_Agreements> agreement." PEDIA GCON_Agreements (Right of Passage): "You agree to allow his or her military units to move through your $LINK<territory=GCON_Territory> and use your transportation network (roads and railroads)." Roads in neutral territory, and roads in a tile that becomes yours when you take a city, work normally (the territory rule above; the War Academy's "Early Industrial Railroad Intrusion", https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/military/early-industrial-railroad-intrusion/, depends on this).
  • Units that win an attack advance into the tile only if it is empty. README-V 1.21f: "Fixed bug involving extra movement costs if the unit can't advance after combat." The exact advance rule is UNVERIFIED.

1.10 Naval units

  • Ships can't slip between two land tiles on a straight-line move (code, third round). Antal1987's reading of the 1.22 class_Unit::Can_Pass_Tile (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3c-new-movement-possibilities.529671/ #1, 2014, as reported by the fetch tool). A move into a city of the unit's own civ, or by an air unit, always passes. For a naval move from water to water in directions 2, 4, 6 or 8 (E, S, W, N in C3X's enum direction: the moves that pass the corner between two tiles), the code checks the two flanking tiles: "If the direction is even, it checks neighbour tiles (clockwise and counter clockwise) to be water". The move is allowed if either flank is water, and refused only if both are land. So two land tiles that touch only at a corner close the water passage between them. A ship can still enter a coastal city of its own civ (the bypass above), which is how a city acts as a canal (Oni Ryuu, CFC 526285 page 11 #213: "The canals are just the ability to place cities next to one another"). The land branch, in the same post: a land unit can't enter water unless it boards a transport, and on impassable terrain (or wheeled-impassable terrain for wheeled units) it needs a road on both the origin and the target tile.

  • Curragh and Galley must end turns in coast; they risk sinking in sea or ocean. PEDIA PRTO_Galley: "A galley must end each turn in a $LINK<coastal=TERR_Coast> square -- they are in danger of sinking if they end their turn in a $LINK<sea=TERR_Sea> or $LINK<ocean=TERR_Ocean> square. If $LINK<The Great Lighthouse=BLDG_Lighthouse> is built, galleys may safely end turns at sea, but never ocean squares." (PRTO_Curragh has the same rule.)

  • Caravel is safe in sea, at risk in ocean. PEDIA PRTO_Caravel: "may safely traverse $LINK<coast=TERR_Coast> and $LINK<sea=TERR_Sea> squares. If it ends a turn in an $LINK<ocean=TERR_Ocean> square, it is in danger of sinking."

  • The sink flags are per unit: "Sink in Sea" and "Sink in Ocean". EDHELP Unit Abilities: "Sink in Sea Unit dies if it ends its turn on a sea square Sink in Ocean Unit dies if it ends its turn on an ocean square". CFC-RULES: "can possibly sink and die without the right technology". The techs that make sea and ocean safe are given in PEDIA TERR_Sea (Astronomy) and TERR_Ocean (Navigation or Magnetism) for trade and travel. CFC-FAQ (Ginger_Ale, who also wrote CFC-SINK) says those techs remove the risk for every ship: after Astronomy "all ships (even old curraghs and galleys) will be able to end their turns at sea safely"; after Navigation or Magnetism "every ship is safe to end their turns in any tile." This conflicts with PEDIA PRTO_Galley ("never ocean squares", said of the Great Lighthouse only) only if read strictly; no test of galleys in ocean after Navigation was found. Community rule, UNVERIFIED by a test.

  • Sinking chance per turn ended in danger: 50%, or 25% for Seafaring civs (75% survive) (C3C 1.22 test). CFC-SINK: "If you are Seafaring, it's 75% of survival, regardless of difficulty level, terrain, ship, turns spent in water." / "If you are not Seafaring, it's 50%." Tested on curraghs and caravels in ocean, galleys in sea.

  • Ships do not attack land units; they only bombard them (PTW note above: "There are no direct naval attacks on land units -- only bombardment."). script.txt #NOTAMPHIB: "Only Marine units can attack from the sea." and #LANDCONQUER: "Only [ground] units can be used to attack settlements." Whether a ship can attack a land tile that holds only non-combat units is UNVERIFIED.

    • Known C3C 1.22 bug: AI galleys sometimes attack a coastal city directly (the "privateer bug"). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/new-attack-ability-galleys-can-attack-units-in-cities.677328/ (Takhisis, #4, "Civ3 Complete 1.22"): "a roaming AI galley finds a city of mine and sails up close, then declares war and attacks head-on." r16 (#2): it is a variation of the privateer bug and "does not require any ship in port." Not a rule. Flintlock traced the cause (C3X-THREAD page-147 #2940, code and test): "the pathfinder in rare cases generates paths through other civs' cities for AI naval units", and "when I tested with the unmodded EXE, the bug still occurred." The older form, with a Hidden Nationality ship as a city's only garrison, is also stock: tjs282 (post-16376491, page-59 #1179) and Civinator (post-16377056, #1182): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "it happens even with an unmodded standard C3C game." Both are bugs; at peace, ships should not attack a city.

  • Amphibious attack: only units with the Amphibious ability attack from a ship onto land (Marines; Viking Berserk). EDHELP Unit Abilities: "Amphibious Unit may attack from a ship onto land". PEDIA PRTO_Marine: "They can unload from a sea $LINK<transport=PRTO_Transport> into any coastal land square, including enemy cities and enemy-occupied squares." PEDIA PRTO_Berserk: "allows them to attack enemy units or structures directly from within sea-based transport vessels." No amphibious attack penalty or bonus was found in a Firaxis source. Sullla (https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat8b.html, vanilla) says Marines attacking from ships "ignore city defensive bonuses"; alexman (APO-WALLS #1) says the opposite for walls ("When you attack a city with a ground unit (or a marine), the defender gets the defensive bonus of the wall."). See Disputed. Code reading: amphibious attacks get a 25% modifier. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16716406, page-122 #2435): "Amphibious attacks definitely do get a 25% bonus." / "I remember seeing that in the code a while back when I was looking into how the combat odds get calculated." He gives no more detail (a recollection of the odds function, not a quoted line); the post does not say which side the 25% favours (UNVERIFIED).

  • C3C: any unit can step off a ship onto a coastal tile that holds only non-combat units and capture them. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/plucking-workers-from-the-coast.72176/ (Fanny Brice, #1): "ALL units can attack enemy workers along the coast right off of transports." Chieftess (moderator, #9): "It's a new feature to prevent the coastal blockades with units and workers, especially in MP games."

  • Landing. Units unloaded onto land can't move again that turn (CFC artillery article: https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/military/how-to-use-artillery-defensively/ says landed enemy units "cannot move until the next turn"). The exact rule is UNVERIFIED.

  • Submarines are invisible except to subs, AEGIS Cruisers and Destroyers. PEDIA PRTO_Submarine: "They remain invisible until they attack a surface ship ... other submarines and $LINK<AEGIS Cruisers=PRTO_AEGIS_Cruiser> and $LINK<Destroyers=PRTO_Destroyer> can see them plainly." README-V: "You no longer declare war by accidentally passing over a hidden submarine."

  • Privateers hide their nationality (attack without declaring war). PEDIA PRTO_Privateer: "allowing them to attack and be attacked [without revealing the nationality] of the ship."

  • Transports. Ships are boarded by moving onto them from shore or loaded in a coastal city (PEDIA GCON_Transport). Transport units can't carry other transports (Mike Breitkreutz, Firaxis, https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/53195-some-questions-for-the-developers-on-editing-and-the-civ3mod-file: "No. Transport units cannot carry other transport units."). tjs282 (C3X-THREAD post-16826417, page-137 #2737): "The programmed rule is that no transport-unit (i.e. a unit with a non-zero transport-capacity...) can be loaded onto another transport-unit." Flintlock (post-16667922, page-114 #2273): "transports are the only units that never stack", and (#2269) "Units track what unit they're contained in but container units don't track what units are inside them."

  • Ships can enter a land tile only if it holds a city; doing so unloads everything (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16787843, page-134 #2662): "It's technically already possible for sea units to enter land tiles, if those tiles have cities on them." / (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "Moving a transport onto a land tile triggers the unload all operation".

  • What happens to carried units when a ship sinks or loses is UNVERIFIED (presumably they die with it).

1.11 Air units

  • Air units stay in cities, airfields or carriers and fly missions within operational range. PEDIA GCON_Air_Missions: "Air units are not moved around the map like other units. Instead, they are stationed in a city, and assigned missions which they execute immediately against targets within their operational range."
  • Missions (C3C): Air Superiority, Bombing, Precision Strike, Recon, Re-Base.
    • Air Superiority. PEDIA: "The plane attempts to intercept enemy aircraft that attack targets within the inner half of its operational range."
    • Bombing. PEDIA: "The plane flies to the target and attacks, sometimes affecting city improvements, and sometimes population."
    • Precision Strike. PEDIA: "Performed by $LINK<stealth aircraft=PRTO_Stealth_Fighter>, precision strikes destroy city improvements only, unless there are none in which case population is affected." README-V ("Changes"): needs the Smart Weapons advance; "Useful only against cities, this mission targets improvements."
    • Recon. PEDIA: "The plane flies to the target and reveals the map." It can't be intercepted (CFC-RULES: "(Recon and Rebase can not be intercepted)"). C3X-INI: "Under the standard rules, performing a recon mission or intercepting an attacker causes a unit to lose all of its remaining moves", and the recon area is "the stock 5x5 area". Vision from a recon mission is removed at the start of the unit's next turn ("Recon missions expire at the start of a unit's turn").
    • Re-Base. PEDIA: "a distance equal to up to Six times the plane's operational range."
  • Interception chance: 50% per interceptor, 5% against stealth. EDHELP Chance to Intercept Enemy Air Missions: "Default is 50%." ... Stealth Missions: "Default is 5%." alexman (Firaxis), APO-ZOC #11: "A fighter on air superiority has a 50% chance to intercept a bomber (5% if the bomber is stealth)." / "If it fails to intercept, it can try to intercept the next incoming bomber." / "If it succeeds, it's done intercepting for that turn."
  • Which fighter intercepts: the first available, not the best (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16521876, page-81 #1609): "instead the game picks the first available fighter to intercept." The interception roll happens as the plane flies over the target tile (post-16858118, page-150 #2987, and the routine Unit::try_flying_over_tile, post-16395511, page-63 #1253, which (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "rolls for interception, does air combat, and also plays the flying animation"). Other stock details from #1222: "Normally the game does not give any indication when a plane dodges interception." and "a plane with multiple movement points can bomb multiple times but only perform reconaissance once per turn". Ground anti-air kills outright (post-16512698, page-79 #1575: "air combat involves instant kills"), which matches korn469's "simple yes or no" below.
  • Flak and Mobile SAM defend only their own tile. alexman (APO-ZOC #11, as summarized): Flak and Mobile SAMs only defend against aircraft attacking their own tile. SAM Battery: PEDIA BLDG_SAM_Missile_Battery: "[SAM Missile Batteries] attack enemy air units that attempt to attack the SAM site's city." Anti-air stat, BIQ: Flak 2, Mobile SAM 4, AEGIS Cruiser 3, Battleship 2, Destroyer/Cruiser/Carrier 1.
  • Anti-air units (C3C): up to 4 per tile fire, each at one tenth of its anti-air value against the plane's defense. Tested by BomberEscort (CFC-AA, 500 trials per case, C3C 1.00), with alexman agreeing (#13):
    • #7: "Chance Air Unit is Destoyed By AAUnit #1 = 1 - [AirD/(AirD + (AAUnitD#1 * 0.1))]"; the next unit fires only if the one before missed ("It is done as above because AAUnit#2 only gets a chance to fire if AAUnit#1 Misses..."); "AAUnits must be sorted in decending order".
    • #5: "If more than 4 AA in a square, only the best four will defend..." / "Hit Points and Experience do not affect interception by AA." / "Stealth Ability does not affect interception by AA." / "Terrain has no effect." / "Radar Towers have no effect." / "A Defense of zero will result in always being shot down, this is why Stealth Aircraft now have a Defense...".
    • Results fit the formula: 1 Flak (AA 2) against a defense-2 plane gave "48/500 Destroyed----- 9.6%" (formula 9.1%); 2 Flak gave 17.6% (formula 17.4%).
    • Same rule reported by justanick (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/air-battle-questions-only.565813/, #4): "In fact having up to 4 units will have a chance to attack you. However, only 0.1 times their attack value is applied." and Ozymandias (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/air-units-bombing-mechanics-in-the-open.678152/, #2): "The real strength of each AA unit is 1/10th of the value shown in the editor." / "The maximum number of AA units that are given a chance to shoot down the plane is 4."
    • CFC-FAQ: "the chance to intercept an air unit with stealth and without stealth is the same".
    • AA fire is a single yes/no roll: korn469 (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/120873-has-anyone-figured-out-the-aa-formula-yet, #12): "fighters vs. other air units does result in a hitpoint loss, whereas with AA units (either land or sea) it results in a simple yes or no situation".
    • An independent editor test fits the one-tenth rule and shows the interception setting doesn't apply to AA units. Vingrjoe (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anti-air-defense-combat.100020, #1, PTW/C3C era): "one Aegis cruiser with an air defense of 100, and had 20 bombers with defense of only one"; "On 0, 25, and 100 percent chance air interception the results were 90% of the aircraft killed" (one tenth of 100 against 1 gives 10/11 = 91%).
  • SAM Battery: fires with the interception chance, then rolls A/(A+D) at full strength. alexman (CFC-AA #13): "For each bomber, there is a chance equal to the interception chance for the SAM to fire" / "If the SAM fires, it has a A/(A+D) probability to shoot down the plane." justanick (thread 565813, #4): "However here the full attack values applies, but only in 50% or 5% of chances, that will depend on stealth or no stealth." BomberEscort's SAM-only test (CFC-AA #5) gave 3.6%, lower than this predicts; he called it possibly "a statistical fluke" (#20). Whether the SAM adds to mobile AA on the same tile: BomberEscort (#5) "SAM Missile Battery is cumulative with Mobile AA."; the list alexman quotes in #13 says it is not. UNVERIFIED.
  • Air units on interception stay on it when their carrier moves (README-V 1.21f).
  • Air and sea units in a captured city are destroyed. CFC-CSE (Xerxes314): "air and sea units caught in a city are automatically destroyed. They have no combat value." README-PTW: "Fixed bug that caused air units to not be destroyed if they are not in a city." Which units this covers exactly is UNVERIFIED.
  • Airfields (PTW+) work like an airport for air units; one use consumes a worker; pillaging destroys them. PEDIA GCON_Airfields: "Airfields function in the same manner that a city with an airport would for air units." / "Airfields can be pillaged like a terrain improvement, which also destroys the airfield."
  • Helicopters carry foot soldiers only, 3 at a time (BIQ capacity 3). Other capacities (BIQ): Transport 6, Galleon 4, Carrier 4 air units, Nuclear Submarine 1 tactical nuke. PEDIA PRTO_Helicopter: "Helicopters may carry foot soldiers only -- no mechanized units allowed." PEDIA GCON_Transport: "To board a helicopter a ground unit must start its turn in a city with the chopper and use the Load command button."
  • Paratroopers airdrop from a city with an Airport (or an airfield, PTW 1.04f). PEDIA PRTO_Paratrooper: "When a paratrooper is active in a city with an $LINK<airport=BLDG_Airport> it may perform an airdrop." README-PTW 1.04f: "Added ability to airdrop from airfields." Drop range is the operational range (BIQ: Paratrooper 6, Modern Paratrooper 8). C3X-INI: "Under the standard game rules, units lose all their remaining moves after airdropping." Re-basing range is six times the operational range (C3X-INI: "For standard game rules, set to 6", matching PEDIA's "Six times"). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16823297, page-136 #2720) says "the factor of six is baked into the logic" (in a discussion of a C3X rebase feature, so it may describe the mod's copy). Two stock limits on airdrops, tested (post-16539545, page-85 #1690): "you can't airdrop into an enemy city even if it's undefended." / "You also can't airdrop onto an enemy worker."
  • A bomber that dodges SAM damage still loses one move (C3X-INI: "there is no indication that anything happened in that case except for the bomber losing one movement point").
  • Air unit stats (BIQ, C3C): Fighter A4 D2 B3 range 6; Jet Fighter A8 D4 B3 range 9; F-15 A8 D4 B6 range 9; Stealth Fighter A8 D6 B6 range 12; Bomber D2 B12 RoF3 range 10; Stealth Bomber D5 B18 RoF3 range 16.
  • An intercepted mission always fails; the two planes then fight a normal battle to the death. tjs282 (thread 565813, #9, C3C): "Interception =/= Shootdown." / "(1) Is a Fighter scrambled to intercept the Bomber? (result based on 'Intercept rate')" / "If yes, Bombing mission fails, goto (2)" / "(2) Does the Fighter defeat the Bomber? (result based on relative A/D values and HP)" / "If yes, Bomber will crash'n'burn (Fighter may be damaged)" / "If no, Fighter will crash'n'burn (Bomber may be damaged)". Ozymandias (thread 678152, #4) quotes a FAQ: "If an aircraft is intercepted it will always fail to bomb the target even if it wins the air battle." The game's messages have both outcomes (script.txt #AIRINTERCEPTED "We were shot down by an enemy interceptor!", #AIRINTERCEPTORDESTROYED "We shot down an enemy interceptor!", #AIRINTERCEPTFAILURE "Our interceptor was shot down!", plus #AIRSAMINTERCEPTED and #AIRFLAKINTERCEPTED). Which unit uses attack and which defense in that battle is UNVERIFIED (presumably the interceptor attacks).
  • A fighter intercepts at most once per turn and needs a turn to activate. CFC-FAQ: "A plane can only engage another plane once per turn." tjs282 (thread 565813, #9): "a Fighter can only be scrambled once per turn". Kirejara (#12): "Interceptors never launched in the round they are ordered to perform CAP". tjs282 (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/air-unit-interception-range.678374/, #2) calls the delay a bug: the mission "does not activate on the interturn directly after being selected".
    • Code (third round): the delay is a human-only interface bug, and one interception ends the mission. C3X-INI, "INTERCEPT LOST TURN BUG": "When a human player sets a fighter to intercept mode, the game discards all of its remaining movement points, hence it cannot intercept during the next interturn. This bug does not affect the AI and is part of the interface logic, not the game logic." The intended rule (which the engine should follow) is that a fighter set to air superiority can intercept in the next interturn. Once per turn is stock: C3X-SRC (patch_Unit_set_state_after_interception) says the mod "can't knock them out of the interception state as soon as they intercept something like in the base game". So the stock game takes the fighter off its mission after one interception and gives it its full moves again (Unit_get_moves_after_interception returns the max move points).
  • Several fighters on air superiority raise the interception chance in PTW and C3C, not in vanilla. Catt (Apolyton thread 120873, #8): stacking fighters "did not increase the chances of interception" in vanilla, which changed in PTW and Conquests. Jaybe (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/72889-how-do-intercept-and-air-supremacy-really-work, #4) cites the PTW strategy guide: the chance "DOES increase as you have more units on Air Superiority available." alexman's per-fighter 50% roll (above) is the C3C model.

1.12 Nuclear weapons

  • Manhattan Project enables nukes for every civ. PEDIA BLDG_Manhattan_Project: "Allows the construction of nuclear weapons by {all} civilizations. Nuclear Weapons can {Not} be built until this Wonder has been completed."
  • Tactical Nuke: bombard range 6, can be carried by nuclear subs. PEDIA PRTO_Nuke: "They can be loaded onto nuclear subs and launched from the sea." / "The bombard range of a Tactical Nuke is 6 squares." Needs Aluminum and Uranium. BIQ: 300 shields.
  • ICBM: unlimited range, can't move from the city that built it. PEDIA PRTO_ICBM: "An [ICBM] ... has an unlimited range" / "They cannot be moved from the city that builds them but can strike any target on the map." BIQ: 500 shields.
  • Using nukes angers the world. PEDIA PRTO_ICBM: "Nuclear weapons, if used, have a tremendous negative effect upon world opinion."
  • SDI (small wonder, needs 5 SAM Batteries): 75% chance to stop incoming nukes. PEDIA BLDG_SDI: "Offers a 75% chance of intercepting enemy ICBMs." EDHELP: "the selected Small Wonder will cause enemy missile attacks to fail 75% of the time." It also stops tactical nukes (https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/135536-strategic-missile-defense, MattPilot: "Infact it shoots down everything with the "nuke" flag."). C3X-THREAD posters are split. Erlend Philip Wood (post-16497435, page-78 #1545, testing) says nukes in general (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "will be targeted by the Strategic Missile Defense, with a probability of 75% to get shot down"; Alekseyev_ (post-16495192, #1542, "IIRC") says non-ICBM nukes are not intercepted. Predator145 (post-16395546, page-63 #1254), on Cruise Missile and Nuclear Weapon units: "Yeah, only SDIs can shoot them down. But interceptors and SAM batteries as well as Air Defense value units can't."
  • Nuclear units are immune to nukes; nuke damage is fixed in code. Civinator (C3X-THREAD post-16395572, page-63 #1256): "The nuclear flag (not the ICBM or tactical nuke strategy) also makes a unit immun against nuclear attacks." This matches justanick's "All nuclear weapons are immune" below. Flintlock (post-16896015, page-163 #3246): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "Modifying damage from nuclear explosions isn't easy since it's hardcoded."
  • Blast effects: each unit in the blast has a 50% chance to die, and the city loses half its population. CFC-CSE (Valant2, vanilla): "Has a 50% chance to destroy each unit caught in its blast area" and a nuke "reduces a city's population by 50%." WarpStorm (Apolyton thread 73776 below, 2002): "Every unit and building has a 50-50 chance of dying outright."
  • C3C 1.22 nuke effects (tested by justanick, CFC-BOMB122 #1): "All nuclear weapons are immune against nuclear attack." / "All other units are destroyed at a chance of 2:1." / "All units that survive that loose 2 hitpoints." / "All units with 2 HP, 1 HP or no HP are killed instantly." / "Each building is destroyed with 50% probability." / "50% of all citizens are killed, the amount surviving is rounded up to integer." "2:1" is ambiguous (a 2/3 or a 1/2 kill chance); the vanilla-era 50% per unit fits "1 in 2". UNVERIFIED which. vmxa (thread 413455 below, #2) says survivors "lose half their hit points" and "Three hits will kill all units".
  • Blast area: the target tile and the 8 around it. Proteus_MST (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/73776-effects-of-nuclear-weapons, Sep 2002): "Every Unit at Ground Zero and all adjacent Tiles of a Nuke has a Chance of 50% of dying." vmxa (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/nuclear-weapons.413455/, #2, 2011): "It will wreck all 9 tiles in the radius of ground zero." / "They will be polluted and all roads and mines and such will be removed." Community, consistent. The exact fallout (pollution) pattern is UNVERIFIED.
  • ICBMs can't be moved at all, in any version (community). Fiddlin Nero (thread 413455, #7): "In Vanilla/PTW you can't move the ICBM at all"; vmxa (#8): "You cannot move icbm's in C3C either". README-PTW: "ICBM's can no longer auto-bombard."

1.13 Capture, enslavement, pillage

  • Non-combat units caught alone are captured. PEDIA PRTO_Worker: "Note that workers may be captured and used by rival civilizations." Artillery with no escort is captured too (thread https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/122803-siege-city-defense, Kef: "If they get captured, you're doing it wrong."). What a captured Settler becomes (Settler, or Worker(s)) is UNVERIFIED.
  • Captured workers are foreign and work at half speed (C3C). README-C3C: "CAPTURED WORKERS: In order to differentiate a foreign (i.e., captured or enslaved) worker unit, it now displays different unit art than the standard worker." / "Note: Captured workers work at 50% the speed of regular workers."
  • Barbarians destroy the workers and settlers they catch. https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/236659-unusual-barbarians (Quintillus): "They can't build them, and when they capture workers or settlers they are destroyed."
  • Any defense-0 unit is captured when an enemy enters its tile; attack-0 units can't take cities. EDHELP Defense Strength (Unit Statistics): "Note that units with a defense strength of 0 are automatically captured when an enemy unit moves into their square." EDHELP Attack Strength: "Note that units with an attack strength of 0 cannot capture cities." script.txt #COMBATCONQUER: "Only [combat] units can capture cities and improvements." / #NONCOMBATANT: "Non-combat units may not attack." EDHELP Immobile: an immobile unit "is not able to take any movement actions, though it may be captured."
  • You can only capture a unit type you could build; otherwise it is destroyed (C3C, community). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/captured-units-dying-instead-of-switching-sides.622068/ (JustLuthor, #1): capturable units "just die off, instead of becoming mine"; Oni Ryuu (#2): "The unit must be available to your civilisation and you need to learn the tech before you can capture it." / "The unit cannot have an attack or defence value." tjs282 (#3) narrows it: a civ whose unique unit replaces a capturable unit can still capture the generic one, so "it's only having the right tech (and the capturable unit having A+D=0) that is important." Oni Ryuu (#5): "Other nations could capture the cannon but not the hwach'a because it never becomes available to them." Code reading confirms it. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16577386, page-93 #1847): "the game does not let you capture units you couldn't have built yourself." Civinator (post-16377888, page-60 #1191): "Decisive for capturing a unit is, that the civ that attacks that unit can build the attacked unit themselves." Thorvald of Lym (post-16408200, page-67 #1321, community) adds that workers on a tile the attacker can't move into are lost: "they're killed instead of captured."
  • Air units caught by a land unit are destroyed, not captured (community). Civinator (C3X-THREAD post-16616478, page-105 #2095): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "there is a special programming, that destroys every mobile air unit when a land unit of another civ is drawing on the tile"; tjs282 (post-16374946, page-58 #1152): air units (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "in the base-game, get destroyed on capture". Alekseyev_ (#1153) says Hidden Nationality air units are an exception; UNVERIFIED.
  • Hidden Nationality and cities (community, Civinator, C3X-THREAD post-16192126, page-25 #496): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the human player can capture cities with HN land units, but only after declaring war to the owner of the city" and "The AI HN unit can capture the city without declaring war". The AI half looks like a bug.
  • Barbarians never enslave (Civinator, C3X-THREAD post-16049378, page-4 #64: "Barbarians cannot enslave").
  • Captured settlers stay settlers (Civilopedia). PEDIA GCON_Nationality (C3C and vanilla): "Captured workers and settlers retain their original nationality as well, and, if ordered to join a city, become foreign nationals within that city." A conflicting recollection, eris (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/capture-settler-but-get-a-worker-instead.314474/, #4, in a Civ4 thread): "In Civ 3, you got two workers for capturing a settler." No test found; the Civilopedia wording is preferred.
  • Capturing a unit counts as an act of war (UNVERIFIED in a fetched source; one search excerpt says so).
  • The "Capture" unit action only applies to capturable special units (kings, princesses) in game modes outside scope. EDHELP Capture: "the unit has the capability to capture units which are vulnerable to being captured (i.e. king units, princess units, etc.)"
  • Enslave (C3C): 1/3 chance after each win to turn the loser into a set unit type. PEDIA GCON_Enslavement: "A unit with the "enslave" capability has a 1/3 chance of enslaving a defeated unit everytime it wins a battle. ... the unit is turned into another unit type (as specificed in the editor) and is immediately under the control of the victorious player." Stock enslavers: Privateer, English Man-O-War (makes another Man-O-War: PRTO_Man-O-War), Mayan Javelin Thrower (makes a Worker: PRTO_Javelin_Thrower). BIQ slaveUnitType: Worker. Only the attacker enslaves (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anyone-know-exactly-how-enslavement-works.107253, Willem: "It's the unit that is doing the attacking."). Code (third round): the roll is out of 100 and succeeds below 33, so it is 33%, not exactly 1/3. C3X-SRC patch_rand_int_to_enslave: "lim is 100, enslaving happens if the return value is < 33". A kill by bombardment can also enslave in stock: C3X-INI's mod option "Stops units from enslaving a target they've destroyed by bombarding as opposed to direct combat", and the code credits a defensive-bombard kill to the bombarder through the same Unit_score_kill.
  • Pillage destroys a tile improvement. EDHELP Pillage: "If checked, the selected unit may pillage terrain improvements." Pillaged barricades fall back to fortresses (PEDIA GCON_Barricades) and pillaged airfields are destroyed (PEDIA GCON_Airfields). Pillaging counts as the unit's move, except for C3C armies (TimBentley, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-concerning-healing-units.113864/, #13: "Pillaging counts as a move for units other than armies."; CFC-FAQ: "Armies in Conquests can also pillage without using up any movement points."). Which improvement goes first when a tile has several: one community statement, Vasyl-67 (C3X-THREAD post-16725406, page-124 #2468): "The order of destroying improvements on tiles is hard-coded in the game: railroad / road and mine (irrigation) / fort." Untested. Pillaging a barricade costs all remaining moves; the AI won't pillage itself into a trap (code reading). Flintlock (post-16401798, page-64 #1270): the pillage code "also checks whether the tile has a barricade, if it does the unit loses all of its remaining moves". Same post: "There is a condition stopping AI units from pillaging tiles if doing so would leave the unit itself stranded."

1.14 Upgrades

  • Where: ground units in a city with a Barracks, ships with a Harbor, air units with an Airport. PEDIA BLDG_Barracks: "A city with a [Barracks] can be used to upgrade ground units." (BLDG_Harbor, BLDG_Airport say the same for naval and air units.) README-PTW: "Added information about upgrading units to civilopedia entries for Barracks, Harbor, and Airport." The new unit's strategic resources must be available in that city (Tom's Hardware, https://forums.tomshardware.com/threads/upgrades.167686/, "The Stare": "You must have any required strategic resources in that citys' resource display.").
  • Cost = (new shield cost - old shield cost) x multiplier gold. Multiplier 3 in C3C, 2 in vanilla/PTW. EDHELP Unit Upgrade Cost: "if the base unit costs 20 shields and its upgrade costs 40 shields, the difference is 20 shields. Under this example, then, the exact cost for the upgrade would be 60 shields (20 x 3 = 60)." BIQ upgradeCostPerShield 3. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/upgrade-cost.94826 (Ginger_Ale): "times 2 (in Vanilla civ and PtW) and times 3 in Conquests."
  • Leonardo's Workshop halves the cost. PEDIA BLDG_Inventor's_Workshop (all three versions): "Obsolete military units can be upgraded at half price."
  • Experience is kept, except elite drops to veteran (community, consistent). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/upgrade-or-disband.231677 (Tone, #13): "elites are dowgraded to vets but all others hold their status." CerberusIV (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/90368-1.htm, #3, 2003): "if you later upgrade the unit it goes back to being veteran and can again be promoted to Elite". Velociryx (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/44520-1.htm, vanilla 2002), on upgraded elites: "Win a couple more fights with them, and they're right back to their elite status." The Firaxis-relayed leader rule ("Each time a unit is upgraded, however, it can once again produce a great leader", APO-1GL) is consistent with an upgrade clearing elite status.
  • An upgraded unit is fully healed (community; see 1.1 Healing: Pyrrhos, thread 266551).
  • Upgrade All (Shift-U) upgrades every unit of the active unit's type; fortified units stay fortified. README-V: "Upgrade All: To use the Upgrade All order (SHIFT-U) you must activate a unit. All units of this type will then be upgraded." README-V 1.21f: "Upgrade all now leaves fortified units fortified."
  • Unique units are in the normal upgrade chain (README-V 1.17f: "Civ-specific units are now part of the regular upgrade chain."; README-V 1.21f: "War chariots now upgrade to knights.").
  • Units inside armies can never be upgraded (all versions). CFC-ARMY: "Units in armies cannot be upgraded." player1 (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/49454-1.htm, #19, 2002, second-hand): "FIRAXIS said several times, that UPGRADEABLE armes were too powerfull (unbalancing) in beta."
  • The upgrade target must be buildable in that city (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16636518, page-108 #2157): "you can't upgrade to a unit type unless you can also build that type in the city in question." / "This also explains why upgrading armies requires a military academy." (post-16750370, page-130 #2581): "the game relies on its usual CanBuildUnit function to gate upgrades", and "Upgrading all units in all cities is actually part of the base game, the hotkey for it is Shift+U." So resources, techs and the building check (Barracks, Harbor, Airport) all come from the build test.
  • A unit becomes unbuildable once its upgrade is buildable (code reading). Flintlock (post-16832498, page-140 #2794): "connecting iron for swordsmen annoyingly makes warriors unbuildable." A unique unit follows the same rule after its Golden Age: tjs282 (post-16978459, page-187 #3732) says it stays buildable only if "your UU is the last link in an upgrade-chain, or if you haven't fulfilled the requirements" (techs and resources) to build the upgrade; timerover51 checked the stock BIQ and agreed (#3733: "you are correct and I stand corrected"). Android Assembly (post-16884159, page-158 #3145): giving a unit an "innocent" upgrade target makes it go obsolete.
  • Elite status is lost on upgrade (community): bacchant (C3X-THREAD post-16644543, page-110 #2198): "When a unit is upgraded it loses its 'elite' status." (Consistent with the elite-to-veteran drop above.)
  • Barracks-type buildings: veterans, healing and upgrades come from one flag (community). tjs282 (C3X-THREAD post-16388661, page-62 #1239): "If the Barracks is modded so that doesn't produce vets, then it won't heal or upgrade units either".
  • Whether an upgraded unit keeps its remaining moves: UNVERIFIED.

1.15 AI and difficulty

  • Difficulty does not change combat odds except through the barbarian bonus. CFC-CSE (Valant2): the difficulty level "has NO effect on attack/defense calculations." No AI attack or defense bonus field exists in the editor's difficulty tab per CFC-RULES and EDHELP.
  • The AI gets free units, not combat bonuses. Thread 37490 (Pyrkaige, editor table): "Number of extra defensive units: 0 0 0 2 4 8" and "Number of extra offensive units: 0 0 0 1 2 4" (Chieftain..Deity), and "At Monarch and above, the AI civs get extra offensive and defensive units when they start the game."
  • AI barbarian bonus. TheNiceOne (thread 37490): "the AI has still a 200% bonus against barbarians even though the human player loses its entire bonus on deity." This conflicts with EDHELP's "any player (human or AI)" (see Disputed).

Disputed

  1. Stacking of defense bonuses: additive vs multiplicative. EDHELP calls the fortify, river and fortress bonuses "cumulative" and Valant2 (CFC-CSE) says "Bonus percentages add". vulture (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/64407-civ-3-combat-calculations-with-retreat-odds): "I believe the factors combine together as I've done above, rather than adding together to give a 70% increase". The manual (p. 110, via royfurr in thread 7886) says terrain is figured "BEFORE THE CITY SIZE AND WALLS TAKE EFFECT", which some read as compounding. Additive has the Firaxis help text behind it, and Xerxes314's calculator and WarpStorm ("You add the bonuses first") also add. No test separating the two was found. The C3X mod's code (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c) leans additive in two places, neither a disassembly of the unit-combat function: its tile-bombard re-implementation, which says it keeps the game's rounding, uses base_defense * (100 + terrain_bonus + building_bonus) / 100; and its defender-ranking helper (counter_defender_selection_defensive_bonus_percent) sums terrain, the town/city/metropolis bonus, the building bonus, fortress (barricade twice), fortified and river into one percentage before applying it.
  2. Fortify inside walls or a fortress. EDHELP says cumulative. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/defense-bonuses-question.21715 (search excerpt): "the wall bonus supercedes the fortification bonus" and "in a fortress, there is no point in Fortifying." A related claim is that units in cities count as fortified automatically. Neither claim was confirmed. (Thread 287626, "Fortress vs. Fortifications", was read and has nothing on this.)
  3. ~~Promotion against barbarians.~~ Resolved: halved. The 1.22 code doubles the promotion ratio when the loser's civ ID is 0, "if ( !Target_Unit->Body.CivID ) _Comb_Exp_Promote_Ratio *= 2;" (CFC-LCODE #8, §1.2), which matches Theoden (thread 115547) and CFC-FAQ's table. Catt's "the promotion odds do not apply" (thread 80836) is not supported.
  4. ~~Army cost.~~ Resolved: 400 in every version. The vanilla and PTW rule files store Army cost 40 in tens of shields, the same 400 as C3C (raw read, §1.4). CivFanatics FAQ v3.0's "200 shields" matches no default rule file.
  5. Do armies retreat? https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/are-armies-fast-units.84239/ (Longasc): "No, Armies NEVER retreat even if they have increased movement." Chieftess: "Armies DO retreat, atleast in C3C." README-V says an army inherits "mobility" only when all its units have it. DeepO (Apolyton 59461, vanilla): an army of Modern Armor "will not retreat from any other fastmover", which says nothing about slow opponents.
  6. ~~Retreat formula.~~ Resolved: Antal1987's reading of the 1.22 code (526285 page 4, #62) has random(defender bonus + 50) < attacker bonus, which is Oystein's 100 * att / (def + 50) (§1.5). Still open: the measured cap near 66 for edited bonuses.
  7. Barbarian bonus for the AI. EDHELP: the bonus applies to "any player (human or AI)" at the game's difficulty. TheNiceOne (thread 37490): the AI always uses the Regent value (200%).
  8. Leonardo's Workshop. Civ3's Civilopedia says it halves upgrade costs (all versions). A CFC post (Randy, thread 94826) says "Militaristic Halves the upgrade cost of units", and some posters describe free upgrades (Civ2-style). Trust the Civilopedia; the militaristic claim is unconfirmed. Against it: maurcus tested it in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/things-civilopedia-cant-explain.470744/ (#4): "it doesnt appear militaristic does anything with upgrade costs". The opening post's halving came after capturing Leonardo's Workshop.
  9. ZOC hit chance. Harovan (APO-ZOC): the shot always fires and works like a normal attack. Willem: "more like 30%". Flintlock's reverse-engineered 1 - 16D/(16D + A) (thread 682034) is the most specific.
  10. Seafaring and sinking. CFC-SINK found survival depends only on the trait (50% vs 75%). Older posts tie it to sea vs ocean squares; the unit's Sink in Sea / Sink in Ocean flags decide where it can sink, the trait decides how often.
  11. Military Academy's effect on armies: +25% or a smaller divisor. EDHELP Miscellaneous Effect Flags (Wonder Properties): "Increased Army Value Each army owned by a civilization that has built a structure with this attribute has its overall attack and defense values increased by 25%." CFC-ARMY, CFC-FAQ and Arathorn: the divisor in A + TA/N drops from 6 to 4. tom2050's tests (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/increased-army-value-flag.424279/, #14, 180 battles per setup): "Didn't see any difference", possibly because the wonder was placed in debug mode. Unresolved; the help text is Firaxis's, the formula is the community's. A raw read of the C3C BIQ shows the Military Academy carries the "Increased Army Value" flag (and the vanilla/PTW Military Academy does not), so the flag the help text describes is the one in play.
  12. Great Wall against barbarians: all combat, or only defense in its own city. PEDIA BLDG_Great_Wall: "Doubles unit combat values versus $LINK<Barbarians=GCON_Barbarians>." EDHELP's flag text: "Doubles defense value vs Barbarians of city that the wonder resides in". Xerxes314's vanilla calculator applies +100% to attack and defense against barbarians. Unresolved.
  13. Vanilla/PTW army blitz. README-V 1.17f: "Armies now have the zone of control and blitz ability." Puppeteer (thread 489054, #2): "Vanilla and PTW armies don't blitz; you're just seeing each unit in the army take a turn as the collective health goes down." The readme is Firaxis's; the post is a 2013 recollection.
  14. ~~Militaristic and promotion.~~ Resolved: Militaristic doubles the chance. The routine Antal1987 posted (CFC-LCODE #8) halves the promotion ratio when CheckBonus(race, 0) is true, and index 0 is RB_Militaristic in the C3X header for the same executable (§1.2). His "no militaristic check" missed what that call tests.
  15. Can bombardment destroy Great Wonders? justanick (CFC-BOMB122, C3C 1.22): yes, until the owner reaches the Industrial Age; zerksees (thread 278312, C3C 1.22) destroyed Sun Tzu's with a trebuchet. Dominus Romae (thread 128868, #9, "At least in PTW 1.27f and C3C 1.22"): no. TimBentley (#12): the Great Wall itself can be bombed away in a town of size 6 or less, "a rather annoying bug". C3X-THREAD (code reading): Flintlock (post-16725029, page-124 #2465): wonders can be destroyed by bombardment "but only in the ancient era" (§1.7). That agrees with justanick that the window closes, but puts the end at the Middle Ages, not the Industrial Age, and does not say whose era counts. zerksees's Sun Tzu (a Middle Ages wonder) fits it only if the owner was still in the ancient era. Lean: Flintlock's reading, as the code source; the era test (owner's or attacker's) is open.
  16. Marines and city defense bonuses. Sullla (strat8b, vanilla): Marines attacking from ships "ignore city defensive bonuses". alexman (APO-WALLS #1): a marine attacking a city still faces the wall bonus. alexman tested; Sullla's line is a one-line unit note. C3X-THREAD: Flintlock recalls from the combat-odds code that "Amphibious attacks definitely do get a 25% bonus" (post-16716406, §1.10); which side it favours is not stated, so it settles neither claim.
  17. PTW city-bombard targeting (C3X-THREAD posters against alexman's test). See §1.7: alexman's PTW test gives 50% unit / 25% population / 25% buildings; Predator145, BlueSox14 (relaying Sullla) and Flintlock (a recollection) give 1/3 each. Lean: alexman (tested). Out of scope for the C3C engine either way.
  18. Captured settlers. PEDIA GCON_Nationality implies captured settlers stay settlers; eris (in a Civ4 thread) recalls two workers. Untested.

Resolved since the first pass: fast-defender retreat (CFC-RSTUDY: same formula on defense) and building/citizen bombard defense 16 (README-V 1.17f, BIQ, alexman's tests) are now in the main text.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • The per-round formula A/(A+D) and 1 HP per round are community-derived (alexman's 2003 bombardment description assumes the same model). The code stores round odds on a 1024 scale (§1.1, via C3X). Rounding of modified strengths is shown only for tile bombardment (integer truncation after added bonuses); unit-vs-unit rounding is inferred.
  • Stack defender: the exact weighting of defense and current HP, and the tie-break (lowest attack? cheapest?).
  • Promotion chances: now read from the 1.22 code (§1.2). Still open: what the Status & 2 bit that forces a promotion is, and so whether "two wins in a turn always promote" has any basis. (Third round: C3X's header names unit status bits 0x1 USF_SKIPPED_FULL_TURN_WITH_DAMAGE, 0x4 USF_USED_ATTACK "the unit has attacked at least once this turn", 0x8 USF_PERFORMED_AIR_RECON, 0x40 USF_USED_DEFENSIVE_BOMBARD, 0x80/0x100 sentry, and leaves 0x2 unnamed, so the code readings do not identify it.)
  • Leaders: whether naval or air elites could make leaders before C3C; how "more than one leader in one game turn" happens (presumably only after the first one is used).
  • Retreat: whether a 3-MP unit can retreat from a 2-MP unit (two recollections say no, and C3X-THREAD posters WildWeazel and Shmelkin state "2+ can retreat from 1" as the stock rule, §1.5; still no test); whether a retreating attacker keeps its remaining moves; whether armies retreat; the cap above 66. The roll itself is now from code (§1.5).
  • Defensive bombard: whether it rolls against the attacker's attack (tjs282, "IIRC") or defense. (That it never kills, even with a lethal unit, is now in §1.7 from C3X-INI. Third round: one shot per attack from the strongest eligible unit, and no shot at an attacker with defence below 1, which points to defence; §1.7.)
  • Size of the "Bombardment in Ports" vulnerability. The C3C target order is fixed per C3X-INI ("always goes after units first"); whether walls come before units (justanick) is not stated there.
  • Which units can fortify; whether units in cities get the fortify bonus automatically; whether fortifying speeds healing in a city without a Barracks.
  • Movement: entering a tile with fewer moves left than its cost (one community statement says it is allowed, §1.9); the cost of crossing an unbridged river (one MP per community posts, untested).
  • Naval: galleys in ocean after Navigation (CFC-FAQ says safe, the Galley entry does not say); what happens to cargo when a transport dies; whether ships can attack a coastal tile holding only non-combat units; whether units that land from ships lose their remaining moves.
  • Air: which unit attacks and which defends in an interception battle (Zeekater, thread 100020 #4: "the defense of the bomber is weighed against the attack of the fighter, just like in regular combat", untested); whether a SAM Battery adds to mobile AA on its tile; whether a stealth fighter can intercept (MoonWolf, thread 86619: "stealth fighters haven't the ability to shoot down incoming bombers/fighters").
  • Nukes: whether "2:1" means a 2/3 or 1/2 kill chance; the fallout pattern; launch restrictions.
  • Capture: whether capturing counts as declaring war; whether a Leader caught alone is captured or killed.
  • Pillage: order of improvements removed (one untested statement, Vasyl-67, §1.13).
  • Capture: the nationality of units the AI captures. Spoonwood (C3X-THREAD post-16305156, page-41 #808): "captured artillery type units for an AI don't have a nationality"; justanick (post-16305660, #816): "units captured by AI retain their nationality." Unresolved.
  • Nukes and SDI: whether SDI stops tactical nukes as well as ICBMs (MattPilot and Erlend Philip Wood: yes; Alekseyev_: no, "IIRC"), §1.12.
  • Upgrades: whether an upgraded unit keeps its moves. The elite-to-veteran drop and the full heal are consistent community reports but untested.
  • Town walls giving ZOC: two community sources (civ-wiki.de, justanick), no Firaxis text.
  • Every URL read for this section, with what it gave, is listed in reading-log-01.md.

Back to the top

2. City economy

How a Civ3 city turns tiles into food, shields and commerce, and what eats them. Scope is the base game, PTW and Conquests at their final patches (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes are out of scope.

Source key

Local sources come from the installed Civ3 Complete (Steam) and are quoted exactly:

  • [PEDIA] - Conquests Civilopedia, Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (C3C 1.22). Cited by entry key, e.g. #GCON_Food.
  • [BIQ] - default C3C rules, Conquests/conquests.biq, read with the repo's QueryCiv3 reader. Values are raw BIQ fields. Shield costs are not quoted from the BIQ here: the building cost field as read (e.g. Library 8) does not match the in-game cost (80), so the reader layout for that field is unconfirmed.
  • [EDHELP] - C3C editor help, Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, cited by topic.
  • [ED-SUPP] - Conquests/EditorSupplement.txt.
  • [RM129] - vanilla patch notes, README.txt (sections 1.16f to 1.29f).
  • [RMPTW] - civ3PTW/readme.txt.
  • [RMC3C] - Conquests/readme.txt (v1.20 and v1.22 notes).
  • [PEDIA-V] / [PEDIA-PTW] - the base-game and PTW Civilopedias, Text/Civilopedia.txt and civ3PTW/Text/Civilopedia.txt.
  • [SCRIPT] - Conquests/Text/script.txt (in-game message texts), cited by key.
  • [MANUAL] - the Steam manual PDF, https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/3910/manuals/manual.pdf (text extract; page numbers from the printed folios, approximate). It is mostly base-game text but already lists the C3C traits (Agricultural, Seafaring), so treat it as a Civ3 Complete edition with older passages left in.
  • [C3X-INI] - https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini and [C3X-NOTES] https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/tree/master/Notes, the config file and notes of Flintlock's C3X executable mod for C3C 1.22. Only their descriptions of stock 1.22 behaviour ("Under the standard game rules", "In the base game") are used; they come from reading the executable. The mod's own options are out of scope.
  • [C3X-THREAD] - https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (the C3X mod's forum thread, 192 pages, 2021-2026), cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable, so his statements about "the base game" are code readings; other posters' claims are play reports and may come from modded games. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".
  • [BIQ-V] / [BIQ-PTW] - the base-game civ3mod.bic and PTW civ3PTW/civ3X.bix. QueryCiv3 refuses these older formats, so they were decompressed with QueryCiv3's Util.ReadFile and the RULE, GOVT, WSIZ and DIFF records were read at the same field offsets as the C3C file. The offsets give the same values as C3C wherever the rules did not change, which supports the reading. Treat the values as a careful raw read, not a parsed one.

Thread CFC 18881 ("Anyone know the formulae for rush-building?") is in the Civ2 forum and is not used here. Section 5 (Disputed 4) has the details. Thread CFC 139564 ("List of bugs I've experienced"), which the inventory lists as the source of a "50% switching penalty", is a Civ4 thread. CFC 251335 ("Pollution bug") is about Civ1, and CFC 189234 ("capturing cities") is Civ4. None of these are used.

Web sources are cited by URL. A fetch tool extracted their quotes, so they may differ slightly from the page text. Spot-check any web quote before treating its exact wording as load-bearing. The corruption formulas are transcribed from alexman's article and are worth re-checking against the page before implementation.

1. Tile yields

  • Base terrain yields (C3C defaults). Each worked tile gives its terrain's food/shields/commerce plus improvement bonuses. BIQ values:

    Terrain Food Shields Commerce Irrigate Mine Road Cities allowed
    Grassland 2 0 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Plains 1 1 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Desert 0 1 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Flood Plain 3 0 0 +1 0 +1 yes
    Tundra 1 0 0 0 +1 +1 yes
    Hills 1 1 0 0 +2 +1 yes
    Mountains 0 1 0 0 +2 +1 no
    Forest 1 2 0 0 0 +1 yes
    Jungle 1 0 0 0 0 +1 yes
    Marsh 1 0 0 0 0 +1 no
    Volcano 0 3 0 0 0 0 no
    Coast 1 0 2 - - - no
    Sea 1 0 1 - - - no
    Ocean 0 0 0 - - - no

    Source: [BIQ] TERR records. The Civilopedia tables agree on the land values, e.g. [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "Floodplains 3 4 if irrigated. ... Hills 1 cannot be irrigated." and #TFRM_Mine: "Hills 1 shields, 3 if mined. ^Mountains 1 shields, 3 if mined." The water values are under UNVERIFIED (open question 1).

  • Bonus grassland (grassland "with shield") gets +1 shield. Source: [PEDIA] #TERR_Grassland_with_Shield: "These have a rock outcropping to symbolize this and produce a $LINK<shield=GCON_Shields> if worked in a $LINK<city radius=GCON_Radius>." [PEDIA] #TFRM_Mine: "w/ outcropping 1 shields, 2 if mined."

  • Rivers add +1 commerce to every tile they border and supply fresh water for irrigation. Source: [PEDIA] #TERR_River: "Any square with a river along its edge produces one extra $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce> if worked by citizen laborers within a $LINK<city radius=GCON_Radius>."

  • Irrigation needs a river, a lake or an irrigated tile next to it, until Electricity. Source: [PEDIA] #TFRM_Irrigation: "Only squares adjacent to fresh water (river or lake) or to another irrigated square may be irrigated. After the discovery of $LINK<Electricity=TECH_Electricity>, you can irrigate a tile without proximity to fresh water."

  • Roads give +1 commerce on all the land terrains listed (per-terrain RoadBonus). Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Commerce table, e.g. "Grasslands 0 1 with road. ... Forests 0 1 with road." [BIQ] RoadBonus=1 for every land terrain except Volcano.

  • Railroads add +1 food on an irrigated tile and +1 shield on a mined tile. They add nothing on an unimproved or forest tile. The Firaxis texts give no amount, so the numbers come from community tests (section 7 §4.3 has the full set). Sources:

    • [PEDIA] #TFRM_Railroad: "Railroads also increase the output of $LINK<irrigation=TFRM_Irrigation> or $LINK<mines=TFRM_Mine>."
    • Yeti, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/does-railroad-provide-extra-shield-on-mined-grassland-plain.74236/ (C3C era): "If there's a mine, RR adds 1 shield." and "If there's irrigation, RR adds 1 food."
    • David Weldon, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 (vanilla): "RR through forest does nothing. RR through unimproved squares? Nothing."

  • Bonus resources on a tile add (food/shields/commerce). From [BIQ] GOOD records (type 0 = bonus, 1 = luxury, 2 = strategic):

    Resource F/S/C Resource F/S/C Resource F/S/C
    Cattle (0) 2/1/0 Wheat (0) 2/0/0 Game (0) 2/0/0
    Fish (0) 2/0/1 Whales (0) 1/1/2 Oasis (0) 2/0/0
    Gold (0) 0/0/4 Sugar (0) 1/0/1 Tobacco (0) 0/0/1
    Tropical Fruit (0) 1/0/1 Gems (1) 0/0/4 Silks (1) 0/0/3
    Ivory (1) 0/0/2 Incense (1) 0/0/2 Spices (1) 0/0/2
    Furs (1) 0/1/1 Dyes (1) 0/0/1 Wines (1) 1/0/1
    Horses (2) 0/0/1 Iron (2) 0/1/0 Saltpeter (2) 0/0/1
    Coal (2) 0/2/1 Oil (2) 0/1/2 Rubber (2) 0/0/2
    Aluminum (2) 0/2/0 Uranium (2) 0/2/3

    Source: [BIQ] GOOD. Patch history: [RM129] 1.17f: "Game bonus resource now gives +2 food to its tile."

  • Harbor: +1 food on Coast/Sea/Ocean tiles. Offshore Platform: +1 shield on them. Commercial Dock (C3C): +1 commerce on water tiles that already make 1. Source: [PEDIA] #BLDG_Harbor: "Coastal, sea, and ocean squares produce one extra $LINK<food=GCON_Food>." #BLDG_Offshore_Platform: "causes all worked coastal, sea, and ocean squares to produce one $LINK<shield=GCON_Shields>." #BLDG_Commercial_Dock: "produce one extra Commerce in every water square that already produces one."

  • Colossus: +1 commerce in its city's tiles that already make at least 1. Source: [PEDIA] #BLDG_Colossus: "causes its city to produce one extra $LINK<Commerce=GCON_Commerce> in every square that already produces at least one."

  • Lakes: [PEDIA] says a fresh-water lake gives 2 food ("Fresh Water Lake 2 cannot be irrigated."). [RMPTW] says lakes are not their own terrain type: "Fresh water lakes are not a terrain type. They are composed of coastal tiles surrounded by land." So a lake tile is a Coast tile flagged as fresh water. Its food is under UNVERIFIED (open question 1).

  • City radius: tiles within 2 squares of the city that are also inside its cultural borders. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Radius: "within the city's $LINK<cultural borders=GCON_Territory>, and ^* within two squares of the city."

Government tile modifiers

  • Despotism (and Anarchy) tile penalty: any tile yield above 2 is cut by 1. Both governments carry the BIQ flag. Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two $LINK<food=GCON_Food>, $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields>, or $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce> in a despotic government instead produces one less."
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "In Despotism, the standard government, all tiles that produce 3 or more food/shields/commerce will produce one less." Also: "That's why grassland (2 food), when irrigated (+1 food) in Despotism, will only produce 2 food (2 + 1 – 1 = 2)."
    • [BIQ] GOVT TilePenalty=1 for both Anarchy and Despotism, 0 for all others.
    • The editor's wording differs (see Disputed): [EDHELP] "Flags (Government Properties)": "Standard tile penalty Under the selected government, no tile may generate more than 2 food and shields."
  • The penalty still applies during a Golden Age. Source: CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "The bonuses from a Golden Age, however, are still subject to the Despotism tile penalty."
  • Republic and Democracy trade bonus: +1 commerce on tiles. Sources:
    • [BIQ] TradeBonus=1 for Republic and Democracy only.
    • CFC Civilopedia mirror, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/ (Republic, Democracy): "Adds 1 commerce to any tile already producing at least 1".
    • [EDHELP] "Flags (Government Properties)": "Standard trade bonus Under the selected government, each tile generates an additional +1 to commerce." This wording omits the "already producing 1" condition (see Disputed).

City center tile

  • Civ traits change the center tile. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Strengths:

    • Agricultural: "the center city square and irrigated deserts produce one more food."
    • Commercial: "The center city squares of all cities and metros produce extra $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce>, and less $LINK<corruption=GCON_Corruption> is experienced."
    • Industrious: "the center city square of all cities produces extra $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in cities and metros."
    • Seafaring: "Each city built along the coast receives a commerce bonus in the center city square".

    C3C Agricultural detail, from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn): "Provides one bonus food in the city square in all governments except despotism." and "In despotism, the bonus food only appears if the city is by fresh water."

  • A city tile normally gets a road. It gets a railroad when the owner has Steam Power and the city is captured. Sources:

    • [RM129] 1.29f: "Changed city code so that a road/railroad is not placed under a city if a road/railroad is not allowed on the terrain type the city is built upon."
    • [RMC3C] 1.20: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."

  • The city tile is always worked. [MANUAL] p.75: "The terrain a city occupies is especially important, because it is always being worked. You cannot take the workforce off this square".

  • City-tile yield formula (community-tested; no Firaxis text). The city tile does not get "free irrigation" as in Civ2. It has a fixed food value and size-based shield and commerce bonuses. Sources disagree on details by version, so each claim is marked:

    • Food is 2, whatever the terrain (3 for Agricultural, see the trait note above). Food bonuses on the tile are lost.
      • tjs282, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/city-tile-yield.665936/ (C3C, 2020): "a town-tile will only ever yield 2 food (or 3 if Agricultural and on freshwater/ out of Despotism)". justanick, same thread: "food production of the city tile does not depend on the tile".
      • German CivWiki, https://www.civ-wiki.de/wiki/Stadtfeldertrag_(Civ3) (rev. 54333, 2023): "Stadtfelder erzeugen immer mindestens zwei Nahrungseinheiten" ("city tiles always produce at least two food"), and Agricultural gives three, reduced to two by the Despotism penalty unless the city is on fresh water or already size 7 or more.
      • Nero Would, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/49358-1.htm (vanilla 1.21f, May 2002): "Food production is always 2 for city centre tiles".
      • Code reading: [C3X-INI] city_center_food_yield describes stock as "All CC tiles produce enough food to feed one citizen regardless of terrain or resources, as in the base game", independent of "the agricultural bonus (+1 food on city center), the despotism yield penalty". So the value is one citizen's food (the BIQ food per citizen, 2 by default), not a hard-coded 2. [C3X-INI] also confirms the Agricultural exception: "Under the standard game rules, the Despotism tile penalty does not apply to food yield from city tiles of Agricultural civs with fresh water."
    • Shields: at least 1, plus size bonuses.
      • DaveMcW, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/78438-1.htm (PTW era, Feb 2003) lists "Shields from unimproved terrain", "+Shields from bonus resources", "+1 if city size >= 7", "+1 if industrious and size >= 7", "+1 if city size >= 13", "+1 if shields==0 so far" and "+1 if in golden age/mobilization". He adds: "you only get the 0-shield-terrain bonus if the city is size 6 or less."
      • Nor Me and Thoth, same thread: the Industrious bonus comes only at size 13 or more in PTW ("I only get this in size >= 13. That's in PTW 1.14f." / "The Industrious bonus was pushed back to Metros in PTW.").
      • Bamspeedy's C3C "not bugs" list (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/compiled-list-of-bugs-and-non-bugs-not-a-dicussion-thread.70063/, #33): "All civs get 1 extra shield in cities and metropolises (industrious gets 1 more than other civs in metropolises)." He lists the Civilopedia's "industrious civs get bonus shields in cities and metropolises" as a minor text bug.
      • German CivWiki (above): "+ 1 für Städte < 7, falls Grundertrag = 0", "+ 1 für Städte ≥ 7 und < 13", "+ 2 für Städte ≥ 13", with Industrious +1 more at 13 or more.
      • justanick, CFC 665936: "zero is sort of rounded up to 1 to prevent the possibility of zero production."
    • Commerce. The German CivWiki gives, for an ordinary city, "1 Handel, Stadtgröße < 7", "2 Handel, Stadtgröße ≥ 7 und < 13" and "3 Handel, Stadtgröße ≥ 13". Commercial adds "+2 Handel" at 7-12 and "+3 Handel" at 13 or more. Republic/Democracy, a river, the Colossus and a coastal Seafaring city add +1 each, and resources add 0-4. The capital's base is "4 Handel" whatever its size, and the higher of the two calculations is used. The page's own maximum ("4 + 2 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 4 = 14") does not match its table, so treat the Commercial figures with care. Section 8 (traits) has the CFC FAQ wording: Commercial city centres get extra commerce at City size and again at Metropolis size. Nero Would (codehappy 49358, 1.21f) saw a different rule then, "If the resulting Gold is less than 4, increase to 4", which he called "new in 1.21f". That is probably the capital rule.
    • Despotism. The −1 penalty on tiles above 2 applies to the city tile too. Nero Would (1.21f): "Under Despotism, production would be reduced to 2/2/3".
    • Status: consistent community findings across vanilla, PTW and C3C, with the Industrious bonus moving from size 7 (vanilla) to 13 (PTW/C3C). No Firaxis statement was found. Thread CFC 535361, cited in earlier drafts, is in the Civ2 forum and is not used.

  • Founding on forest or jungle clears the tile to its base terrain. Section 7 §9 quotes piratebrun (Apolyton 85881): it "is converted to its base terrain." Nero Would (codehappy 49358): "In the case of Forest, use the production of the underlying terrain (Grassland, Plains or Tundra)." Chicken Pizza (CFC 665936) notes a forest "gets auto-cut and the grass under happens to be bonus." So inca911's "cleared to grassland" (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes) holds only where the underlying terrain is grassland (see Disputed 10).

  • Settling on a food bonus loses it. Source: CFC City Placement (C3C), https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/city-placement/: "If you settle on food bonuses, you lose the food bonus."

    • Shield and commerce bonuses are kept (community). Alekseyev_ ([C3X-THREAD] post-16337774, page-51 #1010): "settling on food resources deletes all the extra food, while settling on prod or money resources does not cause such a loss." Virote_Considon (post-16985727, page-190 #3788) asks for cities to get (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the food bonuses of resources on tiles they settle, as is already the case for shield and commerce bonuses". This fits the fixed one-citizen food value above.
    • Despotism penalty exception on the city tile (community, confirmed in code by Flintlock). tjs282 and justanick (post-16336379 and post-16336821, page-51 #1005/#1008) give two exceptions: the town has fresh water, or it has an Aqueduct and has reached city size. Flintlock (post-16337576, #1009): "that exception to the tile penalty is clearly apparent" in the code. He adds (post-16695556, page-118 #2360) that "the penalty is applied on a per-tile basis".

2. Food, growth and city size

  • Each citizen eats 2 food per turn. Source: [BIQ] FoodConsumptionPerCitizen=2. [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "[Every citizen must eat two food per turn or die.]"
  • Growth: surplus food fills the food box. When the box is full it empties and the city gains 1 citizen. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "When it is full, the storage is emptied and the city grows, adding one citizen to its population."
  • Food box sizes: 20 for sizes 1-6 (town), 40 for 7-12 (city), 60 for 13+ (metropolis). No editor field exists for these, so they appear to be hard-coded.
    • Realms Beyond micromanagement guide (1.17f), http://www.realmsbeyond.net/civ/mmguide.html: "At and below six food boxes are 20 squares, while they're 40 from size 7-12."
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/granaries-dont-work.53078/ (MadScot, 1.29f): "A size 5 city needs 20 food to grow" and a size 7 city "needs a full 40 to gro to size 8".
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/pottery-is-underrated.683382/ (Spoonwood): "But, the box changes from 40 total food to 60 total food iirc." and "that test confirms that changing from size 12 to 13 keeps 20 food in the box".
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/is-there-a-way-to-manipulate-the-number-of-accumulated-food-required-for-city-growth.338390/ (Czulu): "However i cant find that option in civilization 3 conquests editor."
  • AI cities' boxes are scaled by the difficulty cost factor. The human factor is 10. AI factors are Chieftain 20, Warlord 12, Regent 10, Monarch 9, Emperor 8, Demigod 7, Deity 6, Sid 4 ([BIQ] DIFF CostFactor). Source: [EDHELP] "Cost Factor (AI Bonuses)": "Determines the cost factor that is applied to AI growth, shields, and research. The player's cost factor is always 10."
  • Granary: when the city grows, only half the box is used up, so it keeps half its food. The Pyramids give a Granary in every city on the continent (C3C). Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Granary: "effectively doubles the rate of a city's growth by causing only half of a city's $LINK<food store=GCON_Food> to be depleted when the city grows."
    • [BIQ] Granary flag DoublesCityGrowthRate.
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Pyramids: "Puts a $LINK<Granary=BLDG_Granary> in every city on the same continent."
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/the-case-for-food-or-how-to-become-economically-dominant/: "A town size 6 or below has a food box of 10 when the Granary is full."
  • Granary at the 6→7 and 12→13 steps: disputed (see Disputed). The best-tested statement is that the granary keeps half of the previous box (10 food at 6→7, 20 food at 12→13) inside a box that has grown larger. Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/pottery-is-underrated.683382/ (Spoonwood): "The granary keeps half of the food in the box that it had at the previous size." Patch history: [RM129] 1.17f: "Fixed infinite city growth exploit (involving granaries and size 6 cities)."
  • The granary only keeps food above the half-way mark. It does nothing until the box is at least half full, and the half-way "line" appears only then. Source: Bamspeedy's C3C "not bugs" list, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/compiled-list-of-bugs-and-non-bugs-not-a-dicussion-thread.70063/ (#43): "When you get a granary (or pyramids), the food box must be at least half full before the 'line' appears" "(showing you that the food required is halved)."
  • The displayed "population" is cosmetic.
