    • "Rival civilizations cannot benefit from the roads or rails within your territory without a right of passage agreement."
    • "You can always 'see' squares in friendly territory."
    • Resources inside borders need no colony.
    • "You can eject rival military units from your territory".
    • The city radius is only the tiles within 2 that are inside the city's borders (#GCON_Radius): "within the city's cultural borders, and within two squares of the city."

  • Tourist attractions. A great wonder older than 1000 years earns gold for its city, but only wonders with the "Tourist Attraction" flag do.

    • Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Tourist_Attraction: "1000 (years) - 1500 +2 (gold) … 2251 - 2500 +12". Wonder entries say so one by one, e.g. #BLDG_Mausoleum: "This Great Wonder can become a tourist attraction."
    • C3C BIQ flags it on: Pyramids, Hanging Gardens, Colossus, Great Lighthouse, Great Library, Oracle, Great Wall, Sistine Chapel, Copernicus, Shakespeare, Leonardo, JS Bach, Newton, Hoover Dam, United Nations, Temple of Artemis, Statue of Zeus, Mausoleum.
    • It is not set on: Sun Tzu, Magellan, Smith's, Universal Suffrage, Theory of Evolution, Manhattan Project, Cure for Cancer, Longevity, SETI, Internet, Knights Templar.
    • The bit's decoding comes from the repo's BIQ reader. It agrees exactly with the Civilopedia (C3C): the 18 flagged wonders are exactly the 18 entries that say "can become a tourist attraction".
    • The age is counted in game years, not turns. The C3X mod's config, describing the 1.22 code (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini): "the standard 1000 years" for 2 GPT and "the standard 1500" for 4 GPT.

3.11 Culture victory (as relevant)

  • Thresholds. One city with 20,000 culture wins. The civ-wide threshold depends on map size: Tiny 60,000, Small 80,000, Standard 100,000, Large 130,000, Huge 160,000. The BIQ fields are GAME.OneCityCultureWin and GAME.AllCitiesCultureWin.
    • Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Victory: "A single city with 20,000 culture points will satisfy this victory condition." … "Tiny: 60,000 Small: 80,000 Standard: 100,000 Large: 130,000 Huge: 160,000".
    • CFC FAQ (standard map): "have either 20,000 culture in one city, or 100,000 culture across your empire".
    • The rule "no other civ can have over half your culture points" (dac, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/58212-culture-victory) is from memory: UNVERIFIED.

3.12 Cultural opinion: diplomacy, propaganda, resistance

  • Cultural opinion. Each civ holds a cultural opinion of every other, set by their culture ratio. Defaults are from Dan Magaha (Firaxis), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42123-propaganda-chances:

    Opinion (target's view of you) Your culture : theirs Propaganda success Initial resistance Continued resistance (per resister per turn)
    disdainful of 1:3 3% 90% 80%
    dismissive of 1:2 5% 80% 70%
    unimpressed by 3:4 10% 70% 60%
    impressed with 1:1 20% 60% 50%
    admirers of 2:1 25% 50% 40%
    in awe of 3:1 30% 40% 30%
    • Magaha: "Chance of resistance means that if you take one of their cities, the chance of resisters in the city is 90%," / "and each turn there is an 80% chance that each resister will continue to resist." / "the more culture advantage you have over another civ, the easier it is to take over cities".
    • C3C editor help, "Culture Ratio": "if the Germans had 3000 culture points and the Americans had only 1000, the Americans would be said to be "in awe of" the Germans."
    • The BIQ CULT section has ChanceOfSuccessfulPropaganda, CultureRatioPercentage, CultureRatioNumerator/Denominator, InitialResistanceChance and ContinuedResistanceChance.
    • C3C BIQ: the six CULT records match Magaha's table exactly. For example, "in awe of" is propaganda 30, ratio 300% (3/1), initial 40, continued 30; "disdainful of" is 3, 33% (1/3), 90, 80.

  • Diplomacy. Culture affects AI attitude, and the diplomacy screen shows the opinion. The size of the effect is UNVERIFIED.

    • Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Culture: civ culture "comes into play when you … conduct diplomacy with a rival".
    • script.txt: "Our people are [$CULTURAL_OPINION0] the $CIVADJ1 cultural achievements."

  • Propaganda (spy mission). A success makes the city defect. Some sources say it can instead cause only unhappiness.

    • Democracies are immune. Courthouses, capitals and WLTKD resist it. Cities in disorder are easier targets.
    • Manual: "Cities of a Democracy are immune to propaganda. Enemy capitals and cities with Courthouses are less likely to revolt. Also, it is easier to push a city already in civil disorder into open revolt".
    • Bluemofia, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/da-rules-explained.210035/post-5161431: "propaganda will either cause the citizens to join your civilization, or simply cause unhappiness."
    • labels.txt has the unhappiness reason "All the propaganda surrounding your regime is confusing."
    • script.txt has both outcomes as messages to the target civ: #ESPIONAGE_MISSED_PROPAGANDA_LOST_CITY "We have lost $CITY0 because of a propaganda campaign!" and #ESPIONAGE_MISSED_PROPAGANDA "A propaganda campaign in $CITY0 has upset its citizens." The spy's side reads "The citizens of $CITY0 have overthrown their government and joined our cause!" on success, and "…are not sufficiently sympathetic to our cause" on failure. So a propaganda campaign can end in defection or in unhappiness only.
    • C3C editor help, "Resistant to Propaganda (Improvement Flag)": "citizens in the city in which the selected improvement is built are less likely to defect." In the C3C BIQ only the Courthouse has it.

3.13 Resistance in captured cities

  • Which citizens may resist. On capture, each foreign national of the defeated civ may resist, with the "initial resistance" chance from the opinion table. Your own former citizens don't resist when you retake a city, but citizens born under the enemy do.
    • Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Resistance: "a comparison of the civilization cultural values of the two civilizations determines if any of the foreign nationals will resist."
    • Manual: "If you recapture one of your cities from the Romans, the folks who were previously under your rule won't resist. Any new citizens created after the city was originally taken, however, might resist".
  • Resisters:
    • don't work;
    • don't eat;
    • block hurrying;
    • block WLTKD;
    • count double toward culture flips.
    • Civilopedia (C3C): "Resisters are unwilling to work under the new regime."
    • 1.29f readme, Changes v1.29f: "Corrected Resistance Civilopedia chapter to reflect resisting citizens do not consume food."
    • Flips: §3.14.
  • Ending resistance. Each turn, each resister keeps resisting with the "continued" chance, modified by the owner's government and by the garrison ("the more the better"). Making peace with the mother country ends resistance.
    • C3C editor help, "Resistance Modifier (Government Properties)": "the modifier to a single population point's chance of continuing to resist each turn. Positive numbers mean that the resisting population has a greater chance of continuing to resist".
    • Civilopedia (C3C): "Resisters can be quelled by ending the war or garrisoning strong military units in the city -- the more the better."
    • Manual: "They stay that way until you make peace with their mother country or 'win them over'". Also: "A more impressive culture, a government that allows more personal freedom, and a greater supply of luxuries all help."
    • script.txt: "Our troops have quelled $NUM0 $<#0:resister:resisters>!"
  • Garrison rule (WillJ, from Mike B. of Firaxis). Each unit can quell at most one resister per turn, whatever its strength. That is, units × the difficulty's "citizens quelled by military", which is 1 by default (§3.4). The garrison caps how many of the resisters that fail their "continued resistance" roll actually stop resisting.
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/the-inner-workings-of-resistance-revealed/ and its forum thread https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-inner-workings-of-resistance-revealed.56487/ (WillJ, Jun 2003, PTW). The article thanks "forum member Mike B. FIRAXIS", and "Much of the credit for the information in this article goes to him."
    • "the maximum number of resistors that can be quelled on one turn is the number of units stationed in that city." / "Note that unit strength does not matter."
    • Excluded: "Air units, water units, artillery units, settlers, workers, and other non-ground and/or non-combat units".
    • Nothing else matters: happiness, "distance to capital, culture of the city captured, etc., doesn't matter either."
    • Example from the article: with 6 resisters at a 50% continuation chance, about 3 are expected to give up, but with only 2 ground combat units only 2 can be quelled.
    • Resisters "are the first in line to die from starvation and disease".
    • Test: KrikkitTwo, 56487 page 2 #24 (Jun 2005), with a modified ruleset: "So the quelling process is ONLY based on the number of quellers (military units)". A city "will never get rid of resisters without units". WillJ, #23: for resistance alone "there's no point in having more units than the number of resistors".
    • How armies count is UNVERIFIED. vondrack and DeepO, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/58829-1.htm #2/#6 (Aug 2002), guess "1-4 units, depending on how many of them is actually in the army" (DeepO: maybe +1 for the army itself). Neither tested it.
  • Government modifier on resistance (±5 points). Governments rank Democracy, Communism, Republic, Monarchy, Despotism, Anarchy. If the capturer's government ranks higher than the loser's, the resistance chances (initial and continued) drop 5 points; if lower, they rise 5. There are two exceptions where they rise anyway: a Republic capturing from a Monarchy, and a Democracy capturing from a Communist civ.
    • WillJ (article): "If your gov is higher up on the above list than theirs, it goes down by 5%." / "republic civ captures city of civ under monarchy: +5%" / "democratic civ captures city of communist civ: +5%".
    • C3C BIQ, the per-government-pair ResistanceModifier (row = capturer, column = loser), confirms this and adds the C3C governments:
      • Democracy vs Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy and Republic: −5. Democracy vs Communism: +5.
      • Republic vs Anarchy and Despotism: −5. Republic vs Monarchy, Communism and Democracy: +5.
      • Communism vs Democracy: +5. Communism vs Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy and Republic: −5.
      • Monarchy vs Anarchy and Despotism: −5. Monarchy vs Communism, Republic and Democracy: +5.
      • Despotism vs Anarchy: −5. Despotism vs Monarchy, Communism, Republic and Democracy: +5.
      • Anarchy vs the five other base-game governments: +5.
      • Fascism: −5 vs Anarchy, Despotism, Republic and Democracy; −10 vs Communism; 0 vs Monarchy.
      • Feudalism: −5 vs Anarchy and Despotism; +5 vs Monarchy, Communism and Fascism; 0 vs Republic and Democracy.
      • Every government vs Fascism or Feudalism is 0, except Feudalism vs Fascism (+5).
    • The editor help calls the field "the modifier to a single population point's chance of continuing to resist each turn". WillJ's charts apply it to the initial chance as well.

3.14 Assimilation and nationality

  • Nationality. Every citizen has a nationality. Citizens born in a city take the owner's nationality; captured citizens keep theirs. Captured workers or settlers that join a city become foreign nationals.
    • Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Nationality: "Each civilization produces citizens of its own [nationality], a characteristic that persists until assimilated" / "Captured workers and settlers retain their original nationality as well, and, if ordered to join a city, become foreign nationals within that city."
    • CFC Info Center: "the citizens in the conquered city will retain their nationality".
  • Assimilation (what the sources say; the engine follows the owner's ruling that foreign citizens never assimilate, though they can be starved out). Each turn, each foreign national has a per-government percentage chance (GOVT.AssimilationChance) of taking the city's nationality. A culture flip does not change citizens' nationality.
    • C3C BIQ GOVT.AssimilationChance: Anarchy 1, Despotism 1, Monarchy 2, Communism 4, Republic 2, Democracy 4, Fascism 2, Feudalism 3.
    • WarpStorm, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/81314-nationality-assimilation #3 (Nov 2002), gives the same pre-C3C figures: "Communism and Democracy have the best chance of assimilation with a 4% chance a turn (per citizen I think)." / "Republic and Monarchy are slower with a 2% chance." / "The rest are very slow with only a 1% chance."
    • A precondition, per Firaxis' text: the city must have been yours longer than it was theirs. Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Assimilation: "When a foreign national has been a part of a minority population longer than not, he or she assimilates and becomes a member of the majority nationality." Mad Bomber, 81314 #5: "assimilation begins at the point in which a city has belonged to your civ more than the civ that possessed" it, so "if you capture Paris (founded turn #1) at turn # 271 then you will never be able to assimilate the population." Traelin, #6: "It only works this way if the Civ that originally contained the city is still in the game." player1, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/57182-1.htm #3 (Jul 2002), heard instead "(by Firaxis)" that "assimilation only starts after you have more of your own pop in that city then foreigners".
    • Editor tests found no effect, which supports the owner's ruling. Hondo, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/44876-holding-onto-conquered-cities #1 (Nov 2001, base game 1.0x): "No matter what percentage I set this to it doesn't seem to make any difference." whosurdaddy, 57182 #1 (Jul 2002): "I set the assimlation rates to 90% for each government type", and after "20 or 30 turns" foreign citizens "are still retaining their old nationality"; "this editor option does not completely control the assimilation percentages". Both tests are consistent with the time precondition above, so neither shows that assimilation never happens.
    • C3C editor help: "Determines the percentage chance, per turn, that a foreign national in city with the selected government type will assimilate into the current nationality of that city."
    • alexman (Firaxis), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/51715-4.htm #118: "the rate is between a 1 and 4 percent chance per citizen per turn, depending on your government."
    • CFC Info Center: "How fast this happens depends both on their national culture and the culture of the city they currently reside in," "as well as the government type of their current city."
    • CKS, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/culture-flips.661194/: "Your citizens don't change nationality in a flip".
  • Effects of nationality:
    • culture-flip input F (§3.15);
    • resistance (§3.13);
    • "mother country" unhappiness while at war (§3.6);
    • greater vulnerability to the mother country's propaganda;
    • lower city defence while resisting;
    • Fascist culture gating (§3.10).
    • CFC Info Center: resisters "will lower the defense value of the city so that Babylonian forces can easily retake it."

3.15 Culture flips (cultural reversion / "City Defection")

  • Firaxis' qualitative list (Civilopedia (C3C), #GCON_Conversion):

    • Defections are made more likely by: "The presence of foreign nationals of the other civilization in the city", "The application of propaganda by the other civilization", "Civil disorder in the city", "The proximity of foreign territory", "The proximity of a foreign capital".
    • They are made less likely by: "The presence of ground combat units in the city", "We Love The ____ Day", "The proximity of your capital".

  • Formula. Per interturn, for each rival civ:

    P = [(F + T) * Cc * H * (Cte / Cty) - G] / D (the community calculators' form)

    P = [(F + T) * Cc * H - G * (Cty / Cte)] / D (the owner's ruling, used by the engine). Here the ratio scales the garrison, as in Soren's worked example "16 - (4 X 2) = 8". Both forms give zero chance at the same garrison.

    Sources:

    • Dan Magaha (Firaxis), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/culture-flipping-exposed.14337/. He describes the terms but not the arithmetic.
    • Soren Johnson's worked examples, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/51715-1.htm #30 and 51715-2 #36/#48.
    • Flip Calc, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/flip-calc-calculates-the-probability-of-culture-flips.53157/: "P=[(F+T)CcH*(Cte/Cty) - G]/D".
    • anarres, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/stopping-culture-flip.69295/page-2 #24 (PTW era), the same form: "P=((F+T)*Cc*H*(Cte/Cty) - G)/(2000 * D)", with "D = relative distance to capitals" and "Government type is not relevent at all".
    • F: foreign nationals (of that civ), resisters twice. Magaha: "The number of foreign nationals in the city in question (resisters are counted twice)".
    • T: city-radius tiles inside that civ's borders, of 21. Magaha: "The number of the 21-tile city-radius squares … that fall inside your cultural borders." Soren: "they count just as much as citizens."
    • Cc = 2 if that civ has more historical culture in the city than the owner, else 1. Magaha: each city "remembers the total amount of culture generated by any civ who has ever occupied it".
    • H = 2 in disorder, 0.5 in WLTKD, else 1. Magaha: "Civil Disorder in a city doubles the chance" / "We Love the King (or whatever) Day in a city halves the chance".
    • Cte / Cty: the two civs' total culture. Soren confirmed the ratio always applies, "it is the latter...", 51715-2 #48.
    • G: the owner's land units with attack ≥ 1 and defence ≥ 1 in the city. Magaha: "any unit with at least 1 point of offensive and defensive capability". Soren: "16 combat (don't count artillery) units". alexman, 51715-4: every unit in an army counts, "including the actual army unit"; "If the army is empty, it doesn't count at all though"; air units don't count. Troops count against each rival's roll separately (Soren: "one spearman could negate both the Greek and the French citizen").
    • D: the die size, 2000 × d, where d is the ratio of the city's distance to the rival capital over its distance to its own capital, capped to 0.25–4. That makes D range 500–8000. Soren: "The capital factor affects how big the 'die' is". Soren via DeepO, 51715-3 #69: "it goes from 500 to 8000, with an average of 2000". Flip Calc v1.5 distance: max(x,y) + 0.5·min(x,y).

  • Code reading: the formula sits inside the flip routine. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16902815, page-166 #3314): "the formula to compute the risk is embedded in the method that flips cities." (post-16892444, page-160 #3198): "The game internally doesn't break out each factor like culture ratio, foreign citizen count, disorder, and so forth". So no separate function confirms the community terms. Asked whether rioting raises the chance (post-16926492, page-173 #3449): "Yes, I believe it's twice as likely to flip." That agrees with H = 2.

  • Garrisons can bring the chance to zero. justanick (C3X-THREAD post-16318921, page-46 #909): "Starving actually helps against flip risk and flip risk can be reduced to zero", though it takes very many units unless your culture is clearly higher. Brenshar (#908) says the opposite; the 1.17f readme below sides with justanick.

  • Patch history:

    • 1.29f readme, Changes v1.16f: "It's slightly easier to culturally acquire cities."
    • 1.29f readme, Changes v1.17f: "It is now possible to completely suppress a city's cultural reversion with enough military units."

  • Timing and consequences. A city never flips on the interturn it was captured. On a flip, the units inside are lost, citizens keep their nationality, and the new owner gets a free defender.

    • tjs282, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/culture-flips.661194/: "a newly-captured town will never flip on the first interturn."
    • Units: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/armies-captured-in-culture-flips.90904/ ("All units that are in the city are lost"); Strat Compilation: "if the city revolts you loose the troops in it!"
    • Flips happen at peace too. justanick, 661194: "Once at peace you cannot undo a flip by retaking the city".
    • The BIQ game option: Bluemofia, "Allow Cultural Conversions: Allow cities to convert to a rival civ culturally."
    • Halcyon, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/cultural-conversion-question.87425/ #2 (May 2004, C3C): "All the city defenders die." / "The 'capturing' civ gets one free defender in the city." Grille, #3, on the owner's units outside the city but inside its radius: "No, you keep them."

  • The receiving civ can refuse a flip. A human receiver is asked, and an AI receiver can also refuse. Either way the city stays with its owner. How the AI decides is UNVERIFIED.

    • script.txt #WE_CONVERT_CITY offers "Great! Install a new governor." / "We don't want $CITY1. Rebuff the rebels."
    • #THEY_REFUSED_TO_CONVERT_CITY: "the people of $CITY1 have risen up in revolt and pledged allegiance to the $CIVNOUN3! However, the $CIVADJ2 government refuses to acknowledge their claim! Thus, we have regained control of $CITY1!"
    • A separate message, #WE_CONVERT_CITY_WITH_WONDER, announces a flipped city that "contains $WONDER3", so wonders go with the city.

  • Free defender quality. Players kill flip-backs by cutting the city off: an isolated city flips back with a weak defender.

    • anarres, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/invading-when-youre-low-on-culture.61562/ #6 (Aug 2003, PTW): "as long as that city has no trade route to the AI capital" / "they will only get a Rifleman at best as a defender when it flips back". ainwood, #1: "By pillaging and blocking the roads, the city was cut-off from the empire" / "the strongest unit would only ever be a rifleman, or even a spearman in the early industrial age".
    • This suggests the defender is the best one the new owner could build in that city, given its resource connections. That is an inference.

  • Magaha's ranking of the factors, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/39825-1.htm #1 (Jan 2002, the Apolyton copy of "Culture-Flipping Exposed"). He gives them in order of importance:

    1. distance to the capitals ("if you're closer to your capital than the other city is to its capital, you've got a better chance of getting a flip");
    2. the civs' culture ratio;
    3. the city's historical culture;
    4. disorder;
    5. WLTKD;
    6. garrison ("This factor is relatively low on the totem pole", "this shows you why cities can flip even with huge militias garrisoned in them").

    Relayed in 39825-2 #32: "I would assume this is measured in tiles."

  • Community observations (untested):

    • Only to a civ with a stake in the city. A city flips only to a civ whose nationals live in it or whose territory overlaps its radius. DeepO, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/62844-1.htm #11 (Sep 2002): "If there were a bunch of Americans inside, and a city does not overlap with the Aztecs, it will never flip to the Aztecs." This follows from the formula, since F + T = 0.
    • No flips to dead civs. Explorer579, 39825-5 #130 (Oct 2002): "I have never seen a civilization regaining a city through 'cultureflip' after it has been destroyed." vmxa1 (39825-2 #35) says the same.
    • Capitals. DeepO, 62844 #16: capitals can't flip "(or at least I have never heared of a capital flipping)". This is still UNVERIFIED.
    • Courthouses don't help. Purest Warrior, 39825-3 #86 (Feb 2002): "Building a courthouse does not lower the chances of culture-flipping."
    • Patch 1.17f garrisons. Jameschipmunk, 39825-4 #91 (Apr 2002), on 1.17f: "if you have enough troops in the city it will NOT flip." This matches the readme. Soren's die range is relayed by Fitz, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/49417-1.htm #5 (May 2002): "Soren said the distance to the capitols has a range of dividing by 500 to 8000."

Where our implementation (C7Engine/C7GameData/CultureFlip.cs) stands against the sources

Point Sources CultureFlip.cs Note
Formula shape and terms as above Same formula; F counts only that civ's nationals, resisters twice; T counts tiles within MaxRankOfWorkableTiles (21 by default, excluding the city tile) Matches.
Where Cte/Cty applies Flip Calc and the Babylon II calculator (Chieftess, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/babylon-ii-civ3-culture-flip-calc.53181/ #1, "P=[(F+T)CcH*(Cte/Cty) - G]/D") multiply influence. Soren's example "16 - (4 X 2) = 8" instead scales the garrison. Scales the garrison by Cty/Cte (owner's ruling) Same zero point, but different slope when the ratio is far from 1. Flagged in code.
Integer truncation of Cte/Cty Fitz speculated truncation (51715-3 #78); unconfirmed. DaveMcW's HTML Flip Calc 1.0 (https://forums.civfanatics.com/attachments/flipcalc1-0-html.34465/, from thread 53228) keeps it real-valued: var P = ( (F + T) * Cc * H * CR - G ) / D, with CR the enemy's culture over yours and no Math.floor. Its distance factor is a radio choice of 4, 2, 1, .5 or .25 (D = 2000 * GetRadio(...)), and it compounds over turns with 1 - Math.pow(1-P, turns). Real-valued UNVERIFIED (both calculators are community models).
Distance metric Magaha: "I would assume … pathfinding". Calculators use max+½min straight-line distance. Straight-line max+½min Disputed; flagged in code.
Propaganda as a flip factor The Civilopedia lists "The application of propaganda by the other civilization" as making defection more likely Not modelled (propaganda handled as its own mission) Possibly only the propaganda mission itself, not a modifier. UNVERIFIED.
Capitals No source exempts them; d is capped at 4. DeepO (62844 #16) had "never heared of a capital flipping". Allowed, D = 8000 UNVERIFIED either way.
Refusal script.txt: the receiver may "Rebuff the rebels"; the AI may refuse too Not checked in this pass See "The receiving civ can refuse a flip".
Dead civs Players never saw a flip to a destroyed civ (39825-2 #35, 39825-5 #130) Not checked in this pass Dead civs should be skipped.
Courthouse Civilopedia: courthouse resists propaganda only No effect on flips Consistent with sources.
Order of rolls among several rivals No source Likeliest first UNVERIFIED.
Free defender type "The 'capturing' civ gets one free defender in the city" (Halcyon, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/cultural-conversion-question.87425/ #2, re-fetched). A city cut off from the AI capital gets "only … a Rifleman at best" (anarres, 61562 #6). Best-defence buildable land unit Consistent if "buildable" means buildable in that city with its own resource connections. Exact type UNVERIFIED.
WLTKD and disorder ×0.5 and ×2 Same Matches. Civilopedia adds that "prolonged" disorder leads to defection; no extra factor is known.
Fascism, Retain Culture, assimilation Change F and the culture totals over time Inputs only Make sure F, the city memory and Cte/Cty follow assimilation, Fascist xenophobia and capture rules (§3.10, §3.14).

Disputed

  • When happy citizens are enough for WLTKD.

    • Civilopedia (C3C): "There must be more happy citizens than content citizens." Dan Magaha (Firaxis), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/44579-1.htm #3: "There must be more happy citizens than content ones."
    • The manual says "at least as many happy citizens as content citizens", with the example of 5 happy and 4 content celebrating; that example satisfies both readings.
    • Players in the same Apolyton thread report that ties celebrate. star mouse, #9: "I'm pretty sure I have seen WLT?D when the number of happy citizens was the same." Unregistered, #14: "I'm sure. # happy >= #content will trigger wltkd." Neither cites a test.
    • CFC FAQ: "at least half the citizens must be happy".

  • Fifth border threshold: 20,000 vs 100,000. Civilopedia (C3C): "(10, 100, 1,000, 10,000, 20,000, etc.)". C3C editor help: "0, 10, 100, 1000, 10000, 100000". CFC Info Center: "increase by a factor of 10 each time". TemporalAnomaly's Law I: "threshold values are of the form 10^n c.p." The editor's BorderFactor^n description supports 100,000, and the C3C BIQ has RULE.BorderFactor = 10. The 20,000 may be confused with the one-city culture victory.

  • Content base at Deity in early patches. Skanky Burns (Dec 2001, base game ~1.16f): "On Deity it is 2 people". The C3C Civilopedia, the C3C BIQ and Spoonwood give Deity 1. BIQ-V (1.29f) and BIQ-PTW (1.27f) also give Deity 1 (and Chieftain 4, Warlord 3, Regent 2, Monarch 2, Emperor 1, as in C3C). So if Skanky Burns was right, it was an early-patch value that 1.29f no longer has.

  • WLTKD: corruption and waste, or waste only.

    • The manual says WLTKD "lowers the levels of corruption and waste". Magaha (Firaxis) wrote "The corruption and waste in a city is reduced, effectively increasing the city's shield output".
    • The C3C Civilopedia says only "Waste in the city is reduced", and alexman's article applies it to waste only.
    • Tassadar, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/we-love-the-king-days.34249/: "it cut down shield waste like a courthouse but it doesnt cut down commerce corruption."
    • Thoth, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/35096-1.htm #4 (Nov 2001): it "reduces corruption (more than a courthouse does)".
    • T-hawk, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/we-love-the-king-day-defeats-corruption.13177/ #9 (vanilla era): "(WLTKD applies to shield production only, not commerce income.)", working "exactly as if the city had an additional courthouse". This matches alexman's waste-only model (an extra courthouse step for waste, section 2 §5).
    • The measured C3C rule is waste only; Firaxis' wording is looser.

  • Wonder culture values. Three sources disagree:

    • The C3C BIQ (§3.10): Great Library 6, Sistine 6, Shakespeare 8, Newton 6, Cure for Cancer 3, JS Bach 6, Hoover 2.
    • CFC's Civilopedia page (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/wonders/, probably the base game): Great Library 6, Sistine Chapel 6, Shakespeare 6, Newton 6, Cure for Cancer 4.
    • The Complete manual chart: 5, 4, 5, 5, 5, plus JS Bach 5 and Hoover 3.

    It is not a difference between the final patches. BIQ-V (1.29f) and BIQ-PTW (1.27f) have the same culture values as C3C for every wonder (Great Library 6, Sistine 6, Shakespeare 8, Newton 6, Cure for Cancer 3, JS Bach 6, Hoover 2; the shared buildings match too). The CFC page and the manual chart must reflect an earlier patch or an error. The BIQ values are the defaults in all three final versions.

  • Distance for flips. Pathfinding distance (Magaha, hedged: "I would assume this is measured in tiles") vs straight-line max+½min distance (the calculators, and the border metric in §3.10).

  • Where Cte/Cty applies in the flip formula (see the table above). The engine follows the owner's ruling.

  • Assimilation. The BIQ ships 1–4% chances (§3.14), and the Civilopedia describes assimilation. Two editor tests saw none, and the engine follows the owner's ruling that it never happens.

Resolved in the 2026-10-20 deep pass (kept for the record)

  • Which governments suffer war weariness. This is a version difference. Feudalism exists only in C3C, and the C3C BIQ gives it WarWeariness = 1, the same as Republic (§3.5).
  • Communism draft rate. C3C BIQ GOVT.DraftLimit for Communism = 2, as the 1.17f readme fix says.
  • Celebration benefits by government. Firaxis' list of WLTKD benefits (Magaha, 44579 #3) has nothing government-specific, and the C3C editor has no such flag. The manual's sentence is treated as a Civ2 holdover.
  • Content or happy faces from wonders. The C3C BIQ stores the Hanging Gardens, Mausoleum, Shakespeare, JS Bach and Cure for Cancer effects as content faces (§3.2). The Civilopedia wording is right for C3C, and the "happy" wordings are older or loose.
  • How war weariness ends. Both sources are right. Unhappiness is expressed only while at war (justanick: "During peace WW is suspended"). The points decay about 1/20 per peace turn and carry into a later war (TimBentley, 61628 #66; Oystein, #55).

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • How the luxury-slider share is rounded per city. (The "+50% Luxury Output" flag is on no building in any version: BIQ-V, BIQ-PTW and the C3C BIQ, §3.3.)
  • Where martial law and entertainers fall in the order of face application. The rest of the order has only a community worked example (§3.2).
  • How many citizens become unhappy from "mother country" aggression. Forum guesses range from "one more unhappy" to "about 50%" of the foreign nationals.
  • How armies count toward quelling resistance and toward the flip garrison G. alexman says every unit in an army counts for G, but nobody has tested armies for resistance.
  • Exactly how the Police Station (−25%?) and Universal Suffrage (−1 per city?) reduce war weariness in C3C. The BIQ has only on/off flags, and the numbers are community measurements.
  • How long disorder must last before the government falls, and whether that happens at all outside Democracy's war-weariness revolt. Also how the improvement destroyed by a riot is chosen, and whether "sabotage to ongoing projects" exists.
  • Border edge cases: whether civ culture matters for contested tiles, and Crimso's "first to expand keeps it" objection to Law IV.
  • What the civ-wide culture levels (Lvl. Multiplier: 1000, 2000 … 32000) do besides the labels.
  • How much AI attitude depends on cultural opinion.
  • The "less than half the leader's culture" condition for culture victory.
  • Flip items: the order of rival rolls, whether capitals can flip, the free defender's exact type, propaganda as a flip modifier, integer truncation of the culture ratio, and how the AI decides to refuse a flip.
  • Which nationality a drafted unit or citizen has, and whether foreign nationals are drafted or sacrificed first. Toasty (base game) says only your own nationals can be drafted; this is unconfirmed.
  • Whether specialists count as content for the WLTKD "happy vs content" test.
  • Whether WW points from different enemies are summed before the level is applied (C3X-THREAD: the anarchy test divides total WW by the number of contacts, §3.5; the per-city unhappiness rule is still not shown), or applied per enemy and then summed. The article and Larkin (for war happiness) say per enemy, rounded down, then added. MadScot (61628 #2, Aug 2003) said instead that "the total active WWP for all civs is compared to the threshold values". Third round, code: the 1.22 player record stores war weariness per opponent, int war_weariness[32]; in struct Leader (C3X's header for the exe, https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/Civ3Conquests.h). So the points are kept per enemy. That fits the per-enemy model but does not prove how the level is applied, since the code that reads the array is not published.
  • Code-level field names that bear on this section (third round, same header, names given by C3X's reverse engineering, no logic): each city has turns_of_flip_immunity, so the stock game keeps a per-city timer during which the city can't flip (this fits "a newly-captured town will never flip on the first interturn"; the length is unknown). Each city has Total_Cultures[32], a per-civ culture total, which is the city's memory of every civ's culture behind the flip formula's Cc term (C3X's culture code adds to Total_Cultures[civ_id] of the owner). The city's unhappiness breakdown has separate percentages for no reason, crowding, war weariness, "Agresssion" (the mother-country aggression term), propaganda, draft, oppression (pop rush) and this-city and other-city improvements, plus UnhappyFacesDueToPropaganda, UnhappinessTurnsFromPopRushing, DraftCount and UnhappyTurnsDueToDrafting. So mother-country aggression and enemy propaganda are separate unhappiness causes in the engine.

Back to the top

4. Science, techs and wonders

This section covers research cost, beakers, eras, tech trading, the AI's research choices, free-tech sources, and all Great and Small Wonders in Conquests (C3C 1.22, with Vanilla 1.29f / PTW 1.27f differences where sources give them). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes are out of scope.

Source key. Local files read for this section, from the Steam "Civilization III Complete" install (.../Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete/):

  • BIQ = Conquests/conquests.biq (C3C 1.22 default rules), read with this repo's QueryCiv3 (BLDG, TECH, RULE, WSIZ, DIFF sections). Building costs are stored at 1/10 scale ("In Civ3 files, building costs are stored at 1/10th of their actual value", C7Engine/C7GameData/ImportCiv3.cs). Shield costs below are BIQ x 10.
  • Pedia = Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (Firaxis' C3C Civilopedia). This is not OpenCiv3's C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt, which is our own text.
  • README-V = README.txt (Vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f). README-PTW = civ3PTW/readme.txt. README-C3C = Conquests/readme.txt. EdSupp = Conquests/EditorSupplement.txt.
  • EDHELP = the official C3C editor help, Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm (decompiled), cited by page name.
  • C3X-THREAD = https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (the C3X mod's forum thread, 2021-2026), cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable; his statements about the base game are code readings. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".
  • MANUAL = the Steam manual PDF, https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/3910/manuals/manual.pdf (text extract; page numbers are the printed folios and approximate). It is mostly base-game text (see the note in section 2).

4.1 Research cost

  • Formula (Vanilla 1.29f, reverse-engineered). Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/research-cost-formula-v1-29f.29485/ — "Research Cost = [MM * [10COST * (1 - N/[CL1.75])]/(CF * 10)] - Research done so far". The thread also gives a simplified form: "Research cost = DOWN{MMDM(COST/100) [1 - (N/(CL1.75))]} - Research done so far." The CivFanatics FAQ repeats the first form: https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Research Cost = [MM * [10COST * (1 – N/[CL1.75])]/(CF * 10)] – Research done so far".
    • MM (map size factor) — "MM = map modifier(tech rate on world sizes tab in the editor)". BIQ WSIZ tech rate: Tiny 160, Small 200, Standard 240, Large 320, Huge 400. These match the thread's values.
    • COST — "COST = technology cost as on the civilization advances tab in the editor." The BIQ cost is the base (for example Bronze Working 3, Integrated Defense 360).
    • Check on a Standard map. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/ — "For a standard map (tech rate = 240), the AI needs 24 times the base cost of each technology in beakers." alexman's example: "So if Bronze Working were an empty tech, it would have a value of 256/18=14", where 18 = 72 beakers / 4 per turn.
    • N, CL ("civs that know it" discount) — "N = number of civs on the diplomacy screen that have discovered the tech." "CL = number of civs left in the game". Only contacted civs count toward N. Version note from the same thread: "CL1.5 in 1.21f is now CL1.75 in 1.29f." The 1.21f thread gives a different rounding form: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-trading-valuation.21925/page-2 — "Research cost = MMCOST/10 - UP{MM/100 * UP[COST10N/DOWN(CL*1.5)]} - Research done so far".
    • CF / DM (difficulty) — "CF = AI cost factor(as on the difficulty tab in the editor)" and "For the purposes of the research cost formula, CF has a maximum value of 10." Qitai's correction in the same thread: "Cost factor = {20, 12, 10, 9, 8, 6} for the various difficulty level", which gives DM {10, 10, 10, 11.1, 12.5, 16.7}. The author confirms: "the Monarch figure is 11.1". BIQ DIFF cost factors in C3C: Chieftain 20, Warlord 12, Regent 10, Monarch 9, Emperor 8, Demigod 7, Deity 6, Sid 4. Implementation reading: research cost x 10/min(CF,10), so x1.0 at Regent and below, x1.11 Monarch, x1.25 Emperor, x1.43 Demigod, x1.67 Deity, x2.5 Sid. The Demigod and Sid values are an UNVERIFIED extrapolation, because the thread predates PTW's extra levels.
    • Whom it applies to. Same thread: "This formula is relevant for research cost of the AI civs, not just the human player" and "techs are now more expensive for the human player on Monarch, Emperor and Deity." See Disputed, item 1.
    • Since 1.29f the AI pays the same (Regent) price at every level; the difficulty factor scales the human's cost. alexman (by then a Firaxis software engineer), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/66516-1.htm (Apolyton "The Chieftain Research Myth", 4 Nov 2002) — "On Chieftain, technologies used to cost the same as Regent for the human, but were twice as expensive for the AI." / "But since 1.29f, Chieftain technologies cost half as much for the player, and are constant for all levels for the AI". alexman again, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/page-2 (#31, 30 Jun 2003) — "the research cost of each tech for the AI is the same for all difficulty levels." / "The AI gets a production bonus (or penalty) which exactly cancels the increase (or decrease) in cost". Qitai (#32) dates it: "the research cost changes is effective vanilla civ3 1.29, not PTW." The cost-factor editor text agrees that CF is about the AI: EDHELP "Cost Factor (AI Bonuses)" — "Determines the cost factor that is applied to AI growth, shields, and research. The player's cost factor is always 10." C3C confirmation (player): https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/late-game-tech-rate-confusion.696592/ ([C3C], tjs282, 2025) — "the AI always pays the 'Regent' price for an unknown tech" and "your 'unknown tech' beaker-costs will be 1/0.8 = 1.25x times the AI's" at Emperor. C3C multiplayer check: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/technology-list-and-cost.193586/ (AutomatedTeller, C3C MTDG, 2007, Emperor, Standard map) — "the cost of pottery is 2, which if you use that formula, becomes 60 (240 102 / 8)." Owner ruling for the engine: the human factor is capped at 10 (no discount below Regent). alexman's "half as much for the player" on Chieftain conflicts with that cap; see Disputed, item 1.
    • Exact rounding. TechCalc 2.01 (a tool "For all versions of Civilization III. (Patched versions)"), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/utility-techcalc.38631/ (Grey Fox, Dec 2002) — "ResearchCost = Down((MM/CF)(Cost-Up.1(CostN/Down(CL*1.75))))"; mydisease's restatement in the same thread: "ROUNDDOWN((MM/CF) * (Cost - (.1 * ROUNDUP(10 * COST * N/ROUNDDOWN(CL * 1.75)))))". Here MM/CF is the per-civ multiplier (24 at Regent on a Standard map), and CL counts the civs left, including your own. With 5 civs, each known holder cuts 1/8.75 of the cost: fe3333au, same 193586 thread — "The cost reduces by 11.43% of the Beaker Cost each time another known civ pocesses it."
  • Future techs. BIQ RULE: Future Tech Cost = 400. The editor field was added in Vanilla (README-V: "Editor: On General Settings, added Future Tech Cost."). EDHELP "Future Tech Cost" — "Determines the cost of each "future technology". Default is 400." MANUAL p.125: after the last named advance "they can begin researching futuristic advances" / "When your civilization accumulates enough scientific research to finish one unit of Future Technology, you can research another. Each Future Technology you discover adds to your final score". "Each" in EDHELP suggests a flat base cost (400 in place of the BIQ tech cost, then the usual multipliers). How 400 enters the formula, and whether later future techs cost more, is UNVERIFIED.
  • Map size and difficulty enter as multipliers (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16624227, page-107 #2127): "the game already has a multiplier on all tech costs for each map size" (the WSIZ tech rate above).
  • The current research is not re-checked each turn (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16638420, page-109 #2167): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "The game has a function that determines if a given tech is a research option for a given player" / "the game doesn't check every turn if the current research project is still allowed." So research already under way continues even if it would no longer be a valid choice.
  • Integer rounding. Commerce-to-beaker conversion floors, so some beakers are lost. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (vorlon_mi) — "Some of those calculations use the "FLOOR" function, to produce an integer result that drops the fractional parts."

4.2 Minimum and maximum research time

  • C3C: 4 to 50 turns. BIQ RULE: MinimumResearchTime = 4, MaximumResearchTime = 50. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "For all three games, the minimum research length is 4 turns." and "For Conquests, that was upped to 50 turns as the maximum."
  • Vanilla history. README-V (1.16f): "The maximum number of turns it can take to research an advance has been increased from 32 to 40." README-V (1.29f): "Editor: On General Settings, added Min. Tech Turns." / "added Max. Tech Turns."
  • What the maximum means. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (justanick) — "The time for researching a tech is limited to 4 turn as the minimum and 50 turns as the maximum." and "You cannot use up more than 50 turns of actual research, no matter how little you spend". jarred! in the same thread: "it'll still take the 50 turn max to finish a 200 beaker tech". Reading: as long as at least some research is funded, the tech completes on turn 50 at the latest.
    • One beaker a turn is enough (1.29f/PTW). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-role-of-the-specialist-citizen.108789/ (Bede, Jan 2005, on 1.29f and PTW scientists at 1 beaker) — "If you employ only one you can research any technology at a total cost of 40 beakers at a rate of one beaker per turn." That is the 40-turn cap of those versions. C3C: alexman (Firaxis Games Software Engineer), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/117372-50-turn-techs (4 Nov 2003) — "scientists now give 3 beakers each, so you actually waste research potential when researching a tech with a single scientist and 0% science." The waste only makes sense if the tech arrives at the cap however little is spent. So the cap is a forced completion after the maximum number of funded turns, not a cost reduction. Whether a turn with zero beakers counts toward the 50 is UNVERIFIED.
  • Editor text. EDHELP "Maximum Research Time" — "Determines the maximum number of turns in which any technology can be researched. Default is 40." (the help text predates the C3C BIQ value of 50). EDHELP "Minimum Research Time" — "Determines the minimum number of turns in which any technology can be researched. Default is 4."
  • What the minimum means. https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 (David Weldon) — "Yes, there is a minimum of 4 turns for a tech advance, no matter how much overkill you use." See Disputed, item 2. Whether beakers above the 4-turn pace are lost or banked is UNVERIFIED.
    • Tested floor, and where 3-turn techs come from (Vanilla 1.0x). https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33948-2.htm ("How science works and why there IS a 4 turn cap") — korn469 set the editor tech rate to 1 and "there was still a four turn rate cap"; David Weldon called that "the final confirmation". The 3-turn reports in the same thread come from changes during the research: Capt Dizle, "You can get tech in three turns if you start at four and your tech production increases during the 4 turn period", and "Probably has to do with the tech being discovered by an AI civ during the period I am researching it"; TrainWreck20 got two 3-turn techs "during my 'golden age'". Reading: the floor is applied when the cost is set, and a later rise in output or fall in cost can still finish the tech in 3 turns. This is UNVERIFIED for C3C.
  • Slider floor. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (vmxa) — "at the start you cannot drop below 20% and research, unless you have a scientist running." Treat this as UNVERIFIED as a rule. It probably reflects the 50-turn cap, since research has to be fast enough to beat 50 turns.

4.3 Beaker overflow and loss

  • Excess from the completing city is lost; later cities feed the next tech. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-few-questions.225457 (TimTheEnchanter) — "The cities are processed one at a time in reverse order of their creation." and "Any excess beakers from the city that gets you to the next tech are lost." ElephantU, same thread: cities processed after the completing one add their research to the new tech.
  • Simpler player statements (all extra lost). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/are-extra-science-beakers-wasted.63391 (Rik Meleet) — "When a tech is researched all extra beakers are lost." https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "there is no shield or food carryover for cities, just like there is no beaker carryover when researching a technology." See Disputed, item 3.
  • Measured loss. The overflow mod's tracker found about 5% of beakers lost: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/mod-tech-overflow.398296/latest — "generated 7,562 research and lost 375 (4.96%)" (as reported in search results; not re-fetched).
  • A 2002 test of the overflow. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/science-slider-when-1-turn-from-tech-change-it-or-not.24350/page-2 (Jim, Vanilla, Jun 2002) — "The ~494 beakers were lost forever." / "Thus, excess beakers are lost." T-hawk (page 1): "I've tested this. The beakers don't carry over."
  • Switching research throws away the beakers (Vanilla manual). MANUAL p.123 — "you can interrupt research in progress." / "Of course, by changing their focus, you lose all of their work on the advance you order them to abandon." Players at the time agreed: same thread page 1, Sir Yelof, "you lose all the beakers you put into researching a tech if you switch techs in midstream"; Tassadar, "you restart at 0"; regoarrarr, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33948-1.htm — "if you switch techs that research is gone forever." kring (24350 page 2): "Switch to another tech in the middle of a turn; you can then switch back." So a switch that is undone within the same turn costs nothing. T-hawk claimed that only half is lost (see Disputed, item 9). No later patch note changes this rule.
  • Receiving the tech you are researching. MANUAL p.123 — "If by chance you're given the civilization advance your scientists are currently researching, your Science Advisor immediately switches the research effort to a new topic of your choice." README-V (1.16f): "Traded advances that cause an era transition, now allows the player to select the advance to research in the new era." Whether the stored beakers survive is disputed (Disputed, item 9).
  • Theory of Evolution discards stored beakers. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/theory-of-evolution-toe.60765 (Gengis Khan) — "All beakers stored when the ToE is completed are lost."
  • Research is applied at turn start. Pedia GCON_Research — "At the start of each turn, the science output of each city is added to the research project currently in progress".
  • C3C scientists. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "Scientists now produce 3 beakers per turn instead of 1."

4.4 Eras: required vs optional techs

  • Four eras. BIQ ERAS: Ancient Times, Middle Ages, Industrial Ages, Modern Times.
  • Every required tech of the current era must be known before advancing. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 (Plotinus) — "a civ cannot progress from one era to another without first researching all the non-essential techs from the earlier one". "Non-essential" is evidently a slip for "required". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/certain-clarifications.22543 (Mr Spice) — "Yes, you have to possess them, but there are plenty of other ways to accuire techs than to research them on your own."
  • You cannot research into the next era early. The structural evidence is in the BIQ. The first-tier techs of each later era have no prerequisites in the BIQ: Monotheism, Feudalism and Engineering (Middle); Nationalism, Steam Power and Medicine (Industrial); Rocketry, Fission, Computers and Ecology (Modern). The editor forbids cross-era prerequisites. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 — GRM7584: "Era-specific techs can only have prereqs in their era, or NONE-era"; Paasky quotes the editor error "The required tech must be in the same era". So the era gate, not prerequisites, is what keeps next-era techs closed. The manual says so directly: MANUAL p.123 — "To move forward from one scientific age to the next (and gain access to the advances in that age), you're required to successfully research almost all of the advances available in your current age. The only exceptions are advances that fall into the category of optional." Its list of optional Ancient advances (Horseback Riding, Literature, Monarchy, Republic) includes Horseback Riding, which the C3C BIQ below does not mark optional. The manual's list is older text; the BIQ is authoritative.
  • Trading and the Great Library cross eras. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/tech-prerequisites-in-previous-eras.472427/ (Quintillus) — "tech trading and the Great Library techs are always across eras." README-V (1.16f): "Traded advances that cause an era transition, now allows the player to select the advance to research in the new era."
  • Optional techs never block advancement. BIQ flag NotRequiredForEraAdvancement. The editor checkbox is "Not Needed for Era Advancement", and the F6 chart marks these techs: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/73736-what-technologies-don-t-you-need (vondrack) — "the optional techs have a slashed circle in the upper right corner."
  • Free techs above your era wait for their prerequisites. EDHELP "Free Techs (Civilization Properties)" — "Note that advanced technologies may be given as "free techs" but a civilization will not receive them until all required prerequisites have been fulfilled." EDHELP "Era (Civilization Advance)" — "Selecting "none" will make the technology unavailable for research by any of the civilizations in the game, but you can assign that technology to any civilization as a "free tech"."
  • Era-None techs. README-V: "Editor: Updated ERA_NONE techs to work as expected (you can never research them and they don't impede era advancement but they can be assigned as free techs)." README-PTW: if a civ cannot reach a non-era prerequisite, the techs sprouting from it "will not be required for advancement to the next era."
  • C3C optional techs (BIQ).
Era Techs Optional (not required)
Ancient 21 Literature, The Republic, Monarchy
Middle Ages 22 Chivalry, Printing Press, Music Theory, Democracy, Economics, Navigation, Free Artistry, Military Tradition
Industrial 23 Nationalism, Communism, Sanitation, Espionage, Amphibious War, Advanced Flight, Ironclads, Fascism
Modern 17 none

4.5 Conquests tech-tree differences (vs Vanilla/PTW)

  • Radio removed; Ironclads and Fascism added. README-C3C (1.20): "Radio Tech removed", "Electronics requirement removed from Motorized Transportation", "Civil Defense requires Electronics", "Advanced Flight now requires Electronics", "Radar Towers require Advanced Flight". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — Ironclads: "requires Steam Power, required for nothing, allows Ironclads unit, optional tech"; Fascism: "requires Nationalism, required for nothing, allows Fascism Government, optional tech". The BIQ confirms both (Industrial, optional, costs 100 and 130).
  • Philosophy became a flagged bonus tech. Same thread: "Philosophy now gives a bonus tech to the first civ to research it." BIQ: Philosophy has BonusTechToFirstCivThatResearches.
  • Diplomatic tech moves. Same thread (Arathorn): "Map trading moved from available with Map Making to Printing Press." and "Communications trading moved from available with Map Making to Navigation." README-C3C notes MPTournament rules moved map trading back ("MPTournament: Map Trading moved back to Map Making").
  • Tech count. Thunderfall, same thread: "it is now 81 with the removal of Radio". The C3C BIQ has 83 era techs (21+22+23+17). The 81 figure is UNVERIFIED and may be pre-1.22 or may exclude the two new optional techs. Android Assembly (C3X-THREAD post-16829069, page-139 #2774): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "there are 83 techs with conquests, but there were 82 with vanilla", which matches the BIQ.
  • Tech purchase price. Same thread (Arathorn): "Changed calculation on tech prices, in terms of gold required to buy." The new formula is UNVERIFIED.

4.6 Tech trading and tech brokering

  • Who can trade a tech. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 (Hugin) — general techs "can be traded with any other civilization, as long as that civ has the prerequisite technologies". The BIQ has a CannotBeTraded tech flag; no C3C default tech sets it.
  • What makes a tech tradeable (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16839866, page-142 #2840): the tech must be owned by the seller, "be an available research option for the buyer", not be flagged untradeable, and, for the AI, "pass some kind of flavor threshold". "era none techs are not available as research options, so they can't be traded either". This agrees with the manual's "can research" wording.
  • AI-to-AI trade rate. BIQ DIFF "AI to AI trade rate", exposed in Vanilla (README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Exposed AI to AI trade rate."). C3C values: Chieftain 110, Warlord 120, Regent 130, Monarch 140, Emperor 150, Demigod 160, Deity 170, Sid 200.
    • Meaning (Firaxis editor help). EDHELP "AI to AI trade rate (AI Bonuses)" — "This value is a percentage (acceptable values range from 100% to 1000%) which is multiplied by the total value (in gold) of the initiating civ's offerings, which is used by the "other" AI civ to determine whether a deal is acceptable or not. For example, with a rate of 120, an AI civ offering 100 gold to another AI civ for something would actually be worth 120 gold to the other AI civ." / "This rate directly affects the frequency and aggressiveness with which AI civs trade amongst themselves." It does not apply to the human: WarpStorm, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/68856-1.htm (Nov 2002) — "AI to AI trade rate only applies to AI players, not to the human players."
    • Possibly only when the buyer is short (secondhand Soren Johnson). Same thread, WarpStorm: "Soren said that the AI to AI trade rate is deficit spending only." Catt's reading: "only if the buying AI cannot afford full price will the Trade Rate come into play". This matches Soren Johnson (Firaxis), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42905-1.htm (20 Feb 2002, 1.17f era) — AIs "might be willing to sell a tech for down to half-value, but only if the other AI has nothing else to give for it." The EDHELP text describes no such condition. Whether the multiplier applies to every AI-AI deal or only to deals the buyer cannot otherwise afford is UNVERIFIED.
    • Other Firaxis points on AI-AI trading (Vanilla 1.17f). Same 42905 thread, Soren Johnson: "The AI's do not give techs away for free." AIs are "now doing per-turn gold offers for techs", and "They aren't allowed to sign per-turn deals they can't pay."
  • AI price drops as more civs know a tech; monopolies are dear. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "techs are very expensive to buy or research when no one knows them". The FAQ also names the classic brokering trade: "called a '2fer' or 'xfer' (2 for 1)."
  • Brokering. Buy a tech from one AI and resell it to the others. https://civfanatics.com/?p=171 ("Zero Percent Science") — the author calls himself a "Day Trader" and sells a tech for "a few gold per turn for 20 turns". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/science-broker-analysis.10012 (Baron23) — "The AI also plays tech broker at times".
  • AIs pass techs on among themselves. https://civfanatics.com/?p=170 — "they'll trade it amongst themselves if you give it to one so give it to all of them at the same time."
  • The AI's trade value differs from its research value. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/page-3 (alexman) — "The AI places a value on each tech for trading purposes, which (according to Soren) is actually different". Vanilla also changed this: README-V (1.21f) "Adjusted advance trading rates for AI." The full trade-valuation formula is UNVERIFIED and belongs to the diplomacy section.
  • Player-observed trade-value factors (C3C, no formula). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-do-ais-value-techs-in-market.126379/ (2005) — TimBentley: "The things that make the AI value techs more include enables government, drafting, or mobilization", "the more people that know a tech (particularly if it's a monopoly) will affect the price.", "Optional techs are worth less." Khan_Asparuh: "Is it optionnal - 2/3 of the price." and "If you already have some beakers invested in a tech, it's cheaper." TimBentley: "As far as I know a formula to determine the trade value has not been computed." A built wonder lowers the trade value of its tech but not its research cost: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-much-techs-cost.62580/ (Catt, Aug 2003) — "the trade cost drops to 10 or 11 gold after the build" (Free Artistry after Shakespeare's) and "the research cost of Free Artistry stays unchanged by the building of Shakespeare's Theater". These are observations, not a tested formula.

4.7 AI research choice (alexman) — already in C7Engine/AI/DevelopmentAI.cs

  • Method. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/ — "The AI places a value on each technology it can research next. The values depend on the various things allowed by each technology, as well as on the turns needed by that AI civ". It was found "by stripping all technologies from their benefits, adding them back one by one". "These values are cumulative." The values feed a weighted random choice: "techs with higher value were chosen more often" (alexman, quoted in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-tech-research.657856/), and tjs282 there: "still basically just a weighting-factor for a pRNG dice-roll".
  • Values. "/turns" means divided by the turns the AI needs for the tech. Per turn: Empty tech 256, SS component 261, Government 259, Conscription 259, Mobilization 259, No trade 149. Flat: Defender (no resource) 198, Attacker (no resource) 134, Defender (resource required) 70, Naval transport 34, Resource 16, Double worker speed 8, Diplomats 8, MPP 8, ROP 8, Alliances 8, Embargoes 8, Trade over ocean 8, Attacker (resource required) 6, Wonder 6, Irrigation 4, No disease 4, Trade over sea 4, Bridges 2, Double wealth 2, Map trades 2, Communications trade 2, Small wonder 2, Specialist 2, Unit (no A/D/NT) 2, Improvement 2, Other tech 1, Recycling 1, Precision bombing 1, Worker job 1, Bonus tech 0, Reveal map 0, Sacrifices 0. "Optional: divide by 1.5".
  • Worked checks. "Iron Working allows swordsmen and iron, so it has a value of 22 more than if it were just an empty tech". That is Resource 16 + Attacker-needing-resource 6. And "for the Romans who build Legionaries (attacker and defender), Iron Working is worth an additional 70 points!", which is Defender-needing-resource 70.
  • Version. The base list dates from PTW-era play on 1.29f rules (Bamspeedy cites "Civ3 1.29f"; Qitai on page 2: "the research cost changes is effective vanilla civ3 1.29, not PTW"). The War Academy copy of the article (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/what-will-the-ai-research-next/) has only the original list, ending "Empty Tech Cost: 256/turns" / "Optional: divide by 1.5". C3C update: alexman, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/page-4 (#69, 21 Nov 2003) — "A small update with new values for Conquests:" "Cannot be traded: 149/turns", "Bonus tech: 0", "Reveal Map: 0", "Sacrifices: 0", and "luxury, bonus, and strategic resources have the same value (16), even if these are required by units and improvements." His #70 (1 Dec 2003) first claimed that C3C values resource-needing units like the others, then withdrew it: "Edit: Nevermind. My C3C test was flawed." So the 1.29f/PTW list plus these four entries is the C3C list.
  • "No trade: 149/turns" — resolved. It is the "Cannot Be Traded" tech flag, named as such in alexman's C3C update above ("Cannot be traded: 149/turns"). The BIQ has it (TECH.CannotBeTraded, flag byte 2 bit 3). The comment in DevelopmentAI.cs that says "no BIQ tech flag found" is wrong. No default C3C tech sets this flag, so standard games are unaffected.
  • "Other tech: 1" — resolved. alexman, same page (#77, 5 Nov 2004) — "A tech gets 1 point for each 'other tech' of which it is a direct prerequisite." It counts each tech that lists this one as a prerequisite. Tension: by the C3C BIQ as read with QueryCiv3, Iron Working is a prerequisite of Construction, yet alexman's early Iron Working example totals exactly 22 (16 + 6). Either the 2003 example left out the +1, or the prerequisite differed in the tree he tested. At 1 point this is negligible.
  • One function values techs for both research and trade (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16719849, page-123 #2448): "There is a single function inside the game that evaluates the worth of any given tech for the AI." / its valuations are "roughly equivalent to the amount of gold the AI would ask" / "It looks like the AI especially likes techs that unlock units with new AI strategies," / "AIs leading military civs put more emphasis on unlocking units." The first point fits C3X-INI ("worth roughly one gold in trade, and also affects what the AI chooses to research", section 8 §3.6), and goes against alexman's relay of Soren that the trade value "is actually different" (4.6); Flintlock's is the code reading. The militaristic emphasis is a term alexman's list does not have.
  • Flavors left out on purpose. Same page, alexman (#77): "Flavors are more complicated than other things in the list, so I have not included them here." Ambiorix: "Since they're not used in a standard game, I haven't included them in the calculations." Default C3C techs have flavors = 0 in the BIQ.
  • How the values become a choice. alexman, 45559 page 2 (#24): "it seems that there is a random factor involved" and "the higher the AI value of a tech, the more likely it will be chosen." Qitai (#34): "There is a RNG involve in the choosing of what the AI research." Ambiorix (#35) proposed, untested, a chance proportional to value: "Would it make sense to say that the chance of tech one being chosen would be 200/(200+50) ?" DaveMcW, from his C3C model, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ais-favorite-techs.123345/ — "These are the 20 favorite techs of the Conquests AI." and "The top 5 or so techs in an era will be researched at close to 100% probablility, but it gets harder to predict as you go down the list." Owner ruling for the engine: a weighted roll on these values (DevelopmentAI.cs uses linear weights). The exact weighting is not documented.
  • Worked C3C totals (Doc Tsiolkovski, same 123345 thread; player arithmetic). "Nationalism: 259 (conscription) + 259 (mob) + 198 (defender) + 134 (attacker) + 8 (MPP) +2 (specialist) =860", "Divide by 1.5 (optional) = 573.3333333"; "Steam: 16 (Coal) + 1 (RR) + 6 (Iron Works) = 23". TimBentley corrects him: "the values for conscription and mobilization are divided by the number of turns." So the per-turn items shrink as research time grows. The model is reported "80%+ accurate at least" (Beorn-eL-Feared) against play.
  • Other nuances in the thread. Unique units count only when they differ from the unit they replace ("Bowmen... count as both attacker and defender"). Only direct effects count (Sanitation gets nothing for Battlefield Medicine). A benefit counts even if the civ already has it (Magnetism still gets Trade over Ocean). Flavors: Ozymandias, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-tech-research.657856/ — "I'm 99% certain that the effect of a Flavor is 5%." This is UNVERIFIED and not in DevelopmentAI.cs. Vanilla also fixed an AI bug: README-V "Fixed AI bug giving value to worthless techs."

4.8 Free techs

Great Library

  • Effect. Pedia BLDG_Great_Library — "The civilization that builds the [Great Library] gets any Civilization Advance already discovered by two other known civilizations for free." BIQ flag GainAnyTechKnownByTwoCivs; cost 400, Literature, obsolete with Education.
  • Only contacted civs count. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-library-question.78293 (sealman) — "You get all the techs that you do not have that are known to at least 2 civs that you have contact with." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-great-library-work.26689 (Catt) — "If you only know one civ, the Great Library will do nothing for you."
  • Timing. Same thread (robinm) — "The Library checks all civs you have met at the beggining of each of your turns".
  • The "two civs" is a constant in the code. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16884982, page-158 #3147): changing the count is "only a matter of overwriting '2' in the game code with some other number." tjs282 and justanick (post-16345754 and post-16346025, page-52 #1029/#1033): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "You need to have contact with 2 civs that have a tech you donnot have."
  • Contact must run both ways (code, third round). C3X wraps the stock Leader_update_great_library_unlocks (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c). To share the library across hotseat humans it has to set contact on both sides: "we must mark that "this" has contacted the AIs and vice-versa otherwise the techs won't be granted". The test is bit 0 of Leader.Contacts[]. So the stock grant counts only civs with mutual contact.
  • Obsolescence. Vanilla: README-V "Great Library: This Great Wonder is now obsolete when Education is researched." Obsolescence is checked after the turn's grants. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/strange-tech-glitch.112341 (Renata) — "it is possible to gain techs long past education in circumstances like yours". robinm (26689) — "you continue to recieve all of the other techs the GL made available to you for that turn only."
  • Only the owner's Education makes it obsolete. https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42514-1.htm (Feb 2002) — Ballz1998: "The great library only becomes obsolete after the owner discovers education."; Gramphos: "That is true. You need to have the advance that stops a wonder from working to stop it." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/obsolete.100124/ (gunkulator, 2004) — "In Civ3, the wonder is obsolete only if the owner has the tech." The same holds for every wonder (4.11). A captured or gifted library works again for an owner without Education: sealman (same thread), a captured wonder "starts working again"; OPD, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/94996-1.htm (2003) — "the city with the GL was gifted to me, giving all techs up to the IA."; Aggie, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-guide-to-great-wonders-and-how-to-use-them.97063/page-2 (C3C, 2004) — "give the TGL city away just before you have Education and then retaking it." Owner ruling for the engine: one pass in editor order with no era limit, so a captured library can lift its owner past Education.
  • One turn behind. https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/74795-1.htm (steven8r, 2003) — "The civ w/ the GL doesn't learn the new tech until the turn AFTER it is learned." This fits robinm's start-of-turn check. "Contact" means the civ appears on your diplomacy screen: Theseus (same thread), "I think 'contact' is just knowing another civ, i.e., they are able to appear in your F4 screen."
  • The AI does not exploit it specially. Soren Johnson (Firaxis chat, 7 Nov 2001), quoted in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/84522-1.htm — "the Great Library is one of the Wonders the AI prefers" / "the AI likes the Pyramids, the Great Library, the Art of War, the Hoover Dam, Sistine Chapel maybe?". Bamspeedy (same thread): "The AI puts a value on a tech that has a wonder, not favoring one wonder over the other." (player test).
  • Prerequisites and editor order. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers-newbie-questions.318752/page-275 (justanick) — "You can only gain those techs that you have the prerequisite for." "Music theory seems to be one of them, amphibic warfare is another one and possibly space flight,too." "As i recall it the techs are checked in the order in which they are listed in the editor." So within one turn, a tech whose prerequisite is granted later in the list waits a turn. PTW fix (README-PTW): "Fixed a bug with non-era techs that allowed players to potentially receive them for free from the Great Library (NOTE: this also fixes a similar bug that would allow players to receive techs that they do not have the prerequisites for if the order of the techs in the editor was changed)."

Theory of Evolution

  • Effect. Pedia BLDG_Theory_of_Evolution — "Grants two Civilization Advances as soon as completed." BIQ flag TwoFreeAdvances; 600 shields, Scientific Method, never obsolete.
  • Which techs. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/theory-of-evolution-toe.60765 (Yndy) — "First tech is the one you were currently researching." The second is the next one you pick. Turner: "Then you get a pop up box asking you which tech you'd like to research next." Stored beakers are lost (Gengis Khan, quoted in 4.3). See Disputed, item 6 for "random techs".
  • More player reports. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/science-slider-when-1-turn-from-tech-change-it-or-not.24350/page-2 (RebelX210, Vanilla 2002) — "Theory of Evolution will finish up the tech you are currently researching and give you one more." T-hawk (page 1): ToE "forces you to switch your current tech TWICE, losing half each time." https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/62071-1.htm (Bella Hella, 2002) — "you can choose the techs you want after getting ToE." NDCSPURS (97063 page 2, C3C) — "set your research so that it is complete the same turn as ToE" so the stored research is not wasted.
  • Editor wording. EDHELP "Miscellaneous Effect Flags (Wonder Properties)" — "+2 Free Advances: Two free, randomly selected advances are granted". This is the one source for "random"; the players' reports describe a current-research-plus-next-pick pattern.
  • Patch fixes. README-V: "Fixed bug which sometimes caused you to not get any techs from Theory of Evolution." README-PTW: "Fixed bug with free tech popups from Theory of Evolution."

Philosophy (C3C)

  • Effect. Pedia TECH_Philosophy — "{Bonus Tech} The first civilization to research this tech is awarded a free tech." BIQ: only Philosophy carries the bonus flag.
  • The player picks the free tech, from what is researchable at that moment. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/free-tech-tricks.90177 (DaveMcW) — "Finish research or pop Philosophy from a hut, and you can pick any of the Middle Age techs for free!" This is the case where Philosophy was the last required Ancient tech, so the era advanced first. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — "If you manage to get Philosophy first, you get Republic as your free tech!"
  • How the pick works: the next tech you choose after the popup. https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/102197-3.htm (Apolyton "Express Path to Republic in C3C", Nov 2003) — ducki: "as long as you don't leave the F1-F6 screens, your tech choice isn't final." He adds that the engine "needs the 'tick' provided by the closing of the popup." Catt says the "trade tech before selecting a new tech" trick was used, that is, trading for a prerequisite before choosing, to widen the choice. Players report picking freely among what is researchable: 102197-2, SirOsis, "Then I chose Monarchy as my free tech."; steven8r, "used my freebie on Literature--no one else had it." Dominae, 102197-1: "To get The Republic free from Philosophy, you need to research Code of Laws first." nbarclay, 102197-3: "If a player hasn't done much trading to the AIs, he might get stuck with something cheap like Mysticism or Warrior Code." Firaxis framing: alexman (Firaxis Games Software Engineer), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/117372-50-turn-techs — "Philosophy now provides a free tech to the first one to discover it (like it used to do in earlier versions of Civ)."
  • The free tech counts as a first discovery for Scientific Leaders. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-do-you-get-a-scientific-leader.211436 (AutomatedTeller, 2007) — "You can also get one for researching philosophy and getting that free tech". Two leaders in one turn from a Philosophy slingshot are reported in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/2-x-sci-leaders.292238 (xyresic, "Both on the same turn.") and https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/scientific-leader-probability-generator-excel.69141/ (ThinkerRCB, odds "1/400").
  • Version note. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — "none of them work in PTW." The free-tech tricks are C3C (Philosophy) and Vanilla/C3C (Scientific). The CivFanatics FAQ lists the Philosophy rule among Conquests changes: "Philosphy now grants a free technology if researched first".

Scientific trait

  • Effect. Pedia GCON_Strengths — "Scientific city improvements (like research labs) are easier to build and the civilization receives a free Civilization Advance at the start of every era." https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Upon entering a new age, they receive a free technology (one of the starting technologies for that age)."
  • Selection is random among the new era's researchable techs, and seems to avoid techs AIs already have. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/free-tech-tricks.90177 (DaveMcW) — the trick yields "a different free tech that none of the AIs have"; with the Russian triple slingshot there is "a 33% chance to learn Education or Gunpowder as your scientific tech". README-PTW: "Improved random free tech selection for the Scientific civ trait."
  • Firaxis on the selection rule. Mike Breitkreutz (Firaxis, lead programmer for PTW), quoted by Catt in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/65178-1.htm (16 Jan 2003, from a CFC GOTM spoiler thread) — "It is supposed to select, at random, one tech from the new era that is available to the player" "(i.e., it has no prerequisites)." In 1.29f the pick was biased by list order: "As you can see, Rocketry is heavily favored (simply because of it's placement within the list)." His Modern-era odds were about 91% Rocketry, 7% Ecology, 1% Fission and 1% Computers. "This is unintentional and all 4 techs should have an equal chance of being selected." / "I will look into improving this for the next PTW patch." The README-PTW line above is that fix.
