So the intended rule (PTW and C3C) is a uniform random pick among the new era's researchable techs. DaveMcW's "a different free tech that none of the AIs have" is consistent with the pick excluding techs you already hold, for example ones just traded for. Whether techs known to AIs are excluded is UNVERIFIED.
  • 1.29f test. Same thread, Catt (Oct 2002): he reloaded before the Modern Age and tried all four research choices, and "In all cases I received Ecology as the free tech." So the pick did not depend on the current research, and it was not re-rolled on reload. The thread does not explain why it was Ecology rather than the favoured Rocketry.
  • Players' later summary. CommandoBob, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/scientific-trait-question.448033 #3 (2011, C3C): "Now you can get any of the Tier 1 techs of the next age, but the selection process is random", while in vanilla "you had no choice which tech you got; it was always the same", which matches the 1.29f list-order bias above.
  • Timing. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — after the "new era" popup, the free tech shows up once the turn is processed ("check your research screen").

Scientific Great Leaders (C3C)

  • Spawn. Pedia GCON_Leaders — "Each time a technology is researched for the [first] time by any civilization there is a chance that a Scientific leader will arise (this chance is increased slightly if the researching civilization has the "scientific" strength." Rates: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "has a 3% chance of appearing if a Civ is the first to discover a tech (5% for Scientific civs)." See Disputed, item 4. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/scientific-leader-probability-generator-excel.69141/ (socralynnek) — "But it's hardcoded to be 3% for non-scientific civs and 5% for scientific civs." He finds "no difference between 1.00 and 1.22 regarding the probability of getting SGLs." AutomatedTeller, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-do-you-get-a-scientific-leader.211436 — "there is a 3% chance if you are the first to research a tech that isn't known by anyone." / "That chance is 5% if you are a scientific civ." Turner, same thread: "Scientific Great Leaders are only in Conquests. In Vanilla/PTW, you only get Military Great Leaders." pol1: "he/she will appear in Your capital." Huts: AutomatedTeller, "though you can't get them from huts - at least, I have never gotten one" (observation only). Chamnix, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-scientists.270481/ #16/#18 (C3C): "you cannot get an SGL from a tech popped from a hut", after about 100 hut techs with none; Othniel (#19) notes others claim the opposite, so still UNVERIFIED. Several at once: vmxa (#5) says SGLs have no one-at-a-time check, unlike MGLs, and Son_Of_Dido (#14) that "SGLs can be stocked and hoarded". That fits the code side: the one-at-a-time rule is tied to the editor's battle-created unit (C3X config: "a player can't spawn a battle-created unit from combat while they already have one"), and the SGL is not that unit.
  • Uses. Pedia — "A Scientific Leader can be used to increase the scientific output of all cities of his civilization, thus starting an Age of Science" and "can also be used to hurry the construction of any [improvement] (including a Great Wonder)". Pedia GCON_Age_of_Science — "a 20-turn period in which the scientific research of each city in that civilization's empire is 25% more productive."
  • Age of Science is broken in the shipped game. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "this functionality does not work. The button is there, but it does nothing." Post 2 of that thread says the same about 1.22. The executable confirms it: Antal1987, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/patch-framework-for-c3c-v1-22.526966/ (May 2014, disassembly of the 1.22 exe). At the place the bonus belongs, the original bytes are NOPs, annotated "_Total_Bulbs = (_Total_Bulbs * 1.25);". His "Useful Patches" list, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/useful-patches-for-civ-3-conquests-v1-22.526285, carries a "Fix of the science age bug". So in the shipped 1.22 game the Age of Science button has no effect, and the intended +25% was meant to apply to the civ's total research. The C3X config (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini) explains the mechanism: "The bonus science points generated by a scientific golden age are not actually counted towards research progress. They appear only on the interface", because a setting "which controls whether or not science age bonus points are included while gathering all research points" is "turned off while gathering points to update research progress". So the bonus beakers show on screen but never reach the tech. Antal1987's own analysis (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/infinite-golden-age-and-science-age.526292/, May 2014, C3C 1.22): "When the Game initiates Science Age, it sets Science Age Status to 1 and Science Age End to Current Turn + 20" (#1). His excerpt of the code is if ( _this->Science_Age_Status & 1 && Current_Turn <= _this->Science_Age_End ) / _Total_Bulbs = (_Total_Bulbs * Science_Age_Ratio);, and he locates the bug in the order of operations: the research total is updated before the multiplier is applied (#12, as summarised by the fetch tool). His proposed fix is _this->Research_Bulbs += Current_Turn_Research_Bulbs * (Science_Age_Ratio - 1). So the intended rule is +25% of each turn's beakers into research, for 20 turns (the end test is <=, so it covers the turn it starts plus 20). That intended rule, not the bug, is what the engine should follow. The CFC FAQ (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/frequently-asked-questions-civ3-play-the-world-conquests.170282/) says it "is bugged, and it doesn't work."
  • Rule switch. README-C3C: "Implemented Allow Scientific Leaders Rule". EdSupp: "Allow Scientific Leaders Rule has been added and is active as a Default Rule. With this disabled only militaristic leaders are allowed."

4.9 Great Wonders (C3C 1.22)

Cost = BIQ cost x 10 (shields, before any production modifiers). Culture = BIQ culture per turn; Pedia GCON_Culture: "The amount of culture each Wonder or improvement contributes doubles after 1000 years." Traits = the BIQ strengths that can trigger a Golden Age. TA = can become a Tourist Attraction (BIQ flag, matching the Pedia GCON_Tourist_Attraction list). Effect quotes are from the Pedia BLDG_* entries.

Wonder Cost Prereq tech Obsolete Culture Traits TA Effect (Pedia) Notes (BIQ)
The Pyramids 400 Masonry — 4 Rel/Ind/Agr yes "Puts a Granary in every city on the same continent."
The Colossus 200 Bronze Working Flight 3 Com/Exp/Sea yes "one extra Commerce in every square that already produces at least one." coastal only
The Great Lighthouse 300 Map Making Magnetism 2 Com/Exp/Sea yes "Galleys and other early naval units can move safely in and out of sea squares, and all naval unit movement rates are increased by one." coastal only
The Oracle 300 Mysticism Theology 4 Rel yes "Doubles the effect of all Temples in the Empire"
The Great Library 400 Literature Education 6 Sci yes "any Civilization Advance already discovered by two other known civilizations for free."
The Hanging Gardens 300 Monarchy Steam Power 4 Ind/Agr yes "three unhappy citizens content in its city and one unhappy citizen content in all other friendly cities."
The Great Wall 300 Construction Metallurgy 2 Mil/Ind yes "Doubles unit combat values versus Barbarians. Places a Wall in each city of the continent"
The Temple of Artemis 500 Polytheism Education 4 Rel yes "Puts a Temple in every city on the same continent." new in C3C
The Statue of Zeus 200 Mathematics Metallurgy 4 Mil/Rel yes "produces an Ancient Cavalry every 5 turns." "A city must have access to Ivory" new in C3C; needs Ivory
The Mausoleum of Mausollos 200 Philosophy — 2 Sci/Sea yes "makes three unhappy citizens content in its city." new in C3C
Sun Tzu's Art of War 600 Feudalism — 2 Mil no "Puts a barracks in every city on the same continent."
Knights Templar 300 Chivalry Steam Power 2 Mil/Rel no "produces a Crusader every 5 turns." new in C3C
Sistine Chapel 600 Theology — 6 Rel yes "Doubles the effects of all Cathedrals in your cities."
Leonardo's Workshop 600 Invention — 2 Mil yes "Obsolete military units can be upgraded at half price."
Copernicus' Observatory 400 Astronomy — 4 Exp yes "Doubles scientific research in the city where it is built."
Magellan's Voyage 400 Navigation — 3 Com/Exp/Sea no "The movement rate of all naval units is increased by one." coastal only
JS Bach's Cathedral 600 Music Theory — 6 Rel yes "Decreases the number of unhappy citizens on the continent by two per city." continental
Shakespeare's Theater 450 Free Artistry — 8 (none) yes "Eight unhappy citizens in the city are made content." also lets the city grow past size 12 (BIQ AllowsCitySize3)
Newton's University 400 Theory of Gravity — 6 Sci yes "Doubles scientific research in the city where it is built."
Smith's Trading Company 600 Economics — 3 Com/Sea no "Pays maintenance costs for all trade-based city improvements (Harbors, Marketplaces, Banks, Airports, Commerical Dock and Stock Exchanges)."
Universal Suffrage 800 Industrialization — 4 Agr no "Reduces war weariness in all cities."
Theory of Evolution 600 Scientific Method — 3 Sci no "Grants two Civilization Advances as soon as completed."
Hoover Dam 800 Electronics — 2 Ind/Agr yes "Brings the effects of a hydro plant to all cities on the same continent." "a city must have a river within its radius."
The United Nations 1000 Fission — 4 Com yes "Initiates the possibility of a Diplomatic Victory."
The Manhattan Project 800 Fission — 2 Mil/Ind no "Allows the construction of nuclear weapons by {all} civilizations." needs Uranium
SETI program 1000 Computers — 3 Exp no "Doubles scientific research in the city where it is built."
The Internet 1000 Miniaturization — 4 all 8 no "Puts a Research Lab in every city on the same continent." new in C3C
Cure for Cancer 1000 Genetics — 3 Sci/Agr no "Makes one unhappy citizen content in every city."
Longevity 1000 Genetics — 3 Sci no "All cities get a population increase of two instead of one when the Food Storage box is full."

Cross-checks:

  • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) agrees with the BIQ on the new wonders: Statue of Zeus "200 shields, Mathematics, requires Ivory, 4 cpt, Rel and Mil, produces Ancient Cavalry unit every 5 turns, expires with Metallurgy"; Mausoleum "200 shields, Philosophy, 2 cpt, Sci and Sea, 3 unhappy citizens made content, never expires"; Temple of Artemis "500 shields, Polytheism, 4 cpt, Rel"; Knights Templar "300 shields, Chivalry, 2 cpt, Rel and Mil ... expires with Steam Power". Thunderfall: "There are now 11 SWs and 29 GWs". The BIQ also has 29 GWs and 11 SWs.
  • C3C changes to existing wonders (Arathorn, same thread). Shakespeare's "also allows a city to grow beyond size 12 and now costs 450 shields". Colossus "traits from Expansionist, Commercial, Religious to Expansionist, Seafaring, Commercial". Universal Suffrage "trait from Militaristic to Agricultural". Pyramids and Cure for Cancer "add trait Agricultural". Leonardo "still halves the upgrade cost". SirPleb: "The Great Wall has the new feature of putting walls in every town on the same continent."
  • Vanilla/PTW wonder costs and culture (raw read of the decompressed civ3mod.bic 1.29f and civ3PTW/civ3X.bix 1.27f at the C3C BLDG offsets; see section 3's source note). Culture and content-face values are the same as C3C for every shared wonder and small wonder. Costs are the same except two that C3C raised, which matches Arathorn's list above: the Great Wall 200 (C3C 300) and Shakespeare's Theatre 400 (C3C 450). PTW adds the Internet (1000, culture 4), as in C3C.
  • Vanilla wonder patches (README-V 1.16f). "United Nations Wonder: This Great Wonder is now enabled by Fission." "Art of War: The effects of this Great Wonder are now restricted to the continent it is built on." "Adam Smith's Trading Company: This wonder now pays the maintenance costs for all trade-based city improvements ... instead of all improvements with a maintenance cost of one gold."
  • Tourist attraction income (Pedia GCON_Tourist_Attraction). A wonder qualifies after it has "stood the test of time" and then earns gold: 1000–1500 years +2, 1501–1750 +4, 1751–1875 +6, 1876–2000 +8, 2001–2250 +10, 2251–2500 +12, 2501+ +14. Arathorn: "It starts at 2 gpt after 1000 years and can grow as high as 14 gpt." The editor help gives the rule in turns instead: EDHELP "Miscellaneous Effect Flags (Wonder Properties)" — "A Great Wonder can become a Tourist Attraction once it has "stood the test of time" for over 100 turns." / "An attraction that has been built for over 100 turns but less than 201 turns produces two gold for its owning city; one that's been around for over 200 turns but less than 301 produces four gold, etc." Resolved: years. The C3X mod's config, describing the 1.22 code (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini, tourism_time_scale_percent): "if set to 200, it would take 2000 years to begin generating 2 GPT tourism, instead of the standard 1000 years, and 3000 years for 4 GPT, instead of the standard 1500. This setting is effectively a scaling factor on the tourism time thresholds." The editor help's turn wording is loose.
  • Research-doubling wonders apply to the base science (player claim). Pfeffersack, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-guide-to-great-wonders-and-how-to-use-them.97063/ (C3C, 2004), on putting Copernicus, Newton and SETI together: "There is no special benefit in doing so," because "the boni are calculated from the base science income." UNVERIFIED; how the multipliers combine belongs to section 2.
  • Hoover Dam makes no pollution (player correction). Same thread, CIVPhilzilla: "The Wonder and the dams created by it don't produce any pollution." The guide's author accepted it.

4.10 Small Wonders (C3C 1.22)

Small Wonder Cost Prereq tech Other requirement Culture Effect (Pedia)
Heroic Epic 200 — "The civilization must have had a victorious army." 4 "Increases the chances of the appearance of leaders from victorious combat."
Iron Works 300 — (BIQ none) "must have Iron and Coal in its city radius" 2 "Production increased by 100% in the city where it's built." (BIQ also has pollution 4)
Forbidden Palace 200 — "at least ten cities (on a standard size map)" 2 "Gives similar benefits as the Palace but still suffers some corruption." It also raises the optimal city count.
Military Academy 400 Military Tradition "must have had a victorious army." 1 "can build armies without the benefit of a leader. Armies from this civilization are more effective in combat."
The Pentagon 400 — "at least three armies in the field." 1 "increases the unit capacity of all the civilization's armies from three to four."
Wall Street 300 — "at least five Stock Exchanges" 2 "5% interest every turn with a maximum cap of 50 gold per turn."
Apollo Program 500 Space Flight Aluminum (BIQ) 2 "enables the civilization to begin construction of the Alpha Centauri Spaceship."
Strategic Missile Defense 500 Integrated Defense "at least five SAM Batteries" 1 "Offers a 75% chance of intercepting enemy ICBMs."
Intelligence Agency 400 Espionage — 1 "allows the player to undertake Espionage missions."
Battlefield Medicine 500 — "at least five hospitals" 1 "Allows military units to heal in enemy territory."
Secret Police HQ 200 Espionage Communism only (BIQ required government) 0 Second Forbidden-Palace-style anti-corruption centre; "Changing from Communism will cause the Secret Police HQ bonuses to stop."
  • Small wonders cannot trigger Golden Ages: https://civfanatics.com/?p=151 — "Note that small wonders cannot trigger golden ages".
  • Iron Works tech gate: Iron Working and Steam Power. The BIQ gives Iron Works no required tech, but it needs Iron and Coal (BLDG resources 1 and 3). The resources themselves are gated (BIQ GOOD prerequisites): Iron needs Iron Working, Coal needs Steam Power. MANUAL p.125–126: "Iron Working: The strategic resource Iron appears on the World Map." and "Steam Power: Workers can upgrade roads to railroads. The strategic resource Coal appears on the World Map." The goods must be in the city's own radius. Pedia: "must have Iron and Coal in its city radius"; EDHELP "Required Goods Must Be Within City Radius (Small Wonder Flag)": "goods connected via the trade network do not qualify". The AI values it as part of Steam Power: Doc Tsiolkovski's "Steam: 16 (Coal) + 1 (RR) + 6 (Iron Works) = 23" (4.7).
  • Forbidden Palace city count (editor rule). EDHELP "Miscellaneous Effect Flags", Reduces Corruption: "The number of cities a player must have to enable construction of any wonder with this flag is equal to the Optimal Number of Cities divided by 2." See section 2 for OCN values.
  • Small Wonders are destroyed on capture. MANUAL p.130 — "Small Wonders in a city are always destroyed when the city is captured."
  • Secret Police HQ fix. README-C3C 1.20: "Secret Police HQ no longer gives corruption OR OCN bonus when player leaves Communism."

4.11 Wonder rules

  • One per world, never rebuilt. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Great Wonders can only be built once, by one civilization, and only they get the benefit of that wonder." "However, Small Wonders can be built by each civilization". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers.117535/page-32 (Turner) — "once a great wonder is gone, no one can rebuild it." Same thread: small wonders can be rebuilt (paraphrase in the fetch result).
  • Only one city per civ may build a given wonder at a time. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "First off, you can only build a wonder in one city."
  • Wonders cannot be hurried with population, gold or forest chops; a city with rushed shields cannot switch to a wonder. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "you can’t rush a wonder by sacrificing population (Despotism, Communism, Fascism)," / "by rushing it with gold (other governments), or by forest chops." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/partial-rush-build-of-wonders.8145 (Ferd) — "Once you rush something, the wonders are greyed out for that city and you can't switch to them." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/disbanding-question.18365 (Bamspeedy) — with forest or disband shields in the box, "that particular city won't be able to switch to a wonder." Pedia GCON_Hurry_Production only mentions "improvements or units".
  • Switching shields between wonders is free. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/building-wonders.8756 (Ruffin) — "switch all the production over to the new wonder (no penalty in Civ3)". This is the standard "prebuild" (Palace or another wonder as placeholder): https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers.117535/page-32. Apolyton tip on the timing limit: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 (Travathian) — "If it gets to one turn left you can switch to other units/improvements, but NOT wonders."
  • Switching between categories (unit ↔ improvement ↔ wonder): no penalty (Firaxis manual). MANUAL p.61 (new-features list) — "No penalty for changing projects: The penalty for changing production in mid-project is gone--except for any shields lost as overrun." MANUAL p.141 ("Note for Experienced Players") — "There is no penalty for switching production in mid-stream, unless the new project costs fewer shields than are already accumulated, in which case you forfeit the excess shields as overrun." Players agree: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/57954-solution-to-avoid-micromanagement (Franses) — "Shields are carried over in case you change production to something that costs more (or less) than the shields produced." No later readme brings a penalty back. The 50% "categories" rule found online (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/help-build-a-wonder.174050/) is from a Civ2 forum thread, so it is excluded. The limits on switching to a wonder after rushing or chopping are the ones above.
  • A rival completes the wonder you are building: switch, and the excess over the new item is lost (Firaxis manual). MANUAL p.130 — "If you are building a Great Wonder in one of your cities and the same Wonder is completed elsewhere before you finish, you must convert your production to something else. Any excess shields are lost, so be careful what you choose." This matches the players: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/wonder-building.27531 (Archullus) — "Switch to a less favourable wonder; or Get abortive production cost."; https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/building-wonders.8756 — after switching to a Bank the poster was "wasting about 400 shields". So the shields stay in the box, a switch to another wonder keeps them all, and a switch to something cheaper loses the excess when it completes. Vanilla also added notices: README-V ""beginning wonder" pop-ups now only appear if you have an embassy or spy with the corresponding civ." The "reimbursed in cash" claim (forums.civfanatics.com/threads/list-of-bugs-ive-experienced.139564) is from a Civ4/BtS thread and is excluded.
  • Obsolete wonders: the effect stops, the culture continues. MANUAL p.130 — "The achievement of later advances can negate the benefits of older Wonders. The cultural benefits of a Wonder continue to accrue unless the Wonder is destroyed." and the glory and culture points "continue to accrue even if new advances make the Wonder's specific benefit obsolete." Players: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/obsolete.100124/ (Red Ant) — "They will still generate culture points for you, though."; crazyluke2001 — "When it becomes obsolete it no longer has an effect." Obsolescence is per owner: gunkulator (same thread) — "In Civ3, the wonder is obsolete only if the owner has the tech." Contrast Civ2: "In Civ2, the wonder would be become obsolete if any civ discovered the appropriate tech." EDHELP "Rendered Obsolete By (Wonder Properties)" — "Determines which improvement (technology) renders the selected wonder obsolete (if any)." README-V: "Players now receive a message when a wonder is made obsolete." Whether an obsolete wonder still counts toward a Golden Age trigger is UNVERIFIED (Golden Age section).
  • Captured and razed wonders. MANUAL p.130 — "If a Great Wonder is captured (along with the city it's in), its benefits go to the new owner." / "Great Wonders are not destroyed when an enemy captures the city in which they exist. However, if a city possessing one is razed, that Wonder is lost forever and cannot be rebuilt." The captor gets the effect but not the culture (players): https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/captured-great-wonders-where-are-the-culture-points.72979/ — wilbill, "When you capture a wonder you get its effects (if they're still active) but not the culture."; SuperBeaverInc., "Captured Wonders never produce culture." The culture rules belong to section 3.
  • Hurrying (Vanilla manual). MANUAL p.130 — "If you want to complete construction of a Wonder faster than the city that is building it can generate shields, you have only one option: use a leader. There is no other method of hurrying a Wonder project." C3C limits this further; see the next item.
  • Great Leader hurry.
    • Vanilla/PTW: military leaders could rush Great Wonders. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "In Conquests the rushing ability is limited to Small Wonders, but in Vanilla or Play the World it can rush a Great Wonder."
    • C3C, military leader: Pedia GCON_Leaders — "A Military Leader can be used to either create an army or to hurry production of an Improvement or Small Wonder in a city." Arathorn — "Military Great Leaders (MGLs) can no longer rush Great Wonders."
    • C3C, scientific leader: Arathorn — "An SGL can rush a wonder (either Great or Small)."
  • Golden Age from wonders. Pedia GCON_Golden_Age — "If a civilization builds a Great Wonder (or Wonders) that corresponds to [both] its unique strengths, its Golden Age can be triggered." BIQ RULE Golden Age duration = 20. Details belong in the Golden Age section.
  • Free buildings from wonders: the code looks only at Great Wonders (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16058309, page-6 #105): the stock code considers only Great Wonders, in two places: "it loops over all improvements and picks out the great wonders" and "it calls a function that returns in which city some great wonder is built". So a Small Wonder given a "free building in every city" effect in the editor would do nothing. No stock Small Wonder has such an effect, so the stock rules are unaffected.
  • Coastal, river and resource requirements (BIQ). Colossus, Great Lighthouse and Magellan's need a coastal city (CoastalInstallation). Hoover Dam needs a river (MustBeNearRiver). Statue of Zeus needs Ivory, Manhattan Uranium, Apollo Aluminum, and Iron Works Iron + Coal.
  • Wonder victory (C3C option, only if enabled). README-C3C — "The "Wonder Victory" is actually a Time Limit type of victory condition; i.e., when the Wonder Victory is in effect, the game ends immediately upon completion of the last Great Wonder." Victory-point games are out of scope.

Disputed

  1. Difficulty and research cost. AI side resolved: since 1.29f the AI pays the same cost at every level and gets a compensating bonus or penalty instead. alexman (Firaxis), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/66516-1.htm — "constant for all levels for the AI"; 45559 page 2 #31 — "The AI gets a production bonus (or penalty) which exactly cancels the increase (or decrease) in cost". The FAQ's Chieftain line ("is 2 for the AI – meaning everything takes twice as long to do, from researching a tech, to building a worker") describes that net effect, or the pre-1.29f rule ("twice as expensive for the AI", alexman). Human side still disputed below Regent: mydisease's 1.29f thread caps CF at 10 ("CF has a maximum value of 10"), so a human's cost would never fall below Regent. alexman says "since 1.29f, Chieftain technologies cost half as much for the player", which is 10/20 with no cap. Owner ruling for the engine: cap at 10 for humans. Above Regent the sources agree (x10/CF; confirmed in C3C by AutomatedTeller's Emperor Pottery = 60 and tjs282's "1.25x").
  2. Minimum research time. 4 turns (BIQ RULE; EDHELP "Default is 4"; FAQ "For all three games, the minimum research length is 4 turns"; David Weldon; korn469's tech-rate-1 test) vs Special_K, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 — "You can research an advance in 3 turns, it just takes a ton of science." The 3-turn cases reported in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33948-2.htm involve output rising, or the cost falling, part-way through (4.2). Best reading: the floor is set when research starts, and later changes can beat it by a turn. The maximum is 32, then 40, in Vanilla (README-V 1.16f) and 50 in C3C (BIQ/FAQ).
  3. Overflow. "all extra beakers are lost" (Rik Meleet; FAQ "no beaker carryover"; Jim's and T-hawk's 2002 tests) vs "excess from the completing city is lost, later cities' beakers go to the next tech" (TimTheEnchanter / ElephantU, city order = reverse founding order). The second is more specific and consistent with the first as a simplification.
  4. Scientific leader chance. Mostly resolved for 3% / 5%: Arathorn; Turner (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-leader-abilities.88505/latest); socralynnek, "hardcoded to be 3% for non-scientific civs and 5% for scientific civs"; AutomatedTeller (211436); the 2008 "2 x Sci Leaders" arithmetic (0.03 x 0.03, 0.05 x 0.05). The outliers are Ginger_Ale, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/leaders.102561 — 2% for non-scientific civs — and Sullla (a C3C beta tester), https://sullla.com/Civ3/conquestsed.html — "there is a 2-5% chance that a leader forms". No Firaxis number; the Pedia says only "increased slightly".
  5. Can a C3C military leader rush anything? Pedia, FAQ and Arathorn: yes, improvements and Small Wonders. EdSupp: "NOTE: Military leaders can not rush." HellboundHeathcliff, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-leader-abilities.88505/latest — "Conquests MGLs can only rush improvements and small wonders or create an army." EdSupp is the outlier. The C3X config (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini, code reading) adds that human MGLs can't hurry Great Wonders in stock (allow_military_leaders_to_hurry_wonders is a mod option, default off), and that the AI's own leader routine has "a bug that allowed the AI to rush any kind of city production, including units and (with an MGL) wonders". Third round, code: the stock Unit_can_hurry_production decides by the leader's kind (LK_Military = 1, LK_Scientific = 2). C3X lets an MGL hurry wonders by relabelling it scientific for the call (patch_Unit_can_hurry_production). C3X-INI adds that scenario-placed leaders without a kind "are allowed to hurry production of great wonders and spaceship parts", unlike "normal MGLs". So a stock MGL can't hurry spaceship parts either, while an SGL can hurry Great Wonders (as PEDIA says).
  6. Theory of Evolution techs. Current research plus next pick (Yndy, Catt 1.21f; RebelX210, "will finish up the tech you are currently researching and give you one more"; Bella Hella, "you can choose the techs you want after getting ToE") vs random (philippe, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-great-library-work.26689 — "thoery of evolution picks random techs"; EDHELP "Two free, randomly selected advances are granted"). Turner describes three techs in one well-timed turn (current research, then two picks). The player reports are concrete and agree with each other. The editor wording may describe the flag loosely.
  7. Wonder data from the CivFanatics wonder guide (https://civfanatics.com/?p=151, as extracted by WebFetch; not re-verified line by line) differs from the BIQ on Copernicus' Observatory (Invention vs BIQ Astronomy). It also lists Universal Suffrage under "Industrialisation" and spells several names differently. The BIQ is authoritative for C3C 1.22.
  8. ~~Shields after losing a wonder race~~ — resolved by the manual (MANUAL p.130, "you must convert your production to something else. Any excess shields are lost"); see 4.11.
  9. Beakers on a research switch, or when you receive the tech you are researching (Vanilla-era sources only).
    • Voluntary switch: all lost (MANUAL p.123, "you lose all of their work on the advance you order them to abandon"; Sir Yelof, Tassadar, regoarrarr) vs half kept (T-hawk, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/science-slider-when-1-turn-from-tech-change-it-or-not.24350/ — "if you do it voluntarily, half of your beaker count applies to the new tech", and "if you switch again your accumulated research is halved again").
    • The researched tech arrives by trade, hut or the Great Library: beakers lost (David Weldon, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33948-1.htm — accumulated beakers "irrevocably lost" if you get the tech another way; MustPost, 33948-2, "I wasted all of the beakers") vs kept (eighty, 24350 page 2 — "if you get a tech from goody hut, trade, great library or theory of evol, you get to keep your beakers", with "(I hope, someone correct me if I am wrong)").
    • A small vanilla test on the voluntary switch: kring, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/63400-sciences-surplus-or-science-waste (30 May 2002): "I changed research in midstream, then changed right back in the same turn as a test. Suddenly, I needed the full amount of time again." That fits "all lost" (or a halving followed by the turn display), and Nym in the same thread: "all accumulated beakers are lost if you change the tech researched." Jaybe: "I don't think anyone from Firaxis has spoken on the subject."
    • No C3C test found. The cost formula's "- Research done so far" term shows that stored beakers count against the tech they were spent on.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • Whether the 1.29f research formula (CL1.75, rounding as in TechCalc) is unchanged in PTW 1.27f and C3C 1.22. The C3C demogame use (193586) and tjs282 (696592) suggest it is, with no C3C test of the CL1.75 term. DM for Demigod (CF 7) and Sid (CF 4) is extrapolated.
  • How Future Tech Cost (400) enters the cost formula, and whether successive future techs cost more.
  • What happens to beakers beyond the 4-turn minimum pace (lost vs banked). For the 50-turn cap, the sources point to forced completion after 50 funded turns (Bede; alexman's "waste" remark). Whether zero-beaker turns count is open.
  • C3C behaviour of beakers on a research switch, and on receiving the tech in progress (Disputed 9).
  • The AI research roll: how the values weight the random choice (DevelopmentAI.cs assumes linear; owner ruling is a weighted roll). The Flavor weight "5%" (Ozymandias) is unconfirmed. The Iron Working example (22) vs the "+1 per direct dependant" rule, given the BIQ's Construction prerequisites.
  • AI-to-AI trade rate: does it apply to every AI-AI deal (EDHELP) or only to deals the buyer cannot otherwise afford (Soren, secondhand)? Also the C3C "changed calculation on tech prices" formula (diplomacy section).
  • Scientific trait free tech: whether techs known to AIs are excluded (DaveMcW) beyond the uniform pick among available new-era techs (Mike Breitkreutz, PTW fix).
  • Theory of Evolution: random pick (EDHELP) vs current research plus next pick (players); see Disputed 6.
  • Whether an obsolete wonder still counts toward a Golden Age trigger (Golden Age section).
  • ~~Tourist attraction age: counted in years (Pedia) or turns (EDHELP).~~ Resolved: years (C3X config, 4.9).
  • Unread sources: see reading-log-04.md.

Back to the top

5. Governments

Scope: base game, Play the World (PTW) and Conquests (C3C), patched (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes are out of scope. Where PTW/vanilla differ from C3C, the difference is called out. C3C is the target ruleset.

Sources used

Key Source
PEDIA Civ3 Complete install, Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (the C3C 1.22 in-game Civilopedia). Entries are cited by key (GOVT_Despotism, GCON_Unit_Support ...). The government stat blocks are mirrored online at https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/
BIQ Conquests/conquests.biq (C3C 1.22 default rules), GOVT / RULE / DIFF / RACE sections, read with the repo's QueryCiv3 BIQ reader (field names from QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Govt.cs).
BIQ-PTW civ3PTW/civ3X.bix (PTW) and civ3mod.bic (vanilla) GOVT sections, raw-decoded with the same record layout. The two files have identical GOVT data.
EDHELP Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, the official C3C editor help (decompiled). Cited by page name.
README README.txt (vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f) and Conquests/readme.txt (C3C 1.20 to 1.22).
SCRIPT Conquests/Text/script.txt (in-game advisor and popup text).
CFC-GOV https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/ ("This article covers Civilization 3 Complete V1.22."). It is the Info Center copy of Theov's forum guide "Governments: Civilization 3 Complete V1.22" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/governments-civilization-3-complete-v1-22.276379/, 26 May 2008). The text is the same, including "2 to 8 turns" and the "4×2 + 3×4 = 20" example.
JS-TABLE "Civilization III: Conquests Government Breakdown" by Jesse Smith ("Firaxis Games, Producer, C3C"), posted by Tavis (Firaxis), 2 Nov 2003: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3c-government-breakdown.67419/
BUGLIST Bamspeedy, "Compiled list of bugs and non-bugs", 27 Nov 2003 (C3C 1.00 era): https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/compiled-list-of-bugs-and-non-bugs-not-a-dicussion-thread.70063/
BIQ-PRTO conquests.biq PRTO section, RequireSupport field (QueryCiv3 Prto.cs), dumped with QueryCiv3 for this pass.
CFC-WW https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/how-does-war-weariness-work/
CFC-CORR https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/everything-about-corruption-c3c-edition/
CFC-ESP https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/a-study-of-espionage-missions/
CFC-FAQ https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/frequently-asked-questions-civ3-play-the-world-conquests.170282/
C3X-THREAD https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (the C3X mod's forum thread, 2021-2026), cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable; his statements about the base game are code readings. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".

Note: the repo copy C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt is OpenCiv3's own text ("It is not Civ3's Civilopedia"), so it is not used as a source here.

C3C has eight governments: "There are eight forms of government that you can use to control your civilization." (PEDIA GCON_Governments). They are Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy, Republic, Feudalism, Communism, Fascism and Democracy. There is no Tribalism in stock C3C. Tribalism appears only in mods. Vanilla and PTW have six (no Feudalism, no Fascism): the BIQ-PTW GOVT section has count 6.

5.1 Master table (C3C 1.22, from BIQ, matching PEDIA)

BIQ enum decoding:

  • Corruption: 0 Minimal, 1 Nuisance, 2 Problematic, 3 Rampant, 4 Catastrophic, 5 Communal. This matches PEDIA's labels one for one.
  • Hurrying: 0 none, 1 forced labor, 2 paid labor.
  • War weariness: 0 None, 1 Low, 2 High.
  • Worker rate: 2 = 100%. EDHELP Worker Rate: "The "base" worker rate is 2. Each digit increases the rate by 50%. Thus, 1=50% work rate (bad), 2 = 100% (normal), and 3 = 150%".
Government Prereq tech Corruption/Waste Tile penalty Trade bonus Worker rate Hurry Draft/turn Military police Free units Town/City/Metro Gold per extra unit War weariness Assimilation %/turn Rate cap Spies / Diplomats Immune to Other flags
Anarchy (transition type) Catastrophic (4) yes no 1 (50%) none 0 0 0/0/0, FreeUnits = -1 1 None 1 10 Regular / Regular - TransitionType=1, RequiresMaintenance=0
Despotism none (DefaultType) Rampant (3) yes no 2 (100%) forced 2 2 4/4/4 1 None 1 10 Regular / Regular - DefaultType=1
Monarchy Monarchy Problematic (2) no no 2 (100%) paid 2 3 2/4/8 1 None 2 10 Regular / Regular -
Feudalism Feudalism Problematic (2) no no 2 (100%) forced 2 3 5/2/1 3 Low 3 10 Regular / Regular -
Republic The Republic Nuisance (1) no yes 2 (100%) paid 1 0 1/3/4 2 Low 2 10 Regular / Regular -
Communism Communism Communal (5) no no 2 (100%) forced 2 4 6/6/6 1 None 4 10 Veteran / Regular - allows Secret Police HQ
Fascism Fascism Nuisance (1) no no 4 (200%) forced 2 4 4/7/10 1 None 2 10 Veteran / Regular - Xenophobic=1, ForceResettle=1
Democracy Democracy Minimal (0) no yes 3 (150%) paid 1 0 0/0/0 1 High 4 10 Regular / Regular Initiate Propaganda (mission index 6)

(Spies/Diplomats: BIQ SpiesAre/DiplomatsAre 1 = Regular, 2 = Veteran. EDHELP Spy Experience: "Determines the experience level of spies (Conscript, Regular, Veteran, Elite) under the selected government.")

  • Firaxis corroboration. JS-TABLE lists the same values for the seven non-Anarchy governments: cost per unit, T/C/M support, worker rate, draft limit, MP limit, corruption, hurry and WW. For example, "Feudalism | 3 | 5/2/1 | 2 | 2 | 3 | Prob. | Force | Low | Req. Maint" and "Fascism | 1 | 4/7/10 | 4 | 2 | 4 | Nuis. | Force | None | Xeno/Resettle". Its legend says: "Req. Maint/Maint = Must pay city improvement maintenance".
  • Trade bonus. Tavis (Firaxis), same thread, page 1: "Democracy and Republic are the only ones that get the Trade (Commerce) Bonus in the Epic Game".
  • BIQ RequiresMaintenance is 1 for every government except Anarchy (0). TilePenalty is 1 for Anarchy and Despotism only. TradeBonus is 1 for Republic and Democracy only.

PTW / vanilla differences (BIQ-PTW)

  • Only 6 governments (no Feudalism, no Fascism).
  • Communism unit support 2/4/8 in PTW/vanilla. C3C has 6/6/6.
  • Republic unit support 0/0/0 at 1 gold in PTW/vanilla. C3C has 1/3/4 at 2 gold.
  • All other GOVT fields match C3C: corruption, worker rate, hurry, draft, MP, war weariness, assimilation, rate cap and immunity.
  • Corroboration for the vanilla Communism value: CFC-FAQ-era GameFAQs guide (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/misc/civilization-iii-faqstrategy-guide-v3-0/, v3.0, Jan 2002): "under the non-democratic forms of government (Monarchy, Communism), you can support 2 units for each town (size 6 or below), 4 units for each city (size 7-12), and 8 units for each metropolis (size 13+)".
  • C3C note on Republic: CFC-FAQ: "in Republic in Conquests, you get very little unit support - and each unit past the limit costs 2 gpt to maintain!"

5.2 Per-government detail

Anarchy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Catastrophic: no production, no taxes, no research
Tile penalty Yes (Despotism penalty)
Workers 50%
Hurry Impossible
Draft / MP 0 / 0
Unit support No upkeep (BIQ FreeUnits = -1, per-size 0/0/0)
Maintenance None (RequiresMaintenance = 0)
War weariness None
  • PEDIA GOVT_Anarchy: "During that period corruption and waste are so high that no production occurs and no taxes are collected. Scientific research comes to halt, as well. There is no improvement maintenance when a civilization is in Anarchy."
  • CFC-GOV: "No city production, no research, Despo Penalty (see Despotism). Specialists still work. No maintenance costs or unit upkeep needed." BIQ agrees on the penalty: TilePenalty=1 for Anarchy.
  • Hurry: SCRIPT #GOVERNMENT_CANNOT_HURRY: "We are in the midst of a revolt, sir. We cannot hurry production." PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production: "You have [no] production if your civilization is in Anarchy."
  • Draft: PEDIA GCON_Draft: "A civilization in Anarchy may not use the draft."
  • EDHELP Corruption Modifier (Difficulty Level): "The Corruption Modifier has no effect on players whose government experiences catastrophic corruption, cities in civil disorder, or capital cities."
  • Unit upkeep is zero. PEDIA GCON_Unit_Support lists Anarchy at "0 0 0" free units, but the forum figures put the per-unit cost at zero as well:
    • MAS (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/allowed-units.191722, 8 Nov 2006): "Anarchy in all versions: 0/0/0 | 0 gpt".
    • Lanzelot (thread 276379 page 2, Jan 2026): the unit cost is "0 for Anarchy, 2 for Republic, 3 for Feudalism, 1 for pretty much any other government".
    • This fits CFC-GOV's "No maintenance costs or unit upkeep needed" and settles the BIQ reading of FreeUnits = -1 as "no upkeep".
  • Specialists still produce. Grille (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anarchy-gamble.62465/, 26 Aug 2003, vanilla): "Like any other specialist, a scientist does not refuse to work during anarchy." Also: "During anarchy, some gpt income may be achieved by hiring some taxmen" (28 Aug 2003). Grey Fox (same thread, 27 Nov 2003, C3C): "I researched steam power through my 9 turn anarchy".
  • Forum claim only (Chamnix, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/surviving-anarchy.308243, 28 Jan 2009): "He is in anarchy - no MP, no luxury slider, no war weariness." No MP and no WW match the BIQ. The luxury slider claim is not checked; Anarchy's BIQ rate cap is 10.
  • C3C 1.00 bug (BUGLIST, "Moderate bugs"): rioting cities did not need food, which "makes it possible to do alot of research during anarchy by turning many citizens into scientists". It is not stated whether a later patch fixed this.

Despotism

Field Value
Corruption/waste Rampant (distance factor x3/2, see 5.6)
Tile penalty Yes
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor (population)
Draft / MP 2 / 2
Unit support 4/4/4, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Start government Yes (DefaultType)
  • Tile penalty, PEDIA GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two food, shields, or commerce in a despotic government instead produces one less."
  • EDHELP Flags, "Standard tile penalty": "Under the selected government, no tile may generate more than 2 food and shields." This wording is loose. The rule is -1 on any yield above 2, and it includes commerce:
    • PEDIA GOVT_Despotism: "produces one less".
    • CFC-GOV: "Any square which produces more than 2 food, commerce, or shields produces 1 less."
    • CFC FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/, covers 1.29f/1.27f/1.22): "all tiles that produce 3 or more food/shields/commerce will produce one less".
    • Louis XXIV (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-is-standard-tile-penalty.108487/, 28 Dec 2004, PTW): "A tile that produces 4 food would only produce 3." He adds that the help file "appears to be slightly inaccurate for the tile penalty".
  • EDHELP Flags, "Default type": "All civilizations start the game under the selected government."

Monarchy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Problematic
Workers 100%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 2 / 3
Unit support 2/4/8, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
  • PEDIA GOVT_Monarchy: "Corruption and waste are significant, but are ameliorated to an extent by loyalty to the King."

Feudalism (C3C only)

Field Value
Corruption/waste Problematic
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 3
Unit support 5/2/1, 3 gold per extra unit
War weariness Low
  • PEDIA GOVT_Feudalism: "your vassals are wary of continual warfare and war weariness may result. You do not suffer the production problems associated with despotism, however. Maintenance cost per unit per turn is 3 gold instead of 1."
  • Note the inverted support curve: towns support more than metropolises. CFC-GOV: "Good for civs with small towns (under pop 6). You might actually want small towns because of the unit support".
  • Feudalism pays building maintenance in the shipped game.
    • BIQ: RequiresMaintenance=1. JS-TABLE: "Req. Maint".
    • Tavis (Firaxis), thread 67419, 2 Nov 2003: "No maintenance with Feudalism had a very negative side effect - the AI wouldn't switch from it!!". So free maintenance was a pre-release design that was dropped.
    • player1 fanatic, same thread, page 2: "all governments need to maintain buildings (since ability is removed from Feudalism)".
    • Contrary reports: BUGLIST lists "German version has Fuedalism still having Maintanence free improvements" as an unconfirmed bug, from second-hand hearsay. In thread 276379, Mursi lives (Feb 2010) says he paid none in a 100k game, and Spoonwood and CKS reply that maintenance is still paid. Treat free maintenance as not a stock rule.
  • Feudalism war weariness is the Republic level. BIQ gives both WW level 1 ("Low"); the field is a three-value enum (None/Low/High). Supporting statements:
    • Arathorn, C3C changes list (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/, 10 Nov 2004): "low war weariness (like Republic)".
    • gunkulator (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/monarchy-or-feudalism.114078/, 14 Mar 2005): "WW for Feudalism and Republic are the same." This is from play experience, not a stated test.
  • "Feudalism pikemen support" is not in stock C3C. PEDIA, BIQ GOVT and EDHELP contain no unit-type-specific support rule. UNVERIFIED as a stock rule; probably a mod.

Republic

Field Value
Corruption/waste Nuisance
Trade bonus Yes: +1 commerce on tiles already producing at least 1
Workers 100%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 1 / 0 (no martial law)
Unit support 1/3/4, 2 gold per extra unit (C3C)
War weariness Low
  • PEDIA GOVT_Republic: "your government is affected by war weariness, which can cause significant civil disorder problems in times of war, especially if you are the aggressor. Maintenance cost per unit per turn is 2 gold instead of just 1."
  • CFC-GOV: "Adds 1 commerce to any tile already producing at least 1 = the commerce bonus." CFC FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/): "for every tile that produces one commerce, you'll get an extra". EDHELP's "each tile generates an additional +1 to commerce" omits the >= 1 condition. No source says zero-commerce tiles get the bonus.
  • Support applies to all units, not only military ones (see 5.3). SebT27 (thread 276379 page 2, Jan 2026) says the guide's "Military over the cap cost 2 gpt" distinction "must be wrong (or outdated...)". Theov, the guide's author, agrees: "true, it's all units - except the ones that don't require upkeep".

Communism

Field Value
Corruption/waste Communal: the same in every city, independent of distance (see 5.6)
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 4
Unit support 6/6/6 (C3C), 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Espionage Spies are Veteran
Special Can build the Secret Police HQ (a second "palace" for corruption)
  • PEDIA GOVT_Communism: "One positive aspect of Communism is its effect upon corruption and waste: all cities suffer the same, limited effects."
  • PEDIA BLDG_Secret_Police_HQ: "Only civilizations under Communist government can build the Secret Police HQ. Changing from Communism will cause the Secret Police HQ bonuses to stop."
  • README (C3C 1.20): "Secret Police HQ no longer gives corruption OR OCN bonus when player leaves Communism."
  • README (vanilla 1.17f): "Corruption calculations for Communism are now flat for the number-of-cities factor."
  • C3C 1.00 had a bug where the SPHQ kept working after leaving Communism. BUGLIST, Major bug: "Secret Police HQ, still works even when you are no longer a commie." The 1.20 README line above fixed it.
  • Corruption: alexman (Firaxis software engineer), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/55285-1.htm, 10 Jul 2002, vanilla: "Communism does not incur corruption due to distance, but it does incur corruption due to number of cities."
  • CFC-GOV: "you can whip foreign people in newly conquered cities to prevent revolts". UNVERIFIED as a distinct rule. The only statement found on which citizen a whip removes says the opposite, with the author unsure. Ision (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-benefits-of-whipping-citizens.76152/, 13 Mar 2004): "natural born citizens will always die first", "I am not completely 100% sure on this one."

Fascism (C3C only)

Field Value
Corruption/waste Nuisance
Workers 200%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 4
Unit support 4/7/10, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Espionage Spies are Veteran
Flags Xenophobic, Forced Resettlement
  • PEDIA GOVT_Fascism: "Your cities lose population points upon a change to fascist government, Newly conquered cities also lose population, and do not generate culture until a popular majority has been assimilated."
  • EDHELP Flags, Forced Resettlement: "When switching to the selected government, each town in the nation loses 1 population, each city loses 2 population, and each metropolis loses 3 population."
  • Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16848272, page-147 #2928) puts it more simply: "I've always found it silly how you lose a pop point in every city when switching to fascism". That is looser than EDHELP's 1/2/3 by size level; it is a passing remark, not a description of the code. EDHELP is the Firaxis text and the more specific; keep 1/2/3 unless a test says otherwise.
  • EDHELP Flags, Xenophobic: "no culture is gained in cities where less than half of the population of the city is from the same race as the controlling civilization."
  • CFC-GOV: "Higher chance of espionage mission success". Explained by SpiesAre=2 (Veteran), the same as Communism.
  • "Fascism can't be bribed / is immune to propaganda" is not a stock rule.
    • Fascism's ImmuneTo is -1 (none).
    • The per-government "Cannot Bribe" setting is the CanBribe field of the GOVT-vs-GOVT matrix. In conquests.biq it holds the uninitialized value 0xCDCDCDCD for every pair, so it does not distinguish Fascism.
    • The Fascism and Feudalism matrix entries were simply never filled in. BUGLIST, Moderate bug: "The resistance + propoganda modifiers for any and all governments vs. Fascism and Fuedalism have not been set". It adds that the modifier against Fascism "is always 0". This matches the zero Fasc/Feud columns in 5.11.
    • No source gives Fascism special protection. It does have the Veteran spy bonus.
  • Tavis (Firaxis), thread 67419 page 2, 2 Nov 2003: "To clarify - Fascism DOES require maintenance".
  • Roundman, thread 67419 (2 Nov 2003), clears up a rumour: "Fascism was forced labor for rush jobs, not for all production."

Democracy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Minimal (distance factor x3/4)
Trade bonus Yes
Workers 150%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 1 / 0
Unit support 0/0/0: every unit costs 1 gold
War weariness High. Can cause a revolt into Anarchy
Propaganda Immune
  • PEDIA GOVT_Democracy: "war weariness is a significant problem and war must be entered into only after much consideration."
  • Propaganda immunity: BIQ ImmuneTo=6 (Initiate Propaganda). SCRIPT #ESPIONAGE_MISSION_INITIATE_PROPAGANDA_IMMUNE: "The $CIVADJ1 people are immune to propaganda." CFC-ESP: "Democracy is immune against propaganda."
  • CFC-GOV, Anarchy: "It also happens when WW reaches 100% when under Democracy." CFC-WW, Democracy "Level 3: Revolt".
  • LordKestrel (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-war-weariness-work.61628/page-3, 18 Jun 2004), on the WW revolt: "Your civ descends into Anarchy, just like if you had clicked the Revolt button".
  • Worker rate 150% affects only Worker-type terraforming, not city shields. Keyser Soze 0 (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/32558-1.htm, 5 Nov 2001): "the worker efficiency is referring to tile improvement workers - they work faster."

5.3 City size categories and unit support

  • Size thresholds, BIQ RULE: MaximumLevel1CitySize=6, MaximumLevel2CitySize=12, level names Town, City, Metropolis.

    • EDHELP Level One City Max Size: "Default is 6." EDHELP Level Two City Maximum Size: "Default is 12."
    • CFC-GOV: "Town: 1-6 population / City: 7-12 population (requires fresh water or an aquaduct) / Metro: 12+ population (requires a hospital ...)". (Metro is really 13+, per the RULE maximum of 12 for level 2.)

  • Support rule, PEDIA GCON_Unit_Support: "Generally, each military unit must be supported with one gold per turn from its treasury. However, each form of government defers these costs for a certain number of units, depending upon how many towns, cities, or metros the civilization controls."

  • Formula, CFC-GOV: "4 towns and 3 cities under Monarchy have 4×2 + 3×4 = 20 unit support. That means that every unit over the cap of 20 cost you 1gpt." For each extra unit, the cost is UnitCost gold: Republic 2, Feudalism 3, all others 1.

  • EDHELP Free Units Per Town: "how many "free" units the selected government is allowed per level one city. Note that this number is above and beyond any free units allowed by default." The "by default" link points to the government FreeUnits field (0 for all real governments, -1 for Anarchy).

  • AI-only difficulty bonuses, BIQ DIFF (AdditionalFreeSupport / UnitSupportBonusForEachSettlement):

    Level Bonus
    Chieftain to Regent 0/0
    Monarch 4/1
    Emperor 8/2
    Demigod 12/3
    Deity 16/4
    Sid 24/8

    EDHELP Additional Free Support: "how many extra units the AI can support, in addition to any existing governmental bonuses ... cumulative with all other support bonuses."

  • Which units count: every unit whose type has RequireSupport set. In stock C3C that is every unit except the leader "King" units, and except foreign (captured) units.

    • BIQ-PRTO: RequireSupport=1 for 110 of the 141 C3C unit records, including Settler, Worker, Scout, Explorer, Leader, Army and the Princess. The other 31 records are the civ-specific King units (Lincoln ... Theodora, flag King), and all have RequireSupport=0. King units only appear in the Regicide-style special game modes, which are out of scope.
    • EDHELP Requires Support (Unit Statistics): "If checked, players will have to pay a support cost (gold) for the unit."
    • Workers and settlers count against the free slots:
      • Speedo (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-unit-support-work.76197, 22 Jan 2004): "remember workers and settlers count, though".
      • The vanilla-era guide says the same: "This count includes Workers, and also includes any Settlers that haven't built or joined cities yet."
      • justanick (thread 276379 page 2, Jan 2026): "All normal units including workers and settler must pay 1 gtp in a democracy because there is no free unit support."
    • Foreign units are free:
      • justanick, same post: "Foreign unit are free of unit support."
      • Lanzelot, same page: "Unit support has to be paid for every unit except for foreign units."
      • Captured workers keep their nationality (PEDIA GCON_Nationality: "Captured workers and settlers retain their original nationality").
    • PEDIA's "each military unit" (GCON_Unit_Support) is therefore loose wording.
    • Code (third round, C3X re-creation of the stock count, Flintlock, C3C 1.22). C3X's patch_Leader_count_maintenance_free_units (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c) counts a unit as free if ((unit->Body.RaceID != this->RaceID) || (type->requires_support == 0)): a unit of another nationality, or a type without RequireSupport. That is the foreign-units rule above. (The mod adds one case of its own, camp-spawned barbarian units.) Its upkeep total, used in place of the stock Leader_sum_unit_maintenance only when a mod option is on, is (Unit_Count - government free units - AI free units - free units) × cost per unit, floored at 0. The AI free units are Bonus_For_Each_City × Cities_Count + Additional_Free_Support from the game's difficulty level, so the per-settlement bonus counts every city whatever its size. A player with no cities pays nothing: "Players with no cities don't pay unit maintenance, per the original game rules". The stock routine pays building and unit upkeep in random order: the building sum "is called in two places as part of the randomization of whether improv or unit maintenance gets paid first". Whether units inside an Army count separately depends on what Unit_Count holds, which the code doesn't show.
    • Flintlock gives the stock formula in words (C3X-THREAD post-16398188, page-64 #1264): "The formula for unit maintenance is max(0,(N-F-K)*C), where N is the total number of units that player has, F is the no. of free units from gov't and difficulty, K is the no. of king-flagged and captured units, and C is the maintenance cost per unit from gov't type." This matches the C3X re-creation above. tjs282 (post-16396708, page-63 #1259): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "(excess) unit-maintenance costs are tied to the current government-type, not the unit-type".
    • Civ3 has no shield upkeep. Flintlock (post-16906674, page-167 #3321), giving his view of the design history (not a code reading) on why Civ2's shield upkeep was dropped: "One of the reasons they dropped that for Civ 3 was that the AI could never handle it well."
    • Not checked: whether units inside an Army are charged separately from the Army unit.

  • PTW/vanilla PEDIA (civ3PTW/Text/Civilopedia.txt, Text/Civilopedia.txt, GCON_Unit_Support) lists "Communism 2 4 8" and "Republic 0 0 0", which matches BIQ-PTW. MAS (thread 191722, 8 Nov 2006): "Republic in vanilla/Civ3: 0/0/0 | 1 gpt" and "Republic in Conquest/C3C: 1/3/4 | 2 gpt".

5.4 Martial law (military police)

  • EDHELP Military Police Limit: "how many military units (units with attack rating greater than zero) can be garrisoned in a city to reduce unhappiness."
  • CFC-GOV: "Military Police Limit: Max number of units in a city that makes 1 unhappy citizen content."
  • Citizens quelled per unit come from the difficulty level: BIQ DIFF MilitaryLaw=1 on all levels. EDHELP Number of Citizens Quelled by Military: "how many rioting-citizens are quelled with the presence of military units."
  • Limits: Despotism 2, Monarchy 3, Feudalism 3, Communism 4, Fascism 4. Republic, Democracy and Anarchy: 0.

5.5 Hurrying (rushing) and the draft

  • Method by government, PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production:
    • "Under Despotism, Communism, Feudalism, or Fascism you hurry production in your cities by forced labor. Disillusioned by your harsh rule, citizens will leave your city".
    • "Under Monarchy, Republic, and Democracy you can pay overtime ... This costs gold from your treasury; the amount depends upon how much work remains to be done."
  • Forced labor rate. BIQ RULE CitizenValueInShields=20. EDHELP: "how many shields are produced by "sacrificing" a point of population ... Default is 20 shields."
    • README 1.21f: "The first citizen is now worth 20 shields instead of 40 shields, just like all the other citizens."
    • README 1.17f: "Eliminated 'Despot Pop Rushing'." (The exact meaning is not stated.)
  • Pop-rush unhappiness lasts 20 turns. BIQ RULE TurnPenaltyForEachHurrySacrifice=20. README 1.21f: "Draft anger and hurrying unhappiness reduced to 20 turns." It was 40 in 1.17f: "Unhappiness from population rushing production increased to 40 turns."
  • Pop-rush limits:
    • SCRIPT #HURRY_NOT_ENOUGH_PEOPLE: "Rushing this project would cost the lives of too many citizens!"
    • Half-the-city cap and empty-box doubling. Several forum posts agree, but none is a Firaxis source:
      • eg577 (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/hurry-production.95782/): "you cannot "whip" more than half of your citizens at once" and "twice that many citizens if there are no shields contributed yet".
      • DBear (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-is-pop-rush-done-now.128518/, C3C 1.22): "you cannot pop rush more than half your population at the same time".
      • Dominae (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/73546-1.htm, 10 Jan 2003, PTW era): "in any given turn, you can only sacrifice up to half your population to poprush".
      • Bluefrog (same thread): "If you have no shields in the build box, the cost of rushing is doubled. This is true for pop- and cash-rushing."
      • Ferd (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/massive-despot-rush-strategy.10093/, 23 Nov 2001, vanilla before 1.17f): "each citizen is good for rushing 20 shields" and "rushing cost is doubled if the shield box is empty."
      • Section 2 (city economy) §4 has the full hurry sourcing.
    • No hurrying in disorder or resistance. SCRIPT #HURRY_CIVIL_DISORDER: "We cannot hurry production while our city is in civil disorder." SCRIPT #HURRY_RESISTANCE: "We cannot hurry production while our city is in resistance."
    • Whip unhappiness follows the population. Catt (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/63161-1.htm, 1 Oct 2002, vanilla) reports this as from Firaxis programmers, second-hand: "unhappiness moves to nearest city if a city is abandoned". From his own play-testing: it "travels with a settler created in disbanding a city used to pop-rush too often". Zero, in the same thread, reported a test where "it seemed to stick to only cities". UNVERIFIED.
    • Wonders cannot be pop-rushed. CFC-FAQ: "you can't rush a wonder by sacrificing population (Despotism, Communism, Fascism)".
  • Gold rush rate. BIQ RULE ShieldValueInGold=4. EDHELP Shield Value in Gold: "how many gold pieces it costs to buy each shield when hurrying production of a city improvement. Default is 4 gold."
    • Empty production box doubles the cost. Forum (petermarkab, same thread): "Multiply by 8 if no shields have been contributed."
    • Unit vs improvement vs wonder multipliers are UNVERIFIED (see open questions).
  • Leaders: EditorSupplement.txt: "NOTE: Military leaders can not rush." Great (scientific) leaders can (PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production).
  • Draft:
    • PEDIA GCON_Draft: "When a civilization develops Nationalism its citizens can be drafted ... A city in a Democracy or Republic can draft [one] citizen per turn ... Communism, Despotism, Fascism, Feudalism, or Monarchy can draft [two] ... You may only draft citizens from a city with a population of 7 or higher."
    • The limit is per city, not per civ. PEDIA GCON_Draft: "Each city can draft some of its population each turn, depending upon the government currently in effect." EDHELP Draft Limit: "how many units can be drafted (converting population points into a unit) by the selected government type per turn."
    • Draft anger lasts 20 turns: BIQ RULE TurnPenaltyForEachDraftedCitizen=20. Ginger_Ale (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-about-drafting-in-civ3.107429/, 16 Dec 2004): "it makes 1 citizen per unit drafted unhappy for 20 turns."
    • Forum claims of a Communism draft rate of 3 (eldar and Ginger_Ale in thread 107429; Inverse Icarus in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/53252-1.htm, 2002) are wrong for the target patches. BIQ says 2, and README 1.17f says "Fixed Communism Civilopedia entry to reflect the correct draft rate of 2." Franses (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/55285-1.htm, 11 Jul 2002) checked it in the editor: "Communism indeed has a draft rate of 2 now."
    • Units are conscripts: Spearman, Pikeman, Musketman, Rifleman, Infantry or Mech. Infantry by tech.
    • README 1.29f: "Corrected the Communism draft rate listed in the Draft Civilopedia entry to 2."

5.6 Corruption types (government factors only; full model in the corruption section)

CFC-CORR (C3C), quoted:

  • Distance factor Gd: "Gd = 3/2 for Rampant corruption (Despotism) / 3/4 for Minimal corruption (Democracy) / 1 otherwise".
  • Optimal-city-number factors: "Gr = 0.1 for minimal or nuisance corruption / 2 for communal corruption / 0 otherwise" and "Gp = 3/8 non-communal corruption / 3 for communal corruption" (Gp multiplies the number of corruption-reducing wonders: Forbidden Palace, SPHQ).
  • Communal distance: "For communal governments, distance corruption does not actually depend on distance. Each city is considered to be the same distance from the Palace." The value is "d = (MaxD) / 4 where MaxD = (MapW+MapH)/4".
  • Communal rank: "all cities have the same rank, which is half the total number of cities in the empire, rounded down."
  • Catastrophic (Anarchy) is fixed and bypasses the formula (EDHELP quote under Anarchy).

5.7 Revolution, anarchy length, and switching

  • When you can switch:
    • Script: "We cannot have a revolution until we discover different forms of government." Each government needs its prerequisite tech (table above). EDHELP Prerequisite: "Selects the civilization advance (if any) required before this government type is enabled."
    • You cannot re-revolt during anarchy. SCRIPT #ALREADYREVOLTING: "Sir! we are already undergoing a revolt..!"
    • Revolution is started from the Domestic Advisor (PEDIA GCON_Revolution).
    • Learning a new government's tech triggers an offer. SCRIPT #NEW_GOVERNMENT_AVAILABLE: "our people yearn for a change in government to ... Shall we encourage a revolution?"
  • Involuntary anarchy:
    • PEDIA GCON_Disorder: prolonged disorder can lead to "An overthrow of your government and descent of your empire into anarchy."
    • Democracy war weariness can do the same (CFC-WW level 3 "Revolt"). Code reading: Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16199613, page-27 #523): "a player gets kicked into anarchy if their WW is over 90", where "it checks against total WW divided by the number of contacts the player has." See section 3 §3.5.
  • Transition government. BIQ: Anarchy has TransitionType=1. EDHELP Flags: "When changing governments (i.e., having a revolution), civilizations temporarily transition to the selected government."
  • Anarchy length:
    • PEDIA GOVT_Anarchy: "Anarchy seldom lasts longer than a few turns."
    • The domestic advisor reports the remaining turns. SCRIPT: "We should have the government back under control in about $NUM0 turns."
    • Vanilla (Firaxis, first-hand). Soren Johnson, "Did Firaxis change the length of Anarchy in 1.17f?" (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42739-1.htm):
      • 22 Feb 2002, 21:41: "oh, you wanted to know if there is a random factor. well, there is. it's between 1 and 5 turns. plus 0 - 3 extra turns depending on number of cities".
      • Same thread, 22 Feb 2002, 13:01: "anarchy length is increased by number of cities".
      • 21 Feb 2002: "If you play a religious civ, anarchy always takes 1 turn."
      • Catt (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/63506-1.htm, 2 Oct 2002, vanilla) gives the same figures and says Soren confirmed them "way back in February": "the length is always between 1 - 5 turns on a random basis, with an additional 0-3 turns depending on number of cities". Theseus, same thread, writes "1-3 from # of cities" instead. planetfall, same thread, saw a reload turn an 8-turn anarchy into 5, which suggests the random part is rolled when you revolt (one report).
      • So vanilla non-Religious anarchy is 1-8 turns. Padma's 2003 relay (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/going-to-monarchy.51241/) repeats this ("always between 1 - 5 turns on a random basis" plus "an additional 0-3 turns depending on number of cities").
    • C3C adds one turn.
      • SirPleb, C3C changes list (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/, 12 Nov 2004): "When revolting to change the government the period of anarchy is one turn longer in C3C."
      • CFC-FAQ (C3C-aware, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/): "For non-Religious civilizations, the formula is: 1 (2 for Conquests) + random number between 1-4" "+ number between 0-3 depending on size of your empire". For Religious civs: "anarchy only lasts 1 turn in Vanilla and Play the World, and 2 turns in Conquests."
    • C3C observed range. justanick (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/duration-of-anarchy.683384/, 19 Apr 2023):
      • "the anarchy duration of non-religious tribes in C3C can take values between 3 und 9 turns."
      • "Small empires incur 3 to 7 turns of anarchy, mid sized empires 4 to 8 turns and larger empires 5 to 9 turns."
      • He saw five different values at one empire size (5-9, Napoleon scenario, Emperor). That fits a 1-5 random part rather than the FAQ's 1-4.
      • Later (same thread, May 2026): "very small empires incur 2 to 6 turns of anarchy."
      • None of his posts give the city-count thresholds for the size bands.
    • Synthesis (C3C, non-Religious). Soren's vanilla formula plus SirPleb's extra turn is 1 + rand(1..5) + (0..3 by city count), which gives 2-9. This matches justanick's bands and the C3C reports of 9 turns:
      • vmxa (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-to-define-time-of-anarcy.440076, 16 Sep 2011): "Yes in C3C you can get 9 turns and I have done it. It was thought that 8 was the max, but not in C3C."
      • Turner (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/number-of-turns-for-revolution-calculation.108456/): "8 and 9 turn anarchy was seen."
      • The city-count thresholds and the exact random range (FAQ 1-4 vs Soren 1-5) are still unconfirmed (see open questions).
    • Older or looser statements:
      • CFC-GOV: "It lasts from 2 to 8 turns, dependent on the size of your civilization / map size." This is too low a maximum for C3C. justanick (thread 276379 page 2): "Actually 2 to 9 turns."
      • Lanzelot (thread 440076) adds difficulty as a factor for the human: non-Religious anarchy runs "from 2 to 9", with longer lengths more likely as map size, town count and difficulty increase. This is an opinion; nothing else supports a difficulty term for the human player.
    • Religious, C3C: 2 turns, which looks like 1 if you revolt from the new-tech popup.
      • Turner (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers.100914/page-5, 9 Oct 2004, C3C): "it's 1 turn in C3C when you revolt as soon as it asks you too." and "It's two when you do it during your turn."
      • Qitai (thread 62465, 27 Aug 2003): if you accept the popup offered on learning the tech, "the revolt is considered being done LAST turn."
      • justanick (thread 683384): "Religious tribes always incur 2 turns of anarchy". Revolting right after a tech, "the old turn does still count towards the total turns of the anarchy."
      • BUGLIST, under "NOT BUGS": "Religious civs now have up to 2 turns of anarchy."
      • Apolyton, "How long does anarchy take?" (https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/138928-how-long-does-anarchy-take, Sep 2004): punkbass2000, for Religious civs, "Two turns in C3C, one turn previously." Modo44 (page 2, 27 Sep 2004): "Well, in C3C the minimal Anarchy period is 2 turns, religious civ or not." and "there is no more ultra fast switching for religious civs in C3C." Tarquinius in the same thread recalls a different vanilla formula, "S+R+1=Turns of Anarchy" with S and R each 1-3 ("IIRC"); Soren's first-hand formula outranks it.
      • Lanzelot (thread 683384) reports, as hearsay, that the change was made to stop an interturn revolt exploit.
    • Re-revolting. CKS (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anarchy-question.452205, 24 Jan 2012): "In vanilla/PTW, re-revolting changes the length of the anarchy - possibly shorter, possibly longer." Also: "In Conquests you get the same length of anarchy, though it looks a turn shorter". Forum only.
    • Golden Age during anarchy: a single anecdote says it halved the anarchy. garyg (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/you-say-you-want-a-revolution.116180, 6 Apr 2005): "once accidently triggered my GA on my first turn of Anarchy and it cut the time in half." UNVERIFIED.
  • Religious trait. PEDIA GCON_Strengths: "Religious civilizations do not experience prolonged periods of anarchy during revolutions". No wonder affects anarchy length in C3C: the Statue of Zeus entry (BLDG_Zeus) only says it "produces an Ancient Cavalry every 5 turns". The "Statue of Zeus/Liberty shortens anarchy" idea is Civ2.
  • AI cap. BIQ DIFF MaxGovernmentTransitionTime: 0 (no cap) for Chieftain, Warlord and Regent; Monarch 4, Emperor 3, Demigod 2, Deity 2, Sid 1. EDHELP: "the maximum number of turns the AI must remain in the transition government type ... The default value of zero means there is no maximum".
    • justanick (thread 683384) gives slightly different observed caps: "the maximum anarchy duration is zero at Sid, one at Deity, two at Demigod, three at Emperor and four at King". (He means Monarch; Civ3 has no "King" level.) He adds: "At regent and below the anarchy duration rules for AI are the same as for the human player." He also says the Religious 2 turns overrides the AI cap. His Deity and Sid values are one lower than the BIQ (see Disputed).
  • Government-locked buildings switch off when you leave the government. README C3C 1.20: "Fixed error with Government required buildings not turning off once switched from that government."

5.8 AI favourite / shunned government

  • EDHELP Favorite Government: "If a government is selected, the civilization will immediately switch to this government type as soon as it becomes available."
  • EDHELP Shunned Government: "the civilization will avoid this government type at all costs, and if it is forced to operate under this government type, it will immediately switch to another government type as soon as another government type becomes available."
  • C3C defaults (BIQ RACE, Favourite / Shunned):
Civ Fav Shun Civ Fav Shun
Rome Republic Communism Iroquois Communism Monarchy
Egypt Despotism Republic England Democracy Fascism
Greece Democracy Despotism Mongols Despotism Democracy
Babylon Monarchy Despotism Spain Monarchy Despotism
Germany Republic Communism Scandinavia Monarchy Republic
Russia Communism Democracy Ottomans Republic Despotism
China Communism Feudalism Celts Despotism Feudalism
America Democracy Communism Arabia Republic Despotism
Japan Feudalism Republic Carthage Republic Communism
France Republic Fascism Korea Monarchy Republic
India Democracy Despotism Sumeria Monarchy Republic
Persia Monarchy Republic Hittites Fascism Republic
Aztecs Despotism Monarchy Netherlands Monarchy Despotism
Zululand Despotism Republic Portugal Democracy Fascism
Byzantines Republic Feudalism Inca Communism Republic
Maya Feudalism Democracy Barbarians none none
  • Code reading: the AI scores governments in gold, and the favourite is a weight. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16217016, page-30 #583): "the AI evaluates governments in points, where 1 point = 1 GPT maintenance." Terms he lists: "Not having the tile penalty is worth 8 points for each city the AI controls," and corruption tiers count per city, the milder the more (the notes give minimal 12, nuisance 8, problematic and communal 4, rampant 2; (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "minimal is worth 12 points per city, nuisance is worth 8 per, problematic & communal are worth 4 per, and rampant 2 per.") "Shunned/favorite government is worth 12.5%". There is "a separate function that calculates a point value for any given gov't for the AI" (post-16715493, page-122 #2429), and "the contributions from corruption and tile penalty are multiplied by the AI's city count" (post-16719849, page-123 #2448). So in the code the favourite and shunned settings tilt the score by 12.5% (up or down, presumably), rather than forcing EDHELP's "immediately switch". This is the code reading against the Firaxis help text; it fits chiefpaco's and Propaganda's observation that AIs often pick other governments (section 8, Disputed 8). Whether the 12.5% is added to or multiplied into the score is not stated.
  • "Favourite Despotism" (Egypt, Aztecs, Zulu, Mongols, Celts) means the AI never voluntarily leaves the start government, if read literally. UNVERIFIED in play.
  • Tavis (Firaxis), thread 67419 page 2, 2 Nov 2003, at C3C release: "only the Hittites have Fascism as their Preferred Gov't and France & Portugal as their Shunned". The 1.22 BIQ also has England shunning Fascism. Either the data changed after release, or Tavis left England out. The BIQ value stands for 1.22.
  • Forum claims that the favourite and shunned settings drive AI attitude rather than (or as well as) AI government choice. Neither is tested, and both conflict with EDHELP:
    • wilbill (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/some-thoughts-on-despotism.69616/, 23 Nov 2003): "favored and shunned governments only effect the AI's attitude toward other civs" and the AI "will always go to the 'best' govenment available".
    • wilbill (thread 61628 page 2, 13 Jan 2004): "Favorite/Shunned govs have nothing to do with WW - only with AI attitude toward you."

5.9 War weariness

  • Who suffers. PEDIA GCON_War_Weariness: "citizens living under a Republic, Feudalism, or Democracy become [war weary]". BIQ WW levels: Republic 1, Feudalism 1, Democracy 2, all others 0.
  • PEDIA, same entry: "When [you] initiate the conflict your people will tolerate it for a while ... When someone else declares the war, especially a traditional enemy, your people are more forgiving. Carrying on a defensive war, that is keeping your troops at home, is more tolerable ... the war weariness remains until the war stops."
  • Mitigation: Police Station ("combats war weariness", PEDIA BLDG_Police_Station) and Universal Suffrage ("Reduces war weariness in all cities", PEDIA BLDG_Universal_Suffrage).
  • The WW factor accumulates under every government; only the effect is government-specific. Soren Johnson (Firaxis), "War weariness carryover" (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/55847-2.htm, post #33, 11 Sep 2002, vanilla 1.29f era):
    • "there is a war weariness factor that is maintained the same way throughout your entire game".
    • "The only time this factor affects your citizens is if you are at war while in a demo/rep."
