    • On peace: signing a peace treaty does lessen WW, but "the factor is still there, hidden away. It decays gradually behind the scenes."
    • So switching from Monarchy or Communism to Republic or Democracy in mid-war brings the stored factor into effect. Oystein (thread 61628 page 4, 7 Feb 2005) agrees: "The calculation of wwp is independant of government."
  • Version of CFC-WW. The article is Oystein's forum research (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-war-weariness-work.61628/, Oct 2003). It was done on vanilla: "I have not PTW". It was done by save-file inspection: "I have figured out where wwp is stored in the savefile".
    • alexman briefly claimed a C3C change and then retracted it ("My bad. Sorry.", page 2, 29 Nov 2003).
    • Oystein retested on C3C (page 2, 20 Feb 2004): "Everything seems to be the same." Treat the CFC-WW numbers as valid for C3C.
  • Formula, CFC-WW (community research; vanilla, retested on C3C as above):
    • Points: "Each civ have one wwp number against each of the other civs."
    • Levels: "-1: - 0 wh (war happiness) / 0: 0 - 30 normal, no effect / 1: 31 - 60 / 2: 61 - 90 / 3: 91 - 120 / 4: 121 -".
    • Republic: "Level 1: 25% unhappy people / Level 2: 50% / Level 3: 50% / Level 4: 100%".
    • Democracy: "Level 1: 50% unhappy people / Level 2: 100% unhappy people / Level 3: Revolt".
    • War happiness: "Level -1: 25% happy people" (all governments).
    • Combining: "Number of unhappy people is round down. The number from each civ is added together and subtract 25% for police station and 1 for US (Universal Sufferage)."
    • Point events:
      • "Subtract 30 wwp if the AI attacks you, except when AI is provoked by: use of nuclear weapons, failed spy mission"
      • "Add 1 wwp if you have units in enemys territory when in war."
      • "Add 1 wwp for each lost unit without defence value / improvement pillage/bombed / unit that are bombard down to 1 hp"
      • "Add 2 wwp when a human attacker is defeated"
      • "Add 2 wwp when a unit with defence value is attacked. (Even if you win)"
      • "Add 16 wwp when a size 1 city is captured 17 wwp for bigger cities."
    • Decay: "Subtract 1 wwp if level >= 1, no enemy inside your territory and no units in enemys territory." and "Subtract 1/20 of current wwp each turn in peace (round up)".
    • Bug (as reported): "For a human-AI battle, the AI gets the same penalty as the human".
    • Feudalism (Low) uses the Republic (Low) table. The BIQ level is the same enum value, and see the Feudalism notes in 5.2.
  • Clarifications from the same thread (Oystein unless noted):
    • Threshold: "you need 31 wwp to get ww. 30 wwp gives no effect." (page 3, 19 Apr 2004)
    • Rounding: "The number from ww is rounded down." (page 3, 11 Mar 2004)
    • Cap: "The total number of unhappy citizen from ww can not exceed number of citizen." (page 2, 17 Oct 2003)
    • Police Station: "If you have 125% or more ww, the police station would not have any effect since you are above the limit anyway." (same post)
    • Per-civ storage: "the system stores one number for each of the other civs." and "20 turns of peace does NOT mean that ww is gone." (page 3, 23 Apr 2004)
    • Combat: "If you attack and kill an AI, none gets wwp. If the AI wins, both get wwp." (page 2, 20 Feb 2004)
    • Flak: "Bombers shot down by flaks does not add to wwp" (page 3)
    • Phoney war: Grille, page 4: "A phoney war doesn't add ww points."
    • Culture flips: Lord Emsworth (page 4, 11 Mar 2009, tested): "No WW from flipped cities." Recapturing a flipped city does add WW (page 5).
    • War happiness: negative points carry over too. Oystein, page 4: "You will get back the WH."
    • Overflow (binyo66, page 3, read with a save tool): "the highest WW is 999. After that it resets to 0". Single report.
  • AI turn-order bug (C3C 1.00). BUGLIST, Minor bug, quoting a user: "When an AI player attacks an earlier AI both gets war weariness, when he attacks a later AI nothing happens." Oystein (thread 61628 page 1, 13 Oct 2003) describes the same thing: "the first player gets penalties as a human, the second gets penlties as an AI".

5.10 Senate / forced ceasefire

  • Civ3 has no Senate. No Senate, ceasefire-forcing or forced-peace rule appears in the PEDIA, SCRIPT (advisor and popup texts), labels.txt, diplomacy.txt, BIQ GOVT fields or EDHELP. The only "Senate" mentions in Civilopedia.txt are historical prose (e.g. DESC_GOVT_Republic).
  • The Civ2 Senate is the origin of the idea. In Civ3 the equivalent pressure is war weariness, and the Democracy WW revolt.
  • Status: verified by absence in the game files. One forum statement says it outright. Kramsib (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/84801-1.htm, 1 May 2003), on Republic: "there is no senate and no penalisation for having your units out of your cities". So there is also no Civ2-style unhappiness from units abroad. The only war-related unhappiness is war weariness.

5.11 Espionage / propaganda modifiers (GOVT-vs-GOVT matrix)

BIQ stores three values per ordered pair of governments: CanBribe, BriberyModifier and ResistanceModifier. The editor shows BriberyModifier as "Propaganda Modifier Vs". EDHELP: "higher means a better chance of bribing ... Selecting "Cannot Bribe this Government Type" effectively prevents bribery from occurring."

C3C BriberyModifier (row = BIQ record, column = "vs"). It matches the CFC-ESP "Government modifier" table exactly:

row \ vs Anar Desp Mon Comm Rep Demo Fasc Feud
Anarchy 0 0 0 0 0 0 0 0
Despotism 15 0 -10 -15 -20 -30 0 0
Monarchy 20 15 0 -5 -10 -25 0 0
Communism 25 20 5 0 -10 -20 0 0
Republic 30 25 10 8 0 -5 0 0
Democracy 35 30 20 10 5 0 0 0
Fascism 0 0 -5 -20 -10 -10 0 0
Feudalism 20 15 0 -10 -10 -20 -25 0

C3C ResistanceModifier (row/col as above):

row \ vs Anar Desp Mon Comm Rep Demo Fasc Feud
Anarchy 0 5 5 5 5 5 0 0
Despotism -5 0 5 5 5 5 0 0
Monarchy -5 -5 0 5 5 5 0 0
Communism -5 -5 -5 0 -5 5 0 0
Republic -5 -5 5 5 0 5 0 0
Democracy -5 -5 -5 5 -5 0 0 0
Fascism -5 -5 0 -10 -5 -5 0 0
Feudalism -5 -5 5 5 0 0 5 0
  • EDHELP Resistance Modifier: "the modifier to a single population point's chance of continuing to resist each turn. Positive numbers mean that the resisting population has a greater chance of continuing to resist".
  • Propaganda success per citizen, CFC-ESP: "spy + government + culture - 5 * units - improvement - capital + nationality", and "If at least 3/4 of the citizen are persuading, the city will flip."
  • CanBribe is 1 for all pairs in PTW/vanilla and the uninitialized value 0xCDCDCDCD in C3C. Treat it as "can bribe" (no government is protected by it).
  • Assimilation. EDHELP Assimilation Chance: "the percentage chance, per turn, that a foreign national in city with the selected government type will assimilate". Values are in the master table.
    • Precondition: PEDIA GCON_Assimilation: "When a foreign national has been a part of a minority population longer than not, he or she assimilates and becomes a member of the majority nationality."
    • Forum readings of that precondition, all second-hand or anecdotal, PTW era:
      • Mad Bomber (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/81314-nationality-assimilation, 27 Nov 2002): assimilation starts once a city "has belonged to your civ more than the civ that possessed the City previously".
      • Jaybe (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/57182-1.htm, 27 Jul 2002): "they convert when they have been in your hands LONGER than their own civ."
      • Traelin (thread 81314) adds that this only applies while the original owner is still in the game.
    • Warpstorm (thread 81314) gives rates that match the BIQ: "Communism and Democracy have the best chance of assimilation with a 4% chance a turn (per citizen I think)", Republic and Monarchy 2%, the rest 1%.
    • War: PEDIA GCON_Nationality: "When at war, foreign nationals become unhappy more quickly than others and are more likely to be affected by hostile propaganda from their mother country."

5.12 Rate cap (max slider rate)

  • BIQ ScienceRateCap = 10 for every government in C3C, PTW and vanilla.
  • EDHELP Rate Cap: "Determines the maximum science funding (0-10) that the selected government can engage in. Default is always 10 (100%)."
  • So stock Civ3 has no per-government slider cap (unlike Civ2's 60/70/80%).
  • Weak corroboration, Apolyton (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/44662-low-science-quick-monarchy-tips): "I changed to a 100 percent science rating." (The government is not stated.) Mods set lower caps. A modder's Apolyton post (seen only as a search snippet) used Anarchy 5, Despotism 7 and Monarchy 8.

5.13 Implementation notes (C7 cross-check)

  • C7/Lua/civ3/ruleset.json governments[] matches BIQ on corruption, hurry, rateCap (10), war weariness, draft, MP, workerRate, free units and unitCost.

  • It has no fields for:

    • AssimilationChance
    • ImmuneTo (Democracy propaganda immunity)
    • DiplomatsAre / SpiesAre
    • Xenophobic / ForceResettle (Fascism)
    • the GOVT-vs-GOVT propaganda and resistance matrices

    Check whether the engine hardcodes these elsewhere before relying on them.

  • BIQ fields Toggle1/Toggle2/Toggle3 (QueryCiv3 names; meaning unknown) are uninitialized (0xCCCCCCCC) for the two C3C-only governments, Fascism and Feudalism. That strongly suggests the game ignores them.

5.14 Capital loss and civil war

  • Civ3 has no civil war (empire split on capital capture).
    • No civil-war rule, popup or advisor text exists in the C3C game text. Conquests/Text/script.txt and Civilopedia.txt mention "civil war" only in historical prose.
    • Every forum source found for the split rules is about Civ1 or Civ2:
      • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/splitting-civilizations.6857 (Civ2 Strategy forum).
      • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civil-war.131672 (Civ2 forum: "A civil war occurs when you capture an AI's capital that is ranked higher than you are in the power graph").
      • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/empire-split-by-fall-of-the-capital-when-playing-with-less-than-7-civs.674921/. Civ1 forum; it quotes Rome on 640k a Day, a Civ1 strategy guide.
    • Civ3 players say it is missing:
      • RegentMan (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civil-war.84798/, 13 Apr 2004): "Loved them in Civ II. Missed them in Civ III."
      • Early Civ3 reports (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/34719-1.htm, 19 Nov 2001) describe no split. ElitePersian: "it doesnt do anymore than the actual loss of a city".
    • Status: verified by absence, plus forum agreement.
  • The palace moves to another city for free when the capital is lost.
    • Cavalier_13 (thread 34719): "you see the capital shift to the new city at no cost to the CPU AI." D4everman: "in the same turn it was moved to another city". Akaoz adds that it destroys "the civs spaceship if any". README 1.17f: "Fixed bug which did not destroy an enemy spaceship if the enemy capital was razed." and "Fixed bug which allowed civs with no capitals."
    • Which city gets it, after razing or abandoning: the "Free Palace Jump" article (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/free-palace-jump/, DaveMcW, 2003, vanilla/PTW testing; forum thread https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/free-palace-jump.47304/). Each city scores:
      • 3 points per national citizen and 1 per foreign citizen;
      • 1 / 2 / 3 per neighbouring town / city / metropolis "within 8 tiles walking distance from the target city (a 17×17 square)", not counting the old capital;
      • 1 per military unit.
      • "If there is a tie for the most points, the palace jumps to the first city in the database, which is usually the oldest city."
    • The same scoring in the C3C 1.22 code. C3X-SRC (injected_code.c, https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c, read 2026-10-20) rates candidate cities for a lost secondary palace with a comment that "The criteria we use to rate it are identical to what the base game uses to find a new location for the palace." Its rating, per candidate city other than the lost one and the capital:
      • rating += Population.Size, plus 2 * City_count_citizens_of_race (candidate, leader->RaceID). That is 3 per national citizen and 1 per foreign one, as DaveMcW found.
      • plus the units in the city (count_units_at with a unit filter the header leaves unnamed, UF_4);
      • plus, for each other own city on the 289 tiles of the 17×17 square round it (FOR_TILES_AROUND (tai, 0x121, ...)), 3 if its size is above the city maximum (a metropolis), 2 if above the town maximum (a city), else 1;
      • the best rating wins, and a tie keeps the first city in the list (if (rating > best_rating)).
      • This confirms DaveMcW's formula for C3C, including the 17×17 square and the tie rule. It is code that re-creates the stock rule, not the stock function itself. The "walking distance" in the article is the square, not a path.
    • Not confirmed: whether the same scoring applies when the capital is captured rather than razed or abandoned. The C3X function runs whenever the palace is lost, but it is the mod's copy. justanick (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/looking-for-the-formula-on-how-the-palace-gets-relocated.703374/, May 2026) quotes the same formula and says it was found "after weeks of testing". Charon117's test saves in that thread showed a result that did not fit, and the thread does not settle it.
  • Losing the capital does not by itself cause anarchy or a government change. No source reports it, and the C3C texts have no such rule.

Disputed

  1. Anarchy length (C3C, non-Religious). It is mostly settled as 1 + rand(1..5) + (0..3 by city count) = 2-9 (see 5.7). Two points are still disputed:
    • The random part: Soren (vanilla) says "between 1 and 5", and Catt's 2002 relay of Soren (Apolyton 63506) repeats "1 - 5". The CFC-FAQ says "random number between 1-4". justanick saw five values at one empire size, which supports 1-5.
    • The minimum: the CFC-FAQ formula gives 3. justanick first wrote "between 3 und 9", then "very small empires incur 2 to 6 turns".
    • Lanzelot also suggests a difficulty term for the human; no other source supports it.
  2. Religious anarchy in vanilla.
    • Soren Johnson (codehappy 42739, 21 Feb 2002): "If you play a religious civ, anarchy always takes 1 turn." The CFC-FAQ agrees.
    • For C3C, punkbass2000 and Modo44 (Apolyton 138928, Sep 2004) agree with the CFC-FAQ: 2 turns in C3C, 1 before.
    • The GameFAQs guide's "no period of anarchy between governments" is wrong for 1.17f+. It may describe the observed effect of revolting from the new-tech popup (see 5.7, Turner/Qitai).
  3. AI anarchy cap at Deity and Sid. BIQ MaxGovernmentTransitionTime: Deity 2, Sid 1. justanick's observations (thread 683384): Deity 1, Sid 0. Monarch 4, Emperor 3 and Demigod 2 agree. The engine may use the value as an exclusive bound, or justanick may have miscounted. Not resolved.
  4. Gold-rush rate. 4 gold per shield, doubled from an empty box, is well sourced (section 2 §4). The older recollection of "about 2 gold per shield for improvements and higher for units and wonders" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anyone-know-the-formulae-for-rush-building.18881) is in the Civ2 General Discussions forum. It is Civ2 data and should be ignored for Civ3. The "ms×2 + ms²/20" unit formula on CFC's "Section D" FAQ page (https://civfanatics.com/?p=128) is from the Civ1 FAQ.
  5. Communism support. 6/6/6 (C3C BIQ and PEDIA) vs 2/4/8 (PTW/vanilla BIQ and PEDIA, and the vanilla guide). These are version differences, not errors.
  6. Republic support. 1/3/4 at 2 gold (C3C) vs 0/0/0 at 1 gold (PTW/vanilla). Version difference, confirmed by the PTW/vanilla PEDIA tables and MAS (thread 191722).
  7. Metropolis threshold. CFC-GOV says "Metro: 12+". BIQ RULE (max level-2 size 12) and the vanilla guide ("metropolis (size 13+)") say 13+. playshogi (thread 67419 page 4) says "1-6 town", and 13+ for metros.
  8. Favourite/shunned Fascism. Tavis's release-time list (Hittites favour it; France and Portugal shun it) vs the 1.22 BIQ, which also has England shunning it.

Resolved in this pass and moved to the main text: Despotism tile penalty wording (5.2 Despotism), trade bonus scope (5.2 Republic), pop-rush rate (20 per citizen, 5.5), Feudalism maintenance (5.2 Feudalism).

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  1. The C3C anarchy city-count thresholds: how many cities give +1, +2 or +3 turns. Is it pure city count (Soren: "number of cities") or does map size enter (CFC-GOV, vmxa)? Needs a controlled test.
  2. Gold hurry cost by item type: unit vs improvement vs wonder multipliers, and any non-linear term. Still open; see section 2 open question 4. The Civ2 and Civ1 formulas once cited for it are discarded (Disputed 4).
  3. Pop-rush: the half-the-city cap and empty-box doubling rest on consistent forum statements (5.5), not on a Firaxis source. Still open: whether foreign citizens are whipped first. CFC-GOV implies yes; Ision guesses "natural born citizens will always die first".
  4. Whether units inside an Army pay support individually, and whether Leaders really cost upkeep in play (BIQ RequireSupport=1). Related but separate: for suppressing resistance an Army counts by its contents. vondrack (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/58829-1.htm, 13 Aug 2002): "it will be counted for 1-4 units, depending on how many of them is actually in the army." Jaybe (Apolyton 116300, 25 Oct 2003): "An Army of 4 (3 combat units plus the Army unit) seems to count as 4 for resistance suppression."
  5. Direction of the GOVT-vs-GOVT matrices: is the row the initiator's government or the target's? CFC-ESP gives no explanation. The pattern suggests rows are the spy's government (the Democracy row has the largest bonuses), but this is an inference. EDHELP Bribery_Modifier_Vs_Government_Properties_.htm (titled "Propaganda Modifier Vs"), read this pass: "Determines the bribery modifier, if any (higher means a better chance of bribing) against a unit of the selected government type." So the government picked in the selector is the target and the government being edited is the one doing the bribing or propaganda. In the file each GOVT record carries its own block of one entry per government (QueryCiv3 Biq.cs: "Each GOVT record has a GOVT_GOVT relationship with every government"), so the record is the edited government. Read together, a row is the acting (bribing or propagandising) government and a column the target, which fits the pattern above. Still an inference from the editor text, not a test. (The CanBribe field reads 0xCDCDCDCD, uninitialised fill, in every C3C cell.)
  6. "Feudalism pikemen support" and "Tribalism veteran chance": not stock C3C (no data in PEDIA, BIQ or EDHELP). Probably from mods.
  7. Whether favourite-government AIs really "immediately" revolt when the tech arrives, as EDHELP says, and how the AI chooses when it has no favourite. C3X-THREAD (code reading): the choice is a per-government score in gold per turn, with the favourite or shunned government weighted by 12.5% (5.8). That argues against an absolute switch rule. What triggers the AI to start a revolution at all is still unpublished. Forum posts (wilbill) claim the setting only affects attitude; untested.
  8. Whether a Golden Age or WLTKD removes the Despotism tile penalty. No source found. PEDIA GCON_We_Love_The_King_Day lists only lower flip chance, less waste and weaker enemy propaganda. Dan Magaha (relayed, codehappy 25003) says WLTKD is "city-specific and can happen repeatedly", separate from the Golden Age.
  9. Whether a Golden Age during anarchy shortens it (garyg anecdote, 5.7).
  10. Whether the C3C 1.00 bugs listed in BUGLIST were fixed by 1.22: anarchy research through rioting scientists, and AI war weariness depending on turn order. The SPHQ bug was fixed in 1.20 (README).
  11. Free Palace Jump scoring when the capital is captured rather than razed or abandoned. The C3C scoring itself is now confirmed from code (5.14).

Resolved in this pass:

  • Which units count against support (5.3).
  • Feudalism uses the Republic WW table (5.9).
  • CFC-WW holds for C3C (5.9).
  • "Fascism cannot be bribed" is not a rule (5.2 Fascism).
  • Anarchy has zero unit upkeep (5.2 Anarchy).
  • Re-revolting: vanilla/PTW re-rolls the length, C3C keeps it (5.7; forum only, CKS).

Back to the top

6. Diplomacy, espionage and AI attitude

Scope: the base game (1.29f), Play the World (1.27f) and Conquests (1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes are excluded.

How to read this section:

  • Every rule has a source and a short verbatim quote. "Local install" means the files of a Steam Civilization III Complete install (Conquests 1.22), read directly: Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt, Conquests/Text/script.txt, Conquests/Text/diplomacy.txt, Conquests/Text/labels.txt, the readmes, and Conquests/conquests.biq (read with this repo's QueryCiv3 parser).
  • Vanilla-only text comes from Text/Civilopedia.txt in the same install.
  • "Manual" means the Steam manual PDF (https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/3910/manuals/manual.pdf), read as a text extract. It is the Civ3 Complete edition, but its diplomacy chapter is mostly base-game text. Page numbers are the printed folios, approximate.
  • "EDHELP" means the Conquests editor help (Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, decompiled), cited by topic name.
  • "C3X-INI" means the C3X mod's default.c3x_config.ini (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini). The mod is out of scope. Only its descriptions of what the unmodded C3C 1.22 executable does are used.
  • "Code-level" marks findings from people reading the C3C 1.22 executable.
  • "C3X-THREAD" means the C3X mod's forum thread, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (2021-2026). Posts are cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable; other posters report play, sometimes modded. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".
  • Community findings are labelled with the tester, version and date where the source gives them. CivFanatics studies use a sign convention where a negative attitude number is good for you.
  • Anything without a source is marked UNVERIFIED.

6.1 Contact and the diplomacy screen

  • The Diplomacy button needs first contact. Source: vanilla README.txt (manual errata, local install). Quote: "The Diplomacy button becomes enabled after your first contact with another civilization."
  • Foreign Advisor lines need contact with both civs. Source: vanilla README.txt, Fixes v1.17f. Quote: "Relationship lines are no longer displayed on the Foreign Advisor unless the player has contact with both civilizations."
  • There are five attitude states, which the advisor mouse-over shows. Source: vanilla README.txt (1.17f). Quote: "The five states an AI can be in on the Diplomacy screen, can now be seen on the mouse over advisor messages". Their names are in Conquests labels.txt: "gracious", "polite", "cautious", "annoyed", "furious".
  • Communications (contacts) can be traded. They are introductions to a third civ, and Printing Press unlocks the trade. Sources: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements, quote: "{Communications}. These are introductions to a third civilization you can grant or receive from a rival." Also conquests.biq TECH flags, where Printing Press has "Enables Communication Trading". Vanilla Civilopedia TECH_Printing_Press, quote: "Can now trade communications between civilizations."
  • PTW fix: the AI cannot trade communications before it has the tech. Source: civ3PTW/readme.txt. Quote: "Fixed bug that allowed the AI to trade communications before they had the tech that allowed it."
  • A world map does not give contact. Source: CivFanatics, "Basic trading tips" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/basic-trading-tips.71236/). Quote (as surfaced by search): "trading World Maps will show you the world known by the other civ but will not give you contact with civs you have".
  • First contact comes from units meeting. Source: the Civ3 manual (Steam manual.pdf, Civ3 Complete edition, text extract, printed p.~161, "Negotiations"). Quote: "You make first contact whenever one of your units crosses paths with one of theirs." Also p.~63 ("Making contact"): "You still generally make first contact with your opponents by running across their units". The exact geometry (adjacent tile or line of sight) is not stated. GordonJ (CFC, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/first-post-why-cant-i-see-all-the-civs.120770, C3C, Jun 2005) says he had to get "within a space of their units to actually start negotiating" (one player's account).
  • Contact is permanent. Same manual page. Quote: "After communications are set up, they're never lost."
  • All civs start at peace. Manual p.~154. Quote: "You are naturally at peace with all your rivals at the start of the game". Also p.~158: peace is "the natural state when you first meet a rival".
  • First contact gives an automatic peace treaty. Being at peace and having a peace treaty are the same state. Manual tutorial (printed p.~51-52), meeting the Germans: "Unless we declare war against the Germans, we'll come out of this encounter with an automatic peace treaty with them". Manual p.~163: the "Peace treaty" trade item "is only available if you are at war". Flintlock, reading the stock war-plotting code (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/all-about-ai-aggression-levels-and-war-plotting.703785/ #1): "the only way the peace treaty check wouldn't pass is if the target is already at war with every other player in the game". So the code's peace-treaty bit (C3X Civ3Conquests.h, Relation_Treaties[32], "& 1 // 1 = peace treaty") is set for every pair that has met and isn't at war. Whether breaking it by declaring war on a newly met civ costs reputation, rather than only attitude, is still disputed (see Disputed).
  • Units in your territory invite contact. Manual p.~162 lists, among ways to open talks: "Move one of your units into the other nation's territory and wait for them to contact you (not recommended unless your intention is to annoy your rival)."
  • At war, the leader may refuse to meet. Manual p.~162. Quote: "If you are at war with the leader you attempt to contact, he or she might refuse to meet with you". script.txt NODIPLOMACY: "The $CIVNOUN0 refused to acknowledge our envoy!"

6.2 Embassies

  • Writing enables embassies. An embassy is built in the rival's capital and costs gold. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Embassies. Quotes: "When a civilization learns $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>, it can begin to build its intelligence network. The first step is to build [embassies] in rival capital cities." and "Embassies cost gold from your treasury to build, and diplomatic missions cost gold to execute." In conquests.biq, Writing has the flag "Enables Diplomats".
  • You can make agreements when either side has an embassy. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements. Quote: "When your civilization builds an $LINK<embassy=GCON_Embassies> in a rival's capital city, or when a rival builds one in your capital, you can negotiate [Diplomatic Agreements]".
  • An embassy shows the other civ's wars and peace. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/). Quote: "you can see what civilizations they are at war/peace with on the F4 Foreign Advisor screen."
  • Establishing an embassy always succeeds and costs gold by formula (see 6.11). The conquests.biq ESPN base cost is 20. Source: CivFanatics, "A Study of Espionage Missions" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/a-study-of-espionage-missions/). The study says Build an Embassy and Investigate City always succeed. A local conquests.biq dump gives "Build an Embassy | diplomat=True base=20".
  • War closes the embassy automatically. Source: Civ3.com FAQ, quoted on Apolyton (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/41397-espionage-you-can-t-steal-techs-from-civs-you-are-at-war-with). Quote: "your embassy in their capitol is automatically closed".
  • An embassy closed by war reopens at peace. Manual p.~157. Quote: "It is only necessary to establish an embassy once with any particular civilization. Even if you manage to get it closed down (through war with that civilization), it reopens when peace is declared." The same page says the embassy menu lists "the civilizations you have contacted, are not at war with, and do not already have an embassy with." So you cannot open a new embassy with a civ you are at war with. Vanilla README.txt (Fixes v1.16f): "Incorrect popup concerning placing new embassies with civilizations you're at war with no longer displays."
  • The first investigation of the capital is free. Manual p.~157. Quote: "When you first establish the embassy, you can investigate the enemy's capital without risk or penalty."
  • What an embassy reveals. Manual p.~155. Quotes: "With an embassy, you can learn about your opponent's diplomatic connections with others" and "The Military Advisor gets a complete list of their forces from an embassy." A planted spy also gives unit locations (p.~168): "the Spy gives him their locations as well."
  • An embassy improves attitude by 2. See 6.7 (former Disputed item).
  • Wonder pop-ups need an embassy or a spy. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: ""beginning wonder" pop-ups now only appear if you have an embassy or spy with the corresponding civ." Also: "Spaceship parts pop-ups now only appear if you have a spy with the corresponding civ."

6.3 Diplomatic agreements: kinds and the techs that enable them

  • There are four agreement kinds. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements. Quotes:

    • Military Alliance: "You agree to declare war on a despised third party."
    • Right of Passage: "You agree to allow his or her military units to move through your $LINK<territory=GCON_Territory> and use your transportation network (roads and railroads)."
    • Trade Embargo: "An agreement that neither of you will $LINK<trade=GCON_Trade> with or [allow] trade with a third party."
    • Mutual Protection Pact: "An agreement to declare war upon any civilization that attacks your partner."

  • Tech gates in Conquests (conquests.biq TECH flags, local dump):

    • Writing: Diplomats, Military Alliance.
    • Map Making: Right of Passage.
    • Printing Press: Communications.
    • Navigation: Map Trading.
    • Nationalism: Mutual Protection Pact, Trade Embargo.

    The Conquests Civilopedia agrees. TECH_Writing: "Can now negotiate military alliance." TECH_Map_Making: "Can now negotiate right of passage". TECH_Navigation: "Can now trade $LINK<world maps=GCON_Maps> in diplomacy." TECH_Nationalism: "Can now negotiate mutual protection pact and trade embargo". CivFanatics FAQ, quote: "you cannot trade World/Territory Maps until you research Navigation."

  • Vanilla differs (see Disputed). In vanilla, Writing also gives Right of Passage and Map Making gives map trading. Source: vanilla Text/Civilopedia.txt. TECH_Writing: "Can now negotiate military alliance and right of passage". TECH_Map_Making: "Can now trade $LINK<world maps=GCON_Maps> in diplomacy."

  • An MPP needs Nationalism on only one side. Manual p.~160. Quote: "If a peace treaty and embassy are in place between two civilizations and either has discovered Nationalism, they can negotiate a mutual protection pact." cort (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33796-1.htm, Nov 2001) says the same: an MPP needs "only one civ to know Nationalism". (Former UNVERIFIED item.)

  • Every agreement needs an embassy. Manual pp.~159-160:

    • Military Alliance: "Once you have an embassy with a friendly nation, you can sign a military alliance against a common enemy."
    • Right of Passage: "You can sign a right of passage if you have an embassy with a friendly nation."
    • Trade Embargo: "If you have discovered Nationalism and have an embassy, you can arrange a trade embargo with an ally."
    • MPP: as quoted above (peace treaty plus embassy).

    Manual p.~63: "An embassy also opens the possibility of diplomatic agreements beyond a simple peace treaty." Also Konquest02 (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/86477-1.htm, Oct 2003) lists the options that need an embassy: "Right of Passage, Mutual Protection Pact and alliances against other civs." No source names a road or sea link as a condition for agreements; that link is a condition for resource trade only (6.4).

  • Owner ruling (engine): Right of Passage needs Writing plus an embassy. Sources: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Embassies, "When a civilization learns $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>, ... The first step is to build [embassies] in rival capital cities. That done, you can sign Right of Passage $LINK<agreements=GCON_Agreements> and Military Alliances". Vanilla Civilopedia TECH_Writing: "Can now negotiate military alliance and right of passage". Aeson (Apolyton, "1.17f bug: right of passage", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/46862-1.htm, Apr 2002): "Writing (and establishing an embassy) is what makes ROP agreements possible." The C3C BIQ flag puts ROP on Map Making instead (see Disputed).

  • Agreement terms are 20 turns. Manual pp.~159-160, for each of MA, ROP, embargo and MPP. For example: "A right of passage agreement lasts for 20 turns. At the end of that period, either party can cancel the agreement without consequences." and "The pact lasts for 20 turns." To leave without a reputation mark, wait for expiry: "To cancel a pact without consequences, you must wait for it to expire."

  • Leaving an MA or MPP does not end the war. Manual p.~159. Quote: "Leaving the military alliance won't cancel the state of war with the third party, of course."

  • Peace with the common enemy destroys an alliance. Manual p.~159. Quote: "if either party to the alliance makes peace with the third party, it effectively destroys the alliance." Breaking an alliance "is remembered as a major transgression by all of the other civilizations". Making peace with a civ that is still invading an MPP partner also costs reputation (p.~160): "If you make peace with a civilization while it is still invading your ally, your reputation suffers for quite some time."

  • PTW 1.27: peace ends your alliances against that enemy. Falconius (Apolyton, "1.27 Fixes Military Alliances!", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/97851-1.htm, Sep 2003): "when you make peace, your alliances against your former foe simply end." He says the earlier behaviour that "caused you to get sucked back into a war you just ended is now no more". Arrian, in the same thread, thinks the old problem was an MPP, not an alliance. Player report, not from a readme.

  • Destroying the common enemy ends an alliance without a reputation hit (players). CFC "How to form a strategic alliance", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-to-form-a-strategic-alliance.154837/ (Jan 2006): SimpleMonkey, you "can end an alliance by killing off your mutual enemy" without a rep hit. TimBentley first said the opposite, then: "I haven't seen a way to get a rep hit in this manner." JMK disagreed ("it will be considered as breaking agreement"). Not tested.

  • An MPP triggers on an attack on the partner. script.txt MUTUALPROTECTIONOURWAR: "$CIVNAME0 has attacked our "brothers" in $CIVNAME1. Our $LINK<mutual protection pact=GCON_Agreements> requires us to declare war against $CIVNAME0." Whether the declaration alone or the first attack is the trigger is unclear. Loopy (CFC, Civ3 vanilla, Nov 2001, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/mutual-proctection-do-you-sign-on-or-not.8546/): "I don't think declaring war triggers the MPP, but the first attack" (he hedges). WonderPup, same thread: MPP partners "will declare war with your enemy on the next turn."

  • Peace is made separately with each enemy. Manual p.~161. Quote: "You must make peace separately with each opponent (even those allied with a civilization with whom you have already negotiated peace)."

  • An embargo is not a war. Manual p.~160. Quote: "A trade embargo is not a declaration of war, though it's sometimes enough of an affront to inspire one."

  • The AI won't embargo a civ it trades resources with (tested). Spoonwood (CFC, "Use The Trade Tab as a Guide to Military Alliances", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/use-the-trade-tab-as-a-guide-to-military-alliances.692937/, C3C, Nov 2024, save tests): "Trade Embargoes don't seem to work when you and an AI have peace with a nation if trading a resource or luxury at least." Greece refused even for 1000 gpt: "They would never accept this deal." Persia accepted the embargo after first signing a military alliance against the same civ.

  • AI alliance evaluation uses distance (code-level). C3X-INI, "DIVISION BY ZERO IN AI ALLIANCE EVAL": "When considering whether to agree to a military alliance, the AI computes the distance from its capital to the nearest city belonging to the target of the alliance and divides by that distance." The rest of the formula is not given. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16832498, page-140 #2794): "The crash is caused by a division by zero in the AI's logic to consider whether to sign a military alliance." He adds (post-16833692, page-141 #2810) that the alliance's value to the other side likewise "depends on the distance from your capital to the nearest city belonging to the target of the alliance". So a nearer target is worth more. The crash (target with a city at distance 0) is a bug.

  • Locked alliances are scenario-only, so out of scope. Members have a permanent MPP. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Locked_Alliances. Quote: "A pre-designed scenario (only) might begin with some (or all) civilizations grouped into several "Locked Alliances"."

6.4 Trade routes, resources and embargoes

  • Resource trade needs connected capitals. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Trade. Quote: "Excess resources may be traded to other civilizations if the capital cities of both civilizations are connected."
  • Connection rules. Source: same entry. Quote: "there is a road or railroad running between them, there are harbors in both cities and a visible water route between them, or both cities contain an airport" (paraphrased list; the original is three bullet lines). A road through enemy territory or a blockaded harbor does not count. Quote: "two cities are not connected if the only road/rail that connects them passes through enemy territory, or if a harbor is blockaded".
  • Water routes need techs for every tile type on the route. Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Trade: "In order to use a water trade route, your civilization must be able to safely traverse every square of the route and this depends upon having $LINK<Map Making=TECH_Map_Making>, $LINK<Navigation=TECH_Navigation>, and/or $LINK<Magnetism=TECH_Magnetism>." The terrain entries name Astronomy for Sea instead: TERR_Sea, "[Sea] squares are significant barriers to $LINK<trade=GCON_Trade> and $LINK<travel=PRTO_Galley> until the discovery of $LINK<astronomy=TECH_Astronomy>." and TERR_Ocean, the same "until the discovery of $LINK<Navigation=TECH_Navigation> or $LINK<Magnetism=TECH_Magnetism>." Players (CFC "Pangea Maps and Trading", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/pangea-maps-and-trading.689998/, C3C, Jun 2024, untested): tjs282, "Only one partner needs Astro to make Resource-trade deals"; "Barb-Galleys can cut Coastal trade-routes if they happen to stop on an inconvenient tile."; Spoonwood and justanick say the Great Lighthouse also opens sea routes. The rule file sides with the terrain entries: conquests.biq TECH flags (QueryCiv3 dump, this pass) give "Enables Trade Over Sea" to Astronomy only and "Enables Trade Over Ocean" to Navigation and Magnetism. No tech carries a Coast flag, so coast routes need none. Code (third round): Flintlock's Trade Net X (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/Trade%20Net%20X/TradeNetX.cpp), a speed re-implementation of the stock 1.22 trade network, sets a civ's sea trade bit if it Leader_has_tech_with_flag (..., ATF_Enables_Trade_Over_Sea_Tiles) or owns a wonder with ITW_Safe_Sea_Travel (the Great Lighthouse's flag), and its ocean bit from ATF_Enables_Trade_Over_Ocean_Tiles only. Coast tiles always pass, land tiles only if they hold a city, and every tile on the route must have been explored by that civ. A water tile in the territory of a civ at war with you, or holding one of its combat units, breaks the route. So Spoonwood and justanick are right that the Great Lighthouse opens sea routes. The Map Making in GCON_Trade matches no flag (Map Making carries only Right of Passage). The same dump gives Communication (contact) trading to Printing Press, Map trading to Navigation, MPP and Embargo to Nationalism, Military Alliance and Diplomats to Writing.
  • An embargo blocks resource and luxury trade. Source: Conquests script.txt DIPLOADVICETRADE_THEIREMBARGO. Quote: "We cannot trade resources or luxuries with the $CIVNOUN0 -- they have a trade embargo against us." Also DIPLOADVICETRADE_OUREMBARGO: "We have agreed not to trade with the $CIVNOUN0 -- we have a trade embargo against them."
  • The embargoed civ is told when an embargo starts and ends. Source: script.txt TRADEEMBARGO / TRADEEMBARGOENDS. Quotes: "$CIVNAME0 and $CIVNAME1 have signed a trade embargo against us." and "The trade embargo against us between $CIVNAME0 and $CIVNAME1 has ended."
  • Resource and luxury deals last 20 turns or until war. Manual p.~164. Quote: "Like all trade agreements, a luxuries deal lasts for 20 turns or until interrupted by war between the parties to the trade." The traded good reaches every city connected to the receiver's capital.
  • What can be traded. Manual pp.~163-165 lists peace treaty, diplomatic agreements, embargoes, communications, maps (World Map and Territory Map), luxuries, strategic resources, gold (lump sum or per turn), technology, cities and Workers. Items the other side has but cannot offer "are grayed out", and items the other side does not need do not appear.
  • Only Workers in the capital can be traded, and they keep their nationality. Manual p.~165. Quote: "Any Worker currently in your capital city can be offered for trade. The same goes for workers in your rival's capital. A traded Worker retains its nationality, just like a captured one." Confirmed by players (Apolyton, "How do you trade workers?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/59851-1.htm, Aug 2002). alexman (Firaxis), same thread, on foreign workers showing your nationality on the trade screen: "It's a bug."
  • Technology needs a tradeable prerequisite. Manual p.~165. Quote: "Any civilization advance that one nation has discovered or acquired but the other hasn't and can research is a potential item of trade."
  • Per-turn gold runs for 20 turns; only war stops it. Manual p.~165. Quote: "it commits a leader to pay the specified amount every turn for the next 20 turns. Only the outbreak of war between the trading parties interrupts the required payments."
  • Cities: the AI never trades them in ordinary diplomacy; it can cede them for peace (code reading plus players). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16760037, page-131 #2614): "the reason the devs restricted that to begin with is the AI wasn't able to properly value cities". Kokuryutei (post-16322889, page-47 #938): "AI never agrees to transactions with the transfer/exchange of cities." tjs282 (#939, post-16322917): "And a badly damaged AI will often also concede additional towns during peace negotiations."; (post-16781598, page-133 #2646): "You can't "buy" towns (i.e. offer something in exchange), you can only demand them outright." forestar (post-16990905, page-191 #3807): city trading is "normally impossible during regular diplomacy, but it becomes allowed during dealing with peace treaties." Units: Flintlock (post-16291863, page-39 #771): "very limited unit trading of only workers in the capital", as the manual says. The trade lists stop at 512 cities and 8192 units, a bug C3X fixes (post-16883917, page-158 #3141).
  • Cities can be traded, but the AI rarely does so. Manual p.~165. Quote: "under normal circumstances a leader would rather go to war than trade one away. The option to trade cities exists, however". The CivFanatics FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/) lists "Cities (as part of a peace deal only)" (see Disputed).
  • At war, only a peace treaty can be put on the table first. Manual p.~163. Quote: "This option is only available if you are at war with the rival you're dealing with. In fact, it's the only diplomatic agreement that appears during a war". ixnay (Apolyton, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/57646-how-do-i-get-more-than-peace-treaty-for-ending-war, Mar 2002): "once you put that on the table you have access to all the other options". Zachriel, same thread: "Sometimes, they will give you all their cities for peace."
  • Strategic and luxury goods show grey without a route. Spoonwood (CFC 692937, C3C, 2024): "All strategic resources or luxuries that an AI has will appear in greyed out text" when there is no trade route to your capital.
  • The AI rarely trades its only copy of a luxury. Source: CivFanatics, "Civs and their luxuries", simwiz2 (Civ3 General Discussions) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civs-and-their-luxuries.15068/). Quote: "An AI will almost NEVER trade a luxury that it only has one of." The "never trades its only resource" rule found elsewhere is a Civ4 rule (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-about-trading-resources.368926), so it is not used here.

6.5 Deal durations, renegotiation and peace treaties

  • Ongoing deals run for 20 turns. Source: Conquests labels.txt. Quotes: "This deal may be canceled peacefully after 20 turns." and "This deal will last for 20 turns."
  • "Always Renegotiate Deals" (1.29f) forces renegotiation every 20 turns. Peace treaties are exempt. Source: vanilla README.txt, Additions v1.29f. Quote: "Added "Always Renegotiate Deals" prefs which forces a renegotiation of every non-peace treaty deal every 20 turns."
  • The AI asks to end or renew at expiry. Source: Conquests diplomacy.txt AIRENEGOTIATE. Quote: "Friend $PLAYER0, this pact is about to expire. We believe that its usefulness is over." AIRENEGOTIATEPEACE: "$PLAYER0, our peace treaty is about to expire. For the sake of world peace, we will extend it if you desire."
  • A peace treaty lasts until someone declares war. It has a 20-turn term, and breaking it inside that term costs reputation. Source: CivFanatics, "question about peace treaties" (C3C 1.15) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-about-peace-treaties.84591). marioh, quote: "If you sign a peace-treaty with a civ it will last until you or the other declares war." Taé Shala, quote: "there will be a reputation penalty if you break the treaty within the remaining turns."
  • A bare treaty renews itself; one with attachments does not. Source: same thread (marioh), as summarised in search. Quote: "If you don't have GPT/ROP/MPP/luxuries/resources attached with the peace treaty, then it will automatically renew until war is re-declared by either party."
  • Mobilization lasts until peace. Source: Conquests Civilopedia (Mobilization). Quote: "May not return to Normalcy until a Peace Treaty is signed."
  • There is no ceasefire in Civ3: relations are peace or war. Manual p.~158 ("The Diplomatic States"). Quote: "the relationship between two nations can be expressed as one of two different states: peace or war." None of the vanilla, PTW or C3C diplomacy.txt, labels.txt or script.txt files has a ceasefire string; the only "cease" lines are flavour text in peace replies (C3C diplomacy.txt line ~1554: "We will agree to cease fighting if only to delay the inevitable."). The "16 turns" figure is from the Civ2 strategy guide (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-long-does-a-cease-fire-last.55033, "a cease-fire lasts only 16 turns"), and the CFC thread "How do you get ceasefires?" (173866) is in the Civ4 forum. (Former open question 2.)
  • A peace treaty forbids military units in the partner's territory. Manual p.~158. Quote: "You and your rival agree not to attack each other or even enter the other's territory with military units." And: "Units that violate this agreement may be asked to leave--and their failure to do so immediately can be considered a treaty violation." A peace treaty also makes trade possible: "A peace treaty, when combined with an embassy, also opens up negotiations to several other agreements and, just as importantly, makes trade with the other civilization possible."
  • Peace cannot be renegotiated while its 20 turns run. Vol (CFC, "Trading Reputation" thread, page 2, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/trading-reputation.113376/page-2, Sep 2005): "you cannot renegotiate peace (it is grayed out) when the deal still has turns left on it." script.txt CONFIRMRENEGOTIATEPEACE: "Are you sure we should renegotiate peace?"
  • 1.29f closed a zero-turn-war loophole. Vanilla README.txt, Fixes v1.29f: "Fixed diplowin loophole involving renegotiating peace which allowed 0 turn wars."
  • When the AI will talk peace (Firaxis). Soren Johnson (Apolyton, "When does the AI ask for peace (Soren?)", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/41866-1.htm, post #27, Feb 11 2002, vanilla): "The AI's willingness to discuss peace terms depends on" "how long the war has lasted" and "how much it is winning or losing the war." Also: "the length of time factor is there to prevent people from war-jacking the AI". The minimum length is not given.
  • The AI accepts peace under threat only if it is losing. diplomacy.txt AIPEACETREATYTHREAT (reply to "Our troops approach your cities"): "Fool! Do you think we are frightened of your puny soldiers? Well, as fate would have it, we are. We agree." AINOPEACETREATY: "Not likely! $CIVNAME5 is winning!" Players (Apolyton, "Does 'Our troops approach your cities' ever work?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/71589-1.htm, Dec 2002) report it works only with a real threat. Catt: "it would work for me only in those instances in which I had a large force right outside a city or two." Anecdotal.

6.6 Gold-per-turn deals and reputation

  • What breaks a 20-turn GPT deal. Source: CivFanatics article "Trading Reputation" (https://civfanatics.com/?p=280). Quote: "declare war on that AI (including MA/MPP)", "break an MA or MPP", "the trade route between you and the AI is cut".

  • No reputation hit if the AI ends the deal. Source: same article. Quote: "Of course you get no rep hit if the payee decides to end the deal by itself." Destroying a third party under an MA also ends the deal harmlessly. Quote: "Your rep isn't affected."

  • Reputation is per AI, but news of a betrayal spreads. Source: same article. Quote: "Each AI has a record of your reputation separately." An AI may also treat you as untrustworthy if it knows you broke a deal with another AI it is not at war with. Confirmed by Conquests diplomacy.txt AIREJECTINFORMEDOFBROKENDEAL. Quote: "$CIVNAME5 has informed me that you have broken deals with them in the past."

  • A ruined reputation is effectively permanent for GPT deals. Sources: "Trading Reputation", quote: "once you break a gpt deal you'll never be able to make such deal again". CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ, quote: "will never again for the game accept per-turn payments or deals from you." The article names two workarounds: renegotiating peace, or signing an MA against the betrayed AI ("B will forgive your betrayal to A").

  • An AI war declaration during a deal does not hurt your reputation. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ. Quote: "the AI declares war on you when you have a 20-turn deal going on" (not counted against you).

  • The AI has specific refusal lines for each kind of bad record. Source: Conquests diplomacy.txt.

    • AIREJECTBROKENDEAL: "My people remember too well the deals you have broken with us in the past."
    • AIREJECTPASSAGEVIOLATION: "the last time we had a right of passage treaty with $CIVNAME1, you sort of attacked us, didn't you?"
    • AIREJECTMILITARYRETRACT: "You have failed to fully honor diplomatic agreements with us in the past".

    So the engine tracks at least three kinds of record: broken deals, ROP violations, and alliance/treaty retractions. Each also has an "informed of" version for third parties.

  • The advisor flags liars and betrayers. Source: script.txt DIPLOADVICEFOREIGN_LIAR / _BETRAYAL. Quotes: "$LEADER0 is a [known liar and cheat.]" and "We know that $LEADER0 has [betrayed our friends] the $CIVNOUN1."

  • Sneak attacks. Declaring war with any of your units inside the target's territory counts as breaking an implicit Right of Passage. Source: CivFanatics, "Reputation for sneak attacks" (Civ3, July 2002) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/reputation-for-sneak-attacks.28206). Catt, quote: "Reputation hits come not because of war, but because of declaring war with troops in enemy territory." Lt. 'Killer' M., quote: "having a Scout/worker/any other unit in enemy territory when declaring war is enough to be a ROP-breaker".

  • Whoever declares takes the hit, and a lapsed treaty means war. Source: CivFanatics, "How does the AI calculate reputation?" (Civ3, Jun 2002), Bamspeedy (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-ai-calculate-reputation.26122). Quotes: "The rep hit goes to whoever actually does the declaration of war." and "No peace treaty = war."

  • Razing ruins reputation. Source: "The Road to Diplomatic Victory" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/the-road-to-diplomatic-victory/). Quote: razing an AI city "will destroy your reputation." Also: "if you trash your reputation no amount of gifts and bribery can fix it."

  • Failed espionage hurts reputation. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage. Quote: "Remember though, failed missions can seriously damage your reputation."

  • The manual's definition of reputation. Manual p.~156. Quotes: "Your reputation is based not on how peaceful or how warlike you are toward your neighbors, but on how often you keep your word." Razing and ROP abuse also count: "Savagely razing the city of an enemy or using a right of passage agreement to set up Cannons to bombard your opponent's cities are acts likely to be deplored throughout the known world." And: "Espionage, whether successful or not, can also damage your standing."

  • ROP backstabs ruin trust with everyone. Manual p.~159. Quote: "Using a right of passage to infiltrate your troops for a surprise attack is remembered as a cold-hearted breach of trust by all of the other civilizations."

  • Which deals are "rep-risky" (tested, C3C). Only deals where you give per-turn gold or resources. AlanH (CFC 113376 page 3, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/trading-reputation.113376/page-3, Nov 2009): "You only get a rep hit if you are paying per-turn gold or resources to the AI and that deal is ended prematurely." Optional, same page: "if you're not giving anything from your side, you can safely declare on that AI without any fear for your reputation." tmn (2017), same page, disagrees for agreements: "Peace treaty, Right of Passage and Trade Embargo are all rep-risky." See Disputed for peace treaties.

  • How a resource deal's composition matters (tested, PTW). Bamspeedy (CFC "AI attitude", page 6, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-attitude.44999/page-6, Jul 29 2003): when you give a resource and its route breaks, getting back a resource or GPT costs only "+1" attitude and trading stays possible; getting back only techs, a map or a lump sum costs "+4" and blocks later per-turn deals. Quote: "It doesn't matter who's fault it is." Page 7 (Jul 30 2003): "You can accept lump sum, techs, or maps IF they are also paying you gpt (or giving you a resource) in that deal."

  • A broken reputation still allows bad lump-sum-for-GPT deals. Vol (CFC 113376 page 2, 2005): "Prior to broken reputation, I could get 16 gold for 1 gpt." Afterwards "I could get 3 gold for 1 gpt". Arrian (Apolyton, "Reputation: What's the point?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/53506-1.htm, Jun 2002): "A bad rep will prevent the AI from selling you stuff in exchange for gold per turn" and you "will be unable to get RoP agreements."

  • Reputation can be recovered with civs cut off from the victim. Vol (CFC 113376 page 2, Jul 31 2005): "Your reputation is restored with civilizations that can no longer contact the civilization you offended." And (Aug 1 2005): "if that civ is ever eliminated, everyone will go back to treating you with broken reputation." Destroying the victim before it meets anyone else does work. vmxa (page 3, Dec 2010): "if civ A is eliminated, before civ A mets otherss, no one will know of you rep issue." Bamspeedy (CFC 44999 page 2, PTW): "If you kill the Aztecs before he meets anyone else, you get no attitude or reputation hits." These are player tests in C3C (Vol's save was checked in 1.22 by nullspace).

  • Slow recovery by keeping your word (manual). Manual p.~156. Quote: "Over long periods of time, if you mend your ways by keeping your word to other rulers, the black marks on your reputation can be partially erased and your honor somewhat redeemed." Alliance breaks are "only very slowly erased by time" (p.~159). This conflicts with the "never again" community findings (see Disputed).

  • Declaring war from outside their territory is safe. Whomp (CFC 113376 page 2, Mar 2005): "since you were out of AI territory when you declared you would not get the rep hit." The CFC FAQ counts ships: "declared war on them when you had units in their territory (this includes ships)".

  • Cancel a deal before declaring war. Tone (CFC staff, "Cancel right of passage?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/cancel-right-of-passage.228499/, C3C, Jun 2007): "you cannot cancel any deal in the first 20 turns via this screen." After 20 turns, cancel through the Active button first; cancelling by declaring war means your reputation "will be trashed".

  • Firaxis view: per-turn deals, not sneak attacks, set reputation. alexman (later a Firaxis engineer; Apolyton, "He had a nasty reputation as a cruel dude...", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/64554-1.htm, Oct 2002, his own play experience): "as long as you don't break any per-turn deals, your reputation doesn't take a hit." and "I think sneak attacking must hurt their attitude towards you, not your reputation." Opinion.

6.7 AI attitude: levels and modifiers

Primary source: CivFanatics "AI Attitude Study" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/ai-attitude-study/), tested on PTW 1.01. Quote: "This test was with the 1.01 version of PTW". In this study a negative number is good.

  • Bands. Quotes: "-11 and lower = Gracious", "-1 through -10 = Polite", "0 = Cautious", "1-10 = Annoyed", "11 through 100 = Furious".
  • Starting value. The baseline is 0. Sharing a culture group gives -1. Aggression level adds a difficulty-dependent amount. For example, on Regent-Monarch AL5 gives +3 and AL1 gives -1, and on Emperor-Deity AL5 gives +3 and AL1 gives 0 (study, summarised). Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Culture agrees on culture groups. Quote: "nations of the same global culture tend to start near each other and are more tolerant in diplomacy." C3X-THREAD players say the same: tjs282 (post-16154963, page-21 #405, "AFAIK"): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "same-Culture Civs are also coded to be more friendly to one another initially"; Virote_Considon (post-16428036, page-69 #1369): "Civs have slightly less initial hostility to other civs in their same culture".
  • Government. Same government: -1 (study, summarised). Quotes: "-5 Favorite government (only applies if both you and the AI are in this government)" and "+4 Shunned government (only applies if you are in this government and the AI is not)".
  • Embassy: -2 (good). Study, quotes: "-2 If you have an embassy with the AI" and, in its worked example, "You have an embassy with that civ (-2)". The forum version gives the same line (Bamspeedy, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-attitude.44999/, page 1; requoted by WillJ on page 2 and by Jarek Noschese on page 8). No "+2 embassy" line was found on a re-read of the study page. (Former Disputed item.)
  • Agreements. Quotes: "-5 If you have a Right of Passage with the AI." and "-10 If you have a Mutual Protection Pact with the AI." A common enemy in war gives -3, an alliance -2, and both together -5 (study, summarised). Damage done to a common enemy gives -1 to -3 per act, and razing an enemy city -7, capped near -15. This decays by 1 per turn.
  • Trade. A recent map or tech trade, or an active 20-turn deal, gives -1. Trading or giving a resource gives -5. Signing an embargo gives -1 with the partner and +10 with the victim (study, summarised).
  • Gifts. About -1 per 10 gold of gifted gold or techs, capped at -10. Quote: "This bonus decays at about 10 gold/turn."
  • Culture lead. -1 if you lead in culture, +1 if the AI leads (study, summarised).
  • Tribute. Quotes: "-1 You pay tribute to the AI.", "+2 You demand tribute from the AI. Whether they actually pay the tribute or not." and "+1 If you call the AI's bluff when he demands tribute".
  • Units in territory. Being asked to leave gives +1. Being told to leave or declare war gives +4. Both are temporary and reverse when you comply (study, summarised).
  • Permanent penalties (study). Quotes: "+4 for breaking a peace treaty with that civ.", "+1 if you raze the city of another AI.", "+12 if you raze a city of that civ.", "Nukes: Victim of the nuke adds +32!", "Other civs will add on +16". Summarised from the study: breaking an alliance +12 (others +2); breaking an ROP +4, or +6 with units inside; selling a worker +1; accepting a culture flip +1 and refusing one -1.
  • Failed espionage. Quotes: "+4 for failing at an espionage mission (any mission or attempting to plant a spy)." (temporary) and "+1 for failing at an espionage mission" (permanent).
  • Power lead halves the good effects. Quote: "Power lead: If you have a power lead, most of the good effects (negative numbers) are halved." The study says ROP is the exception.
  • Things with no measured effect (study, summarised): owning resources the AI lacks, sharing a landmass, and culture pressure on border cities.
  • Follow-up findings from the forum thread (Bamspeedy and SirPleb, CFC "AI attitude", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-attitude.44999/, 8 pages, Feb 2003-2005; PTW 1.0x-1.21 unless noted). SirPleb (page 4, Feb 28 2003) thinks "most (maybe even all) of Bamspeedy's discoveries apply exactly the same way in 1.29."
    • War declarations (page 5, Mar 2 2003): the target adds +4 for each war you declare. Neutral civs add +1 for the first and +2 for the second; "there is a cap of +2 that nuetral civs will add on."
    • Alliance against a civ (SirPleb, page 5, Mar 2003): "+10 attitude penalty from the target Civ when you enter an alliance against it", removed when the alliance ends.
    • Starving citizens (SirPleb, page 5): "+1 for each citizen starved", at war or not.
    • War damage recovery is slow and non-linear (SirPleb, page 5): 16 kills gave +17 and were gone after 18 turns of peace. Heavier damage (21 kills, 2 cities, 6 starved) took about 95 turns. +45 had not recovered after 100+ turns.
    • Privateers (page 4, Mar 2 2003): "+1 when you attack their ship (but your privateer dies), and +3 if you do sink their ship." Temporary. alexman (Firaxis), replying to the Apolyton repost of the study ("AI attitude revealed", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/79615-1.htm, 6 Mar 2003): the privateer penalty is meant to show that other nations suspect you paid for the attacks, and "You get a rep hit when you break deals. The use of privateers does not cause war, so it doesn't break deals." (So privateers cost attitude, not reputation.)
    • Disbanding or abandoning a foreign city (page 6, PTW 1.21, Jun 2003): building a worker or settler to disband a city with 50%+ foreigners "is the same as if you razed it." Also: "Donations of cities are treated as a gift".
    • Culture flips (page 5): refusing gives -1, and "This is cumulitive."
    • No penalty for refusing AI offers (page 5, Mar 8 2003): "No attitude adjustments for not accepting an offer they make." The recent-trade bonus lapses: "the 'recent trade' bonus would disappear if you haven't traded with a civ lately."
    • Gifts (pages 3, 4, 6, 7). The cap is -10 when the AI has the power lead and -5 when you have it (page 3). Several gifts on one turn add up. Over 100 gold extends the -10 before decay starts (page 6, Apr 2003): "A 100 gold donation gets them to the max of -10, but then this decreases at -1/turn (or 10 gold/turn)." A 5 gpt deal counted only -9 (page 4). Page 4 (Mar 2003) says "-1 is permanent if you make a donation of 100 gold or higher", but page 7 (Oct 2003) calls the War Academy's "permanent" wording outdated. Treat the permanent -1 as unsettled.
    • Espionage (page 2): of the +4 for a failed mission, "+3 seems to be temporary, +1 is permanent." He recovered the temporary part after "about 20 turns".
    • Nukes (page 3): the victim's +32 and others' +16 were "permanent (for at least the 30-50+ turns that I pushed 'end turn')". Civs that never met the victim also add +16.
    • Asking units to leave lowers attitude each time. Spoonwood (CFC "Trespass Doctrine", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/trespass-doctrine.690905/, C3C, Jul 2024): "Everytime you tell an AI to get out your territory, that decreases their attitude towards you."
  • Attitude starts from the aggression level (code-level). Flintlock (CFC, "All About AI Aggression Levels and War Plotting", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/all-about-ai-aggression-levels-and-war-plotting.703785/, C3C 1.22, Jun 2026, reading the executable): "the game begins with the AI's aggression level calculated as above then adds the other factors on top". The effective level is: "level = civ.AggressionLevel + GameDifficulty // 2 - DifficultyLevelCount // 6" and "return max(-2, min(level, 2))", with "GameDifficulty = 0 for Chieftain, 1 for Warlord, and so forth". His post #8 adds that the game-wide "most" or "least" aggressive setting "overrides all the per-civ aggression settings". He also gives the shunned-government term as 5 points ("it adds 5 points"), where the study measured +4 (see Disputed).
  • What the attitude code remembers (code-level). C3X-SRC (Civ3Conquests.h, https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/Civ3Conquests.h, read 2026-10-20) maps the per-civ-pair memory that the stock attitude function reads: struct Reputation has the counters cancelled_deal, caught_spy, tribute, observed_military_aggression, diplomatic_threats, gift, refused_tribute_demands, icbm, icbm_other and war_damage_memory ("Combat damage taken by 'us' (the Leader object) from this player ... Decays over time."). The C3X demand code comments that the tribute, gift and refused-demand counters "feed into the base game's attitude calculation". This matches the study's list of effects (tribute, gifts, espionage, nukes, war damage, broken deals). The weights are not in the header.
  • Sign of the engine's attitude score (code-level). In the executable a higher attitude score is worse, the same as the study's convention. C3X adds a relation penalty for its (modded) foreign-territory colonies with score += penalty on top of the stock Leader_get_attitude_toward (injected_code.c), and its changelog records fixing a version where the penalty was "improving instead of harming relations".
  • BIQ personality fields (conquests.biq RACE, local dump). Each civ has AggressionLevel (stored -2..2, shown as 1..5 in the editor), FavoriteGovernment, ShunnedGovernment and CultureGroup. It also has the AI build flags "Never"/"Often" for offensive/defensive/artillery land units, settlers, workers, naval, air, growth, production, happiness, science, wealth, trade, explore and culture, plus NoWonders/NoSmallWonders and the Manage/Emphasize governor flags. Examples from the dump: "Germany aggr=2 fav=4 shun=3", "France aggr=-2 fav=4 shun=6", "Mongols aggr=2 fav=1 shun=5". Government indices: 0 Anarchy, 1 Despotism, 2 Monarchy, 3 Communism, 4 Republic, 5 Democracy, 6 Fascism, 7 Feudalism.
  • Barbarians are a RACE entry with no personality. Dump: "A Barbarian Chiefdom aggr=0 fav=-1 shun=-1 culture=-1". There is no diplomacy with barbarians: the Civilopedia GCON_Barbarians describes only villages and raiders, and no diplomacy text targets them. UNVERIFIED as an explicit rule.

6.8 AI trade valuation

  • Where the AI's markup is applied is not found in the code. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16275461, page-36 #709): "The function that calculates the AI's offer is long and I wasn't able to see where it tilts the trade in its favor." (post-16892444, page-160 #3198): "I wasn't able to find the point where the AI decides how much to try to cheat the player." So TheNiceOne's table below has no code confirmation. Tech values from the AI's one tech-valuation function are "roughly equivalent to the amount of gold the AI would ask" (post-16719849, page-123 #2448; section 4 §4.7).

  • The AI-human trade markup is hard-coded. It depends on difficulty, the AI's aggression level and attitude. The editor's AI-AI trade rate does not apply to it. Source: TheNiceOne, "AI-human trade revisited" (1.29, Jan 19 2003) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-human-trade-revisited.42110; mirror https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/86979-ai-human-trade-revisited). The summarised table:

    Difficulty Markup by AL1..AL5
    Chieftain 0% at every AL
    Warlord 0, 0, 1, 2, 3%
    Regent 2% from AL2, +2% per AL (8% at AL5)
    Monarch 3% from AL2, +3% per AL (12% at AL5)
    Emperor 4% from AL1, +4% per AL, capped at 16%
    Deity 5% from AL1, +5% per AL, capped at 20%

    Buying: price = base x (1 + bonus). Selling: price = base / (1 + bonus). Quote: "the AI-human trade bonus may be reduced by 1 or 2 %" (at Polite or Gracious).

  • Tech price base. The human's research cost is divided by the AI cost factor on Monarch and above. Quote: "Monarch 0.9, emperor 0.8, deity 0.6." and "the human player's research cost is divided by the AI's cost factor". These match conquests.biq DIFF CostFactor (local dump: "Monarch costFactor=9", "Emperor costFactor=8", "Deity costFactor=6", "Demigod costFactor=7", "Sid costFactor=4", against Regent's 10).

  • The tech price falls with known civs and with civs that have the tech. Source: same post. Quote: "number of known civs and number of civs that already has the tech". The exact function is UNVERIFIED. The commonly cited "x1.5 to x2 depending on how many civs know it" formula is Civ4 (CvTeamAI::AI_techTradeVal; https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-valuation-of-techs.658224), so it is not used here.

  • One tech-value function for AI-AI and AI-human trades (Firaxis). Soren Johnson (Apolyton, "Yet another AI CHEAT - Technology", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/40548-1.htm, post #25, Jan 23 2002, vanilla): "as more civs acquire each tech, its value goes down, which makes the AI more willing to trade it" and "the same function is used to determine the value of a tech for both AI-to-AI trades and human-to-AI trades". Soren (Apolyton, "AI 'Tech Whoring'", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/48219-1.htm, post #10, Apr 23 2002): "There is an AI function which estimates how much each tech is worth to each player."

  • AI-AI tech deals: full value, half value at worst, only known civs count (Firaxis). Soren Johnson (Apolyton, "v1.17 AI vs AI tech trading", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42905-1.htm, vanilla 1.17f, Feb 2002). Post #2 (20 Feb 2002): the AIs "always try to meet the full value of the tech" and "When necessary, a AI might be willing to sell a tech for down to half-value, but only if the other AI has nothing else to give for it". Also: "the AI's contact each other more often and ... they are now doing per-turn gold offers for techs." Post #16 (21 Feb 2002): "The whole techs-are-cheaper-if-known-by-other-civs rule only applies to known civs. So even if every civ but one know Republic, it is still full value to that last civ if it is still alone." Post #11: a tech the buyer is "close to discovering" is "not worth very much via trade", and "if you are playing at a level above Regent, techs are worth less to the AI than the human because they get research bonuses". Post #21: a buyer one turn from the tech "was only willing to give away very little." So the value inputs he names are the buyer's own research progress, the number of civs known to the buyer that have the tech, and the difficulty. No formula is given.

  • AIs at war do not trade with each other (players). Same thread, page 4: Zachriel (24 Feb 2002): "if the AIs are fighting they don't transfer techs at all." ShuShu (25 Feb 2002): "AIs don't trade with each other when they are at war." This fits the code: the AI-AI negotiation gate is only reached for pairs not at war (C3X-SRC, 6.9).

  • What the AI values in a tech (players, unverified). TimBentley (CFC "how do AIs value techs in market?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-do-ais-value-techs-in-market.126379/, C3C, Aug 2005): "The things that make the AI value techs more include enables government, drafting, or mobilization." He adds "a formula to determine the trade value has not been computed." Bamspeedy (CFC https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/trading-mainly-for-beginners-but-others-may-find-it-useful.24553/, 1.29f, Aug 2002): "I believe the AI values the price for techs partially on how much it would cost you to research it." ixnay (Apolyton 48219, 1.21, Apr 2002): "the AI doesn't seem to value dead-end techs that give a wonder which has already been built". SJ Frank (CFC "Trade is difficulty with AI civs", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/trade-is-difficulty-with-ai-civs.115977/, C3C, Apr 2005): "a monopoly tech costs twice as much to buy." Opinion.

  • Competing sellers lower the price. justanick (CFC "What's a good rule of thumb to trade techs with AI?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/whats-a-good-rule-of-thumb-to-trade-techs-with-ai.700894/, C3C, Dec 2025): "If only one civilization has researched a tech, then the trading value is still high." and "The trading value drops a lot if several potential sellers need to compete on the price." He also gives the research-cost discount per known civ that has the tech, e.g. "In case of 8 civs it is 14/14, 13/14, 12/14, 11/14, 10/14, 9/14, 8/14 and 7/14 of the full price" (a research-cost rule, section 4).

  • How the AI prices maps, ROP, luxuries and contacts (1.29f, tested in play). Bamspeedy (CFC 24553, Jun-Aug 2002):

    • "ROP is based entirely on territory size."
    • "The value of your map is based entirely on how many more tiles you have uncovered than the person your trading it to." And: "If you sell your map to any AI civ, once you end that turn, your map is worthless" (the AIs pass it on).
    • "Luxuries are valued on how many happy faces they add to your civ." justanick (CFC "How does the AI price resources and luxuries?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-ai-price-resources-and-luxuries.695776/, C3C, Feb 2025) agrees: "It is not city count, it is happy faces gained." No tested rule was found for strategic resources.
    • Contacts: "As you continue to get contact with more civs the price of contact with other civs diminish just like techs do."
    • Workers: "They usually cost 25 to 27 gold each" (Arrian, Apolyton 59851, vanilla, Aug 2002, early game).

  • Per-turn gold is worth about 18 gold per 1 gpt. Civ3 Strategy Compilation (CFC, May 2002, vanilla, https://www.civfanatics.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/Civ3_Strat_Compilation.pdf): "Per-Turn Gold amounts are valued by the AI at about 18/20 of their total gold value (both ways across the table)." The same 18:1 rate shows up in C3C loans (below). The compilation adds that the AI needs you to hold "enough gold currently available to make at least 1 payment".

  • Owner ruling (engine): the AI never pays gold per turn beyond its surplus. Sources: Bamspeedy (CFC "AI refusing gold per turn?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-refusing-gold-per-turn.26118/, vanilla, Jun 29 2002) says a patch made the AI keep cash for its own research and pay GPT only from excess: "They usually don't have excess cash until they switch out of despositism." Arrian, same thread: when the advisor says the AI "will never accept" a tech-for-GPT offer, it "literally cannot afford it." joncnunn (Apolyton 71589, Dec 2002): the AI cannot sign a GPT deal that puts it into deficit, whatever its treasury (summarised). CFC FAQ: "On lower levels, at least early in the game, the AI usually has no extra gold per turn." Vanilla README.txt (Fixes v1.21f): "Fixed bug in diplo bargaining AI for per-turn gold deals." Soren Johnson (Firaxis, Apolyton 42905, posts #4 and #10, 20 Feb 2002, 1.17f): "They aren't allowed to sign per-turn deals they can't pay." and "the AI will only trade gold per-turn directly from their income to other civs". JohnM2433 (Apolyton 59508, Aug 2002): "the AI won't pay gold/turn that exceeds its income in gold per turn."

  • AI-AI trade rate only covers a shortfall (Firaxis). Soren Johnson (Apolyton 48219, post #4, Apr 23 2002): "When the AI's trade with each other, they always ask for full value for their techs, maps, comms, etc." and "the AI trading rate determines how much the AI will bend if the other AI does not have enough money". WarpStorm, relaying Soren (Apolyton "Can someone explain AI to AI Trade Rate?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/68856-1.htm, Nov 2002): "AI to AI trade rate only applies to AI players, not to the human players." EDHELP ("AI to AI trade rate (AI Bonuses)") words it as a plain multiplier: "which is multiplied by the total value (in gold) of the initiating civ's offerings ... with a rate of 120, an AI civ offering 100 gold to another AI civ for something would actually be worth 120 gold". The two agree on the multiplier and differ on when it applies; follow Soren.

  • The AI-human markup by aggression (code-level). Flintlock (CFC 703785, C3C 1.22) lists "Adjusting the value of trade offers" among the uses of the effective aggression level. This fits TheNiceOne's table by aggression level.

  • Things that do not change the price (TheNiceOne, summarised): relative culture, relative military, culture group, and favourite or shunned government.

  • Attitude does change the asking price. Source: Zwingli, "AI Attitude and Trade" (Civ3 1.29, Jul 18 2003), Persia at Deity buying monopoly Radio (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-attitude-and-trade.59079/). Quotes: "Gracious** - 681 gpt (620 gpt)", "Cautious - 705 gpt (641 gpt)", "Furious (unlimited demands) - 939 (854)", and "For each attitude point drop below Cautious, Persia charged about 0.32% more for the tech". This conflicts with TheNiceOne (see Disputed).

  • AI-AI trade rate is a BIQ difficulty field. Source: conquests.biq DIFF AIToAITradeRate (local dump: Chieftain 110 rising by 10 per level to Deity 170, Sid 200). Vanilla README.txt, quote: "Editor: Exposed AI to AI trade rate."
