  • Exploits removed by patches. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quotes: "You can no longer demand very large amounts of gold in diplomacy.", "Fixed AI exploit with regards to giving cities away in diplomacy." and "The AI no longer trades during the players turn."

  • The advisor grades deals on five levels. Source: script.txt DIPLOADVICETRADE_DEAL_*, which match diplomacy.txt AIWEAKREJECT/AINEUTRALREJECT/AISTRONGREJECT/AITOTALREJECT. Quotes: "This deal will probably be acceptable.", "We're getting close to a deal here.", "I doubt they will accept this proposal.", "They will probably be insulted by this deal.", "They would never accept such a deal."

  • Selling techs for large GPT can drain the AI economy for 20 turns. Source: "Crippling the AI's Research Capability" (https://civfanatics.com/?p=170). Quotes: "There is a limit to how much the AI will pay you" and "cripple their economic and scientific growth for 20 turns".

  • The AI offers loans. Source: diplomacy.txt AILOANOFFER. Quote: "We are always happy to lend you money, $PLAYER0 - at surprisingly high interest rates, that is." You can ask (USERASKFORLOAN: "Grant us a loan of gold?"), and the AI can refuse (AINOLOANOFFER: "Unfortunately, we cannot afford to grant you a loan at this time."). The terms of an AI-to-human loan are UNVERIFIED.

  • Lending to the AI: about 18 gold per 1 gpt (tested, C3C). dominatr (CFC "Loaning Money", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/loaning-money.449848, Dec 2011): "The AI doesn't seem to accept less than 18 gold for every 1 gpt." Example: "loan 350 gold" for "20 gpt, or 400 gold". Spoonwood, same thread: "At minimum aggression, unless I've soiled my reputation, it's always come as 1 gpt for 18 gold." dexters (CFC "Managing the World: The Machiavellian Doctrine", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/managing-the-world-the-machiavellian-doctrine.116771/, C3C, Apr 2005, tested in play): "The interest rate is affected by attitude of the of the AI Civ towards you" and "Relative military strength has no effect on the interest rate".

6.9 Tribute and demands

  • The AI demands tribute and threatens. Source: diplomacy.txt AIDEMANDTRIBUTE. Quote: "Think well, $PLAYER0: if you give us tribute, our friendship will continue. If not, we will have to seriously re-evaluate our relationship." If you refuse ("call the bluff"), the outcome is AIBLUFFISCALLED or war. Quote: "Very well, then. We shall leave you to think about the future".
  • The AI can also give in to your demands. Sources: diplomacy.txt AIGIVETRIBUTE, quote: "We will give you $GET3 as you request. But we do this not out of fear of you and your minions, but out of a love of peace." The user side, USERWILLGIVETRIBUTE: "We will give you what you want - this time."
  • Attitude effects of tribute: see 6.7 (+2 for demanding, +1 for calling a bluff, -1 for paying).
  • The AI demands only from humans, and aggression sets how often (code-level). Flintlock (CFC 703785, C3C 1.22, Jun 2026) lists among the uses of the effective aggression level: "Chance to demand things from a human player (As far as I know AIs won't demand things from each other.)" and "Chance to declare war on someone who's refused a demand or nuked a third party". C3X-INI agrees that stock AIs do not demand from each other: its option "Allows AI players to demand tribute from each other. The AI uses the same demand selection, acceptance, diplomatic penalty, and possible war logic that the base game uses for demands involving a human player."
  • The demand gates, in order (code-level). C3X-SRC injected_code.c (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/injected_code.c, read 2026-10-20), function try_ai_demand_from_other_ai, whose comment says: "This is the same sequence of demand gates used by Leader::impl_begin_turn for demands against human players. demand_items also assembles the offer list in exactly the same way." The mod's function calls the stock functions (Leader_demand_items, Leader_consider_trade, Leader_would_yield_to_demand, Leader_ai_roll_to_declare_after_provocation) and re-creates only the gates between them. Each check must pass, or no demand is made that turn:
    1. Contact with the target, not at war with it, the AI's per-target diplomacy cooldown at 0 (offline games), and the stock "would meet with" check (ai_can_demand_from).
    2. The stock Leader_demand_items finds something to ask for. The item kinds in its enum are map, contact, strategic resource, luxury resource, gold and technology.
    3. Power: if (32 - this->power_rank > 2 * (32 - recipient->power_rank)) the AI does not demand. power_rank is 31 for the strongest civ, so 32 - power_rank is the civ's place in the power ranking (1 = first). The AI demands only from a civ whose place is at least half its own.
    4. rand_int (32 - this->power_rank) != 0 stops it: a one-in-(own place) roll, so the strongest civ always passes and the fourth passes 1 time in 4.
    5. If the AI has taken more war damage from the target than the target has taken from it (war_damage_memory), it does not demand.
    6. Attitude roll: the roll size is minus the attitude score, at least 1, and the roll must come up 0. Since a higher score is worse (6.7), an AI at Cautious or worse always passes, and the friendlier it is the rarer the demand.
    7. Aggression roll: rand_int (2 - aggression) != 0 stops it, with the effective aggression level -2..2 (6.7). Read as code, that is always at +1 or +2, 1 in 2 at 0, 1 in 3 at -1 and 1 in 4 at -2.
    • After a demand the AI sets ai_diplomacy_cooldown for that target to 0x20 (32), and the target's tribute counter rises. If the target gives in, the demander's gift counter rises. If not, refused_tribute_demands rises and the AI may declare war (Leader_ai_roll_to_declare_after_provocation), but not while the two have a mutual protection pact (Relation_Treaties ... & 4).
    • Whether the target gives in is the stock Leader_would_yield_to_demand, fed by the target's valuation of the demanded items (Leader_consider_trade). Its internals are not shown.
    • This is a 2026 mod's reconstruction of stock gates, not Firaxis text. A human target's own "give in" choice is the player's; the gates above decide when the AI asks.
  • Demanding tribute angers the AI (manual). Manual p.~167. Quote: "This is one of the more effective ways of making the other leader dislike you. In fact, demanding tribute is a good way to incite a declaration of war." Also: "If you're in an unassailable position of power, you might want to dispense with politeness and just demand what you want."
  • Players' reports of when the AI gives in. Apolyton, "does 'take this deal or suffer' ever work?" (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/68602-1.htm, Nov 2002, vanilla): lateralis, "if their millitary is way bigger than yours (your advisor says "compared to them, our millitary is weak") don't even think about it"; Alex says the AI accepted when "My military advisor said that my army was bigger than theirs." way2hot2becool (CFC "Reputations", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/reputations.160891/, C3C, 2006): strong units near their capital mean "they will eventually give in". Anecdotal.
  • Exact rule for when the AI gives in is still UNVERIFIED. The only formulas found ("boldness x 10%", "fear", "dominance") are from Civ1, Civ2 or Civ4 threads (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/some-notes-about-diplomacy.678722/ is Civ1; https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/foreign-relations.76336 is Civ2), so they are not used here.

6.10 Units in foreign territory

  • The AI warns, then demands withdrawal or war. Sources: diplomacy.txt AIBORDERWARNING, quote: "it appears that some of your troops have - inadvertently, I'm sure - wandered into our territory, near $CITY3. Kindly remove them." USERBORDERWAR (the human's version of the same threat), quote: "Remove your forces from our territory or declare WAR!"
  • A promise to withdraw creates a temporary implicit ROP. Breaking that promise counts as backstabbing. Source: Evie, "Reputation for sneak attacks" (Civ3, 2002). Quote (summarised from the page): a "We'll move our troops out asap" reply without a withdrawal creates a temporary ROP.
  • Diplomacy removes units from territory at once. Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Territory: "You can eject rival military units from your territory during diplomatic negotiations." civ3PTW/readme.txt (Changes v1.21f): "Hidden Nationality units are no longer forcibly removed from enemy territory via diplomacy." The same readme (Fixes v1.21f): "Fixed bug where you could not ask an AI civ to remove its units from your territory." Manual p.~77: "You can contact the owner of the stray unit and demand that it be immediately withdrawn. Right-click on the offending unit to do so."
  • Removed units are teleported out. Civ3 Strategy Compilation (CFC, May 2002, vanilla): "Units removed from enemy territory for border violations are teleported to nearest non-offending spot." vondrack (Apolyton "Remove your forces or declare war NOW", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/55198-1.htm, Jul 2002, 1.21f) instead says they go toward "their original destination", and Coracle, same thread, saw a unit that "magically teleports itself to the OTHER side of my civ". Ataxerxes (CFC "Right of Passage agreements", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/right-of-passage-agreements.427477, C3C, Jun 2011): "Your units are ejected, sometimes to odd places." creamcheese, same thread: "they never boot you if you only move workers." The destination rule is disputed; the teleport itself is agreed. (Former open question 8.)
  • The AI's own replies when you tell it to leave. diplomacy.txt AIWILLRETREAT / AIWILLRETURNHOME (it complies), for example: "We will withdraw immediately. My apologies." When it is the AI that warns you and you comply, AIDONTTEST: "Very well. Withdraw them and we will ignore this violation. But do not let it happen again!" AIBORDERWAR treats the presence as war unless you leave: "Your recent movement of troops into our territory constitutes a declaration of war against $CIVNAME1. Send them home immediately or know that YOU will be held responsible for the conflict!"
  • Timing and visibility (community, C3X-THREAD). Erlend Philip Wood (post-16132380, page-16 #313): "you are always asked to leave or declare war on the second turn." He adds (post-16823338, page-136 #2722) that "the AI will only ask them to leave if it can see them". SayHayKid (post-16605848, page-101 #2017): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "In standard game you can enter, but the other Civ will ask you to remove your forces." These are play reports, not tests.
  • When the "leave or declare war" option appears is unknown. Apolyton 55198 (Jul 2002, 1.21f) has guesses only. Hurricane: "The exact number of units required is still unresolved, AFAIK." Fitz: "Contacting a settler/worker never brings up the war version of this demand."

6.11 Espionage

  • Two tiers. Diplomatic missions open with Writing. Spy missions need Espionage and the Intelligence Agency. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage_Missions. Quote: "You can launch certain diplomatic missions at any time after the discovery of $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>." Also "Spy missions become available after you have discovered $LINK<Espionage=TECH_Espionage>." TECH_Espionage: "Build an $LINK<Intelligence Agency=BLDG_Intelligence_Center> and you can conduct $LINK<Espionage Missions=GCON_Espionage>." conquests.biq BLDG has an "AllowsSpyMissions" flag.

  • There are no spy units. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ. Quote: "Spies are not units in the game." Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton, quote: "will no longer require you to use spy units".

  • The mission list (C3C). Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage. Missions: Establish Embassy, Plant Spy, Steal Technology, Steal Military Plans ("reveals the locations of all their military units"), Initiate Propaganda ("If successful, the people will revolt and $LINK<defect=GCON_Conversion> to your side."), Expose Enemy Spy, Investigate City, Steal World Map, and Sabotage Production ("destroy all the $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in the production box of a rival city").

  • Plant Disease was removed in vanilla. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: "Plant Disease: This espionage mission has been removed."

  • Steal Plans shows units on the Military Advisor for one turn. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: "On the turn a Steal Plans mission is successful, when you select the civilization ... you can mouseover a unit and it's location will be marked on that screen's mini-map."

  • Which agent can do what (conquests.biq ESPN, local dump). Each line gives the diplomat flag, the spy flag and the base cost:

    • Build an Embassy: diplomat, not spy, base 20.
    • Investigate City: diplomat and spy, base 10.
    • Steal Technology: diplomat and spy, base 10.
    • Steal World Map: spy only, base 1.
    • Plant Spy: spy only, base 60.
    • Steal Plans: spy only, base 10.
    • Initiate Propaganda: spy only, base 100.
    • Sabotage Production: spy only, base 10.
    • Expose Enemy Spy: spy only, base 80.

  • Requirements. Investigate City and Steal Technology need only an embassy. The other five need the Intelligence Agency, an embassy and a planted spy. Source: CivFanatics, "Successful Espionage" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/successful-espionage.422226). Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton, quote: "only against Civs with whom you've established an embassy and planted a spy".

  • War. Embassy missions stop at war because the embassy closes. A planted spy keeps working. Source: Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton. Quote: "Even if you go to war and your embassy is closed, your spy will continue to function as long as it is not caught."

  • Cost formulas. Source: "A Study of Espionage Missions". Here dist is the distance to your nearest city, level is 1 for a town, 2 for a city and 3 for a metropolis, and the leading number is the BIQ base:

    • Embassy: "20 dist*level + base + pop".
    • Investigate: "10 dist*level + base * pop".
    • Steal Tech: "10 distlevel + base * techstechrate/100".
    • Steal World Map: "1 dist*level + base * mapsize".
    • Plant Spy: "60 dist*level + base".
    • Steal Plans: "10 dist*level + base * unit".
    • Propaganda: "100 dist*level + base * pop * (1 + opp_cult/your_cult)".
    • Sabotage: "10 dist*level + base * shield".
    • Expose: "80 dist*level + base".

    "Carefully" costs 1.5 times "Immediately" and "Safely" costs 2 times. The War Academy page says "multiply by 50% for carefully and double for safely", which reads as halving, but the forum original (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-study-of-espionage-mission.97042/, Oystein, Aug 2004, quoted on page 3) says "3/2 for carefully and 2 for" safely. Spoonwood (page 4, Apr 2017, tested): "safe steal attempts cost exactly twice of an immediate steal attempt," and careful ones 1.5 times. (This corrects the earlier "Carefully halves" reading.) The readme says the cost was exposed in the editor. Quote: "Editor: Added mission cost to Diplomats and Spies page."

  • The editor help gives the formulas without the distance term. EDHELP "Base Cost (Diplomat / Spy Mission Properties)": "Establish Embassy BaseCost

    • TargetCitySize", "Investigate City BaseCost * TargetCitySize", "Steal Tech (BaseCost * Target Player's Number of Techs * Tech Rate) / 100", "Steal World Map BaseCost * ( ( Map Width + Map Height ) / 2 )", "Plant Spy BaseCost", "Steal Plans BaseCost * Target Player's Number of Units", "Sabotage Production BaseCost * Target City's Number of Shields", "Expose Mole BaseCost". Propaganda has a gold term the study lacks: "BaseCost * Target City Size + ( BaseCost * Target City Size * Culture Ratio) + ( ( BaseCost * Target City Size) / 2 * Gold Ratio )". Then: "the cost is then further modified, taking into consideration (where applicable) the distance of the target, the target city's population level, the ratio of citizens between the initiating civ and the target civ, and the safety level modifier". The study's "dist*level" term matches the distance and city-level parts.

  • Success chances (same study):

    • Chance of escaping (Immediate/Carefully/Safely): Embassy 40/55/65, Regular spy 60/75/85, Veteran spy 70/85/95.
    • Success is 80% if not caught, giving Embassy 32/44/52, Regular 48/60/68, Veteran 56/68/76.
    • Plant Spy: Embassy 50%, Conscript 20%, Regular 50%, Veteran 60%, Elite 75%.
    • Embassy and Investigate City always succeed.
    • Expose Enemy Spy always fails if there is no spy.

    The escape table is confirmed by Kool Keith and Jazzmail in "Steal Technology" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/steal-technology.232487/post-5709659). Jazzmail, quote: "You have to multiply the values in the table by 0.8."

  • Plant Spy modifiers by agent level (Oystein, CFC 97042 page 3, Mar 2005): "Conscript -30, Regular/Embassy 0, Veteran 10, Elite 25" (summarised list). An elite embassy had 75% to plant and 65% for the other spy missions. He adds that only regular and veteran spies occur in an unmodded game, and that an exposed agent is still caught with "(90%)" chance.

  • Discovery is separate from success (manual). Manual p.~169 ("International Incidents"). Quotes: "Note that the chance of your treachery being discovered is distinct and separate from your odds of success. You can succeed and still spark an incident." and "the targeted civilization is likely to treat your treachery as an act of war. (A target with which you are good friends, though, might sometimes choose to disregard your act.)"

  • Spy experience comes from government. conquests.biq GOVT "SpiesAre": Communism and Fascism give veteran (2), the others regular (1). "DiplomatsAre" is regular for all. CivFanatics FAQ, quote: "the chances to plant a spy depend on your government, but it is around 50-60%."

  • Failure lockout.

    • Source: civ3PTW/readme.txt. Quote: "If you fail a spy mission, you will automatically fail any spy mission against that same civilization for next 2-4 turns."
    • The study (C3C) differs: a failed Plant Spy sets a flag that clears with a 1/3 chance each turn, and other failures do not set it (see Disputed).
    • MAS (Steal Technology thread), quote: "its more difficult to succeed a second time, as your opponent is weary."
    • The flag is per civ and invisible, and the 1/3 chance is the same every turn. Oystein (CFC 97042 page 2, Sep 2004): "The probability that the flag will disappear is 1/3, no matter how many turns has past." Oystein (page 3, Nov 2005): a failed sabotage does not set the flag. Asked whether the AI is flagged too: "there is no difference between human and AI" (page 1).
    • An earlier vanilla report says a failed mission makes the rest of that turn's attempts fail. player1 (Apolyton "Cost of spying", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/60084-1.htm, Aug 2002); Jaybe disagreed, and Catt had succeeded after a failure only against another target. Anecdotal.

  • A caught spy can lead to war. Source: CivFanatics FAQ. Quote: "If it is not successful, the AI will become mad at you and might declare war." The attitude cost is in 6.7.

  • Propaganda (study, summarised). Your spy is never caught. Each citizen's chance of being persuaded is:

    spy bonus (veteran 10) + government modifier + culture factor

    • 5 x garrison - courthouse (20) - capital (40) + 20 for your own nationals

    The city flips when at least 3/4 of its citizens are persuaded. Otherwise the persuaded citizens become "confused" for some turns.

    • Democracy is immune. conquests.biq GOVT Democracy has ImmuneTo=6, the Initiate Propaganda mission. script.txt, quote: "The $CIVADJ1 people are immune to propaganda."
    • Government modifiers (conquests.biq GOVT_GOVT BriberyModifier; rows are the sender's government, columns the target's, in the order Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy, Communism, Republic, Democracy, Fascism, Feudalism):
      • Despotism 15/0/-10/-15/-20/-30/0/0
      • Monarchy 20/15/0/-5/-10/-25/0/0
      • Communism 25/20/5/0/-10/-20/0/0
      • Republic 30/25/10/8/0/-5/0/0
      • Democracy 35/30/20/10/5/0/0/0
      • Fascism 0/0/-5/-20/-10/-10/0/0
      • Feudalism 20/15/0/-10/-10/-20/-25/0
      • Anarchy 0 across the row.
    • Culture factors (conquests.biq CULT "chance of successful propaganda"): in awe of 30, admirers of 25, impressed with 20, unimpressed by 10, dismissive of 5, disdainful of 3.
    • Courthouse. Conquests Civilopedia, quote: "The effectiveness of enemy spy propaganda missions against the city is reduced."

  • Steal Technology is blocked against civs you are at war with. Source: rysingsun in the "Steal Technology" thread, and the Apolyton thread title "espionage: you can't steal techs from civs you are at war with". Combined with the war closing the embassy, this follows from 6.2. Jaybe (Apolyton "Espionage--worthless?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/107183-1.htm, Jan 2004): an Ambassador "canNOT" investigate a city while at war.

  • A planted spy can steal tech during a war (informal test). Spoonwood (CFC 97042 page 4, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-study-of-espionage-mission.97042/page-4, Apr 2017): "if you have a spy planted, then you can attempt to steal tech during a war." Without a planted spy he could not. The manual (p.~168) agrees in spirit: "Once your Spy is in place, the flow of information is not interrupted during a war". (Former open question 9.)

  • Steal Plans lasts the rest of the turn. Manual p.~168: "For the remainder of the turn, you know the positions of all of that rival's troops." C3X-INI (steal_plans_duration, default 1): "Sets the number of turns for which a successful "Steal Plans" espionage mission reveals the target civ's units."

  • Sabotage: half or all of the shields? The manual (p.~168) says "They'll destroy half of the shields already accumulated for the current project." The C3C Civilopedia says "destroy all the $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in the production box" (see Disputed).

  • Propaganda is easier against a city in disorder (manual). Manual p.~168: "it is easier to push a city already in civil disorder into open revolt than it is to undermine a contented city." Not in the study's formula. Oystein (CFC 97042 page 2, Sep 2004): "If the flip chance of a size 1 city if 30%, then the flip chance of a size 20 city is about 0.004%".

  • When the espionage screen opens. The vanilla README.txt (manual errata, 1.0): "The Espionage button becomes enabled after researching the Espionage advance." The later manual (p.~169) and the C3C Civilopedia GCON_Espionage both say the screen is available after Writing ("This screen is available for use once a player has discovered $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>"). The screen arrived with PTW (Apolyton "UGO Preview Part 2 - ESPIONAGE Screen", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/63254-1.htm). So: vanilla button at Espionage; PTW and C3C screen at Writing.

  • How a mission is rolled (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16571523, page-91 #1812): "The game computes some sort of base chance ahead of time when a spy mission is configured", and "the game doesn't account for city improvements or government until the very end." (said of the propaganda mission). For stealing a tech the base chance is (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the chance not to fail completely": losing that roll means the theft fails and the spy is caught, and "even if you pass that roll there's another roll to determine if you fail to steal the tech but the spy gets away." That is the study's two stages (escape chance, then 80% success), in the other order of description.

  • Every mission is aimed at a city (code reading). Flintlock (post-16878439, page-156 #3119): "Even the code for the espionage screen itself is all written assuming that every mission requires a target city." No poison-water mission exists in C3C (post-16848926, page-147 #2930): "Sadly, I don't see any evidence of this mission in the Conquests EXE." vmxa (post-16876177, page-156 #3105), on the capital as the target: "No palace, no spy."

  • Steal Plans also shows invisible units (play report). MARK V (C3X-THREAD post-16938325, page-177 #3539): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "invisible units become visible as a result of an espionage mission to steal plans", and a failed mission "almost always" brings a declaration of war.

  • The AI uses espionage too. jabroni154 (Apolyton "Espionage and the AI in 1.21", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/49422-1.htm, May 2002) saw an AI propaganda attempt and spy plants. TimBentley (CFC 97042 page 4, Apr 2006): "They use propaganda and sabotage production, for sure." Manual p.~157: "Be aware that enemies can use all the same techniques against your civilization as you use against theirs."

6.12 United Nations (brief)

  • Periodic majority votes. Source: Conquests Civilopedia (Victory, Diplomacy). Quote: "After the $LINK<United Nations=BLDG_United_Nations> is built, there are periodic votes by the council to elect a Leader. To be elected, a candidate must win a majority of all votes."
  • Candidates. Quote: "There are always two, and sometimes three, candidates." A civ qualifies if it "have built the UN", or controls "at least 25% of the world's territory", or "at least 25% of the world's population". If only one civ is eligible, "the second candidate is the civilization with the largest population."
  • The UN owner decides whether to hold the vote, every 11 turns. Source: CivFanatics FAQ. Quote: the choice "comes around every 11 turns". Also script.txt DIPLOVICTORYVOTEOPTION: "Would you like to hold elections for UN Secretary-General?" and "Election was inconclusive; no majority was found."
  • One vote per civ; a majority of all civs in the game. CFC FAQ: "Each civilization gets 1 vote, and can vote for one of the candidates." Manual p.~175: "you must be elected Secretary-General by a vote of the majority of all the civilizations in the world". TimBentley (CFC "the road to Diplomatic Victory", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-road-to-diplomatic-victory.40013/page-2, Sep 2005), asked whether the majority is of all civs or of voters: "All civs in the game." Candidates vote for themselves (eae, CFC 19457, Dec 2003: "my rival voted for himself").
  • Only the UN owner can call the vote. tao (CFC "Diplomatic victory question", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/diplomatic-victory-question.112336, C3C/PTW, Feb 2005): "Only the owner of the UN can call the vote." Turner, same thread: "Actually, it's every 11 turns." Hyronymus: "the non-owners never receive a message if the AI owner decided not to run an election."
  • An AI must be Polite or better to vote for you. Source: AI Attitude Study (summarised). SirPleb (CFC 44999 page 5, Mar 31 2003, quoted again on page 8): "To get someone's vote at the UN it is necessary to have them "polite"." Barely polite (-1) is enough; at 0 (cautious) or worse the AI abstains or votes for another candidate. Bamspeedy (page 6): "donating over 100 gold the turn before the UN vote would be a waste" (the gift cap is -10).
  • TMIT's Guide to UN Victories is Civ4. The often-cited CFC thread 304815 (60% of world population, "friendly" voters) is a Civ4 guide, so it is not used here.

6.13 How attitude and treaties feed AI war plotting (code-level, brief)

The full AI war logic belongs to section 8. This is the part that depends on diplomacy. Source for every bullet: Flintlock (CFC, "All About AI Aggression Levels and War Plotting", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/all-about-ai-aggression-levels-and-war-plotting.703785/, C3C 1.22, Jun-Jul 2026), a pseudocode reconstruction from the executable. His post #8 corrects the exclusion roll, and the text below uses the corrected reading.

  • Each turn the AI may start plotting a war. Quote: "Each AI player has a chance at the start of each turn to begin plotting war." The rolls include "Roll one in (12 + -2 * EffectiveAggressionLevel)", "If already at war, roll one in 16", "If in anarchy, roll one in 8", and "If in a government that experiences war weariness, roll one in 2".
  • MPP partners are never targets. Quote: "Other players with whom the AI has a mutual protection pact are excluded". He also believes recent peace protects: "I believe this blocks war plotting within 8 turns of signing peace." Fergei (page 1, Jul 2026): "The AI will declare war on you, even if you have an MPP, if the MPP is agreed AFTER" the plotting began.
  • Peace, common enemies and alliances make exclusion likelier. The roll is "rolling not one in X", with "X = 1 to start", "X += 2 if the AI has a peace treaty with the target", "X += 2 for each common enemy the AI and the target have" and "X += 2 for each military alliance the AI and the target have". "The AI will skip this check if the target is a human player who is ranked first in power."
  • Attitude weights the target score. Quote: "DiploEffect = 8 if furious toward the target, 4 if annoyed, 2 if cautious, 1 if polite, and 0 if gracious." Other diplomatic terms: "If the AI has a resource trade with the target, X = 3 * X // 4", "For each common enemy that the AI and the target share, X = 2 * X // 3", "If the target has a peace treaty or mutual protection pact with any player, X = X // 2." and "X = X // (AllianceCount + 1)".
  • War is declared when the attack starts. Flintlock (page 2, Jul 14 2026): "War is declared only when the attack actually happens." And: "the AI will not attack you with its HN units while it's gracious toward you."
  • How the AI's declarations happen in play. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16132702, page-16 #318): AI war declarations usually come from it "running its units into its target's cities". Alekseyev_ (post-16987435, page-190 #3796): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "Basically, only surprise attacks as war declarations." Both fit "War is declared only when the attack actually happens" above.
  • Barbarians: no diplomacy, always enemies (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16980354, page-188 #3741): "the unit AI considers units owned by the barbs to be enemies unconditionally" and "Diplomacy with the barbarians is not possible even if they own cities."
  • Shared continent means a city on it. Flintlock (page 2): "Two players are considered to share a continent if they both have at least one city on it."

Disputed

  • Right of Passage tech. Vanilla Civilopedia says Writing ("Can now negotiate military alliance and right of passage"). Conquests BIQ and Civilopedia say Map Making. The C3C GCON_Embassies text still says Writing ("That done, you can sign Right of Passage agreements and Military Alliances"), a leftover from vanilla. Map trading: Map Making in vanilla, Navigation in C3C. Owner ruling (engine): ROP needs Writing plus an embassy. Its sources are listed in 6.3 (C3C GCON_Embassies, vanilla TECH_Writing, the manual's "if you have an embassy", Aeson on Apolyton 46862). Code finding next to the ruling (third round, for the lead): the 1.22 executable has no Writing rule for this. The tech is a rule-file flag, ATF_Enables_Right_Of_Passage_Treaties = 0x200 in the tech-flag enum of C3X's header for the 1.22 exe (https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/Civ3Conquests.h), alongside ATF_Enables_Military_Alliances, ATF_Enables_Mutual_Protection_Pacts, ATF_Enables_Trade_Embargos, ATF_Enables_Map_Trading and ATF_Enables_Communication_Trading. In conquests.biq that flag is on Map Making (6.3). So stock C3C ties ROP to Map Making, as the BIQ and C3C Civilopedia say. The header gives the flag names only; it does not show the embassy check.
  • Does attitude change trade prices? TheNiceOne (1.29) found that attitudes below Polite give no extra markup ("no AI will demand more if falling below catious"). Zwingli (1.29) measured +0.32% per attitude point below Cautious, and Furious cost about 38% more than Gracious when combined with demands. Catt (Apolyton 53506, Jun 2002, "a very limited test"): "AI attitude towards me had absolutely no affect on trade prices." Bamspeedy (CFC 44999 page 2): "I did not test how attitudes affect what civs would pay (or charge you) for things." The engine follows TheNiceOne's table (owner ruling).
  • Spy failure lockout. The PTW readme says any spy mission fails automatically for 2-4 turns after a failure. The espionage study (C3C) says only a failed Plant Spy sets a flag, cleared with a 1/3 chance per turn, and that a failed sabotage sets no flag. A 1/3 chance per turn gives an expected 3 turns, which is close to the readme's "2-4", so the two may describe the same flag with the readme over-generalising "any spy mission". Not tested against the readme. Jaybe (Apolyton "Spies: Useless or Indispensible?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/81355-1.htm, 23 Mar 2003) dates the lockout to PTW: "Back in 1.16 I am pretty sure you could try every turn." and "On the current Windoze version (PTW??) if you fail then it won't do any good to try again for 3-4 turns." Neither post separates a plant-only flag from an all-missions lockout.
  • Sabotage Production: all or half the shields. Manual p.~168: "They'll destroy half of the shields already accumulated for the current project." Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage: "destroy all the $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in the production box of a rival city". The manual's "Losing Improvements" note (p.~143) says sabotage "might scrap the item that the city is currently producing or destroy half the shields". One vanilla report (DISX, Apolyton "Sabotage Production doesn't work?", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/33523-1.htm, Nov 2001): "SABOTAGE SUCCESSFUL it tells me, Japanese has to start the production over." No controlled test found. For C3C, follow the Civilopedia.
  • Carefully: x0.5 or x1.5 cost. The War Academy page reads "multiply by 50% for carefully", but the forum original says "3/2", and Spoonwood measured x1.5 (6.11). Resolved in favour of x1.5; kept here because the War Academy page is the commonly cited copy.
  • Shunned government: +4 or +5 attitude. The study measured "+4 Shunned government". Flintlock's code reading (CFC 703785, 2026) gives "if the other player is in the AI's shunned government, it adds 5 points." The study's numbers are measured at PTW 1.01; the code reading is C3C 1.22.
  • AI reputation. Players disagree on whether AIs suffer reputation for their own treaty breaks. Myartar, quote: "There seems to be NO penelty to the AI on reputaion". ahman (CFC 113376 page 3, Dec 2010): "Maybe they don't even care about reputation between them?" (unanswered). The manual (p.~156) only says "If you habitually break treaties, other leaders will have no qualms about doing the same to you."
  • Does breaking a peace treaty hurt reputation? microbe ("Trading Reputation", C3C) says "there is no such a thing" as peace-treaty reputation, and that breaking a fresh treaty leaves reputation "intact" unless it breaks a GPT deal. Smart (CFC 113376 page 2, Dec 2005): "You cant breake peace treaty without a rep hit." nullspace (same page, C3C 1.22 test of Vol's save): declaring war "breaks a 20-turn peace treaty, and breaks no other deals", yet India's reputation was ruined afterwards. tmn (page 3, 2017): "Peace treaty, Right of Passage and Trade Embargo are all rep-risky." The attitude study separately gives "+4 for breaking a peace treaty with that civ" as an attitude (not reputation) effect.
  • Does reputation ever recover? The CFC FAQ and microbe say never for GPT deals ("will never again for the game accept per-turn payments"). The manual (p.~156) says black marks "can be partially erased" over "long periods of time". Vol's tests (6.6) show a workaround through contact, not time. No timed test was found.
  • Units removed from territory: where to? The Strategy Compilation says "nearest non-offending spot"; vondrack (Apolyton 55198, 1.21f) says toward "their original destination" (6.10).
  • ~~Can cities be traded outside a peace deal?~~ Resolved (6.4, C3X-THREAD): the AI refuses city transfers outside peace talks; Flintlock says the developers restricted it because the AI could not value cities. The FAQ's "peace deal only" is right for deals with the AI. Kept for the record: The CFC FAQ lists "Cities (as part of a peace deal only)". The manual (p.~165) lists cities as an ordinary trade item, Bamspeedy (CFC 44999 page 6, PTW 1.21) treats "Donations of cities" as gifts, and the Strategy Compilation (vanilla) says "AI trades big for cities". The FAQ may mean that the AI only gives up cities to end a war. Random Passerby (Apolyton "Trading Absurdity", https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/49585-1.htm, May 2002, unsourced): "the Firaxis team made it so that the AI will only even consider giving cities in exchange for peace treaties."
  • UN: second candidate when only one civ qualifies. Conquests Civilopedia: "the second candidate is the civilization with the largest population." The CFC FAQ (summarised by the fetch tool) says the highest-scoring civ. EMan (CFC 40013 page 2, PTW, Nov 2005): "If neither 1 or 2 applies, Civ with highest % of World's Population." Follow the Civilopedia.
  • AI-AI trade rate scope. Soren (via WarpStorm, Apolyton 68856): "the AI to AI trade rate is deficit spending only." EDHELP describes a plain multiplier on the initiating civ's offer. See 6.8.
  • Submarine collisions. Vanilla README.txt (Fixes v1.16f): "You no longer declare war by accidentally passing over a hidden submarine." and "The AI no longer reacts to submarines that it cannot see." C3X-INI (C3C 1.22, "SUBMARINE BUG"): "After colliding with an invisible unit, the AI initiates combat, declaring war if necessary." So in 1.22 an AI that bumps into your hidden unit can still start a war. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16055720, page-5 #91) lists it among the stock bugs he fixed: "AI pathfinding collides with invisible units (called the "submarine bug")". Alekseyev_ (post-16310981, page-44 #876): "I think the submarine bug still exists on land." It is a bug; the intended rule (vanilla 1.16f) is that bumping into an unseen unit starts no war.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

Resolved this pass (now in the main text): first contact trigger (6.1, manual), ceasefire (6.5, none in Civ3), MPP before Nationalism (6.3), embassy attitude sign (6.7), embassy/alliance gate (6.3), units teleported when told to leave (6.10, destination disputed), planted spy stealing during war (6.11, informal test).

  1. Exact tech-trade valuation function in Civ3. Known: one function for AI-AI and AI-human trades, value falls as more civs have the tech (Soren), and the AI-human markup table (TheNiceOne). Soren (Apolyton 42905, 6.8) adds that only civs known to the buyer count, that the buyer's own progress lowers the value, and that AIs sell to each other at half value at worst. Unknown: the exact scaling by known civs and by civs with the tech, and the wonder term (Ironikinit, 42905 page 2, Feb 2002, observed only: "The AI does put greater value on techs that yield a wonder, like music theory and astronomy.").
  2. The AI's decision to give in to your demands. The AI's own demand gates are now known from code (6.9: power place, attitude and aggression rolls, war-damage balance, 32-turn cooldown). Whether it gives in is the stock Leader_would_yield_to_demand, whose terms are not published. Players tie giving in to military superiority near their cities.
  3. When the AI agrees to or refuses ROP, MPP, MA and embargo (AINORIGHTOFPASSAGE: "Our bond is not sufficient to allow trust of this magnitude.") Known: ROP is priced by territory size (Bamspeedy), the MA evaluation divides by the distance to the target (C3X-INI), and the AI refuses to embargo a civ it trades resources with (Spoonwood). The attitude thresholds are unknown.
  4. Exact decay of temporary attitude effects other than gifts (10 gold/turn) and war damage (non-linear, SirPleb's samples in 6.7).
  5. Timed reputation recovery (see Disputed).
  6. Terms of an AI-to-human loan (AILOANOFFER). Lending to the AI is about 18 gold per 1 gpt (6.8).
  7. Pricing of cities and strategic resources. Maps, ROP, luxuries, contacts and early workers have player-tested rules (6.8). Cities and strategic resources do not. Observed prices only: Hurricane (Apolyton 49585 page 2, 1.21, May 2002): "Here is an example that the AI indeed will pay nice amounts of cash for other resources than Iron" (a screenshot of 5200 gold for Oil); player1 (page 1): "Iron lacking AI pays a lot of money for it."
  8. AI "won't trade techs with someone at war with its friend" or "won't trade while you're in a war you started". No Civ3 source was found. Such rules are attested for Civ4 only (for example CFC 217576, a Civ4 thread).
  9. When the "remove your forces or declare war" option appears, and the exact rule for where removed units go. (C3X-THREAD play reports, 6.10: the demand comes on the second turn, and only for units the AI can see.)
  10. Whether the MPP fires on the declaration or the first attack.
  11. Embassy-granting wonder (Marco Polo's Embassy): its presence and effect per version were not checked here. It belongs to the wonders section.

Back to the top

7. Map, terrain, resources, workers, barbarians and huts

Scope: Civ3 1.29f, PTW 1.27f, Conquests (C3C) 1.22, standard rules (conquests.biq). Scenarios, mods (C3X etc.) and the special C3C game modes are excluded.

Sources and how they are cited

Primary sources were read straight from the Steam "Civilization III Complete" install (D:\Steam\steamapps\common\Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete\, called <civ3> below). They are local files, not URLs, so each citation gives the file and the entry key.

Tag Source
BIQ <civ3>/Conquests/conquests.biq, the C3C 1.22 default rules. Read with this repo's QueryCiv3 (sections TERR, TFRM, GOOD, RULE, GOVT, DIFF, WSIZ). The quote is the field name and value.
PEDIA <civ3>/Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (C3C). The quote follows the #KEY of the entry.
EDHELP The C3C editor help <civ3>/Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, decompiled. Cited by page name.
README-129 <civ3>/README.txt, the vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f.
README-PTW <civ3>/civ3PTW/readme.txt (PTW 1.27f).
README-C3C <civ3>/Conquests/readme.txt (C3C 1.20/1.22).
README-CMPL <civ3>/Civ Complete Readme.txt.
SCRIPT <civ3>/Conquests/Text/script.txt, the in-game messages.
MANUAL The original Civilization III manual (2001, vanilla). It came from a PDF text extract with no URL available, so page numbers are approximate.
INFOC CivFanatics Civ3 Info Center (Firaxis pre-release info): https://civfanatics.com/civ3/infocenter/
BIQ-V / BIQ-PTW <civ3>/civ3mod.bic (vanilla 1.29f rules) and <civ3>/civ3PTW/civ3X.bix (PTW 1.27f rules), decompressed and read raw (sections TFRM, TERR, GOOD, TECH) with a throwaway scratch script. Used for version differences.
MANUAL-CMPL The Civilization III Complete manual (scratch PDF text extract), "Terrain Charts", printed p.212. It covers Marsh and Volcano, so it is the C3C-era edition.
C3X-INI The base-game bug and behaviour notes in the C3X mod's default.c3x_config.ini (Flintlock / maxpetul), https://github.com/maxpetul/C3X/blob/master/default.c3x_config.ini (read 2026-10-20). The mod is out of scope. Only its descriptions of what the unmodded C3C 1.22 executable does are used; they come from reading the executable's code.
C3X-THREAD https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/ (the C3X mod's forum thread, 2021-2026). Posts are cited as "post-NNNNNNNN" (thread URL plus post-NNNNNNNN) or "page-N #post-number". Flintlock, the C3X author, reads the 1.22 executable; his statements about the base game are code readings. Other posters report play, sometimes in modded games. Only statements about the unmodded game are used. Quotes were re-checked against the posts; the few that could not be are marked "(paraphrase from a summarised fetch)".
CIVWIKI The German CivWiki (civforum.de community), https://www.civ-wiki.de/ . Community-tested rules, cited by page and revision. Quotes are German with an English gloss.

Web sources are cited by URL. Forum findings are community testing, not official documentation, so they are marked as such.

1. Terrain types

1.1 Base table (C3C BIQ TERR)

Terrain Food Shield Commerce Irrigation + Mine + Road + Defence % Move cost City allowed Colony Wheeled impassable Fort / Airfield / Radar / Outpost Pollution / global-warming effect (TERR "PollutionEffect")
Desert 0 1 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Plains 1 1 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes becomes Desert
Grassland 2 0 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes becomes Plains
Tundra 1 0 0 0 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Flood Plain 3 0 0 1 0 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Hills 1 1 0 0 2 1 50 2 yes yes no all yes none
Mountains 0 1 0 0 2 1 100 3 no yes yes all yes none
Forest 1 2 0 0 0 1 25 2 yes yes no all yes base terrain (14)
Jungle 1 0 0 0 0 1 25 3 yes yes yes all yes base terrain (14)
Marsh 1 0 0 0 0 1 20 2 no no yes outpost only becomes Coast (11)
Volcano 0 3 0 0 0 0 80 3 no no yes none none
Coast 1 0 2 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
Sea 1 0 1 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
Ocean 0 0 0 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
  • Source for every cell: BIQ TERR fields Food, Shields, Commerce, IrrigationBonus, MiningBonus, RoadBonus, DefenseBonus, MovementCost, AllowCities, AllowColonies, ImpassableByWheeled, AllowForts/AllowAirfields/AllowRadarTowers/AllowOutposts, PollutionEffect. For example, Hills reads IrrigationBonus=0 MiningBonus=2 RoadBonus=1 DefenseBonus=50 MovementCost=2 Food=1 Shields=1 Commerce=0.
  • Meaning of the bonus fields. EDHELP "Irrigation (Terrain Terraform Bonuses)": "Determines the bonus to food production if a tile of the selected terrain type is irrigated." EDHELP "Road (Terrain Terraform Bonuses)": "Determines the bonus to commerce if a road is built on a tile of the selected terrain type."
  • A zero RoadBonus means no road can be built. README-129: "Restricted roads/railroads from being built on terrain that has no road bonus." So Volcano and water tiles cannot have roads.
  • Meaning of PollutionEffect. EDHELP "Pollution Effect": "Selects the terrain type (if any) that a tile of the selected terrain type will become if pollution stays on that tile for an extended length of time. For example, grassland would become plains, and plains would become desert." Value 14 is "Base Terrain Type".
  • Bonus grassland ("Grassland with shield"). PEDIA #TERR_Grassland_with_Shield: "Some grasslands contain mineral deposits ... These have a rock outcropping to symbolize this and produce a shield if worked in a city radius." README-PTW: "Updated placement of forests and jungles on bonus grassland to leave the bonus shield under the forest/jungle."
  • Fresh-water lakes are not a terrain type. README-PTW: "NOTE: Fresh water lakes are not a terrain type. They are composed of coastal tiles surrounded by land." PEDIA #GCON_Food nevertheless lists "Fresh Water Lake 2 cannot be irrigated", against 1 food for Coastal. See Disputed.
  • Mountains cannot hold cities. MANUAL p.~131 (Build/Join City): "You also cannot build on Mountains." This matches BIQ AllowCities=0. BIQ also has AllowCities=0 for Marsh and Volcano.
  • Wheeled units need a road. MANUAL ch.7: "wheeled units require a road to travel through Jungles and Mountains." BIQ also sets ImpassableByWheeled=1 for Marsh and Volcano.
  • Movement. EDHELP "Movement Cost (Terrain Properties)": "Determines the base movement cost for a tile of the selected terrain type."
  • Version differences (BIQ-V, BIQ-PTW). The vanilla 1.29f and PTW 1.27f rule files have 12 terrains: no Marsh and no Volcano. All the shared terrains have the same yields, bonuses, defence and move costs as C3C. Forest and Jungle PollutionEffect is 12 there (the "base terrain" code before C3C added two terrains; 14 in C3C). In PTW, as in C3C, ImpassableByWheeled is set on Mountains and Jungle.
  • Terrain adjacency limits in the map generator (code reading). Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16696797, page-119 #2363): "already in the base game desert can't border tundra." (post-16826088, page-137 #2730): "I know tundra and desert, for example, are not." [allowed next to each other]. And (post-16892444, page-160 #3198) there are no impassable terrains "under the standard rules."
  • Volcano yield confirmed. MANUAL-CMPL's terrain chart gives the Volcano row as 0 food, 3 shields, 0 commerce, movement 3, defence 80. CIVWIKI Terrain (Civ3) (rev. 37385, 2012) gives Vulkan 0/3/0 and says "Kann von Radeinheiten nicht passiert werden" ("cannot be crossed by wheeled units") and "keine Stadtgründung möglich" ("no city can be founded"). Both agree with BIQ Shields=3. The PEDIA #GCON_Shields line "Volcano 0 cannot be mined" is the odd one out (former Disputed 9).

1.2 Defence bonuses (cross-check)

PEDIA #GCON_Terrain_Combat lists Grasslands, Plains, Desert, Floodplains, Tundra, Ocean, Sea, Coast and Fresh Water Lake at 10%. It gives "Marsh 20%", "River 25%", "Forest 25%", "Jungles 25%", "Hills 50%", "Volcano 80%" and "Mountains 100%", with the footnote " This bonus applies to the defender if attacked from the other side of a river." These figures match BIQ exactly. The structural bonuses listed there are Fortified +25%, Fortress +50%, Barricade +100% and City +50%. The combat section owns those.

1.3 Despotism tile penalty (affects every tile yield)

  • PEDIA #GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two food, shields, or commerce in a despotic government instead produces one less."
  • The same penalty applies under Anarchy per the government section. That is not re-verified here.

2. Rivers

Effect Rule Source
Placement Rivers run along tile edges. PEDIA #TERR_River: "[Rivers] do not run [in] squares: instead they run along the edges of squares."
Commerce +1 commerce to any tile with a river on one of its edges. PEDIA #TERR_River: "Any square with a river along its edge produces one extra commerce if worked by citizen laborers within a city radius."
Fresh water Lets adjacent tiles be irrigated before Electricity. PEDIA #TERR_River: "rivers are sources of fresh water, allowing irrigation of adjacent squares without electricity."
Defence +25% (RULE.RiverDefensiveBonus=25), only when attacked across the river. It stacks with other bonuses. EDHELP "River (Defensive Bonuses)": "Default is 25. Note that this bonus is cumulative with other terrain and fortification-related defensive bonuses." The cross-river condition is from PEDIA §1.2.
Movement No movement bonus along rivers. Crossing a river cancels the road bonus until Engineering. MANUAL p.~59: "This game offers no movement bonus for river travel." and "Until you discover Engineering, your units do not enjoy the road bonus to movement when they cross a river." PEDIA #TECH_Engineering: "Roads now bridge rivers."
Flood plains Flood plains only appear along rivers, so they always carry the river +1 commerce. PEDIA #TERR_Flood_Plain: "[floodplains] appear along river banks". Sullla, https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat7.html: "The base numbers for floodplains tiles are 3 food, 0 shields, and 1 gold".
Ships Ships cannot enter rivers. MANUAL ch.7: "Ships cannot navigate any ground terrain in the game, including rivers, deltas, and flood plains."

3. Water tiles, harbour, offshore platform

  • The yields are in §1.1: Coast 1/0/2, Sea 1/0/1, Ocean 0/0/0 per BIQ. The vanilla and PTW rule files give the same three values (BIQ-V, BIQ-PTW). Other sources give Ocean 1 food (see Disputed 12).
  • Lakes (community). CIVWIKI Terrain (Civ3) (rev. 37385) lists the lake ("Binnensee") at 2 food, 0 shields, 2 commerce, 10% defence, Fish possible, and "Zählt als Frischwasserquelle (wie Flüsse)" ("counts as a fresh-water source, like rivers"). This matches PEDIA "Fresh Water Lake 2" food. Since README-PTW says lakes are Coast tiles (Coast is 1/0/2 in BIQ), the extra food is presumably a hard-coded bonus for Coast tiles enclosed by land. That inference is not sourced.
  • Harbour. PEDIA #BLDG_Harbor: "Coastal, sea, and ocean squares produce one extra food."
  • Offshore Platform. PEDIA #BLDG_Offshore_Platform: "causes all worked coastal, sea, and ocean squares to produce one shield."
  • Movement safety. PEDIA #TERR_Coast: naval units risk loss outside coast "But after the discovery of astronomy, this danger vanishes." PEDIA #TERR_Sea: "[Sea] squares are significant barriers to trade and travel until the discovery of astronomy." PEDIA #TERR_Ocean: "... until the discovery of Navigation or Magnetism."
  • Resources on water. BIQ TERR/GOOD: Coast takes Fish, Sea takes Whales and Fish, Ocean takes none.

4. Tile improvements (worker jobs)

4.1 Allowed terrain and effects

Job Allowed on Effect Tech / resource prerequisite (BIQ TFRM Required, RequiredResource1/2)
Irrigate Desert, Plains, Grassland, Flood Plain. These are the terrains with IrrigationBonus > 0. + the terrain's IrrigationBonus in food (+1 on each). none. Irrigating without fresh water needs Electricity.
Mine Desert, Plains, Grassland, Tundra (+1); Hills, Mountains (+2). + MiningBonus in shields. none
Road All land except Volcano. + RoadBonus commerce (+1); movement cost 1/3 (RULE.MovementAlongRoads=3). none
Railroad A tile that already has a road. Movement free; raises irrigation and mine output (see §4.3). Steam Power + Iron + Coal
Plant Forest Plains, Grassland, Tundra (BIQ WorkerJobAllowed=Plant Forest). Tile becomes Forest. Engineering
Clear Forest Forest Becomes its underlying base terrain; 10 shields go to the nearest city. none
Clear Wetlands Jungle, Marsh Becomes Grassland or Flood Plain. none
Clear Damage Polluted, fallout or cratered tiles Removes the damage. none
Fortress Land with AllowForts=1. +50% defence, ZOC. Construction
Barricade (C3C) A fortress tile. +100% defence; stops enemy ground movement. Construction
Airfield (PTW+) AllowAirfields=1, neutral or own land. Air base. The worker is consumed. Flight
Radar Tower (PTW+) Own territory only. +25% attack and defence within 2 tiles. The worker is consumed. Advanced Flight (C3C 1.20+)
Outpost (PTW+) Neutral or own land. Vision 2/3/4 on flat/hills/mountains. The worker is consumed. Masonry
Colony A tile with a tradable resource outside your borders. Gives access to the resource. The worker is consumed. none

Sources for §4.1:

  • BIQ TFRM: Fortress Required=20 (Construction), Railroad Required=44 RequiredResource1=1 RequiredResource2=3 (Steam Power, Iron, Coal), Plant Forest Required=23 (Engineering), Airfield Required=58 (Flight), Radar Tower Required=63 (Advanced Flight), Outpost Required=1 (Masonry), Barricade Required=20. Mine, Irrigation, Road, Clear Forest, Clear Wetlands and Clear Damage all have Required=-1.
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Irrigation: "Only squares adjacent to fresh water (river or lake) or to another irrigated square may be irrigated. After the discovery of Electricity, you can irrigate a tile without proximity to fresh water."
  • MANUAL p.114 (Irrigate): "A suitable square can always be irrigated if it shares a side or a diagonal with a source of fresh water (terrain with a river running through it, a freshwater lake, or another irrigated square)." So the adjacency includes diagonals.
  • MANUAL p.113 (orders table): the Irrigate row lists "Desert, Grassland, Plains, Flood Plains". The Mine row lists "Desert, Hills, Mountains, Plains, Grassland". The Reforest row lists "Engineering Grassland, Plains, Tundra".
  • PEDIA #GCON_Food: "Hills 1 cannot be irrigated", "Tundra 1 cannot be irrigated", "Desert 0 1 if irrigated".
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Mine: "Grasslands 0 shields, 1 if mined. w/ outcropping 1 shields, 2 if mined. Plains 1 shields, 2 if mined. Hills 1 shields, 3 if mined. Mountains 1 shields, 3 if mined."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Road: "units moving along roads expend one third of a movement point per square, regardless of terrain type." The table there gives +1 commerce with a road on Floodplains, Grasslands, Plains, Hills, Mountains, Forests and Jungles. PEDIA #GCON_Commerce adds "Marsh 0 1 with road."
  • MANUAL p.~86: "All terrain types produce commerce once penetrated by roads."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Railroad: "Workers can build railroads after the discovery of Steam Power, but only if your civilization has access to the Strategic Resources Iron and Coal." MANUAL p.116: "You can only build them where you have already built roads."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Clear_Forest: "contribute 10 shields to the nearest controlled city. The resulting terrain will be either grasslands, plains, or tundra, depending upon the base terrain beneath the forest." BIQ RULE.ForestValueInShields=10.
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Clear_Wetlands: "A worker can clear the jungle or marsh from a square, creating either floodplains or grasslands."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Plant_Forest says "any grassland or plains square". BIQ and MANUAL also allow Tundra. README-129 (v1.16f): "Forests planted on tundra always display as pine." CIVWIKI Bautruppaufträge (Civ3) (rev. 54705, 2024) also gives "Wald aufforsten: 18 (nur Tundra, Ebene, Weideland)" ("plant forest: 18, only tundra, plains, grassland"). So Tundra is allowed; the PEDIA line is incomplete (former Disputed 8).
  • Forest shields only once per tile. README-129 (Changes v1.16f): "Shield bonus from clearing forest can only be received once per game." Community readings agree that the limit is per tile: justanick, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/not-getting-shields-when-cutting-forests.654695/ (C3C, Feb 2020): "You can get the 10 shields only once per tile." and "If the tile has been deforested before, then no shields are credited." CIVWIKI Wald roden (Civ3) (rev. 35891, 2011): the bonus comes when the order is carried out "erstmals auf einem Feld" ("for the first time on a tile").
  • Which city gets the chop (community). CIVWIKI Wald roden (Civ3): the game searches the tiles around the forest in a fixed clockwise order, and "Die Stadt, die auf dem Feld mit der kleinsten Nummer steht, erhält den Waldertrag." ("The city on the lowest-numbered tile gets the forest yield"). "Der Wald muß dazu nicht im Kulturgebiet der Stadt liegen." ("The forest does not have to be inside the city's borders"). "Städte außerhalb dieses Bereichs erhalten keine Gutschrift." ("Cities outside this area get nothing"). Cities building a wonder, small wonder, Palace, spaceship part or Wealth are skipped, so the shields go to the next city in order. TimBentley (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/barbarian-information.267101/, 2008) says the same search order is used for "the governor's order in looking for the next tile to work and forest chops going to cities". tjs282 (thread 654695): "They will not be applied to Great Wonders, Small Wonders(?), or Wealth".
  • README-129 (Fixed v1.21f): "Fixed forest planting on enemy territory exploit." The exploit is not described.
  • Forest shields cannot be used for wonders. Apolyton (piratebrun, quoted in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-play-the-world/85881-questions-about-terrain-and-the-explaination-the-prima-s-strategy-guide-gives): forests "CANNOT be harvested to speed up PALACES, SMALL WONDERS or WONDERS." This is community testing; the cities/production section should confirm it.
  • Fortress. PEDIA #GCON_Fortresses: "They get a 50% defensive bonus if attacked." and "They have a zone of control and fire at passing enemy units without receiving return fire." BIQ RULE.FortressDefensiveBonus=50.
  • Barricade. PEDIA #GCON_Barricades: "A Barricades can only be built on a square already containing a Fortress. Even if unoccupied, barricades automatically stops movement of a ground unit entering its square" ... "They get a 100% defensive bonus if attacked" ... "Barricades become a fortress if their square is pillaged."
  • Airfield. PEDIA #GCON_Airfields: "Construction of the Airfield consumes the worker." and "If it falls into the territory of another civilization, however, the airfield becomes solely useable by the player whose territory it lies in (unless they have yet to research Flight, in which case the airfield is destroyed)." The GCON text says "Airfields can only be built in tiles that have no other improvements, an airfield, a colony, a road or a railroad." PEDIA #TFRM_Airfield says "Airfields can only be built in tiles that are empty or have a road and/or railroad."
  • Outpost. PEDIA #GCON_Outposts: "The range of vision for the Outpost is 2 tiles on flat terrain, 3 on hills and 4 on mountains." ... "If an Outpost falls within the territory of any other civilization, it will be destroyed." ... "Outposts can only be built in a tile that is empty, has a mine, irrigation, a road and/or a railroad. You cannot build an Outpost on a tile with an Airfield or a Colony."
  • Radar Tower prerequisite by version. BIQ-PTW TFRM Radar Tower Required=64, which is Radio in the PTW tech list. C3C BIQ Required=63 is Advanced Flight (C3C removed Radio). So the PEDIA/MANUAL "Radio" text is right for PTW and outdated for C3C (former Disputed 15).
  • Radar Tower. PEDIA #GCON_Radar_Towers: "The range of the effected area is 2 tiles. Friendly units within the Radar Towers range get a +25% attack and defense bonus. This bonus can only be applied once, even if multiple towers overlap." ... "Should a Radar Tower fall into the territory of another civilization, it will be destroyed." README-C3C v1.20: "Radar Towers require Advanced Flight" and "Radio Tech removed".
  • Colony. PEDIA #TFRM_Build_Colony: "A colony is necessary only if the resource is not inside your borders. Note that when a city's borders expand to include a colony, the colony disappears because it is no longer needed. The worker is consumed by building a colony." PEDIA #GCON_Colony: "you need a road connecting the colony to one of your cities to get the benefits of the resource." SCRIPT #COLONY_BARBS: "Our $RESOURCE0 colony was overrun by a tribe of $BARBARIAN1s." So barbarians destroy colonies.
  • Outposts see no farther over water (community). Alekseyev_ (C3X-THREAD post-16335948, page-50 #999): outposts "don't gain any extra LOS when looking at water, unlike normal units."
  • Railroad-to bypasses the resource check (stock bug, code reading). tjs282 (C3X-THREAD post-16915830, page-169 #3371): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the only absolute logic in the unpatched game for railroading a tile is that it must (first) be roadable". Flintlock (post-16916404, #3374): the game "gives a worker the railroad-to command after checking only that it has the tech & resources to build a road", and "the function that checks whether the needed resources are present always reports that they are" when the job is a road. So the railroad-to button can appear, and the order can lay rail, without Iron and Coal. The intended rule is the BIQ's (Iron and Coal needed, §4.1).
  • Workers can't improve tiles they can't enter. Erlend Philip Wood (C3X-THREAD post-16697864, page-120 #2382): "Workers can't develop tiles that they cannot enter." Flintlock (post-16635376, page-108 #2147): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the worker jobs are hard-coded into the game logic, except for what you can change through the editor."
  • City tiles and roads. README-129: "Changed city code so that a road/railroad is not placed under a city if a road/railroad is not allowed on the terrain type the city is built upon." README-C3C v1.20: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."

4.2 Craters, pollution damage

  • PEDIA #GCON_Craters: "When a bombardment attack which eliminates the last unit or terrain improvement in a square, a crater may be formed. Like pollution, the presence of craters reduces a square's production, commerce, and food output by one."
  • MANUAL p.89 ("Pollution's Effects"): "It reduces the production of food, raw materials, and commerce income to zero. Once the terrain is detoxified, production returns to pre-pollution levels." See Disputed.
  • Long-term pollution transforms the terrain (PollutionEffect, §1.1). PEDIA #GCON_Pollution (Global Warming): "It can cause forests and jungles to disappear, and grasslands to become plains, and plains to become desert. World pollution is the sum of all city pollution, nuclear explosions, and meltdowns." SCRIPT #GLOBALWARMING_RISING_COASTLINES: "Coastlines are rising!" This matches Marsh→Coast in BIQ.
  • Sun icon. README-129 (Changes v1.16f): "The Sun Icon on the Info Box represents the amount of global warming affecting the game. If the sun does not display, there is insufficient global warming to affect the terrain. Once the sun displays, the brighter it is, the greater the impact of global warming."
  • What drives global warming (community, vanilla). WarpStorm, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/60991-1.htm (Sep 2002): "It is caused by having pollution icons in your cities and by using nukes." He reports a custom-scenario test with a heavily polluted map and "No global warming", and second-hand that "Firaxis has said that polution on the ground does not affect global warming" (no source given). Zachriel (page 2, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/60991-2.htm, Oct 2002) abandoned his metropolises and removed his polluting buildings and still got warming: "So yes, Global Warming is global." This fits PEDIA's "World pollution is the sum of all city pollution, nuclear explosions, and meltdowns." The thresholds and odds remain unknown.
  • Uncleaned pollution degrades the tile itself. WarpStorm (same thread, "I tested this too"): "the actual polluted tile can turn worse even if the global warming isn't bad if it's not cleaned up soon enough." This matches EDHELP "Pollution Effect" ("if pollution stays on that tile for an extended length of time").
  • Citizens are taken off polluted tiles (code-level). C3X-INI (do_not_unassign_workers_from_polluted_tiles): the option "keeps citizens working the tiles they've been assigned to when pollution spawns there instead of converting them to specialists". So the base game turns a citizen working a newly polluted tile into a specialist.
  • MANUAL p.88: "Pollution appears within the City Radius of any city that is excessively productive (produces lots of shields) or has a very large population. ... The number of symbols corresponds to the probability each turn of a square within the city radius becoming polluted." The pollution formula belongs to the cities section.

4.3 Railroad yield bonus

  • PEDIA #GCON_Worker_Jobs: "Increases irrigation or mine output, and provides unlimited movement." MANUAL p.116: "Railroads also increase the yields of both irrigation and mines."
  • Community tests, Apolyton (David Weldon), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406: "RR through something that has been irrigated = +1 food. RR through something that has been mined = +1 shield." "RR through forest does nothing. RR through unimproved squares? Nothing." "on a coal mine with RR I get 5, not 6".
  • Community tests, CFC (Yeti), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/does-railroad-provide-extra-shield-on-mined-grassland-plain.74236/: "If there's a mine, RR adds 1 shield." and "If there's irrigation, RR adds 1 food." Also "Tundra + Mine + Railroad = 1 food, 2 shields, 1 gold".
  • Sullla, https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat7.html: forests "cannot be irrigated or mined, and they therefore get no benefit from rails." Deserts "with railroads can actually manage 2 food".
  • Working rule: +1 food on an irrigated tile and +1 shield on a mined tile. No bonus on an unimproved or forest tile. A competing reading exists (Disputed).

5. Worker job times

5.1 Base turns (TFRM "Turns to Complete")

Job C3C 1.22 (BIQ) PTW 1.27f (BIQ-PTW) Vanilla 1.29f (BIQ-V)
Mine 12 12 12
Irrigation 8 8 8
Fortress 16 16 16
Road 6 6 6
Railroad 12 12 12
Plant Forest 18 18 18
Clear Forest 4 10 10
Clear Wetlands / Clear Jungle 16 16 16
Clear Damage / Pollution 24 24 24
Airfield, Radar Tower, Outpost 1 1 — (PTW+)
Barricade 16 — (C3C only) —
  • C3C column: BIQ TFRM TurnsToComplete, for example Name=Clear Forest TurnsToComplete=4.
  • PTW and vanilla columns: read from the rule files themselves (BIQ-PTW Clear Forest turns=10, BIQ-V Clear Forest turns=10; the vanilla and PTW jobs are named "Clear Jungle" and "Clear Pollution"). They match Shiplord's PTW table, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/hi-how-many-turns-for-each-worker-action.80432 ("Clear forest 10"). tjs282 (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/new-city-build-order-worker-mgmt-and-railroads.655880/, 2020): "Forest-chopping takes longer in PTW (10 turns for a single non-IND Worker) than it does in C3C (4 turns)." That fits base × forest move cost 2 ÷ 2. KayEss's article (below) says "Clear forest 4 (Vanilla & PtW are 5)"; the rule files show 10, so his 5 is wrong (former Disputed 5).
  • EDHELP "Turns to Complete": "Determines the base rate (how many turns it takes) for workers to complete the selected worker job. This value can be further modified, depending on government type."

5.2 The formula

turns = base × terrain movement cost ÷ (government WorkerRate × worker strength/100 × trait × tech multipliers), with several workers pooling their work.

  • Apolyton (Nero Would, 19 Dec 2001), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/50097-worker-improvement-times: "To calculate the number of turns to complete a job, multiply the "effort" for the job by the basic Movement Cost"; "foreign workers only count as half a worker"; "The Work Rate is 1 for Anarchy, 3 for Democracy and 2 for all other governments."; "Also, divide by 2 for Industrious civs, divide by 2 if you have researched Replaceable Parts." The same page says the effect of roads and railroads on movement cost is ignored, so the base terrain cost is used.
  • EDHELP "Worker Rate (Government Properties)": "The "base" worker rate is 2. Each digit increases the rate by 50%. Thus, 1=50% work rate (bad), 2 = 100% (normal), and 3 = 150% (speedy workers!!)."
  • EDHELP "Worker Strength (Unit Statistics)": "If the Worker Strength value of a worker is set at "50" that worker works at "half" (50%) speed, and thus would take twice as long".
  • Observed data (DoubleEdged, CFC 80432, 1 Mar 2004, non-industrious, Ancient era) matches base × move cost ÷ 2: "Build Mine: (6 grasslands,12 hills,18 mountains)" and "Build Road: (6 in Forest,Hills; 9 in Mountains,Jungle; 3 grasslands)".
  • Jack Merchant (same thread): "'Base' turns is not for your regular non-industrious worker, but for slave workers (who work at half speed)."

Government worker rate (BIQ GOVT WorkerRate)

Government Anarchy Despotism Monarchy Communism Republic Democracy Fascism (C3C) Feudalism (C3C)
WorkerRate 1 2 2 2 2 3 4 2
PEDIA "Worker efficiency" 50% 100% — — — 150% 200% —

PEDIA #GOVT_Fascism: "Worker efficiency 200%". #GOVT_Democracy: "Worker efficiency 150%". #GOVT_Anarchy: "Worker efficiency 50%".

Other modifiers

  • Replaceable Parts. PEDIA #TECH_Replaceable_Parts: "{New Ability} Workers work twice as fast."
  • Captured or enslaved workers. README-C3C: "Note: Captured workers work at 50% the speed of regular workers." README-CMPL says the same. BIQ RULE.Slave=1 (Worker). EDHELP "Slave Unit": "settlers represent two population points, so a captured settler would convert into two workers."
  • Industrious. PEDIA #GCON_Strengths: "Workers complete tasks faster". The PEDIA gives no figure. Tested figure: ×1.5 in C3C, ×2 in vanilla and PTW. KayEss (Kirit Saelensminde), War Academy "Worker moves" (2004, tested in C3C), PDF https://civfanatics.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/civ3_acad_worker_moves.pdf : "The Industrious trait adds 50% to the amount of work that is done per worker turn. In Civilization III vanilla and Play the World this figure was 100%." Same PDF: "an ancient era worker for a non-industrious tribe will do two BWT per actual game turn (GT). For an industrious trait this changes to three." (Former Disputed 2.)

5.2a Worker points per turn, rounding and pooling (community-tested)

  • Per-worker integer output. justanick, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-many-workers-do-i-need-to-do-this.452798/ (C3C, Jan 2012): "Every worker produces every turn an integer number." "performance = (government factor) x (industrious factor) x (slave factor) x (spezific efficieny) x (replaceable part factor)", with "government factor is 1 for anarchy, 3 for democrazy, 4 for fascism and 2 for every other goverment", "industrious factor is 1.5 for industrious civs and 1 for every other civ", "slave factor factor is 0.5 for nonnational units and 1 for every national unit", "replaceable part factor is 2 if RP is researched and 1 when not". "The number is always rounded down, but the total minimum of 1 is never violated."
  • CIVWIKI Bautruppaufträge (Civ3) (rev. 54705, 2024) gives the same formula ("Bautruppleistung = Re × (Ind/100%) × (BT/100%) × (Eff/100%) × Ersatzteile") and the same rounding ("stets abgerundet", "always rounded down", never below 1).
  • Job cost. A job needs base turns × the tile's base movement cost in worker points; roads and railroads on the tile do not reduce it. KayEss PDF: "the base BWT is multiplied up by the movement penalty on the tile." and "building a road on grassland requires 6 BWT (1 x 6), for hills 12 BWT (2 x 6) and for a mountain 18 BWT (3 x 6)." CIVWIKI Bautruppaufträge lists the jobs for move cost 1/2/3 as, for example, "Bergwerk: 12 / 24 / 36" (mine) and "Straße: 6 / 12 / 18" (road). So the mountain multiplier is 3, not 4 (former Disputed 4).
  • Several workers pool their points into one job "bucket". KayEss PDF: "Ganging together a worker and a slave ... should be able to build a road on this flood land tile in 2 turns ([2 + 1] + [2 + 1] = 6 BWT)" and "Exactly as predicted the road is complete".
  • Single player: the stack finishes together and leftover points are lost. KayEss PDF: "In a single player game the dynamics are such that all workers in a stack will finish at exactly the same time even if this involves wasting worker effort." In multiplayer (PBEM) extra workers are freed as soon as the bucket is full. Also: "cancelling one of the workers cancels both their moves for that turn." The forum thread of the article (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/worker-moves.93408/page-2) disputes how much is lost: GD_france (21 Oct 2004): "when a worker is ordered to cancel working, the entire job he made is lost." KayEss (22 Oct 2004) then called his cancellation rules "mainly conjecture". Thot (11 Jul 2004): with a slave, "you save one basic worker turn (bwt), when you move to the tile to be roaded" (untested). Lanzelot, "Interturn Mechanics", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/interturn-mechanics.443195/ (C3C, Oct 2011), puts this work at the start of the interturn: "First all worker actions are calculated." City food, shields and border growth come later in the same interturn.
  • Captured workers and the Industrious trait (resolves former Disputed 3). With the formula above, an Industrious civ's slave makes 2 × 1.5 × 0.5 = 1.5, rounded down to 1, the same as a non-Industrious civ's slave. After Replaceable Parts it makes 3 against 2. This is why some players saw no Industrious bonus for slaves and others did. Spoonwood (thread 452798) adds that slaves "don't lose any speed pre Replaceable Parts" in Anarchy (1 × 0.5 = 0.5, raised to the minimum of 1).
  • Code reading agrees. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16784827, page-133 #2657): "The total number of points needed to complete a job equals the "turns to complete" set in the editor" (on a move-cost-1 tile; he does not mention the terrain multiplier) and "A typical worker contributes 2 points per turn".
  • Derived per-turn points (Spoonwood, same thread): "Slaves have a factor of 1 at 100% worker speed." "Regular workers have a factor of 2 at 100% worker speed." "Industrious workers have a factor of 3 at 100% worker speed."

5.3 Derived turns, one normal native worker, WorkerRate 2, no trait, before Replaceable Parts

This table is DERIVED from base × move cost ÷ 2. The cells marked "obs" match DoubleEdged's observed numbers.

Job Move cost 1 (Desert/Plains/Grassland/Tundra/Flood Plain) Move cost 2 (Hills/Forest/Marsh) Move cost 3 (Mountains/Jungle)
Road 3 (obs) 6 (obs) 9 (obs)
Irrigation 4 — —
Mine 6 (obs) 12 (Hills, obs) 18 (Mountains, obs)
Railroad 6 12 18
Fortress 8 16 24
Plant Forest 9 — —
Clear Forest — C3C 4 / PTW 10 (obs 10) —
Clear Wetlands — 16 (Marsh) 24 (Jungle, obs "Clear jungle: 24")
Clear Damage 12 24 36

6. Natural resources

6.1 Resource list (C3C BIQ GOOD)

  • Source: BIQ GOOD Type (0 = bonus, 1 = luxury, 2 = strategic), FoodBonus, ShieldsBonus, CommerceBonus, Prerequisite, AppearanceRatio, DisappearanceProbability, and the BIQ TERR→GOOD bit array. For example, Name=Iron ... Type=2 AppearanceRatio=160 DisappearanceProbability=800 Prerequisite=7 ShieldsBonus=1.
  • The PEDIA #TERR_* "may contain" lists match the terrain column exactly.
  • The PEDIA bonus tables (#GCON_ResourcesB/L/S) match BIQ, except that Dyes and Incense are swapped. See Disputed.
  • Version differences (BIQ-V, BIQ-PTW). Vanilla 1.29f and PTW 1.27f have 22 resources: no Sugar, Tropical Fruit, Oasis or Tobacco (those four are C3C additions). All shared resources have the same values as C3C except Incense, which is +1 commerce in vanilla and PTW (+2 in C3C). Dyes is +1 in all three versions. The vanilla and PTW Civilopedias (<civ3>/Text/Civilopedia.txt, <civ3>/civ3PTW/Text/Civilopedia.txt) both give "Dyes +0 +0 +1" and "Incense +0 +0 +1", so they match their rule files. The C3C Civilopedia changed Dyes to +2 instead of Incense.
  • README-129: "Game bonus resource now gives +2 food to its tile."
  • CFC Resource reference, https://civfanatics.com/civ3resources.shtml, gives the same prerequisites ("Rubber requires Replaceable Parts", and so on).

6.2 Resource rules

  • Placement count. EDHELP "Appearance Ratio": "In an eight-player game, a value of 160 would mean 2 of each resource per player. ... Setting this value to zero means the resource will be distributed at random. Note that resource placement is also limited by which terrain types can support the given resource." INFOC: "Appearance ratio has no effect on the bonus resources."
  • Disappearance. EDHELP "Disappearance Probability": "A value of 800 would mean that there is a 1 in 800 chance each turn to "lose" the resource. Setting this value to zero means the resource will never disappear. Note that this value is not influenced by use; a resource can disappear even if a civilization has never used the resource." and "Note 2: "Lost" resources will always re-appear somewhere else on the map, so the fundamental amount of resources in a game world will never drop below the amount set in the appearance ratio."
  • Only resources in use deplete (tested; engine ruling). Optional, "When do resources deplete? (Tested)", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/when-do-resources-deplete-tested.329362 (C3C, Jul 2009). Method: Iron's disappearance value set to 1 in the editor, then road, city connection, colony and unconnected roaded tile tried in turn. Findings: "A resource that can deplete, must be available to a city to stand a chance of depletion, being roaded alone is not enough." For a roaded tile inside the borders with no link to a town: "Then it does not deplete." On colonies (revised post #14): "if a resource is only available through a colony, it obviously cannot deplete." Bartleby disagreed from memory ("I've seen colonised resources deplete"), with no test.
