No civ has Settlers often. Byzantines are the only civ with five flags, above Ozymandias's tested 3-4 limit (3.2). alexman (vanilla, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/56961-1.htm, 25 Jul 2002) made the same check on the vanilla .bic: "none of them except the Iroquois have the Explore Units in their build-often list", and the build-never list is empty in the standard setup.

3. How the AI behaves

3.1 Architecture and production choice (Firaxis)

  • Leader AI and unit AI. A leader AI handles the high-level picture and unit AIs ask it questions. Soren Johnson, interview of 26 Oct 2001: https://apolyton.net/forum/apolyton-civilization-site-general/civilization-lost-history/255541-soren-johnson-civilization-iii-interview-2-26-oct-2001 says "A leader AI keeps track of high-level concepts, like whether enough science is being generated". The same interview, as quoted in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/41443-interview-soren-johnson-on-ai?p=926594, says "the unit AI controlling a tank might ask the leader AI which enemy city would be best to attack."
  • Production. The city picks whatever item scores highest. Soren (255541): "the city simply builds the item with the highest value."
  • Personality. Leaders are modelled on history. Soren (255541): "an aggressive ruler preferring despotism and offensive ground units".
  • How the production score works (code readings, C3X-THREAD, Flintlock).
    • "The AI chooses to build whatever has the highest point value." (post-16158745, page-21 #409). "it considers improvements and units separately but it considers all kinds of improvements, wonders or not, together." (post-16149215, page-19 #367).
    • Randomness: randomness "works by adding a random number of points to the AI's valuations of its options." / "IIRC, the random number is chosen between -5 and +5." (post-16360495, page-56 #1101).
    • A unit type it has enough of scores zero: "the AI assigns a value of 0 to catapults when it doesn't want any more" (post-16157851, page-21 #406); the same holds for workers (3.2). His view that "the AI likes happiness regardless of whether or not it needs any more" (post-16150396, page-20 #386) is, by his own account, a guess.
    • An undefended city skips the main loop: "It runs a special bit of logic to pick a defender to build and skips over the main loop", and (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the function with the main loop is 3000 lines alone" (post-16760037, page-131 #2614). There are "(annoyingly) multiple production chooser methods even for the regular civ AI" (post-16859226, page-150 #2996).
    • It ignores resources that buildings would generate: "The AI doesn't account for generated resources at all when deciding what to build." (post-16902815, page-166 #3314).
    • Civs differ little: "the AI is almost identical for all civs." (post-16095472, page-12 #228).
    • Wartime (community): Predator145 (post-16288091, page-39 #764): "During war time, military units get much higher build preference than peace time."
  • Naval and air. Soren (255541): "the AI is capable of launching naval invasions using a variety of land, sea, and air units". C3C added to this. LouLong (61791): "AI improvemement : picking bombardment targets; escorting key naval transports like carriers and transports."

3.2 Build-often / build-never flags (RACE) and unit AI strategies

  • The flags. RACE has three groups of flags:
    • Never flags in Flags[8..9]: Offensive, Defensive and Artillery land units, Settlers, Workers, Naval, Air, Growth, Production, Happiness, Science, Wealth, Trade, Explore and Culture.
    • Often flags in Flags[12..13], for the same categories.
    • Governor flags in Flags[4]: ManageCitizens, Emphasize Food/Shields/Trade, ManageProduction, NoWonders and NoSmallWonders. Source: QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Race.cs.
  • Small effect on units (tested, Ozymandias, 29 Nov 2023). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-unit-builds.686973 found the flags "Don't affect Unit Builds by more than ~5%". He also found "Selecting more than 3 (maybe 4) of these options will "confuse" the AI, causing it to ignore all of your selections". Predator145 (Jan 2025) gives a different, second-hand limit: "Alexmann who worked with Firaxis has confirmed many years ago that 6 is the max effective number". Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-build-often-building-categories.692300/
  • Categories overlap, and the AI favours shield buildings (Firaxis). Soren Johnson in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/56961-1.htm (25 Jul 2002, vanilla): "Trade = harbors/marketplaces/airports" and "Wealth = marketplaces/banks/workers, not just 'Wealth'"; the AI "preprocesses every unit/building to put them into multiple categories" (a cultural bank counts as both culture and wealth); "the AI does place a higher priority on shield-increasing building"; "I would recommend rarely using the Never flags". Asked whether courthouses and markets fall under production and happiness, he answered "actually, the answer is yes in both cases...". The differences between civs were "meant to give the AI's personality".
  • How unit strategies drive behaviour (Firaxis). Soren in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/39744-1.htm (13 Jan 2002): "The AI plans are basically there to allow modders (as well as myself) the ability to give units a "personality"."; "units with the "Land Offensive" plan use a separate function to determine their actions"; "a rifleman might decide to attack if the odds are in his favor"; "a city's governor will look at how many "offensive" and "defensive" units are in the city/continent"; "defensive units look to help defend offensive units out in the field".
  • Strategy prerequisites (Firaxis editor text). EDHELP "AI Strategy Rules" lists what a unit needs before it may carry each strategy, for example Offense: "Offensive Power > 0"; Artillery: "Bombard Strength > 0" and "Must have BOMBARD action"; Settle: "Must have BUILD CITY action"; Terraform (workers): every worker job action plus "Must have AUTOMATE action" and "Must have JOIN CITY action". Antal1987's thread https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/useful-patches-for-civ-3-conquests-v1-22.526285 lists as a known stock bug "worker units need all worker actions checked if it is to be used properly by the AI". AvalancheMaster, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/getting-the-ai-to-use-worker-units-without-all-worker-jobs-enabled.515992/ (Dec 2013), found the limit is the editor's, not the game's: "this is not hardcoded in the game, it's only in the editor." With the Terraform flag set by hex edit, the AI used the unit anyway; Kirejara confirmed it with another editor. So what the game reads is the strategy flag.
  • Unit AI strategies in the code (C3X-THREAD, Flintlock). "each unit type can only have one AI strategy." The AI keeps a count of units per strategy, and "the only time that count is ever decremented is when a unit is destroyed." (post-16531281, page-83 #1641). A unit with several strategies in the editor is stored as several copies in the BIQ (post-16375682, page-59 #1162). Which units need an escort is fixed in the executable: "it's hard-coded in the executable which unit types require escort." / (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "I haven't seen the requires escort flag used anywhere in the unit AI." (post-16553267, page-89 #1765). Civinator (post-16843785, page-145 #2892): "AI units without any strategy are "dead" in the game."
  • Which buildings each category covers (Predator145, 3 Oct 2024, thread 692300; community. Where it differs from Soren above, for Wealth, Soren's vanilla answer is the Firaxis one).
    • Growth: "Granary, Aqueduct, Hospital".
    • Production: Factory and power plants.
    • Science: buildings that multiply beakers.
    • Trade: "Improvements that give a gold income multiplier (market, bank, stock exchange)", plus Harbor and Airport.
    • Wealth: the shield-to-gold project.
    • There is no Military category: "there is no Military category to encourage the AI to build barracks/harbors/walls."
  • Unit mix (tested, Ozymandias, 686973, about 560 AI builds).
    • In peacetime the split is "D : O : other ~= 53% : 43% : 4%". The AI "likes" "between a 3:2 and 4:3 ratio of D:A units".
    • Artillery is about 1% of builds, and "the AI was proven not to take bombard into account."
    • The AI will "Re-Flag" a unit from offence to defence or back as it sees fit.
    • The AI keeps offensive and defensive units in balance ("YES" to "Does the AI seek equilibrium").
    • Builds are partly random, and difficulty and aggression did not change the mix.
  • Unit abilities the AI favours (tom2050, relayed in 686973): it "absolutely loves and adores" extra hit points. Blitz "would only increase the AI building them ... by around 4%."
  • Production is a point score per option (code). C3X-INI: "When choosing what to build, the AI computes a point value for each of its options ... point values are often in the hundreds for the most desirable options." The stock function is City_ai_choose_production (C3X-SRC civ_prog_objects.csv).
  • Artillery stays home. C3X-INI describes its own fix as letting "artillery unit AI to grab escorts from city defenders, causing the AI to use its artillery offensively instead of leaving it in its cities." So stock artillery waits in cities for an escort it does not get.
  • Leader and army bugs (code). C3X-INI on the stock leader AI: "a bug that allowed the AI to rush any kind of city production, including units and (with an MGL) wonders". On armies: "a major bug that prevented the AI from filling its armies" and "some logic that encouraged the AI to mix unit types in armies". This explains the "rare as a white rhino" armies (3.5). Third round (C3X-SRC patch_Unit_ai_move_leader): the stock leader AI first disbands a leader that is on a continent with no city of its civ ("Disband the unit if it's on a continent with no cities of its civ. This is what the original logic does.").
  • Workers: it stops building them once it has "enough" (code reading, Flintlock). C3X thread page 95, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/c3x-exe-mod-including-bug-fixes-stack-bombard-and-much-more.666881/page-95#post-16587093: the AI "assigns a point value of zero to the worker build option when it feels it has enough of them", and its need is computed from a city count (his option "adjusts the city count that the AI uses to evaluate how many workers it needs"). He believes it keeps none in reserve "except for pollution cleanup". Erlend Philip Wood, same page (post 16587268), observed: "The AI would always build a number of Workers equivalent to 80-90% of its number of cities at the peak of its development", and "Setting the 'Build often' flag for Workers makes absolutely no difference."
  • The human can steer governors. Soren (255541): you can "tell its governors to never build artillery and air units."

3.3 Settling: spacing, gaps and expansion

  • Early spacing of about 4 tiles. Dominae, 31 Mar 2002, writing about a 4-tile spacing plan: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/57896-early-gaem-deity-base-spacing-paradigm says "The AI pretty much follows this plan to the letter at all difficulty levels". Aeson, same thread: "I can sometimes build cities at a 5:6 ratio of what the AI does with 4 spacing."
  • Gaps that the AI later fills.
    • Brain, Mar 2005: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/other-civs-encrouching-on-my-borders.113953 says "The AI often leaves big gaps between their cities, which allows you to squeeze in."
    • The AI also fills empty land left inside a human's area. Grogs, same thread: the AI "just walked in and grabbed" an unclaimed area in the middle of his empire. Rohirrim: "If you can't block them completely, they just usually walk right past you and plop a city."
  • No settling in foreign borders or first-ring conflict.
    • Grogs (113953): "You can't build a city within another nation's borders, and the AI cannot build one within yours."
    • SJ Frank: "The AI will never settle a city that has first-ring conflict."
    • Exception: eldar says that if an AI is "reduced to a single settler, they will settle in place (if they can)".
  • Expansion.
    • Ision (92917) says the AI often builds settlers before its growth can support them.
    • "A sprawling Expansionist AI by the mid-Middle Ages is common".
    • The AI "will NEVER attempt a suicide run to find another land mass."
    • SirPleb (68813): "The AIs do not explore effectively, particularly large bodies of water."
    • With a revealed map the AI throws its scouts away. Fergei, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/suicidal-ai-scouts.660070/ (C3C, 30 Jun 2020): "my Expansionist AI Civs all kill / disband their scouts on the first turn"; Kirejara's explanation, which Fergei accepted: "With the map visible for the KI they see no need for scouting". The thread does not say which setting revealed the map; it is a single report.
  • Minimum distance (all players). The rule is one empty tile between cities. C3X-INI: "if it's set to 1, cities cannot be founded adjacent to one another, there must be at least one open tile separating them. A setting of 1 there corresponds to the standard game rules." Antal1987's patch notes (thread 526285, v1) also describe a stock limit that blocks a new city when the settler is "less than 2 tiles from an existing city". For the engine's AI, see the owner ruling at the top.
  • What the site score favours (code readings, C3X-THREAD, Flintlock). (post-16169633, page-23 #442): "the AI is programmed to find locations less desirable the farther they are from its already existing cities", "the AI's city placement is dominated by terrain (it loves grassland)", and "the AI almost completely ignores coastal resources and has no qualms about founding directly on a resource". (post-16149858, page-19 #375): "it doesn't consider how placing a city somewhere will affect the placement of others around it." (post-16831749, page-140 #2788): "There are no knobs inside the AI that control how quickly it expands other than its preference for producing settlers", and its city location evaluator steered it away from overlapping work areas. (post-16575327, page-93 #1841): "Under the standard game rules the AI would be better off packing more cities in closer together". He could not find where the AI is kept from founding inside its own borders (post-16843718, page-145 #2886: "it's not obvious in the AI's logic where this restriction is"). The owner's spacing ruling (top of section) is unaffected.
  • Site choice is a desirability score per tile (code). The stock function is Match_ai_eval_city_location (C3X-SRC civ_prog_objects.csv, signature int (... int x, int y, int civ_id, bool param_4, int * out_breakdown)). C3X-INI's debug overlay "Shades/highlights each tile to show how desirable the AI considers it as a city location". The scoring terms themselves are not published.
  • Settlers are a very strong AI build preference. C3X-INI: "The AI has a very strong tendency to build settlers so you'll need to set these options quite high to slow it down significantly. For example, a perfume of -200% over a 30 turn duration left the AI with about half as many cities after 50 turns in my test."
  • It fills every spot, good or bad (vanilla observations). DrFell, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42759-1.htm (19 Feb 2002, 1.17): "Watch them cover every free space on the board with a city, including completely unproductive tundra." solo, same thread: it founds cities "wherever spots are left until the map is full." Coracle, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/50345-1.htm (May 2002): "It wastes a lot of its resources sending out settlers to every crappy piece of land anywhere." solo (39845, Jan 2002): AI settlers went for an open horse or iron tile and ignored empty tiles without resources. =DrJambo= (48213, Apr 2002) on 1.21f: AI expansion was "Definitely toned down".
  • City limit. README-V 1.17f: "Fixed bug which allowed the AI to keep trying to build cities if the city limit had been reached." The stock limit is 512 (C3X-INI: "For base game behavior, set to 512.").
  • Settler escorts. Settlers do travel with escort relationships: C3X-INI, describing a stock freeze, says the disembark logic does "not consider escort relationships (e.g. for settlers)". How many escorts the AI gives a settler, and when, is not sourced (see UNVERIFIED). The thread on blocking AI settlers that an earlier search found is about Civ5.

3.4 War: when it declares

3.4.1 War plotting (code reading of stock C3C, FLINTLOCK-WAR)

The AI first decides to plot a war, then picks a target. justanick (#13): "It chooses to plot a war first and then chooses a target second."

  • The per-turn chance to start plotting. "Each AI player has a chance at the start of each turn to begin plotting war." Every roll below must pass:
    • "Roll one in (32 - PowerRank)" "where PowerRank = 31 if it's the strongest civ in the game, 30 if it's second, and so forth." "The power values used here are the same shown on the F8 histograph screen." (C3X-SRC struct Leader: int power_rank; // 31 = strongest, 30 = second strongest.)
    • "If the game is set to the lowest difficulty, roll one in 32"
    • "Roll one in (12 + -2 * EffectiveAggressionLevel)" (effective level: 1.3 and 2.1; range 8 to 16)
    • "If already at war, roll one in 16"
    • "If in anarchy, roll one in 8"
    • "Have a capital city"
    • "If have fewer than 2 * OCN cities and there are any open city spots on the capital's continent, roll one in 2"
    • "If in a government that experiences war weariness, roll one in 2"
    • "If do not have at least one defensive unit per city and one offensive unit per two cities, roll one in 2"
    • "If do not have at least two defensive units per city and one offensive unit per two cities, roll one in 2"
    • "If do not have at least two defensive units per city and one offensive unit per city, roll one in 2"
    • "If treasury is too low, roll one in 2 (too low here means < 16 per city" "plus more if the AI has any Wall Street type small wonders to ensure it's receiving the maximum interest possible)"
    • justanick's worked example (#6): strongest civ, aggression 0, a war-weariness government, a mid-sized military and a low treasury gives "in the end it is 1/48 for the most powerful tribe", and "the second most powerful tribe has a chance of only 1/96 per turn." He adds (#13): "Once AI has 2 defenders and 1 attacker per city it is eager to start wars, but not before that."
  • Who can be a target. "Other players with whom the AI has a mutual protection pact are excluded". "Each player has a cooldown timer that may exclude them. I believe this blocks war plotting within 8 turns of signing peace." (the 8 is his belief). "Lastly the AI randomly excludes some players": it rolls "not one in X", with "X = 1 to start", "X += 2 if the AI has a peace treaty with the target", "X += 2 for each common enemy", "X += 2 for each military alliance the AI and the target have". (Flintlock #8 corrected the direction: "This check is reversed from all the others.") "The AI will skip this check if the target is a human player who is ranked first in power."
  • Target score ("Higher values mean more attractive targets"; whether the AI takes the top score or a weighted pick is not stated):
    • "To start, X = 2 * (PlayerCount - 32 + PowerRank)." So stronger targets score higher. Flintlock: "So AIs are more inclined to attack stronger opponents. Not sure if this is a bug."
    • "If the AI has a capital, X = X + 64 // NearestCityDistance" (distance from the AI's capital to the target's nearest city). justanick: "Notably this does not scale with map size."
    • "X = X + DiploEffect where DiploEffect = 8 if furious toward the target, 4 if annoyed, 2 if cautious, 1 if polite, and 0 if gracious."
    • Land units per city of the target (units with the offensive or defensive strategy // cities): "X = X + 4 if LandUnitsPerCity is 0, + 2 if it's 1, + 1 if it's 2, and + 0 if it's 3 or more."
    • "If target is in anarchy, X = X + 1"
    • "If the target is a human player ranked top in power and twice as powerful as the second-ranked player, X = 2 * X"
    • "If the AI has a resource trade with the target, X = 3 * X // 4"; "For each common enemy that the AI and the target share, X = 2 * X // 3"; "If the AI and the target do not share a continent, X = X // 2"; "If the target has a peace treaty or mutual protection pact with any player, X = X // 2."; "If the AI has a peace treaty or mutual protection pact with the target, X = X // 2."; "X = X // (AllianceCount + 1)".
    • Past war damage: "If DamageBalance > 0, X = X // (1 + DamageBalance // max(1, AI's city count))", where the balance is damage the target dealt to the AI minus damage the AI dealt to the target.
    • Flintlock's summary: "the AI tends to declare war on targets that are stronger and closer, that it's angry with".
  • Human-player bias is in the stock code. C3X-INI offers to remove "the AI's special preference for targeting human players when plotting wars. Rules for targeting runaway players still apply". The bias is the two human checks above (no random exclusion for a top-ranked human; doubled score for a runaway human).
  • Unit quality does not deter. The rolls and the score count units per city, not their strength. justanick (#20): "Having units stronger than warriors offers no deterrence whatsover against AI plotting a war against you." Kokuryutei (#19) disagrees without a source.
  • From plotting to declaration. Not in the code reading. Lanzelot (#16), tested "a few years ago" at Emperor or Deity: "nothing can revert that decision, once taken" (MPPs, alliances, trades, gifts and embargoes all failed). Southern_Man (#14) and Fergei (#15) guess about 4 turns from plotting to the declaration; Ruin (#17) speculates that a plot "times out" if the AI cannot attack. Fergei: "The AI will declare war on you, even if you have an MPP".
  • Other ways into war (code).
    • A refused demand or a nuke on a third party (aggression-based chance, 2.1). C3X-SRC names the stock Leader_ai_roll_to_declare_after_provocation. The gates that decide when the AI makes a demand at all (power place, attitude and aggression rolls, war-damage balance, a 32-turn cooldown per target) are in 06-diplomacy-and-espionage.md 6.9, from C3X-SRC's re-creation of the stock sequence. The war roll after a refusal is skipped when the two have a mutual protection pact.
    • The submarine bug. C3X-INI: "The AI runs into invisible units (such as submarines) belonging to other players because it blindly follows paths produced for it by the game's pathfinder ... After colliding with an invisible unit, the AI initiates combat, declaring war if necessary." README-V 1.16f: "The AI no longer reacts to submarines that it cannot see."
  • When the AI accepts peace (Firaxis). Soren Johnson (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/41866-1.htm, post #27, 11 Feb 2002, vanilla): "The AI's willingness to discuss peace terms depends on a) how long the war has lasted and b) how much it is winning or losing the war." The time factor is "there to prevent people from war-jacking the AI by continually declaring war and then agreeing to lop-sided peace agreements." Full entry in 06-diplomacy-and-espionage.md 6.5.
  • Chokepoints (Firaxis). Mike Breitkreutz (Firaxis programmer), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/89862-1.htm (20 Jun 2003): "The AI has always been programmed to seize chokepoints..."; on PTW 1.21f: "I don't believe any changes were made to the AI for 1.21f."
3.4.2 Community observations

These are community observations from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/when-does-ai-declare-war.244088 (several agree with the code above: power, attitude and open land):

  • MAS: "The most important factor is probably military strength."
  • Nergal: "General attitude, ie annoyed or furious are way more likely to attack than Cautious." Also "The Zulus and Germans are pretty much always up for a war" (aggression +2 in RACE).
  • Lord Emsworth: "If the AI gets something from you on per turn basis (esp.for free) it is extremely unlikely that you will be attacked." He also says that tribute demands follow aggression.
  • rysingsun: "they are far less likely to declare war if you have rop with them"; "they don't declare much when there is lots of land still to be settled".
  • civverguy: "they move a couple units in your borders and declare war on you the next turn."
  • I. Larkin: "if AI have reach the point that they can't support their military anymore they declare war no matter what..."
  • Risa (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/102345-1.htm, C3C, 23 Nov 2003): "Once an AI runs out of space to expand, it's almost guaranteed to be involved in wars within 20 turns." This fits the code roll that halves the plotting chance while open city spots remain on the capital's continent (3.4.1).
  • Aeson (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/39845-2.htm, 22 Jan 2002, vanilla): "The AI is actually more willing to declare war if you have something they want and won't trade it to them." This fits the demand path (a refused demand can lead to war), not the plotting code.
  • Several PTW 1.21 players (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/89030-1.htm, Jun 2003) say the AI declares war when it can no longer pay a per-turn deal. alva: "If the AI goes bankrupt, he will declare war on you, yes."; Blaupanzer: "the AI will declare war on a civilizatioin if it is no longer capable of fulfilling a per turn deal with it." dexters calls the idea that the AI ignores its per-turn deals "another myth". No test or code reading; the plotting rolls above have no deal term besides the resource-trade score cut.
  • Units in their territory. The AI first asks you to leave. If you are still there the next turn, it can force you out or go to war. This is a forum summary from the search for "units in our territory" and has not been checked against a primary quote (the turn count is UNVERIFIED). The two-step order (ask, then "leave or declare war") matches the AI Attitude Study in 06-diplomacy-and-espionage.md 6.7 (asked to leave +1; told to leave or declare war +4). Players can make the same request: README-PTW fixed a "bug where you could not ask an AI civ to remove its units from your territory." notyoueither (Apolyton 102345, C3C, Nov 2003): "1 out of 3 or 4 times the AI will respond to demands to leave my territory with a DoW." (one player's count)

3.5 War: how it fights

  • Piecemeal attacks and poor artillery use. Ision (92917): AI attacks are "piecemeal", and "the AI has no concept of proper artillery use". AI armies are "rare as a white rhino".
  • Artillery mostly stays in cities (C3C). From https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/121250-does-the-computer-use-siege-units-in-conquests:
    • Jeem: "The AI does 'attempt' to use ground artillery offensively now, but it's still pretty rare".
    • ChrisiusMaximus: "they still seem to keep them holed up in their cities, and only seem to use them when I attack as a defensive strike."
    • Catt: "the AI won't usea stack of 10+ artillery units, with appropriate defense, to lay siege to a city".
  • Heavy garrisons, defensive escorts, quick naval retreat, small landings. Ision (92917): the AI keeps heavy garrisons in every city and escorts its offensive stacks with many defensive units. It retreats ships to port after minor damage, and its amphibious invasions use too few units.
  • AI-vs-AI wars. Ision: they are "wars of attrition" that drain the winner too.
  • Naval transport escorts (code). C3X-INI: "In the base game, the AI will assign 1, 2, or 3 escort units to its naval transports and carriers depending in their shield cost and the number of units they're carrying."
  • Transports wait for each other; damaged units in your land keep fighting (1.29f observations). https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/59444-1.htm (Aug 2002): Catt saw an AI transport and escort that "waited 3 tiles off-shore" until a second transport joined, and "knocking a single HP off of a transport vessel doesn't result in the transport heading home for repairs". DeepO: "when an AI troop is in your territory, it will not retreat when damaged", and "there are very few improvements to the naval AI" in 1.29f. Jon Shafer (Firaxis), relaying Soren on 1.29f: "all he told me was that he changed the AI offensive/defensive 'bug'."
  • Naval play improves when isolated (PTW 1.27f observation). dexters, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/96269-1.htm (5 Sep 2003): an island AI "promtly sent a fleet of galleys" and landed units over four landings; "it was not uncommon to see civs stuck in islands totally neglect their navies" in his earlier debug games.
  • The "disembark all" freeze (code). C3X-INI: "When using "disembark all" (which the AI always does) on a boat containing units that can't be disembarked, the game" can freeze. So AI landings always unload everything.
  • Multiple stacks (PTW observation). Jaybe, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/84396-1.htm (27 Apr 2003): "Multiple stacks, occasionally diverging to separate targets"; "They still try to enter at the most advantageous entry point". alexman (Firaxis) in the same thread lists "fewer SOD's" among the PTW changes.
  • The unit AI in the code (C3X-THREAD, Flintlock, C3C 1.22).
    • Little planning, no coordination: "Internally the unit AI does little to no analysis or planning, it just performs basic reactions to its surroundings." (post-16163990, page-22 #429). "there is no unit stack AI in Civ 3" / "It's every unit for itself"; "one unit may have multiple escorters but an escorting unit may only have one escortee" (post-16444427, page-73 #1455). "the unit AI for the attackers never considers artillery and vice-versa" (post-16898219, page-164 #3269). A better unit AI exists in the executable but is unused: "the game already includes a half decent unit AI except it's never activated in the base game" (post-16270971, page-34 #668).
    • Artillery: "arty units in a city will never leave that city unless there are too many of them in it" (post-16075105, page-8 #150); "AI artillery will only path toward an enemy city" (post-16729166, page-124 #2476); "The reason AI artillery leaves its stack is because its escorting unit was destroyed" (#1455); captured artillery "must return to a city so it can be assigned an escorter" (#3269). He thinks the stock swarm without artillery "may actually be superior" (post-16937054, page-177 #3521).
    • Armies: "There are two bugs that prevent the AI from using armies in the base game." (post-16111169, page-13 #258); the add-to-army filter "failed to consider that armies have an extra move" and "The reason the AI doesn't attack high health armies is that each unit is processed individually" (post-16304748, page-40 #795).
    • Naval escorts and invasions: "Normally the AI will assign between 1 and 3 escorts for each of its naval transports" (post-16441638, page-71 #1419), and "The AI almost always ends up assigning three escorts to each transport." (post-16857204, page-150 #2981), the only place it weighs unit shield costs. The escort check ignores that escorts are damaged if they can't heal in place (post-16102748, page-12 #233). Loading: "units load up for a naval invasion if they happen to find themselves in a city with a naval transport and have nothing better to do." / (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "There's no way for them to seek out a transport, which is why the AI often sends out transports that are only partially filled." (post-16341094, page-51 #1018). "The AI can't transport caravans between continents. It can't even transport workers between continents, as far as I know." (#2981). It never uses land transports (page-165 #3287) or loads explorers (page-169 #3365).
    • Air: "the AI's fighters and bombers do not coordinate at all" / "the AI does not remember where or when it's been bombed." (post-16523964, page-82 #1623). "the AI doesn't build many air units unless it's triggered to do so by getting bombed itself." (post-16217348, page-30 #586, his belief). The AI has no rebase validity check (post-16783087, page-133 #2653). Cruise missiles: "The cruise missile AI looks for a target within bombard range and attacks if it finds one or otherwise fortifies in place." (post-16089946, page-11 #211). Nukes: it "considers the value of resources on a tile when deciding whether to nuke it" (post-16965737, page-185 #3694). Predator145 (post-16393228, page-63 #1248, play report): the AI will not use its nukes unless it has been hit by one.
    • Tactics it lacks: ZOC ("I don't think the AI understands ZoC at all", post-16537815, page-84 #1671) and stealth attack (post-16853988, page-148 #2959; section 1 §1.1).
    • Disorder: "The AI won't let its cities disorder" (post-16929609, page-174 #3467). Earlier, more hedged (post-16844914, page-146 #2910): "For the AI, I don't think I've ever seen it leave a city in disorder for multiple turns", with an exception for extreme war weariness.
  • Patrol. README-PTW (1.26f): "the AI will no longer use its units to randomly patrol if they have nothing else to do" when the INI option NoAIPatrol is 1; "The default is 0 (meaning the AI will patrol)."

3.6 Workers, sliders, government, trade

  • Too few workers. Ision (92917): "the AIs never-ever build anywhere near enough workers." See 3.2 for the code reading (zero value once it has "enough") and the 80-90%-of-cities observation. vmxa (CFC "No Settlers, No Workers?", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/no-settlers-no-workers.704355/, C3C, Aug 2026, an explanation from play, not a test): "It is not uncommon for AI to be starved of workers on Sid" because "They can build units so cheaply that they hit their support cap and start disbanding workers."
  • Worker habits (Firaxis, PTW). alexman ("Firaxis Games Software Engineer"), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/84396-1.htm (26 Apr 2003), on PTW 1.21f against 1.29f: "The AI no longer sells workers for just 30 gold, crippling themselves and helping the human" (now about 120 gold), and "The AI no longer irrigates grassland tiles in Despotism, unless there is a good reason to do so". README-V 1.17f: "Fixed AI worker loop bug." dexters, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/88537-1.htm (Jun 2003), saw the AI disband workers "rathern than sell off its improvements when it has 0 gold in the treasury". joncnunn (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/76212-1.htm, PTW, 5 Feb 2003) saw the same change in play: "Under the latest PTW patch, the AI stops building irrigation on grassland under despotism." Automated workers put pollution first: Harovan (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/59554-1.htm, Aug 2002): "Cleaning pollution is high on their priority list".
  • Out of money (code). Corrected in the third round. The C3X-INI passage quoted here before (sell buildings, then disband units, then switch to Wealth; "For AI players, the game alternates the order of (1) and (2) each turn"; the research cut every third turn) describes the mod option aggressively_penalize_bankruptcy (default false), not stock. C3X changelog.txt: "Option to aggressively penalize bankrupt players" (Release 14) and "aggressively_penalize_bankruptcy now alternates between selling buildings or disbanding units first for bankrupt AI players and every third turn cuts their research spending". With the option off, C3X-SRC calls the stock Leader_pay_unit_maintenance. What the code does show for stock: building and unit upkeep are paid in random order (C3X-SRC comment: "the randomization of whether improv or unit maintenance gets paid first"), and the stock AI slider routine already cuts spending ("ai_adjust_sliders will already cut AI spending but maybe not all the way to zero"). See 02-city-economy.md §7.
  • AI workers in the code (C3X-THREAD, Flintlock). "for irrigating and mining it orders its workers around "manually"", "it will never tell a worker to replace a mine with irrigation or vice-versa", and the stock bug "The bug is that the logic to handle the despotism penalty uses the scaled yields instead of the raw yields." (post-16191224, page-25 #494). The irrigate-or-mine choice is one yes/no function (post-16563260, page-90 #1793: "It returns only true or false, if true the tile will be irrigated and otherwise it will be mined."). Radar towers: workers "consider every tile on their continent that could have a radar tower" and build on the highest-valued one (post-16589155, page-96 #1907). Predator145 (post-16094435, page-12 #225, play report): the AI does not clear marshes, build airfields or irrigate mined plains after Electricity.
  • Sliders are set by code each turn (code). C3X-SRC: the stock "preproduction player update method" makes a "human-ness test and call to Leader::ai_adjust_sliders" for AI players, and "The rate cap limits AI players' spending on the gold slider (in Leader::ai_adjust_sliders)", where the cap is the government's rate cap. The code reading does not say how the split is chosen.
  • Code reading: the stock AI does move the luxury slider, too little. Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16797044, page-135 #2687): the AI's slider use "it's not aggressive enough on the luxury slider". That corrects Ision's "will NOT use" below, and is consistent with alexman's "best the AI can do without using the luxury slider" if the AI used it little in his test. The owner ruling (the AI keeps the slider) stands.
  • Tiles: every city for itself (code reading). Flintlock (post-16813639, page-136 #2702): "The AI never, as far as I know, considers how to distribute tiles among multiple cities." / "It's every city for itself, choosing the strongest among all the ones it can work." For every player (post-16848272, page-147 #2928): the game "never considers which city would be the best to work a given tile."
  • Upgrades: a gold reserve gates them (code reading). Flintlock first wrote (post-16168856, page-22 #439, "AFAIK") that the AI always upgrades when it can; he corrected this later (post-16937054, page-177 #3521): the upgrade pass is "behind a weird little check", "The reserve amount is 16 gold per city plus enough to earn the AI full interest income" "if it has any wonders that grant that", and "the AI will not upgrade anything when it's below the minimum gold reserve even if the upgrade is free." He adds (post-16583488, page-95 #1884): "the AI isn't smart enough to move its obsolete units to the cities where it has barracks". The later reading supersedes the first.
  • Rush-buying: improvements only, above a reserve (code reading). Flintlock (post-16787843, page-134 #2662): "Its reserve target is 1000/number_of_interest_generating_wonders + 16 * number_of_cities", and "it won't rush an improvement if doing so would leave its treasury below that level." The check is whether rushing "would reduce the AI's treasury enough to reduce its income from interest" (post-16783087, #2653). Spoonwood (post-16286783, page-39 #763, observation): "The AI also does not appear to cash rush anything."
  • Selling buildings: one routine, for replacing power plants (code reading). Flintlock (post-16787843, page-134 #2662): "There's only one routine where the AI considers selling city improvements"; per the thread notes it weighs replacing a plant on production bonus, pollution and upkeep only (summary wording). The production chooser makes the same assumption (post-16823297, page-136 #2720): "the AI's logic for choosing production assumes that buildings with the replaces-others flag are powerplants." Predator145 (post-16345873, page-52 #1031): (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "The AI will not sell obsolete improvements and will only lose them if they can't pay maintenance."
  • Luxury slider. Ision: the AI "will NOT use the luxury slider to compensate for any happiness issues". Instead it uses entertainers. alexman's PTW debug test, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/66928-1.htm (7-8 Nov 2002; six one-city islands): AI cities were "Always irrigating like crazy to compensate for the lack of food from assigning entertainers"; city governor settings "made no difference"; "I think this is the best the AI can do without using the luxury slider." Owner ruling: the engine's AI keeps the slider (top of section).
  • Research rate (vanilla observations). https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/42759-1.htm (Feb 2002): Pius Popprasch saw AI research "remains stable at 40% no matter what you do"; solo saw rates "range from 70% down to 30%". Harovan: the AI does not pay gold per turn when its own balance is not positive.
  • Tech choice: no flavors in the stock game (Firaxis). alexman (Firaxis), https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/102278-1.htm (22 Nov 2003): of C3C's tech flavors, "None of them are currently used" in the epic game; flavors "can't make the AI want to research something more than if it didn't have any flavors"; with every item at 50% the AI researched in the same order as with none. donZappo: "the flavors are not incorporated at all into the epic game." EDHELP "Flavor Page": flavors are "a way to modify how non-human (or AI) civilizations choose which paths to develop along the technology tree". The stock tech valuation is Leader_ai_eval_technology (C3X-SRC); C3X-INI: each point of tech value is "worth roughly one gold in trade, and also affects what the AI chooses to research". README-V 1.17f: "Fixed AI bug giving value to worthless techs."
  • Changes government often. Ision: "Changing governments is a favorite AI past time, doing so FAR too often through the course of a game."
  • Government switching rules (chiefpaco, 27 Apr 2002). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/about-governments.21298
    • A peaceful AI in Monarchy or Communism "often switches to Democracy or Republic".
    • After a long war it "switches to Comm or Monarchy".
    • "If the AI discovers a new govt while in Desp, it switches."
    • chiefpaco: "I think it is entirely based on War & war weariness."
    • It is unclear whether the favourite government is a goal or only affects attitude. Propaganda said that even with Democracy shunned, "all of them always choose Democracy." (see Disputed)
  • Favourite and shunned government (Firaxis editor text and code).
    • EDHELP "Favorite Government": "the civilization will immediately switch to this government type as soon as it becomes available." EDHELP "Shunned Government": the civ "will avoid this government type at all costs, and if it is forced to operate under this government type, it will immediately switch to another government type as soon as another government type becomes available."
    • They also feed attitude. FLINTLOCK-WAR: "if the other player is in the AI's shunned government, it adds 5 points" (higher is worse). 06-diplomacy-and-espionage.md 6.7 has the attitude study's -5/+4.
    • The choice is a score per government. C3X-SRC hooks a stock Leader_ai_eval_government; C3X-INI: "I don't know how perfuming governments affects the AI's willingness to overthrow its current govt. The perfume for sure applies when the AI is selecting a new govt type after anarchy." So the stock AI picks its new government by score after anarchy; how the favourite enters that score is not published. See also 05-governments.md 5.8.
    • Code reading of the score (C3X-THREAD, Flintlock): points in gold per turn, with the tile penalty worth 8 per city, corruption levels 12/8/4/4/2 per city, and "Shunned/favorite government is worth 12.5%" (post-16217016, page-30 #583; full quotes in 05-governments.md 5.8).
    • Tavis (Firaxis), quoted in 05-governments.md: free Feudalism maintenance in testing meant "the AI wouldn't switch from it!!", which shows the switch decision weighs the government's economy.
  • Scientific AIs broker techs. Ision: they gain techs and gold by trading techs on, and often end up with the most gold. Commercial AIs "rarely translates its inherent gold advantage out properly."
  • Early tech trading. eric2075 (68813): "the AI won't trade techs very easily in the early game either."
  • Lopsided deals (Firaxis). Soren Johnson, quoted in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/56854-1.htm (Jul 2002): under a "renegotiate every 20 turns" rule, "if you had a lopsided deal with the AI, IT would decide to renegotiate the deal." In practice badams52 (same thread, 1.21) found that after the AI connected a resource it had bought, "he wont ask to renegotiate the deal with me".

3.7 "The AI never / rarely does X" (community)

Observation Source / quote
Few or no armies Ision (92917): armies are "rare as a white rhino"
Coordinated artillery sieges Catt (121250), quoted in 3.5
Sending ships across ocean to explore Ision (92917): "will NEVER attempt a suicide run to find another land mass"
Ending a galley's turn where it could sink Soren via TheNiceOne (27273): "an AI galley will never end a turn where it could sink."
Building Barracks, Walls or Harbors in time (no Military category) Predator145 (692300)
Using artillery in the field C3X-INI (3.2): stock artillery is left "in its cities"
Filling armies C3X-INI (3.2): "a major bug that prevented the AI from filling its armies"
Weighing unit quality in war plans FLINTLOCK-WAR (3.4.1): only units per city count
Building Explorers alexman (56961, vanilla): "I have never seen the AI build one."
Using leaders properly C3X-INI (3.2): the leader AI can rush "any kind of city production"
Upgrading most units a modder in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-auto-producing-units-but-wont-upgrade.692035/ (search summary): it upgrades "occasionally, but has a habit of leaving most as the near useless initial unit"
Coming back from behind Ision (92917): the AI is "almost always incapable of 'coming back from behind.'"

4. Victory conditions (C3C defaults)

Victory Condition Source / quote
Conquest Defeat all other civs CFC FAQ https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "defeat all other civilizations"
Domination 66% of the land and 66% of the population. Ocean tiles don't count. Sea tiles count for score but not for domination. BIQ-C3C GAME DominationTerrain=66, DominationPopulation=66. PEDIA-V: "Have 66% of the world's land surface within your borders as well as 66% of the world's population within your cities." MANUAL p.31: "conquering and controlling two-thirds of the world's territory". PEDIA-PTW and PEDIA-C3C say "70%" instead; the C3C rule file says 66, so the PTW/C3C text is out of date (or never matched). FAQ: "66% of the land AND population". BIQ GAME DominationTerrain / DominationPopulation (Game.cs). https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/maximizing-your-score/: "Ocean does not matter here, it is not included in your territory nor in the domination limit." and "Sea is important though – it counts toward increasing your territory but does not count towards the domination limit."
Space race Build all 10 spaceship parts after the Apollo Program. The game is won at launch, which follows the tenth part; there is no flight time. Capturing (or razing) the builder's capital before launch destroys its ship, and it may then build a new one. Each civ builds one ship at a time. In the C3C BIQ, Apollo Program is a Small Wonder (isSmallWonder: true, 500 shields, buildSpaceshipParts, BIQ (repo)); RULE NumberOfSpaceshipParts=10 (BIQ-C3C). MANUAL p.174: "The competition ends when either you or one of your opponents launches a spaceship to Alpha Centauri with colonists. The civilization that wins the race to launch wins the game." and "You can construct a second spaceship only if your current one is destroyed--that is, if your capital city is captured while your ship is under construction (the conquerors destroy it on the launch pad)." PEDIA-C3C #GCON_Victory: "the player must be the first to launch a completed spaceship". README-V 1.17f: "Fixed bug which did not destroy an enemy spaceship if the enemy capital was razed." SCRIPT #DESTROYEDSPACESHIP: "We have destroyed the $CIVADJ0 spaceship!" DickK (https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/35851-1.htm, Nov 2001): "once you finish the last part you trigger the end game sequence whether you want it or not". FAQ: "build all 10 required spaceship parts"
Diplomatic (UN) The UN builder is offered an election and may decline it (every 11 turns per the FAQ). Candidates: the UN builder, and any civ with 25% of the land or population; if only one is eligible, the most populous other civ joins. One vote per civ, and winning needs a majority of all votes; otherwise "Election was inconclusive". Details in 06-diplomacy-and-espionage.md 6.12. PEDIA-C3C: "There are always two, and sometimes three, candidates", "have built the UN", "control at least 25% of the world's territory", "control at least 25% of the world's population", "If only one civilization is eligible, then the second candidate is the civilization with the largest population." SCRIPT #DIPLOVICTORYVOTEOPTION: "Would you like to hold elections for UN Secretary-General?"; #NODIPLOVICTORY: "Election was inconclusive; no majority was found." FAQ: "a majority of the votes in the election"; "25% of the land OR population"; "every 11 turns"
Cultural (C3C) One city with 20,000 culture, or a whole-civ total that depends on map size: 60k Tiny, 80k Small, 100k Standard, 130k Large, 160k Huge. The whole-civ total must also be at least twice any rival's; the one-city win has no such condition in the rule text. PEDIA-V and PEDIA-PTW #GCON_Victory: "A city with a Cultural value of 20,000 or if your civilization is worth 100,000 and at least twice as much as any rival, you win." PEDIA-C3C drops the "twice" clause ("A single city with 20,000 culture points will satisfy this victory condition. For your overall culture to do so, a variable number of total culture points is needed, based on map size"), but C3C players still report it: Twonky, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/guide-to-cultural-victory.124277/ (Jul 2005): "Remember you need twice the culure of the next best civ in order to win."; keldr, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/100-k-culture-requirements.356991/ (2010): the values "depend on You having at least twice as much culture as #2". BIQ-C3C GAME OneCityCultureWin=20000, AllCitiesCultureWin=100000 (the Standard figure). FAQ: "have either 20,000 culture in one city, or 100,000 culture across your empire"; in Conquests "60,000 for a Tiny map, 80,000 for Small, 100,000 for Standard, 130,000 for Large, and 160,000 for Huge." SirPleb (104294): "The required amount of culture for the "100K" cultural victory condition is variable in C3C, depending on the map size." BIQ GAME OneCityCultureWin / AllCitiesCultureWin (Game.cs).
Histographic (time) Highest score at the end of the game (2050 AD on default timescale) PEDIA-C3C: "[Civilization III] runs until the year 2050."; "The civilization that generated the highest score -- on average -- wins. Being powerful in Ancient Times is just as valuable as being powerful in the Modern Era." SCRIPT #TIMELIMIT: "WINNER BY SCORE: $CIVNAME1". FAQ: "have the highest score at the end of the game"
Wonder victory (C3C, editor) Build all wonders. It is off by default. It ends the game like a time limit: the game ends when the last Great Wonder is built and the winner is decided as at the time limit. Thunderfall (104294): "Add Wonder Victory (all wonders built)"; repo allowWonderVictory: false; BIQ-C3C GAME WonderVictory off. README-C3C: "The "Wonder Victory" is actually a Time Limit type of victory condition; i.e., when the Wonder Victory is in effect, the game ends immediately upon completion of the last Great Wonder."

C3C's F8 screen shows the domination limit and how far the leader is with its spaceship. Arathorn (104294): "F8 now shows domination limit, spaceship parts completed by the leader, and a lot of other win condition information."

  • Spaceship parts: one city per part. README-V 1.16f: "Palaces and spaceship parts can only be built in one city at a time." Freiherr, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/37264-1.htm (Dec 2001), on the wording: "two or more cities will no longer be allowed to build the same space ship part". PEDIA-C3C: "The components may be built in any order." Seeing rival parts needs a spy: README-V 1.17f: "Spaceship parts pop-ups now only appear if you have a spy with the corresponding civ."
  • Mass Regicide, Elimination, Regicide, Victory Points and Capture the Princess are out of scope (header).
  • Respawn AI Players. MANUAL p.31: "When this option is checked, computer-controlled civilizations that are eliminated early in the game are "reborn" as new civilizations." README-V 1.29f renamed "Restart Players" to "Respawn AI Players". War Academy, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/misc/strategy-for-emperor-and-deity-levels/: after an early wipeout "the computer will respawn that AI, with ADDITIONAL techs, and a new settler". GePap, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/36170-1.htm (Nov 2001): "Killing them means destroying all cities and all settlers." The stock routine is Leader_create_city_for_ai_respawn (C3X-SRC). How "early" is defined is not sourced. Civinator (C3X-THREAD post-16707594, page-121 #2417) lists (paraphrase from a summarised fetch) "the normal C3C respawn logic": the civ gets "1 town, 3 defensive units, 1 offensive unit, 1 settler, 1 worker, 100 additional gold" plus its difficulty start units; there is "Only one respawn of each civ in a game" (post-16708435, page-122 #2422); and the option must be on when the game starts (post-16699411, page-120 #2396). He adds conditions on the site (its old continent, a tile without culture, no foreign unit or border within about 5 tiles; summary wording, not checked). Flintlock (post-16929609, page-174 #3467): "The reason AI players don't get respawned is that there's no space for them."
  • Elimination edge cases (stock bugs). A civ whose last city is lost survives while it has a settler at sea (the "houseboat" bug): Civinator (post-16083900, page-10 #182) says it does not happen with a settler on land, and ynot56 (post-16085984, page-11 #201): "settlers on land are eliminated when the last city of a civilization is taken." Flintlock (post-16084386, page-10 #183) found the related crash: it happens "whenever the civ's stored capital ID doesn't correspond to an actual city." Lord_Hill (post-16224617, page-30 #596): "if your first and only city is destroyed by a volcano, you automatically win by conquest!" These are bugs; the intended rule is elimination when a civ has no cities and no settlers (land or sea), and no win from losing your own last city.
  • Engine limits (code readings, C3X-THREAD, Flintlock). "The 31 civ + barbs limit is baked very deeply into the game, it's even baked into the BIQ format" (post-16888798, page-159 #3163). "The unmodded game's limit of 8192 units is arbitrary." (post-16606824, page-102 #2039). The turn limit is capped at 1000 (page-106 #2110). Cities: 512 (02-city-economy.md §11). In hotseat play "all human players move before any AIs" (post-16384097, page-62 #1222).
  • Retiring. README-V 1.16f: "Retiring from a Conquest only game no longer is recorded as a victory."

5. Score

5.1 Per-turn score and final score

  • Per-turn score. (Territory + 2*HappyCitizens + ContentCitizens + Specialists) * Difficulty
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/maximizing-your-score/ gives this formula. Territory: "“Territory” is the number of tiles which are within your sphere of influence."
    • CFC FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/) gives the same formula.
    • SirPleb, 20 Jan 2002: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/counting-final-points.14617 says "Multiply the total by the difficulty level." He counts 1 per tile, 2 per happy citizen and 1 per content citizen or specialist. He calls it his understanding from testing.
  • Final score is the average of the per-turn scores. Maximizing-your-score: "The total of all your per-turn scores is divided by the number of turns played so far to get your actual game score." So late changes do little. col, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/increasing-final-score.37091: "Unlike Civ2 where your score is calculated at the end, this doesnt help much."
  • Things that don't count.
    • Maximizing-your-score: "Wonders, cultural value, military size, science learned, etc. – none of these affect the score."
    • Bamspeedy (37091): "score doesn't take into account improvements, wonders, great leaders, diplomacy, etc."
    • Future techs are the one small exception. Maximizing-your-score: "it counts for so little in score that it is not worth devoting any energy to it."
  • Each future tech counts like one content citizen in every later turn (tested). Bamspeedy, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/scoring-of-future-tech.31261 (2 Sep 2002, after 1.29f), with a save edited to start with every tech and Future Tech 1 learned in 2950 BC on Deity: "1 FUTURE TECH IS WORTH 1 CONTENT CITIZEN OR SPECIALIST!!". The histograph showed "(1/21)*6 = .3" and then "(2/22)*6 = .5", which is the same per-turn term averaged over the turns played. Cartouche Bee's formula, same thread, with his "*4" dropped: "InternalScore = [(Happy Citizens * 2) + Content Citizens + Specialists + ScoringTiles + (FutureTechs)] * difficulty". Aeson, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/70419-1.htm (Dec 2002), only "17.7 points from 26 future techs on Deity" (fetch summary), which fits a per-turn term earned late. MANUAL p.123: "Each Future Technology you discover adds to your final score". README-V 1.17f: "Fixed bug which miscalculated average scores (esp. for future techs)." This settles the former UNVERIFIED item.
  • Aeson's summary (vanilla), same 70419 thread: "Everything is multiplied by the difficulty factor. 1-6, Chieftain-Deity."; "Population consists of 1 point for every specialist or content citizen in your civ, and 2 points for every happy citizen."; "Territory is 1 point for every tile within your cultural borders."; "Adding up every turn score and then dividing by the number of turns played yeilds the base score."; the bonus is "(2050 - Date) * Difficulty" "for any victory condition".
  • Territory is cultural borders, not city radius. chiefpaco, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/43643-1.htm (28 Feb 2002, one Regent test): territory score is the average land area from F11 ("square miles ... divided by 100") over the turns played, times difficulty. Technoron, same thread: cultural borders, not city radius, decide the land count. PEDIA-C3C #GCON_Victory: the histograph "looks at the total "Score" generated by all civilizations turn by turn over the course of the game."
  • Land and coast tiles; the average is rounded down (Hall of Fame staff). Dianthus, explaining a 0-point score, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/lulthymes-0-point-histograph.114890 (22 Mar 2005, C3C era): "The score for each turn is based on the number of land/coast tiles in your territory" and "the number of happy/content citizens and the number of specialists"; "No points are gained for destroying a civilization." The player "finished in 2050AD" (no finish bonus) and "His average was above 0, but less than 0.5, so the score got rounded down to 0." LulThyme (same thread): "I am not sure how rounding is done for score."
  • Difficulty multiplier. In C3C it is 1 to 8. SirPleb (68813): Conquests multipliers are "Chieftain: 1, Warlord: 2, Regent: 3, Monarch: 4, Emperor: 5, Demigod: 6, Deity: 7, Sid: 8". Before Conquests they were "Chieftain: 1, ... Deity: 6".

5.2 Early-victory ("finish") bonus

  • The bonus. A win before 2050 AD adds (2050 - FinishYear) * Difficulty, and BC years count as negative.
    • Maximizing-your-score: "If you win before 2050AD, you get a bonus which is added to your regular score." Its example is a Regent win in 310 BC: (2050-(-310))*3 = 7080.
    • SirPleb (14617): "The bonus is (2050 - CurrentDate) * difficulty." He says it is the same for every victory type.
    • Bamspeedy (37091): "the finish bonus which is only dependant on what year you win."
  • Based on year, not turns. Because it is based on the year, earlier wins get a larger bonus. BC-era wins benefit most because BC years count as negative. The term "Jesus score" was not found in any source (see UNVERIFIED).

5.3 Demographics screen (community, PTW debug tests)

The demographics screen does not feed the score. Its rows were worked out by Bamspeedy, "How the Demographics works", https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-the-demographics-works.37033/ (19 Nov 2002, PTW, debug mode). Quotes:

  • Approval rating: "The percentage of your people that are happy." Bamspeedy later agreed (post #17, Dec 2002) that specialists are not counted as content.
  • Population: "Add up all the population you get from the city view from all your cities."
  • GNP: "Total gold in all your cities before corruption takes a bite out of it."
  • Mfg. Goods: "Total unwasted shields in all your cities."
  • Land Area: "# of tiles in your territory * 100."
  • Literacy: "% of your citizens who live in a city with a library, university, AND research lab." Edit (post #10): "When you get the literature tech, your literacy goes from a minimum of 0% to 3%, and 3% is added to your literacy rate." Great Wonders with the scientific flag can also count for a city, though not Copernicus's (his edit, summarised).
  • Disease: "% of the tiles in your territory that is floodplains or jungle."
  • Life expectancy: "% of your citizens who live in a city with a granary, aqueduct, AND hospital", between 20 and 99 (summarised).
  • Family size: "The average amount of excess food that each city is producing/2."
  • Military service: "10 years * # of military units / # of citizens."
  • Pollution: the number of currently polluted tiles (post #10, summarised).

Not rechecked for C3C, which added Marsh as a disease terrain.

Disputed

Items marked RESOLVED were settled in the deep pass of 2026-10-20 and are kept so the history can be traced.

  1. Which difficulty the AI uses for content citizens, quelling, barbarian bonus and corruption/OCN. LARGELY RESOLVED (1.2). Each player has its own difficulty level (Leader.player_difficulty, C3X-SRC), and for the OCN percentage the code uses that per-player level (C3X-NOTES ocn_formula.txt). For the AI it is the default level, Regent in the stock rules (alexman 56739: "If you haven't changed that, it's Regent"; BIQ-C3C DefaultDifficultyLevel=2). That matches TheNiceOne (37490) and Bamspeedy's "default AI intelligence" (37490). Pyrkaige's Monarch (68642) is not supported; alexman answered it for C3C 1.0 (Apolyton 101437, 13 Nov 2003, 1.2). Content citizens are settled by Soren Johnson (Apolyton 52411, 1.2: "for the standard bic file, the AI always plays at Regent"). Military law is 1 at every level. Still open: a code or test confirmation for the barbarian bonus (TheNiceOne's 200% at Deity is a claim). Note the separate AI-only OCN term for Monarch and above (1.2), which is tied to the game's difficulty, not the AI's own level.

  2. Deity values. RESOLVED. BIQ-C3C DIFF: Deity cost factor 6, OCN 60%, AI-AI trade 170; Demigod 7, 70%, 160. The 0.7 figure belongs to C3C Demigod. Vanilla/PTW Deity was 70% and 160 (Pyrkaige 37490, 68642; TimBentley 104294).

  3. How the cost factor applies to research. RESOLVED in 04-science-and-wonders.md 4.1. Since 1.29f the AI pays a constant (Regent) tech cost and the human's cost is scaled (alexman, Firaxis: "constant for all levels for the AI"). EDHELP still describes the factor as "applied to AI growth, shields, and research".

  4. Commercial. OCN PART RESOLVED. The code adds floor(base/4) to the base OCN (C3X-NOTES), and Firaxis via alexman (76255): "increases the OCN by 25% (it was 10% prior to 1.29f)". Sullla's "12.5%" was a pre-1.29f corruption reading. The city-tile commerce amount is still open; see 02-city-economy.md Disputed 11. Nor Me (76255, PTW 1.14f) gave "+2 commerce per city and +3 per metropolis" (fetch summary), against the FAQ's +1/+2.

  5. Industrious in C3C. RESOLVED by KayEss's test (07-map-terrain-and-workers.md 5.2): +50% in C3C, +100% in vanilla and PTW. Slaves round down to no gain before Replaceable Parts, which explains stachnie's view.

  6. Agricultural under Despotism. RESOLVED. C3X-INI (code): "the Despotism tile penalty does not apply to food yield from city tiles of Agricultural civs with fresh water." TimBentley's "on a river" is the river case of fresh water. Arathorn's "except despotism" is the case without fresh water.

  7. Seafaring city-tile commerce. RESOLVED: every coastal city. PEDIA-C3C: "Each city built along the coast receives a commerce bonus in the center city square"; Dominae (AU501, 106962); Arathorn (104294). The StrategyWiki "capital" claim (never fetched) has no support.

  8. Favourite government.

    • EDHELP: the civ "will immediately switch to this government type as soon as it becomes available", and avoids its shunned one "at all costs".
    • Code: the shunned government adds 5 attitude points (FLINTLOCK-WAR), so the setting affects attitude too. The government choice itself is a score (Leader_ai_eval_government) whose terms are not published.
    • chiefpaco and Propaganda (21298), wilbill (05-governments.md): it only affects attitude, and AIs "always choose Democracy" even when it is shunned. Not resolved: whether the "immediate switch" happens in play.
    • C3X-THREAD (code reading): Flintlock (post-16217016, page-30 #583): "Shunned/favorite government is worth 12.5%" of the government score. So in the code the setting is a weight in the score, not EDHELP's immediate switch. That is the stronger source and explains the players' "always choose Democracy".

  9. Build-often/never: how many flags are effective.

    • Ozymandias (tested): more than 3-4 and the AI ignores them all.
    • Predator145, citing alexman second-hand: 6.
    • C0rTeZ48 recalls more than 2 Build Often having no effect.
    • The stock rules give every civ 1 to 5 flags (2.1 table), so the default civs sit under any of these limits except possibly the Byzantines (5).

  10. AI amphibious ability.

    • Soren (255541) and the C3C notes say it can mount naval invasions and escorts transports. C3X-INI (code): transports get 1 to 3 escorts by cost and cargo.
    • Ision (92917) and several posters say its landings are too small to hold what they take. dexters (88537, PTW): "landing parties sail back and forth between targets and never are able to land".

  11. UN election interval. CFC FAQ: every 11 turns. Jaybe, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/93177-1.htm (29 Jul 2003), correcting a "20 turns IIRC" post: "11 turns, actually." Other recollections in https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/51960-1.htm (Jun 2002) say 4, 10, 20 or 40, all hedged. Lean: 11.

  12. AI city spacing.

    • Dominae (57896): the AI follows about 4-tile spacing at all levels.
    • Brain (113953): the AI "often leaves big gaps". Vanilla posters (42759, 50345) say it fills every spot.
    • Code gives only the hard minimum (one empty tile) and a per-tile desirability score (3.3). Nobody measured the typical spacing.

  13. The build categories. Soren (56961, vanilla): "Wealth = marketplaces/banks/workers, not just 'Wealth'". Predator145 (692300, C3C): Wealth is "the shield-to-gold project". Soren's answer is the developer's. C3C may differ.

  14. Domination percentage in the Civilopedia. PEDIA-V says 66%. PEDIA-PTW and PEDIA-C3C say 70%. BIQ-C3C GAME stores 66 and 66, and the MANUAL says "two-thirds". Treat 66% as the C3C rule. The PTW figure is not checked against the PTW rule file.

  15. AI extra start units: at game start or at the first city? (37490 page 2, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-difficulty-level-bonuses.37490/page-2.) Spoonwood (21-22 Sep 2025, debug-mode tests on a scenario save at Demigod and Sid): "difficulty level isn't the only factor for the AIs to receive extra units"; extra units appeared only after an AI founded a city, and a 4000 BC retire screen showed equal power bars, "suggesting that AIs don't start in 4000 BC with extra units." justanick (22 Sep 2025): "When you start a regular game, a Sid AI will start immediately with all extra units it is supposed to have"; he guesses the scenario lacked starting positions. Pyrkaige (page 1, 2002): "At Monarch and above, the AI civs get extra offensive and defensive units when they start the game." Largely resolved by code reading: Flintlock (C3X-THREAD post-16708336, page-122 #2421): "AI players receive their set of free units from difficulty level whenever they found their "first" city", that is, "if the owner is an AI and their city count now equals 1, spawn in the free units." So the units come with the first city, as Spoonwood saw; on a random map the AI usually founds it on turn 1, which is what justanick and Pyrkaige saw. It also means an AI that loses all cities and re-founds one gets the units again (Flintlock's explanation of tjs282's report, #2418).

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • "Jesus score." No source uses this term. The likely meaning is the year-based finish bonus, where BC years count as negative, so a BC win gets more than 2050 points × difficulty. Confirm what the requester meant.
  • Culture victory in C3C. The twice-the-rival condition is in PEDIA-V and PEDIA-PTW and in C3C player reports, but not in PEDIA-C3C. Whether eliminated civs count as "the next best civ" is unanswered (Jivilov, 356991).
  • Domination tiles. Which water tiles count. Coast and sea count for territory (but Dianthus, 5.1, says the score counts "land/coast tiles"). Spoonwood (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/histograpgh-attempt-close-but-no-cigar.657407, Apr 2020): "sea squares do NOT count towards your domination limit." Aeson and SirPleb disagree about the domination share (43643), and Maximizing-your-score says sea is excluded from the limit.
  • Seafaring sinking. The tested 50%/75% survival figures are in 01-combat-and-units.md. There is no Firaxis number.
  • Commercial building discount. BIQ-C3C flags Marketplace, Bank, Stock Exchange, Harbor, Airport and Commercial Dock as Commercial, but neither PEDIA-C3C nor the FAQ lists a Commercial building discount. Test it.
  • Industrious city-tile shield bonus. The size thresholds are in 02-city-economy.md, not re-sourced here.
  • Expansionist. The exact goody-hut table shift is in 07-map-terrain-and-workers.md (community tables). PEDIA-C3C's "passive minor barbarians are friendlier" is not quantified.
  • Settlers and workers. How many escorts the AI gives settlers, and when. How the AI scores city sites (Match_ai_eval_city_location) and worker tiles. How it computes how many workers it "needs".
  • War. The delay from plotting to declaring (guesses of about 4 turns), whether the target is the top score or a weighted pick, the exact cooldown after peace (Flintlock believes 8 turns), and how the unit AI uses aggression.
  • AI sliders. Flintlock's reading (3.6): the stock AI uses the luxury slider too timidly. The code sets them each turn (ai_adjust_sliders, capped by the government rate cap). How it chooses the split is not published. Observed research rates were 30-70% (vanilla).
  • Difficulty fields for the AI. Content citizens are Regent's (Soren, 1.2) and military law is the same at every level. The barbarian bonus still lacks a code reading or test.
  • When the AI's extra starting units appear. See Disputed 15.
  • Golden-age trigger. It depends on traits and wonder flags (EDHELP quoted in section 2). Leave it to the golden-age section.

Back to the top

Appendix: where the game still differs

Each compendium section was compared with the engine code. The sourced discrepancies were fixed, with a test each and the source quoted in a code comment. This file lists what the owner ruled on afterwards and what is still open.

Owner's rulings (2026-10-20), now in the code

  1. Industrious city tile: follows the Civilopedia, so the extra shield applies in cities and metropolises.
  2. Resource depletion: left as it is, so only resources in use run out.
  3. Pop rush: follows Civ3's text. Each sacrificed citizen produces 20 shields, and enough are taken to finish the item (the count rounds up).
  4. AI luxury slider: tried in paired AI trials. AIs that never used it scored lower, with more disorder, so the AI keeps the slider. The no-ai-luxury switch remains in AiExperiments.
  5. AI government changes at war: the AI may revolt mid-war when war weariness or a long war presses it, favouring governments without war weariness. This is the default now; trials were within noise.
  6. Great Library: one pass in editor order, with no era limit. A captured Library can take its new owner past Education, as far as the other civs have gone.
  7. Foreign citizens: they never assimilate, but a starving city loses them first.
  8. AI gold per turn: an AI never pays gold per turn beyond its surplus. Under Despotism it puts its surplus into research.

Also done

  • Small fixes:
    • AI start units are granted on the first turn the AI has a city.
    • The 8×8 resistance table now comes from the BIQ.
    • Hoover Dam and the Hydro Plant need a river anywhere in the city radius.
    • Iron Works needs Iron and Coal in its radius. The BIQ improvement flag for this was not being read.
    • A build finishing next turn can't be switched to a wonder.
    • Units join an army only in the city that made it.
  • Previously skipped items:
    • TheNiceOne's AI–human trade markup by difficulty, aggression and attitude. Demigod and Sid are extrapolated (UNVERIFIED).
    • Rate of fire against cities, walls and improvements. Precision strikes still fire one shot (UNVERIFIED).
    • Hut and camp messages use Civ3's script.txt wording, with tribe names from the barbarian list.
  • Missing features:
    • Fascism's Forced Resettlement and Xenophobic rules.
    • Riots: a city kept in disorder may lose an improvement.
  • Sea trade: the deal screen greys out resources when no trade route links the two capitals.

Still open

  • Fascism conquest losses. The Civilopedia says a Fascist civ's "Newly conquered cities also lose population", but gives no amount.
  • Riot target. Which improvement a riot destroys is unsourced. One is picked at random, never a wonder or the palace.
  • Long disorder. Long disorder overthrowing the government is not implemented.
  • Map and combat: global warming, mobilization, bombardment making craters, plague, barricades blocking movement, and colonies overrun by barbarians.
  • Espionage and diplomacy: the "carefully" and "safely" spy missions, AI sabotage and propaganda, AI loans, and the smaller attitude terms.

Back to the top
